# Decisions

Newest at the bottom. Each entry: what, why.

## M00

### D1 Dependency pins
All pins from the M00 prompt were verified with `npm view <pkg>@<version> version` before installing and none changed: three 0.186.1, @types/three 0.186.0, vite 8.3.4, vitest 5.0.4, typescript 6.0.2, @types/node 22.20.5, @types/pngjs 6.0.5, playwright-core 1.56.1, pixelmatch 8.0.0, fast-check 4.10.2, eslint 10.12.0.
Versions the prompt left open (latest at the time, exact, peer ranges checked): pngjs 7.0.0, yaml 2.9.1, tsx 4.23.15, typescript-eslint 8.71.2 (peer: eslint ^10, typescript <6.1.0), @eslint/js 10.0.1 (peer eslint ^10).
Added beyond the prompt's list: `globals` 17.13.0, because the ESLint config needs node + browser globals for `scripts/**/*.mjs`.
`playwright-core` 1.56.1 matches the installed chromium-1194 (1.56.0 would too). Never upgrade it.

### D2 Spike test view
Camera on the +z axis looking at the origin, distance 4, vertical fov = 2*asin(1/4) = 28.955 degrees, so the globe (radius 1) spans exactly the image height (diameter = 540 px; measured silhouette coverage 44.1 %). +x is to the right. Sun fixed at lon -40, lat 20: direction = (cos20*sin(-40), sin20, cos20*cos(-40)) = (-0.604, 0.342, 0.720), unit length. The lit half is the LEFT image half. (The prompt printed x as -0.49; that is inconsistent with its own formula and with unit length, the formula value is used.) Background is pure black.

### D3 Smoke thresholds
distinct colours >= 50; luma std (Rec. 709 luma, all pixels, 0..255) >= 20 (measured 57.6; flat canvas 0); |luma(centre) - luma(top-left corner)| >= 40 (measured 94); mean luma left half >= 1.15 x right half over pixels with luma > 2 (measured 1.52); silhouette (pixels differing > 4 in any channel from the corner pixel) covers 20-60 % (measured 44.1 %). Synthetic empty, noise and checkerboard images are rejected by unit tests.

### D4 Console allow-list
`ALLOWED_CONSOLE_WARNINGS` in `scripts/shot.mjs` is empty: no console warning or error appeared with Chromium 141 + SwiftShader. Any future entry needs an exact-text match and a justification here. Uncaught page errors count as console errors.

### D5 Chromium launch
Switches are the explicit array from the prompt. `--no-sandbox` is not passed by us: Playwright adds it itself when `chromiumSandbox` is false (the default), and Chromium starts fine as root in the container.

### D6 Goldens
`e2e/goldens/spike-test-view.png` was produced in the cloud session. `GOLDEN_MAX_DIFF` is a FRACTION (0.002 = 0.2 %). Mismatches write `test-results/golden/<name>.actual.png` and `.diff.png`. `GOLDEN_COMPARE=warn` logs instead of failing (also for a missing golden).

### D7 CI
Goldens are authoritative in the cloud session; CI sets `GOLDEN_COMPARE=warn` because a different SwiftShader build on GitHub runners may differ slightly. Everything else fails CI. CI uses the full chromium installed by `npx playwright-core install --with-deps chromium` via `CHROMIUM_PATH`.

### D8 E2E server
e2e starts a Vite DEV server programmatically (free port, HMR off) in `e2e/globalSetup.ts` and provides its URL as `inject('baseUrl')`. The e2e project is also serial (fileParallelism false, maxWorkers 1) so only one Chromium runs at a time.

### D9 verify
`verify` = typecheck + lint + `vitest run --project unit` + `vitest run --project perf --passWithNoTests` + `vitest run --project e2e`. Measured about 15 s in the cloud; no sharding needed. `verify:fast` = typecheck + lint + unit (about 6 s).

### D10 Lint details
The Math allow-list selector bans every other `Math.*` member including constants such as `Math.PI` (use a literal). `globalThis` is banned in src/sim and src/ai (it would bypass the global bans). `crypto` / `node:crypto` imports are banned in the pure folders. Boundary rules (imports, DOM globals) apply to src/sim, src/ai and src/data; determinism rules to src/sim and src/ai only; `**/*.test.ts` is exempt from both.

### D11 Spike behaviour
In normal mode the F3 overlay is shown by default and the sun drifts 12 degrees of longitude per second; in `?test=1` the overlay is hidden and nothing moves. Extra debug/test params: `blank=1` (empty canvas for the negative test), `seed=N`, `w=`/`h=` (canvas size in test mode, used by leak tests at 320x180). Styles and the `lang` attribute are set from JS on a wrapper div, not on `<html>` / `<body>`.

### D12 Perf project
`retry: 1` is set for the perf project; `tests/perf.setup.ts` logs it and warns about every retried test. `silent: false` at the root so perfguard factors, retries and shot metrics are visible for passing tests. perfguard reference: 5 M `Math.imul`/array-write iterations, REFERENCE_MS = 5.5 (median on the authoring sandbox); the per-process calibration is a warm-up plus the median of 3 timings.

### D13 CI file location
See `docs/PROGRESS.md` ("CI workflow") for which case occurred.

### D14 Lint bypass hardening and hook install detection
Determinism lint (`eslint.config.js`, src/sim and src/ai): `Math`, `Object` and `Reflect` may only appear as the object of a member expression. Destructuring, aliasing, assigning, passing, returning or wrapping them (`const { asin } = Math`, `const M = Math`, `(0, Math).sin`, `(Math as T).sin`) fails; a plain property read such as `x.Math` is fine. Allowed Math members must be non-computed (`Math[abs]` fails even if a variable is named abs). `Object.getOwnPropertyNames`, `Object.getOwnPropertySymbols`, `Reflect.ownKeys` and any computed member access on Object / Reflect are banned next to keys / values / entries. Every bypass has a case in `tests/lint.test.ts`. The rules guard against accidents, not against deliberate evasion such as `eval` or the `Function` constructor.
SessionStart hook: a missing hidden lockfile in node_modules (npm writes it last) counts as an incomplete install and triggers `npm ci` (90 s timeout). The report field is "npm ci körd denna session" (ja / nej). After a failed or timed-out `npm ci` the hook removes node_modules so that a half-written tree never looks complete and the next session retries. `tests/hook.test.ts` runs the real script against a stub npm.

## M00b

### D15 Portable build
Vite `base: './'` and `build.assetsDir: 'assets'`, so the same files run from any folder (Artifact version folder, a static server under /x/y/, the site root). Two page entries via `build.rolldownOptions.input`: `index.html` and `probe.html`. `worker.format: 'es'` so module workers stay ES modules and import shared chunks from assets. Every chunk therefore lives in assets, which `ROOT_URL` relies on. Tests that build use `tests/helpers/buildDist.ts`, which forces `NODE_ENV=production` (vitest sets `test`, which would bake `import.meta.env.DEV = true` into the bundle; the first artifact e2e run caught exactly that).

### D16 Addresses
`src/app/paths.ts` exports `ROOT_URL` (dev: `new URL('/', self.location.href)`; build: `new URL('../', import.meta.url)`, held in a variable so Vite does not treat it as an asset reference) and `siteUrl(path)`. All runtime data fetches and all page links go through it, also inside the worker. `tests/portable.test.ts` builds the site and fails on `src="/`, `href="/`, url(/ and the string literals "/data/ and "./data/ in dist html, css and assets js; it also scans `src/` for `BASE_URL` and hand-written data paths. `src/env.d.ts` types only `import.meta.env.DEV` (tsconfig `types` stays `["node"]`). The probe link in index.html is hidden in test mode so the golden is unchanged; outside test mode its href is set with `siteUrl`.

### D17 Artifact pack (scripts/artifact-pack.mjs)
Whitelist of served extensions; a dist file with another or no extension fails the run with the list of paths (nothing is skipped silently). Dot-named files (data/.gitkeep) are not published but are reported. The only exception is a data pack named in the data manifest. ASSUMPTION for M01: the source manifest in dist is an object `{name: {file}}` (or `{name: "file"}`) with `file` relative to the data folder; `enc` defaults to raw. The rewritten manifest is `{name: {file, enc}}`, output names are `<file without .gz><container ext>` (`.png`, `.wasm` or `.b64.txt`); the original `.bin.gz` is not published. No manifest (before M01) is fine. Limits checked: 16 MB per text file, 15 MB per binary file, 511 files and 256 MB per artifact; batches have at most 250 files and 60 000 000 bytes. `--prev` ignores index.html and writes `null` only for paths missing from the new build. The files list (files.json) and checksums.txt are written beside the output but are never published. The probe folder is regenerated with `--probe` and otherwise kept, so a normal update does not delete the probe packs (and the probe page keeps working). The link check runs at the end of every pack and fails the run.

### D18 Fragment
`toFragment` masks script and style blocks and comments with private-use placeholders, removes the doctype and the html, head and body tags with `/<\/?(html|head|body)(\s[^>]*)?>/gi`, then restores the blocks. `<header>` and `</html>` inside an inline script are unit-tested. No HTML parser. `<meta>`, `<title>` and `<link>` stay in the fragment (only the wrapper tags go; the skeleton the Artifact adds supplies its own).

### D19 Artifact e2e (e2e/artifact.test.ts)
It builds into a temporary folder and packs with `--probe` there (not into the user's artifact folder, so a stale or hand-made pack can never decide the result). The server mirrors the Artifact: served from /x/y/, 404 for every extension outside the list (also planted .bin/.gz/.zip), index.html inside a minimal skeleton whose head has `<link rel="icon" href="data:,">` (the real skeleton carries the tab icon; without it Chromium requests /favicon.ico). The globe test uses `?test=1` because the server is ours; in a real Artifact the query never reaches the page and a hash grammar arrives in M02. Checks: smoke pixel rules, zero console errors, no 404; a second test loads the animated page, follows the probe link; a third checks the probe table (png, wasm, b64, worker, WebGL2, hash, search all ok).

### D20 Preview routes and results (details for the user in docs/PREVIEW.md)
| route | result | note |
|-------|--------|------|
| Artifact, user's own test page in the real view (iPhone, Claude app) | fungerade | from the user: WebGL2 on Apple GPU, 59 FPS, 262 000 triangles |
| Artifact build served like an Artifact (sub-folder, 404 for unlisted extensions) in headless Chromium | fungerade | e2e/artifact.test.ts |
| Containers png, wasm, b64 + module worker + hash/search in headless Chromium | fungerade | all three give the expected length and FNV-1a |
| Spike published as Artifact | publicerad av orchestratorn, ej bekräftad av användaren | the user answers "klotet syns" |
| Probe table in the real view | ej testat | user pastes the table; if png fails, switch to `--container=b64`, republish and record it here |
| Netlify | EJ MÖJLIG | `netlify-deploy-services-updater` (deploy-site) only accepts a siteId and has no way to send dist; `curl -sI https://api.netlify.com` answered 403 through the cloud proxy. No site was created. |
| Local `npm ci && npm run dev` | fungerade | dev server is what the e2e project uses; probe page loads in dev (worker ok, packs shown as not tested because no probe packs exist there) |
| Local guide on Windows and Mac | ej testat | written from the standard flow, not run on the user's machines |
Default container: png (proven fastest in the real view by the user's earlier test). Not chosen automatically: wasm (the server also gzips it) and b64 (about twice as slow).

## M01

### D21 Bake pipeline and sources
`npm run bake` (`tools/bake/index.ts`): fetch (cached in the gitignored `.cache/bake`), build (`tools/bake/build.ts`, pure), write `public/data/` last through temp files and renames, so a blocked host or a crash never leaves half output. Only `elevation-tiles-prod.s3.amazonaws.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` may be contacted (`tools/bake/fetch.ts` refuses any other host); 403/407 or four failed attempts end in a `BlockedError` that names the URL and says what is blocked. The Natural Earth commit is looked up once with `git ls-remote https://github.com/nvkelso/natural-earth-vector.git HEAD` (result `ca96624a56bd078437bca8184e78163e5039ad19`, the same as the prompt's fallback); later bakes read the SHA from the committed `public/data/meta.json` so a rebake stays reproducible even if upstream HEAD moves (`--ne-sha=` overrides). Natural Earth files: `ne_50m_admin_0_countries`, `ne_10m_populated_places_simple`, `ne_10m_ports` (geojson). Terrarium z4: 256 tiles, 18 345 644 bytes. `--synthetic` swaps in seeded noise and boxes, writes only to `.cache/bake-synthetic`, and `bake:check` rejects a `public/data/meta.json` without a real commit SHA. Node's built-in fetch worked in the sandbox without `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY`.

### D22 Determinism and checksums
No timestamps anywhere; JSON keys sorted; countries ordered by descending area; cities by population; ports by cell. gzip level 9 (memLevel 9) with the header normalised (mtime 0, XFL 2, OS 255). `public/data/checksums.json` = `{version, packs: {name: {file, bytes, sha256, fnv1a, gzBytes, gzSha256, layers: {name: {offset, bytes, sha256, fnv1a}}}}, files: {json name: {bytes, sha256, fnv1a}}}`. `sha256` and `fnv1a` (8 hex digits) are over the UNCOMPRESSED payload and authoritative (`bake:check`, Node tests, and the browser loader checks `fnv1a`, which is pure TS and needs no secure context); `gzSha256` is informational because the deflate stream can differ between Node/zlib versions (`bake:check` prints a note, never fails on it). `public/data/manifest.json` and `public/data/checksums.json` themselves are not checksummed. Two bakes in a row gave byte-identical files (see `docs/PROGRESS.md`).

### D23 Pack layout (`src/data/format.ts`, shared by the baker and the loader)
Two packs. `render` (18 874 368 bytes raw): `height` u8 4096x2048, `bathy` u8 2048x1024, `country` u8 4096x2048. `sim` (4 816 896 bytes raw): Int16 layers first (2-byte aligned, little endian): `meanElev`, `maxElev` (1024x512), `navMaxElev` (256x128); then u8 `land`, `class`, `country`, `coast`, `ruggedness` (1024x512) and `navFlags` (256x128). Offsets are in `public/data/meta.json` (`render.layers`, `sim.layers`) and `public/data/checksums.json`. One render pack (as the prompt says) instead of one file per raster.
Sqrt coding: `metres = max * (code / 255)^2`, land max 9000 m, depth max 11000 m. Code 0 = no land / no water. The smallest code that decodes to at least 1 m is 3 ((3/255)^2 * 9000 = 1.25 m; code 2 gives 0.55 m), so `MIN_LAND_CODE = 3` for heights and depths (the prompt said "code 1"; that would decode to 0.14 m).
Terrain class numbers: DeepSea 0, Shallows 1, Plains 2, Forest 3, Hills 4, Mountain 5, Peak 6, Ice 7. `coast` bits: 1 = land cell next to water, 2 = water cell next to land (8-neighbourhood, longitude wraps). Nav grid (4 x 4 sim cells per nav cell): `navFlags` bits AnyLand 1, AllLand 2, AnyRough (Mountain or Peak) 4, AnyIce 8, AnyCoast 16, plus `navMaxElev`; the prompt did not define the nav contents, this is the minimal useful set. `meta.sim.cosQ15[row]` = round(cos(lat of the row centre) * 32768) for the 512 sim rows (value 32768 near the equator does not fit an Int16, store it as Uint16 or clamp).

### D24 Heights
Terrarium decode `R*256 + G + B/256 - 32768`. Clean on the 4096x4096 Mercator grid BEFORE any mean or max: clamp to [-11000, 9000] (2 pixels were above 9000: the 11 500 m error pixels near 72.4 N 78.8 W), then a 3x3 median (longitude wraps) replaces a pixel only when it deviates more than `SPIKE_MEDIAN_DEVIATION_M = 2000` m from the median: 65 pixels (the Baffin Island error cluster, a few Greenland and Baffin coast pixels, Pacific atoll pixels sitting in deep ocean, a few seamount tops); real peaks survive (Himalaya box max 6727 m before and after). Resampling to the 4096x2048 equirectangular grid: columns line up 1:1, rows are weighted by latitude overlap (area correct), per pixel mean and max. The 56 rows at each pole beyond the Mercator limit (85.0511 degrees) are synthetic: the nearest real row smoothed along longitude with a box of +-32 columns (`POLAR_SMOOTH_COLUMNS`); the polar streaks are visible in the height map (Antarctica interior is Ice anyway). Natural Earth polygons are authoritative for land; land pixels are forced to >= 1 m and sea pixels to <= -1 m.

### D25 Rasteriser (`tools/bake/raster.ts`)
Own scanline fill, pixel centres, half-open row interval (no double counting at vertices), all rings of a feature together under even-odd (holes and islands in lakes come out right whatever the winding). A ring with a longitude jump over 180 degrees is unwrapped and drawn three times (-360, 0, +360) clipped to the grid; edges that run along a pole (Antarctica's closing edge from +180 to -180 at -90) are not treated as jumps. The 50m Natural Earth file has no ring with a jump (already split at +-180), so the real bake never needed the unwrapping; unit tests cover it.

### D26 Thresholds (all in `tools/bake/classify.ts`)
| constant | value | why |
|----------|-------|-----|
| `SPIKE_MEDIAN_DEVIATION_M` | 2000 | prompt; removes the error pixels, keeps peaks |
| `POLAR_SMOOTH_COLUMNS` | 32 | smooth synthetic polar cap |
| `LAND_MIN_PIXELS` | 8 of 16 | land when half or more of the 4x4 pixels are inside a polygon; gave 124 869 land cells outside Antarctica (band 110k to 145k, about 126k expected), not adjusted |
| `RUGGEDNESS_STEP_M` | 12 | ruggedness byte = height range in the cell (max pixel max minus min pixel mean) / 12, clamp 255 |
| `SHALLOWS_MAX_DEPTH_M` | 200 | continental shelf edge |
| `ICE_NORTH_LAT` | 84 | prompt |
| `ICE_COUNTRY_A3` | ATA | Antarctica is Ice everywhere (prompt) |
| `ICE_CAP_MIN_LAT`, `ICE_CAP_MIN_MEAN_M` | 62, 1400 | Greenland's ice sheet (3 670 Ice cells besides Antarctica and the Arctic) |
| `PEAK_MIN_MAX_M` | 5000 | prompt start value, kept (Peak 1.13 % of non-Ice land, band 0.3 to 1.5 %) |
| `MOUNTAIN_MIN_MAX_M` | 1500 | prompt start value, kept (Mountain 12.99 % excluding Peak, band 8 to 18 %) |
| `HILLS_MIN_MAX_M`, `HILLS_MIN_RUGGEDNESS` | 700, 30 | Hills when max >= 700 m or the cell range is >= 360 m (33 588 cells, 27.7 %) |
| `FOREST_MAX_MEAN_M` | 2500 | no forest above |
| `FOREST_PERCENT_BY_LAT` | <10: 75, <25: 12, <40: 35, <60: 60, <70: 40, else 5 | biome rule by latitude (no rainfall data, so the Sahara is only thinned by the low percentage) |
| `FOREST_BLOCK`, `FOREST_BLOTCH_WEIGHT`, salts | 4, 70, 0x9e3779b1 / 0x85ebca6b | integer hash: 70 % from a hash of 4x4 cell blocks (patches), 30 % per cell; no floating point |
| `MIN_PLAYABLE_CELLS` | 8 | microstate limit (prompt) |
| `MAX_CITIES` | 600 | prompt: about 600 largest plus every capital |
| `SNAP_RADIUS_CELLS` | 3 | BFS radius for capitals, cities and ports |
| `COUNTRY_SEARCH_PIXELS` | 12 | render pixels searched when a point is just outside the 50m polygon |
Class order of tests for land: Ice, Peak, Mountain, Hills, then Forest or Plains. Water: Shallows when mean depth <= 200 m else DeepSea. Measured result: 316 497 DeepSea, 33 844 Shallows, 42 230 Plains, 28 273 Forest, 33 588 Hills, 15 740 Mountain, 1 368 Peak, 52 748 Ice cells; not DeepSea/Shallows 33.18 % of all cells, non-Ice land 23.12 %. Box maxima after cleaning: Himalaya 6727 m, Andes 6034 m, Alps 3384 m; highest cell outside the Himalaya/Karakoram box 6034 m (Andes). The prompt's "measured 6563 m" for the Himalaya was lower than the raw grid's 6727 m, so the median filter does not cut peaks.

### D27 Countries, capitals, cities, ports, overrides
Country ids 1..242 follow descending polygon area (`ADM0_A3` is unique, 242 features; Russia id 1; Antarctica is a country and has id 2); painting goes from large to small so a smaller feature wins an overlap. A sim cell's country is the majority of its land pixels (ties: lower id); water cells have 0. `public/data/meta.json` per country: id, a3, `name_en` (NE `NAME`), `name_sv` (NE `NAME_SV`, falls back to `name_en`), `name_long` (NE `NAME_LONG`, extra: `NAME` is abbreviated for some countries, for example "Dem. Rep. Congo"), continent, population (`POP_EST`), cells, `weightedAreaKm2` (sum of the cos-weighted cell areas), capital cell, capital source, playable.
Capitals: places with `featurecla == "Admin-0 capital"` (the prompt also says `adm0cap = 1`, but 3 such records have `adm0cap = 0`, for example Juba, and Hamilton has `adm0cap = 1` with featurecla "Admin-0 region capital"; the featurecla rule is used). The country comes from the country-id raster at the point (not from `ADM0_A3`: South Sudan is `SSD` in the places file and `SDS` in the countries file); if the point is just outside the 50m polygon the nearest country pixel within 12 pixels is used. The capital is snapped to the nearest land cell with the same country id by ring search up to 3 cells (Chebyshev rings, nearest centre inside the ring wins, cells already taken by another city are skipped). A country without a capital post gets its most central land cell (closest to the mean unit vector): 15 countries, among them Antarctica, Greenland (Nuuk is an "Admin-0 region capital"), Israel (Jerusalem sits in the Palestine polygon) and small islands with 1 to 13 cells; the 58 countries without any land cell have no capital cell (null). All 164 countries with >= 8 cells have a capital cell on land with their own id (tested).
Cities: the 600 largest by `pop_max` (excluding scientific stations, meteorological stations, historic places) plus every capital, snapped the same way, one city per cell (capitals first); 27 could not be snapped and were dropped (25 of them microstate capitals with no land cell). 660 kept. Ports: all 1 081 snapped to the nearest land cell with the coast bit (not Ice), one port per cell, ordered by cell: 1 006 kept. A port's country comes from the cell's country id. Ports are in `public/data/ports.json` (the prompt's list did not name a file for them).
`public/data/overrides.json`: `minPlayableCells` 8; `unplayable` (71 countries with fewer than 8 cells and no land neighbour country); `merged` (7 small countries that touch a country with >= 8 cells, merged into the neighbour with the most touching cell pairs: Brunei into Malaysia, Gambia into Senegal, Hong Kong and Siachen Glacier into China, Lebanon into Syria, Luxembourg into France, Palestine into Israel); `neutralNames` (en and sv) for the disputed or unrecognised features Antarctica, Northern Cyprus, Falkland Islands, Chagos Archipelago, Israel, Siachen Glacier, Kosovo, Palestine, Western Sahara, Somaliland. Pack ids are never changed; later milestones apply the lists. Borders are Natural Earth's de facto lines. 164 of 242 countries have >= 8 cells (the prompt expected 150 to 170).

### D28 Loader (`src/data`)
`DataLoader` takes an injected `manifestUrl` and `readBytes(url): Promise<Uint8Array>` (src/data never imports `src/app/paths.ts`; `src/app/debugData.ts` passes `siteUrl()` on `public/data/manifest.json` and a fetch based reader). Pack and JSON files are resolved relative to the manifest (`new URL(file, manifestUrl)`). For `enc: "b64"` the text is decoded to bytes first (`Uint8Array.fromBase64` if present, else a `charCodeAt` loop over `atob`, never `Uint8Array.from(atob(s), fn)`), then the gzip magic `1f 8b` is checked: without the magic the bytes are taken as the already inflated payload (a server may have decoded them). gunzip = `DecompressionStream`. The payload length and fnv1a are checked against `public/data/checksums.json` (`DataIntegrityError`); a flipped byte in the .gz fails the inflate (CRC), one in an inflated payload fails fnv1a, one in a JSON file fails fnv1a. Typed views: `RenderPack`, `SimGrid`, `WorldMeta`, `City`, `Port`, `Overrides`. The fs reader lives in `tests/helpers/dataFiles.ts` only. `src/app/dataPack.ts` (M00b, used by the probe page) is left as it was; it duplicates `fnv1a32`, `decodeBase64` and `gunzip` from `src/data/bytes.ts` (backlog).

### D29 Earlier test touched
`tests/artifactPack.test.ts`, block "data packs from data/manifest.json": the fixture copies a real build, which since M01 contains the real packs and manifest from `public/data`; the fixture now deletes `public/data/` from its copy before planting its own small pack and manifest. Without that the real `render.bin.gz` and `sim.bin.gz` would be unlisted `.bin.gz` files, which the packer correctly rejects. Nothing was weakened: the same assertions run on the same planted pack.

### D30 Debug page and preview
`debug/data.html` is a third Vite entry; the script is `../src/app/debugData.ts`. Mode: `#map-class|height|country` (wins) or `?map=...`, three buttons that rewrite the anchor with `history.replaceState`, hover line with cell, class, elevations and country. `index.html` carries `Debug: klasskarta / höjdkarta / landkarta` with relative links `./debug/data.html#map-...`; `src/app/main.ts` then rewrites the hrefs with `siteUrl()` (so they work inside an Artifact) and hides the row in `?test=1` (the spike golden is unchanged). No address in the page source starts with a slash. The class map is drawn at 1 pixel per sim cell on a 1024x512 canvas; `e2e/debugData.test.ts` classifies the canvas pixels by exact palette colour and golden-compares the page (`e2e/goldens/debug-data-class.png`). `e2e/artifact.test.ts` packs the real build with the png and the b64 container and loads `debug/data.html` through both (checksums verified in the browser).

## M02 (Checkpoint A)

### D31 The globe is the default scene, the spike is kept as `?scene=spike`
`src/app/main.ts` builds the page shell and then starts `bootGlobe` (`src/app/globeApp.ts`); `?scene=spike` runs the M00 spike unchanged (`src/render/spike.ts`). Earlier tests touched, with the reason: `e2e/determinism.test.ts` (golden `spike-test-view.png` and the seed test) now pass `scene=spike`, because the golden and `seed` describe the spike's noise texture and the globe has no seed; nothing else was edited. The smoke test, negative test (`blank=1` hides the canvas), leak test (60 renders at 320x180) and the artifact test run on the globe with their thresholds unchanged. To keep the M00 smoke threshold "left half >= 1.15 x right half" honest the start sun is 67 degrees west of the start view (`DEFAULT_START_TIME`), so the terminator falls on the right half. `parseParams` keeps its result shape (an M00 test pins it): the new `scene` and `quality` come from the separate `parseSceneParams`; the unified parser with the hash grammar replaces both later in M02.

### D32 World units, camera model and limits
One world unit is one Earth radius (6371 km); the sphere is at radius 1 and the terrain is displaced radially by `HEIGHT_EXAGGERATION * height / 6 371 000`. The camera is described by the focus point on the SEA-LEVEL sphere (lat, lon), `heading` (compass bearing the camera looks toward, 0 = north up), `tilt` (0 = straight down, limit 72), and `distKm` = distance from the camera to the focus point (limits 220 and 42 000 km). Up, right and forward come from the analytic east / north frame, so they are defined at the poles. Interaction: left drag grabs the globe (the sphere point under the cursor follows the cursor; heading is locked, so north stays up), release gives inertia (angular velocity, time constant 0.45 s, no fling after a pause of 80 ms), wheel zoom eases in log space (time constant 0.12 s) and keeps the point under the cursor fixed EXACTLY (the look direction is rotated rigidly with the focus, so the heading may change a little, the small roll of a trackball), right drag or ctrl/shift drag turns heading and tilt. While interacting the focus latitude is limited to +-88 degrees (heading locked to north would spin wildly closer to the pole); `setView` accepts +-90 and stays finite (tests at both poles for every heading and tilt). Near and far planes follow the distance. The camera looks at sea level, not at the displaced surface (a mountain-centred focus is in the backlog).

### D33 Sun and time
The sun is a pure function of the fx time `t` (seconds): declination `SUN_DECLINATION_DEG` = 14, sub-solar longitude `SUN_LON_AT_T0_DEG - 360 t / SUN_DAY_SECONDS` (it moves west, one day per 1800 s). `__test.setView({time})` sets the fx clock to `time` seconds; `timeForSubsolarLon(lon)` gives the time for a wanted sub-solar longitude (used by the canonical views and by tests that must put a city on the night side). The prompt left the unit of `time` open; the fx clock is the only clock, so `time` is fx seconds. All of this is in `src/render/config.ts` and `src/render/geo.ts`.

### D34 Terrain shader decisions (`src/render/shaders/terrain.ts`)
- Heights are filtered LINEARLY on the sqrt code by the vertex shader and by `HeightField.heightM` (same maths, so CPU picking matches the picture). The fragment shader never trusts the filtered texture for the COAST: land is a bilinear blend of the binary mask (code 0 = sea) read with `texelFetch`, thresholded at 0.5 with a `fwidth` ramp, so the coast follows the 4096x2048 raster and not the coarse mesh. Land height and sea depth use normalised convolution (land-only and water-only texels), so the coast neither sinks to 0 nor lifts the water.
- Biome tone: cubic B-spline over 4 x 4 sim class cells, land cells only. A plain bilinear mix showed the 4 x 4 block pattern of the baked Forest/Plains rule as a checkerboard.
- Shading normal = slope normal of the exaggerated height field at the resolution of the raster (central differences, step at least 1.5 raster pixels and 1.5 screen pixels, so it does not shimmer when zoomed out) plus the mesh facet normal from `dFdx` and `dFdy` of the displaced position, weighted by `FACET_WEIGHT` = 0.5. The prompt asks for derivative flat shading; on its own it only shows the coarse mesh (156 km facets at the test tier) and Himalaya and Alps looked like smooth blobs.
- Snow line `mix(6900, 1300, smoothstep(8, 65, |lat|))` metres (about 5300 at 28 N, 2750 at 46 N); the first values made the whole Tibetan plateau white.
- Water colour from bathymetry depth: turquoise at the coast to deep blue below 6500 m. Lighting is done in linear space (albedo to the power 2.2, result to the power 1/2.2); ShaderMaterial does no colour conversion by itself. No mipmaps (R8 data textures, LinearFilter).
- Shader modules: `src/render/shaders/common.ts` and `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts` now; the ocean and atmosphere modules belong to item 3 (checkpoint B). The country-id texture exists and is read back in tests, but no shader samples it before item 4.

### D35 Quality tiers (`src/render/quality.ts`)
test = low = 256x128 mesh (65 536 triangles), medium = 512x256 (262 144), high = 1024x512 (1 048 576, "about 1 M"). Pixel ratio caps 1 / 1 / 2 / 2, MSAA off on test and low. Default without `?quality=` is medium (the earlier real-device test ran 262 000 triangles at 59 FPS). `?test=1` forces test, also against `?quality=high`.

### D36 The Himalaya relief control
The prompt compares the luminance variance of the Himalaya view with a flat control (exaggeration 0). The plain variance of the relief image is LOWER than the control's (relief darkens as well as brightens; the colour edges dominate both). The test therefore measures what the relief adds: the variance of the per-pixel luma DIFFERENCE between the relief render and the flat control over the central region (1299 measured, threshold 300), and the same measure of two flat renders against each other must be exactly 0. `__test.setExaggeration(x)` is the test-only switch.

### D37 "No other clocks" test
`tests/fxClock.test.ts` scans `src` for `Date.now`, `new Date`, `performance.now`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `setInterval`, `setTimeout` and `getTime()`. Allowed files with a stated reason: `render/clock.ts`, `app/globeApp.ts` (the one frame driver and the first-frame measurement), `src/app/main.ts` (spike scene), `app/debugData.ts`, `data/loader.ts` (timing logs), `probe.ts`. A later file that needs real time (for example the perf overlay) is added to that list with a reason; cosmetic code must read `fxNow()`.

### D38 Texel readback
`src/render/textureProbe.ts` renders one pass into a 64 x 1 RGBA8 target with a `texelFetch` shader (64 texel coordinates per pass as a `vec2` uniform array) and reads it back with `readRenderTargetPixels`. R8 normalised to float and written back to RGBA8 is exact in SwiftShader (all four textures, 300 scattered texels plus corners and the antimeridian columns, equal to the source bytes).

## M02 (Checkpoint B and the last step)

### D39 Atmosphere (`src/render/shaders/atmosphere.ts`)
One single-scattering function, `atmosphereScatter(ro, rd, tMax, sun, steps)`, serves two consumers: the terrain fragment shader (6 steps from the camera to the surface point: haze and the glow on the disc near the limb) and the back-face additive shell (10 steps, a 96x48 sphere of radius `ATMOSPHERE_TOP` = 1.07 Earth radii, `BackSide`, `AdditiveBlending`, depth test on, no depth write, drawn after the opaque globe). Density falls with `exp(-altitude / ATMOSPHERE_SCALE_HEIGHT)` (0.011 radii, stylised: the real shell is 1.016 and would be a hairline at globe scale); sunlight at each sample is blocked when the sun ray passes inside the unit sphere (soft edge), the colour goes from blue to orange-red as the sun gets low, the result is `radiance + transmittance`. The shell works with the camera inside it (the closest zoom is 220 km). The lit factor is a `max` of two smoothsteps so that it is continuous across the terminator: a hard `up > 0` step drew a visible line on the sea at dusk (found in a screenshot). Numbers are in `src/render/config.ts`.

### D40 Ocean (`src/render/shaders/ocean.ts`)
Eight plane waves on the sphere (wave numbers 260 * 1.75^i per radian, i.e. 154 km down to 3 km), slope 0.065 for the largest and 0.8x per octave, a slow phase warp breaks the crests. Every octave fades out by its period in PIXELS (`smoothstep(16, 56)`), so zoomed out the sea has no stripes or moire and the glint is a smooth patch; the first version (fade at 9..28 px) striped the whole sea at 8000 km. The time is the fx clock, so in `?test=1` the normals are frozen without an extra switch. Fresnel (`OCEAN_F0` = 0.02, power 5) mixes the lit water body with a sky colour (horizon to zenith by the reflection angle, night sky at night); the glint has a broad lobe (strength 0.6, exponent 45) and a tight lobe (1.8, 900), both gated by the sun being above the local horizon.

### D41 Country borders and the palette LUT (`src/render/shaders/borders.ts`, `src/render/palette.ts`)
Four taps of the NEAREST country id texture at +-0.7 pixel (screen-space derivative of the DIRECTION, not of the uv, so there is no seam at the antimeridian): a pixel is on a border when neighbours have a different NON-ZERO id; id 0 (sea) never counts, so coast against sea is no border. The line is mixed into the land colour with `BORDER_STRENGTH` = 0.34. The palette is a 256x1 RGBA8 nearest texture (golden-angle hue walk, index = country id) used for the border line colour and a faint tint of the land (`COUNTRY_TINT` = 0.06). The border e2e proves the claim with the pack bytes: only pixels with two land ids around them change when the strength goes from 0 to 0.34, coast pixels never change. The border line is as coarse as the 4096x2048 raster (about 10 km stair steps when zoomed to the limit); a signed-distance id raster is in the backlog.

### D42 Night lights (`src/render/nightLights.ts`)
The prompt says "for example a 1024x512 light texture drawn on the CPU"; the texture is 2048x1024 (4 MB less than a float texture, 2 MB as R8): at 1024x512 (39 km per texel) the cities were visibly square at the 2500 km zoom of the acceptance test. Each city of `public/data/cities.json` is a soft core at the CENTRE OF ITS SIM CELL (always a land cell; Manhattan itself falls in a water pixel of the 4096 raster) with radius and strength from log10(population), plus seeded satellite lights (mulberry32 from `LIGHTS_SEED` and the city index): deterministic, no assets. The shader multiplies the texture by the land mask and by a night factor, squares it for a defined core, and adds street-light speckle (hash of 4.9 km cells) that fades out when a pixel covers more than about one cell, so it never shimmers. The look textures (lights, palette) are NOT part of `WorldTextures`: `tests/render/textures.test.ts` pins that every entry of `WorldTextures` is an R8 data texture straight over the pack bytes, and they are not.

### D43 Stars and draw calls (`src/render/shaders/stars.ts`, `src/render/stars.ts`)
2600 seeded points (mulberry32, `STAR_SEED`), uniform on the sphere, brightness `rnd^6` (many faint, few bright), size 1 to 2.6 px times the pixel ratio, a little colour. The vertex shader drops the camera translation and writes `gl_Position.xyww` (depth at the far plane), drawn first with depth test and write off; the globe covers them. The scene is now three draw calls: stars (points), globe mesh (65 536 triangles in the test tier) and atmosphere shell (9 024 triangles), 74 560 triangles in all; the Budgetar table of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` has a row per layer.

### D44 Earlier tests touched in M02 B (with the reason)
`e2e/smoke.test.ts` asserted `drawCalls === 1` and `e2e/globe.test.ts` asserted `triangles === 2 * 256 * 128`: both were exact statements about the globe being the only draw call (the checkpoint A note said so). They now assert 3 calls and 65 536 + 9 024 triangles, and the globe test also reads the Budgetar table. The thresholds of the smoke shot itself (colours, luma std, left/right ratio 1.15) are unchanged and hold. `tests/render/textures.test.ts` was NOT edited (see D42).

### D45 One parser, hash grammar, Dev panel (`src/app/params.ts`, `src/app/devpanel.ts`, `src/app/modes.ts`)
`parseRaw(search, hash)` merges query and hash (hash wins); `parseParams`, `parseSceneParams` and the new `parseAppState` all sit on it, and the shapes pinned by earlier tests are unchanged. A hash token that has characters outside the grammar, an empty key or an empty value is ignored (`#perf_a/b` still gives perf). The value of a toggle is `0` (`#perf-0`), because the query cannot be edited and the hash wins over it; the panel writes `perf-0` when perf is active, also when it came from `?perf=1`. `registerDevMode({id, label, params | href | run, toggle?})`: a mode with `params` writes the hash (current hash tokens kept) and reloads, never `location.search`; a link mode (`./debug/data.html`) must be relative and is written through `siteUrl`. A unit test parses the `?` form and the `#` form of every registered mode and requires identical results. `src/app/main.ts` restarts the scene on `hashchange` (stop function: listeners through an AbortController, rAF flag, GL context released with `forceContextLoss`, DOM removed; the packs are loaded once per page and reused) and a generation guard stops a scene that was replaced while its packs were loading. The corner button (48 px, `Dev`) is visible on every page except `?test=1` (hidden so goldens and page screenshots stay clean; `#devbtn` shows it); F2 works everywhere.

### D46 Reports (`src/ui/report.ts`, `src/ui/perfOverlay.ts`)
`showReport(json)` calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText` synchronously as its first act (the click handler of Kopiera rapport calls it directly, so the user activation is still valid; an e2e fake clipboard records `navigator.userActivation.isActive`), then shows the JSON in a read-only textarea with "Markera allt", a Ladda ner link (blob URL with a `download` name; inert in an Artifact, the textarea is the fallback) and a status line that says whether the clipboard accepted. The perf JSON has `schemaVersion` 1: renderer, vendor, GL version, tier, viewport, `fps` (mean, p95 as the fps of the 95th percentile frame time, p95FrameMs, frame count), `renderInfo` (calls, triangles, geometries, textures), `textureMB`, mode flags, user agent. M08 and M24a extend it. The frame times come from the one rAF driver in `src/app/globeApp.ts`; the overlay modules read no clock (`tests/fxClock.test.ts` is unchanged). In `?test=1` there is no frame loop, so the overlay says so instead of showing fps.

### D47 How the pixel acceptance was read (`e2e/looks.test.ts`)
- Cities: `setView` centred on the city (its sim cell centre), distKm 2500, tilt 0, the sub-solar longitude opposite to the city; the "sea neighbour" is the nearest pixel (spiral search with `pick`) whose pick is sea; luma at the projected pixel >= that + 25 (measured: Tokyo 214 against 24, New York 234 against 24, Stockholm 152 against 25).
- Night view: "nattsidans medelluma < dagsidans / 3" is read as the mean luma of the canonical night view against the mean luma of the SAME camera with the sun above the view centre (measured 26.3 against 105.5, ratio 4.0); light pixels above luma 150: 8068 (>= 20 needed).
- Limb: the surface point 0.25 degrees inside the geometric horizon due west of the view centre (the sun side of the `earth` view), brightest pixel of its 3x3 neighbourhood against the median of six corner pixels of space: 124 against 0.
- Glitter: the mirror point is searched numerically (the surface point whose reflection of the view ray points at the sun), the brightest pixel within 3 px against the mean luma of the sea pixels that face the sun (sun dot > 0.3): 254.8 against 55.9; the test asks for +40.
- Goldens: the six canonical views at 960x540, `e2e/goldens/globe-<id>.png`, two renders per view byte-identical, committed once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` after every shader in the list was final.

## M02 fix pass (after the independent audit)

### D48 Pole tear and the polar caps (`src/render/geo.ts`, `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts`)
The audit saw a dark streak along the 180 degree meridian at the south pole (lat -90, distKm 2500) and radial spokes at the north pole. Two causes, both measured: (1) the pole row of the grid is ONE point, but its vertices read the height texture at different u, and the baked south polar row is not flat (codes 29 to 158, up to 3.5 km, times the x12 exaggeration), so the pole had several radii; the vertex shader now reads every pole-row vertex (uv.y 0 or 1) at one common longitude, `POLE_HEIGHT_U` = 0.5 (`heightLookupUv` in `src/render/geo.ts` is the same rule in TypeScript, `HeightField.heightM` applies it at the exact pole, and `tests/render/globeMesh.test.ts` proves one radius for both pole rows of the real mesh on the real packs). (2) That alone did not remove the visible streak: beyond 85.05 degrees the baked rasters are a smoothed copy of the last real Terrarium row (the 56 synthetic rows at each pole, D24 and the backlog), so height and depth there are radial streaks that grow steeper towards the pole (the finite-difference slope normal of the fragment shader reached 0.4 to 0.7 per radian along 170 to -170 degrees, 15 to 30 times the value at 0 and 90 degrees; the north pole is open sea, so its spokes were the depth colour). The shading terms derived from the rasters (the slope normal and the sea depth colour) now fade out between `POLAR_FADE_START_DEG` 84.5 and `POLAR_FADE_END_DEG` 86.5 (the sea settles to `POLAR_DEPTH_M` 2500 m); the geometry itself is untouched (the ridges of the cap are data, a bake fix is in the backlog). `e2e/looks.test.ts` measures the pixel luma minus its 25-sample moving average on four rings around each pole (before: -13.6 to +6.0 south, -4.4 to +5.8 north; after: within +-3); it fails on the old shader. No golden changed: none of the six canonical views looks at a pole closely enough (the verify of the fix pass compares all six against the committed PNGs: 0 px differ in each, no `UPDATE_GOLDENS`).

### D49 Land and sea in the tooltip (`src/render/describe.ts`, `src/app/tooltip.ts`)
The 1024x512 sim cell and the 4096x2048 render raster disagree at coasts (29 920 render land pixels lie in sim water cells, 40 301 render sea pixels in sim land cells): the old pick took class and country from the sim cell and the height from the render raster, so Sydney read "Hav · Grunt hav · 27 m". Land or sea is now decided by the render raster alone, by the rule the pick already used for its height: a position is land when any of the four render pixels around it is land (`sampleLand().mask` > 0), which is exactly when the filtered surface height is above 0 (a test compares the two on 300 000 random positions: no mismatch). The first version of this fix thresholded the bilinear mask at one half like the shader does; that made the Stockholm point of the existing e2e test "the pixel of a projected city shows land or sea" read Hav (a one-pixel coast band is drawn partly as sea but already has a surface height above 0), so the band stays land, as before, and that test is unchanged. The height printed is the mean of the land pixels around the position (always above 0 on land) and minus the bathymetry depth at sea. On land inside a sim water cell the country comes from the render id raster (the land pixel with the largest weight; the id raster is never 0 on land) and the class from the nearest sim LAND cell within 2 cells (else from the height with the bake's own thresholds 700 / 1500 / 5000 m); on sea inside a sim land cell there is no country and the class is that of the nearest sim water cell (else Shallows up to 200 m, the bake's `SHALLOWS_MAX_DEPTH_M`, which a test compares). Inside sim land and inside sim water the sim values are used unchanged. `tooltipText` additionally derives the class from the height when a caller hands it a class of the other kind, so it can never print Hav with a positive height. Results: Sydney (-33.87, 151.2) "Australien · Slätt · 29 m"; the `public/data/cities.json` points of New York and Rio (sim cell water, surface height above 0) read "USA · Skog · 15 m" and "Brasilien · Slätt · 166 m" (the land pixel beside the coast is a 166 m hill; the surface under the point is 18 m). `tests/tooltip.test.ts` scans about 2.1 million positions (every second render pixel, sub-pixel offset) for the invariant.

### D50 Phone layout of the perf overlay and the clock guard (`src/ui/perfOverlay.ts`, `src/app/devpanel.ts`, `tests/fxClock.test.ts`)
At 390x844 the overlay was 326 px wide (`max-width` was applied to the content box, so with padding its right edge sat exactly on the 48 px Dev button) and the open panel (300 px, right aligned) covered its right half including the Kopiera rapport button. The overlay now uses `box-sizing:border-box`, `max-width:min(100vw - 80px, 520px)` (a 16 px gap to the button; on a 960 px desktop it stays left of the panel) and hides itself while the open panel would overlap it: the Dev panel dispatches `DEV_PANEL_EVENT` on `window` with its rectangle (`src/ui/events.ts`, so neither module imports the other) and the overlay hides when the rectangles intersect and shows again on close. D37's clock guard now also bans `Date(` without `new`, `performance.timeOrigin`, `.timeStamp`, `requestIdleCallback`, `setImmediate`, CSS `transition` / `animation` declarations (also the longhands and `el.style.transition = ...`), `@keyframes` and `.animate(` (Web Animations): cosmetic motion must run from the fx clock in JS. No file needed a new allow-list entry. A self-test checks that every token matches a sample of the clock it names and ignores harmless text such as `updateDate(`.

## M03 Sim foundation (determinism, intents, host seam)

### D51 Ticks, turns and the T + 2 schedule (`src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/sim/time.ts`)
`state.tick` counts completed steps (u32). A `Turn` is the tick at whose step its intents run; in M03 one turn is one tick, so `Tick` and `Turn` are the same number (two names so that a later turn spanning several ticks changes the call sites, not the meaning). An intent submitted while the state is at tick T runs in the step that starts at tick T + `INTENT_DELAY_TURNS` (2): `scheduledTurn(T)`. `step(state, {turn, intents})` runs exactly the turn `turn === state.tick`; anything else is an error RESULT (`turn-mismatch`, `bad-turn`, `too-many`) that leaves the state untouched, so the caller, never the sim, decides what a missing or late turn means (lockstep waits, a replay stops). `rules.ts` holds only `TICK_HZ` 10, `TICK_MS` 100, `INTENT_DELAY_TURNS` 2, `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TURN` 4096 and `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 8; a test checks that everything it exports is a UPPER_SNAKE_CASE constant. The structural limits that follow from the array types (`MAX_SEATS` 255 because ownerId is a Uint8, `MAX_DIMENSION` 4096, `MAX_CELLS` 2^22) live in `src/sim/state.ts` and are not tunables. `ENGINE_VERSION` (state.ts, 1) must be bumped with any change of a rule or a constant in rules.ts; snapshots of another engine version are refused.

### D52 State layout and the match header (`src/sim/state.ts`)
Structure of arrays, parameterised by (width, height): `terrain` Uint8, `elevation` Uint16, `owner` Uint8 (ownerId, 0 = unowned), `stamp` Uint32 (id of the intent that last painted the cell), `rng` Uint32Array(4), and the integers `tick` and `rejects`. Header `{engineVersion, mapHash, seed, config, seats}` plus width and height. `mapHash` defaults to the hash of terrain and elevation (a real match passes the pack checksum). `config.debugIntents` is the only option; it travels as a flag word and a snapshot with unknown flag bits is refused. `seatToOwner` / `ownerToSeat` / `NO_OWNER` / `NO_SEAT` are the ONLY conversion (a source test scans src for `seat + 1` and `owner - 1` outside state.ts). `STATE_ARRAY_NAMES` is the fixed order for hash and serialisation; a test lists every typed array on a state and fails when one is missing from the registry, and a per-array test flips one byte (and the top bit of the last word) and demands another hash. `terrain` and `elevation` never change in M03; `stamp` exists so that the id tie-break is visible in the state (and the hash), not only in the order of events.

### D53 The hash (`src/sim/hash.ts`)
FNV-1a over 32-bit words, in a fixed order: header words, scalars, the generator, then terrain, elevation, owner, stamp, each with its length first; narrow arrays are packed little-endian by shifts (never through a typed-array view). Deviation from the plain algorithm: after every word the state is folded with `h ^= h >>> 15`. Reason, shown by a test: plain word-wise FNV-1a only moves bits upward, so a change in bit 31 stays in bit 31 and two such changes in different words cancel exactly (an owner byte of 128 or more in two places would go unseen). The fold keeps the property that a single changed word always changes the result (every step is a bijection). The hash is 32 bits: a false match is 1 in 4.3 billion per comparison, which suffices for tests and for per-turn desync detection; M26 may want a second 32-bit lane (BACKLOG). `hex32` prints it.

### D54 Random numbers (`src/sim/rng.ts`)
sfc32 with the state in `state.rng`, seeded by four splitmix32 outputs and 12 discarded draws; both checked against an independent BigInt implementation for 1000 draws and pinned with literal values for seeds 0 and 1. `nextBelow(rng, n)` is unbiased by rejection sampling and uses exact integer `%`. `step()` draws ONCE per tick even when nothing happens, so the stream position is part of the hash from tick 1 and a snapshot that forgot the generator would be caught by the snapshot-and-resume property; M04 rules draw from the same stream after that call.

### D55 Q15 sin / cos table (`tools/gen/lut.ts`, `src/sim/sinLut.ts`, `src/sim/detmath.ts`)
Angle unit: 1/65536 of a turn (u16, wraps). The table is a quarter wave of 1025 entries (1024 steps, so 4096 steps per turn), `round(sin(i / 4096 turns) * 32768)` in a `Uint16Array` (the top entry 32768 = 1.0 does not fit an Int16). The 4 low bits of the angle interpolate linearly. Measured over all 65536 angles against `Math.sin` / `Math.cos`: the largest error is 0.983 Q15 units (asserted <= 1); the sine of the quarter turns is exact. `npm run gen:lut` writes the file, `tests/sim/lut.test.ts` regenerates the text and demands byte equality. Negative values are made as `0 - x`, not `-x`: the test of the quarter turns caught `-0` coming out of `-(0)`. `isqrt32` is bitwise (no float at all), `isqrt` uses the exactly rounded `Math.sqrt` plus integer correction and is checked against BigInt up to 2^52. `floorDiv` is exact for safe integers (remainder first, then an exact division).

### D56 Intents (`src/sim/intents.ts`, `src/sim/step.ts`)
Only `Noop{seat}` and `DebugPaint{seat, cell}` exist (DebugPaint is valid only when `config.debugIntents`); every intent also has an `id` (u32). M04 replaces both with Spawn and Attack and must APPEND to `INTENT_KINDS` (the index is the wire code). Inside a turn intents run in the order (seat, id, kind, payload) after validation; a second intent with the same (seat, id) is refused as a duplicate, and which copy survives does not depend on the input order (the one that sorts first). `validateIntent(unknown, ctx)` is total and returns a reason code; an invalid intent is a no-op that adds one to `state.rejects` (u32, wraps) and one reject event to the outbox. The ids come from an `IdAllocator` (one counter per seat, from 0): the host hands them out, so the UI submits bodies without ids. There is no replay protection across turns yet (BACKLOG). The first two turns of a match are empty by construction, because nothing submitted at tick 0 can run before tick 2; the golden script respects that, which is why a host-driven run and a direct run of the script are the same run.

### D57 Codec (`src/sim/codec.ts`)
Intent = kind u8, seat u8, id as unsigned LEB128, cell as LEB128 (DebugPaint only); turn = turn varint, count varint, intents. Varints are accepted in their shortest form only, so every accepted byte string has one meaning and re-encodes to itself (a property test over random bytes). Decoding is total and returns `{ok:false, error, offset}`; a count above `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TURN` or larger than the remaining bytes can hold is refused before anything is allocated. The decoder checks only what the bytes can show; whether a seat or cell exists is step()'s verdict. Encoding throws `RangeError` for values the wire cannot hold (a sender-side programming error). Sizes: Noop 3 bytes, small DebugPaint 4, largest 12.

### D58 Outbox (`src/sim/outbox.ts`)
`state.outbox` is NOT part of the state: not hashed, not serialised, never read by step(); three tests (hash with junk in it, bytes with junk in it, a full outbox changing nothing). It holds `Paint` (cell, ownerId) and `Reject` (reason) events, is capped at 8192 events (`dropped` counts the overflow) and is drained by the host after every step, which is also where the diff stream comes from. M03 allows at most 4096 paints per step, but M04 will exceed the cap; the host therefore reads `dropped` and falls back to a full resync (D69).

### D59 Serialisation (`src/sim/serialize.ts`)
Layout in the file header (60 byte header, 8 bytes per cell, a 4 byte checksum), little-endian through `DataView`, so the bytes do not depend on the machine. The checksum is `hashState` of the parsed state, which makes EVERY single flipped bit of a snapshot detectable (tested for all 1024 bits of a small snapshot and for sampled positions of a large one); a hostile snapshot is also range checked (seats, dimensions before any allocation, ownerId <= seats, exact length). `deserialize` returns `{ok:false, error}` and never throws; the arrays it returns are copies (a Node Buffer's `slice` would share memory, so `set(subarray)` is used). `FORMAT_VERSION` 1.

### D60 The golden hashes (`e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json`, `src/sim/scenario.ts`)
One scripted run (seed 1, the 128x64 fixture world of `src/sim/fixture.ts` built from `Math.imul` hash noise and an exact integer division by 15, 4 seats, debug intents on) is hashed at ticks 1, 10, 100, 1000 and 6000. The script is a pure function of (seed, turn): 0 to 3 intents per turn, about one in eight of them deliberately invalid (seat or cell out of range) so that the reject path is in every hash. The JSON also holds the world hash, the engine and format versions and the canary hash. `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 npx vitest run --project unit tests/sim/golden.test.ts` rewrites it; unlike the PNG goldens it is exact and is NEVER compared in warn mode (CI included). Readers: `tests/sim/golden.test.ts` (Node), `e2e/sim.test.ts` and the artifact e2e (Chromium, through `debug/sim.html`), `tests/sim/worker.test.ts`, `tests/host/*.test.ts`, `tests/sim/cli.test.ts`.

### D61 Sensitivity and the broken copy (`tests/sim/golden.test.ts`, `tests/sim/helpers/`)
A stub must not pass: another seed gives other hashes at 10, 100 and 1000; one DebugPaint of one cell changes the hash; a stub that only counts ticks misses the golden. `tests/sim/helpers/nondeterministicStep.ts` is a copy of step that reads `Math.random`; the golden test demands that its hashes differ from the committed ones AND from its own second run. `tests/sim/helpers/hostile.ts` replaces `Math.random`, the transcendental `Math` functions, `Date`, `performance.now` and `crypto` with functions that throw while a full golden run executes (and the copy that reads Math.random is shown to throw under it); the original functions are restored in a `finally`. `tests/sim/sourceRules.test.ts` adds source-level rules ESLint cannot express: no `Map` / `Set` / `JSON` / `BigInt` / `Float32Array` / promises in src/sim, no `.sort()` without a comparator, decimal literals only in the canary.

### D62 The Float64 canary (`src/sim/canary.ts`)
100 000 steps of a dependent chain of +, -, *, / and `Math.sqrt` on doubles (inputs from an integer xorshift), the raw bit patterns of each result folded into the hash through `DataView` with an explicit byte order. These operations are exactly rounded by IEEE 754, which is what the later milestones count on when they use floats in the sim at all (they must not use anything else). Honest limit: Node and Chromium are both V8, so equal hashes prove that the page, the bundler (Vite/Rolldown) and the worker path do not change the arithmetic, not that SpiderMonkey or JavaScriptCore agree; no other engine exists in this sandbox. The canary is the one place with decimal literals, and it uses no transcendental function (a test asserts that `sqrt` is the only `Math` member in the file).

### D63 Host seam (`src/host/`)
`SimHost` = `submitIntent(body)`, `setSpeed`, `advance(dtMs)`, `subscribe(listener)`, `requestSnapshot(): Promise<Snapshot>`, `dispose`. Everything that crosses it is plain data that `structuredClone` copies (`src/host/protocol.ts`); the host reads no clock (`advance` carries the elapsed time, which keeps `tests/fxClock.test.ts` untouched and the host testable). `MainThreadHost` owns the state, copies submitted bodies, stamps ids, schedules for T + 2, runs the ticks the `FixedStepAccumulator` hands out (10 Hz at 1x, 0/1/2/4 speeds, at most 8 ticks per frame, a longer backlog is dropped, paused time is thrown away) and emits ONE diff event per frame (`fromTick`, `toTick`, changed cells with their new ownerId in order, reject count), hash events every `hashEveryTicks`, speed events; every listener gets its own deep copy; a throwing listener is logged and skipped. `createLocalTransport()` is a pair of ports that copy every message with `structuredClone` (so a function in a message throws, a class instance loses its prototype, a transferred buffer is detached) and deliver in order in microtasks. `serveHost(host, port)` and `RemoteHost(port)` are the two halves of a SimHost over a port; with a Worker's port instead of the local pair that is the whole of M24a. A snapshot is the serialised state with its tick and hash plus the host's own bookkeeping (D68). The ownership diff alone rebuilds the ownership array (tested over 1500 and 6000 ticks).

### D64 The worker test (`tests/sim/worker.test.ts`)
A `worker_threads` Worker runs the same code. `execArgv: ['--import', 'tsx']` does not reach the worker thread (the entry loads through Node's own type stripping, which cannot resolve our extension-less imports), so `tests/sim/helpers/simWorkerBoot.mjs` calls the `register()` function of tsx's ESM API inside the worker and then imports the TypeScript worker. The test checks the five golden hashes in the worker, 500 random matches whose per-tick hash trace must equal the main thread's (one persistent worker, 500 round trips), and a `RemoteHost` on the main thread against a `MainThreadHost` served inside the worker over the worker's real port for 6000 ticks.

### D65 Earlier files touched in M03 (with the reason)
`vite.config.ts` (a fourth entry `debug/sim.html`), `src/app/modes.ts` (the Dev panel lists the page, as it does for the data map), `e2e/artifact.test.ts` (one added case: the packed build serves `debug/sim.html` from a sub-folder; no existing assertion changed), `package.json` (`gen:lut`, `sim:run`), `CLAUDE.md` (one command line), `docs/PREVIEW.md` (one line in Swedish). No earlier test, tolerance or golden changed.

### D66 Measured (Node, this sandbox)
Empty-world step at 1024x512: median 0.6 microseconds (budget `budgetMs(1)` about 1.1 ms; the step is O(intents), not O(cells)); a step with 64 DebugPaints about 59 microseconds; `hashState` 2.3 ms and `serialize` 6.2 ms at 1024x512 (checkpoints, not per tick); a 6000-tick golden run of the 128x64 world about 7 ms; the 500-run property suites about 0.1 s each.

## M03 fix pass (after the independent audit)

### D67 The golden scenario now exercises the intent order, and a second seed is pinned (`src/sim/scenario.ts`, `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json`)
The audit showed that no golden hash depended on the canonical order (seat, id) or on the duplicate check: the script never painted one cell twice in a turn. `scenarioBodies` now sends, on one turn in eight, two paints of the same cell from two seats with the HIGHER seat first (so the sort decides the owner), and on another one in eight two paints of one cell from one seat (the later id must win `stamp`). A direct-only ADVERSARIAL variant (`runScenario(..., adversarial = true)`, `scenarioIntents(..., adversarial)`) additionally reverses the raw turn list on half of the turns and repeats one intent (same seat and id) on a quarter of them; a host cannot build such lists, which is why they are a separate pinned set (`adversarial.hashes`, seed 1, the five golden ticks) and not part of the host runs. The reversal does not change the result of a correct step, the repeat adds one reject, so the adversarial hashes differ from the plain ones only from tick 10 on, by the rejects. The file also pins a second seed (`alt`: seed 2, its world hash and the hashes at ticks 10, 100 and 1000), so a script or a fixture world that ignores the seed no longer passes (before, "another seed gives another hash" was satisfied trivially because the header seed is folded into the hash). Chromium (`debug/sim.html`, new hook `adversarialHashes`) is compared with the file for the main seed, the second seed and the adversarial set, so the engine's own `Array.prototype.sort` is covered. `tests/sim/helpers/brokenSteps.ts` holds copies of step without the sort and without the duplicate check (plus a faithful copy as control); the golden test demands that the first changes the main, second-seed and adversarial hashes and the second changes the adversarial hashes. The script changed, so the main hashes of ticks 10 and later changed (tick 1 did not); `ENGINE_VERSION` stays 1 because no rule, constant or array changed: only the test script did. The old values were only ever on this unmerged branch. Wide-value serialisation (owner 0..255, stamp and tick and rejects above 2^16, seeds at both ends of their range, 255 seats) and `createState` with exactly 255 and 256 seats got their own tests in `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` and `tests/sim/state.test.ts`; each of six truncating mutations of `serialize.ts` / `state.ts` now fails them.

### D68 A snapshot is a resume point of the host (`src/host/protocol.ts`, `src/host/mainThreadHost.ts`, `src/sim/intents.ts`)
Intent ids are written into `state.stamp`, so the per-seat id counters of the host are part of the hashed history, and the intents of turns T and T + 1 exist only in the host. A host rebuilt from `deserialize(bytes).state` alone therefore diverged from the straight run (measured on the audit's script: tick 6000 hash differed; counters alone were not enough either). Chosen design (a of the audit's two options): `Snapshot.resume = {idNext: Uint32Array(255), pending: [{turn, intents}]}`, plain structured-cloneable data next to `bytes`, produced by `requestSnapshot()` and consumed by `MainThreadHost.resume(snapshot, options)`, which returns null (never throws) when the bytes do not deserialize, the tick or hash disagree with them, or the resume part has the wrong shape (counter array length, turns below the tick, repeated or unordered turns, intents without a numeric id); the intents inside are refused by `step` like any other input. `IdAllocator` takes initial counters and has `counters()`. Option b (ids from the turn and the index in the sorted turn) was rejected: it would change the meaning of `stamp` and every golden, and ids must stay what a client can predict when it submits. The resume part is NOT inside the checksummed `serialize()` bytes (those stay the state alone, `FORMAT_VERSION` 1), so a durable store (M26 rejoin, M24a restart after a crash) keeps the two together as one `Snapshot` object; `tests/host/resume.test.ts` proves "snapshot at tick k, resume, run on" equals the golden for k in 1, 2, 3, 17, 999, 2501 and 3000, also through `RemoteHost` and `LocalTransport`, and that dropping either part diverges.

### D69 The ownership diff no longer depends on the outbox cap (`src/host/mainThreadHost.ts`, `src/host/protocol.ts`)
`StateDiff.cells/owners` are built from the capped, render-only outbox, which drops silently when more than 8192 events arrive in one step; M04 (a conquest of 100 000 cells in a tick, enclave flips) and the M05 ownership texture would have lost cells with no signal. Chosen (a of the audit's two options): `runTicks` reads `outbox.dropped` before every drain and, if any step of the frame dropped events, the diff carries `fullOwner`, a copy of the WHOLE owner array after the frame, with `cells` and `owners` empty; otherwise `fullOwner` is null. A consumer replaces its mirror with `fullOwner` when it is not null (documented on `StateDiff`; `cloneEvent` and the RemoteHost transfer list copy it). The check is deliberately conservative: reject events share the cap, so a frame that only overflowed with rejects also resyncs (rare, correct, and 8 KB to 4 MB per event at most). Option b (a separate non-lossy changed-cell list in the sim) is the better answer once M04 knows the real bound of changed cells per step; it stays in the BACKLOG as the replacement for this fallback. `tests/host/resume.test.ts` overflows the outbox by 500 events, at exactly the capacity (no resync) and one above (resync), in the middle of a multi-tick frame, and across `LocalTransport`.

### D70 Tighter step budgets (`tests/sim.perf.test.ts`)
The budgets `budgetMs(1)` were about 1000 to 2500 times the measured cost of an empty step (0.4 to 0.8 microseconds) and could not tell an O(intents) step from one that scans or copies a whole array (measured: a loop over the 524 288 owner cells 330 us, `owner.slice()` 260 us, `hashState` 2.2 ms). The empty step is now held to `budgetMs(0.05)` (50 us before the perfguard factor, still the median of 101 steps after 2 warm-ups) and the step with 64 DebugPaints to `budgetMs(0.2)` (200 us, median of 51; measured 47 to 86 us). Checked by mutation: a full owner scan and an `owner.slice()` inserted in `step()` make the perf project fail (362 to 485 us and 264 to 274 us against budgets of 44 to 71 us), and the unchanged code passed three runs in a row.

## M04 Territory sim, Part A

### D71 ownerId is a Uint16 (`src/sim/state.ts`, `src/sim/serialize.ts`, `src/host/`)
Own commit, before any rule changed. `GameState.owner`, `StateDiff.owners` and `StateDiff.fullOwner` go from Uint8 to Uint16, `serialize` writes the owner as `u16` little endian (9 bytes per cell, `FORMAT_VERSION` 2), `hashState` folds it with `foldU16` (two cells per word) instead of `foldBytes`. Reason: M04 gives the tribes the ownerIds after the players (P+1..P+T) and M05's ownership texture reads the same array; a 16-bit field removes the limit from the array type for good. The limit that stays is `MAX_SEATS` 255, because an intent carries its seat as ONE byte on the wire (D57) and a seat is what a Spawn names; widening that is a codec change (a varint seat) and belongs to the day a match needs more than 255 owners (BACKLOG). The `owner-range` check of `deserialize` still demands ownerId <= seats, so a stored value above 255 is refused today. The five golden hashes (seed 1) and the second seed changed ONLY because the owner array is hashed and serialised differently: `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` was re-pinned with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` and nothing else (script, fixture world, rules) changed in that commit; `ENGINE_VERSION` stays 1 (no rule or constant changed, only the width of an array). Earlier tests touched, all by the width only: the layout test of `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` (9 bytes per cell, the stamp offset, the owner byte of the range check; one new test for the 16-bit field), three `Uint8Array` mirrors in `tests/host/*.test.ts` and the serialized size of the golden world.

### D72 ESLint boundary for `src/host` (`eslint.config.js`, `tests/lint.test.ts`)
`src/host` is the code that moves into the M24a Worker, but only `src/sim`, `src/ai` and `src/data` had the import and DOM-global rules. It is now in `BOUNDARY_FILES` (no `three`, `crypto`, `render`, `ui`, `audio`, `app`, no DOM globals). It is NOT in `DETERMINISM_FILES`: the host changes no game rule and legitimately uses `Map` and `console`. The lint self-test got one case with eight forbidden snippets and two allowed ones, linted as if they sat in a fixture file of `src/host`. No existing source file needed a change.

### D73 Units: std-cell, quarter troops, milli-gold, micro-QT (`src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/sim/units.ts`)
The plan's rules were written for a 512x256 grid; the sim runs 1024x512, so a STD-CELL is 2x2 sim cells and a number is converted by what it measures: a distance times `STD_CELL_SIDE` (2), an area times `STD_CELL_AREA` (4), a linear speed times 2, and a RATE of captured cells (cells per second, an area per time) times 4, never 2 (the plan says "omräknas till simceller x4"). The converter is the pair of constants in `src/sim/rules.ts` (it may only export `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` constants, a source test enforces that) plus the functions of `src/sim/units.ts`. Troops are stored in QUARTER TROOPS (QT, `QT_PER_TROOP` 4): a std-cell of Plains costs `BASE_COST` 3 troops = 12 QT, a sim cell a quarter of that = 3 QT = 0.75 troop with no rounding and no ceil to whole troops; `QT_PER_TROOP` was chosen equal to `STD_CELL_AREA` so that this division is exact for any whole `BASE_COST`. Gold is stored in milli-gold (`MILLI_GOLD_PER_GOLD` 1000). There are no decimal literals in `src/sim` (a source test, D61): fractions in `src/sim/rules.ts` are integers with a named scale (`CAPTURE_K_MILLI` 80 = 0.08, `REGEN_SQRT_COEF_MILLI` 350 = 0.35, `UPHILL_PER_STEP_MILLI` 250, `TERRAIN_COST_PCT` 130 = 1.3). The names therefore differ from the plan's by a suffix: calibrate `CAPTURE_K_MILLI` and `BASE_COST_TROOPS` (the plan's CAPTURE_K and BASE_COST). All maths is integer: values stay below 2^53 (a comment at each formula says why), `Math.sqrt` appears only inside `isqrt` (exactly rounded, with an integer correction) and divisions are followed by `Math.floor` or are exact. The remainder of a per-tick amount that is not a whole number of QT is carried in `troopFrac` in micro-QT (`MICRO_QT_PER_QT`), so regeneration near the cap is not truncated to zero.

### D74 Intents: Noop and DebugPaint stay, Spawn is appended (`src/sim/intents.ts`, `src/sim/codec.ts`)
"Spawn / Attack / CancelAttack replace Noop / DebugPaint as the main intents" is read as: the game is played with them; `Noop` and `DebugPaint` (the latter only under `config.debugIntents`) stay because the golden scenario, the sensitivity tests, `debug/sim.html` and `npm run sim:run` depend on them. `Spawn{seat, cell}` is wire kind 2 (after Noop 0 and DebugPaint 1; `INTENT_KINDS` is append-only), layout of DebugPaint (kind, seat, id varint, cell varint), needs no debug switch. `validateIntent` checks the shape only (seat, id, cell range); the state-dependent refusals happen when `step` applies the intent and are `REJECT_TERRAIN` (8: not Plains / Forest / Hills / Mountain), `REJECT_OWNED` (9) and `REJECT_SPAWNED` (10: the seat has a capital already); each counts in `state.rejects` and puts one `OUTBOX_REJECT` in the outbox, and changes nothing else. Every place a new kind must be added is listed in the heads-up of the M03 audit (BACKLOG); this time they were `copyBody`, `withId`, `comparePayload`, `intentSize`, `putIntent`, `assertEncodable`, `Reader.intent`, the test arbitraries and the `StepFn` copies in `tests/sim/helpers/brokenSteps.ts`. Seats and owners: the match header has `seats` (players first, then tribes) and `players`; a tribe is a seat with `ownerId > players`. Tribes spawn through the same intent, sent by the scenario tooling (never by `src/sim`). `MAX_SEATS` stays 255 (D71): a seat is one byte on the wire.

### D75 The capital disc and the Spawn algorithm (`src/sim/spawn.ts`) - an ambiguity of the plan
The plan says "huvudstadsskiva med radie 8 celler", "K = START_DISC_CELLS = 197, heltalspunkter i radie-8-skivan" and, in the same breath, that the rules are ported from the 512x256 design (a std-cell is 2x2). Read through the converter, radius 8 std-cells would be radius 16 sim cells (797 integer points). Read literally, 197 is a count of the cells a Spawn claims, and a Spawn claims sim cells. Chosen (the literal reading): `START_DISC_RADIUS_CELLS` 8 and `START_DISC_CELLS` 197 are in SIM cells (about 300 000 km2 at the equator, a third of Sweden), a test recounts that 197 is the number of integer points in the disc. To switch to the converted reading change the two constants (16 and 797) and re-pin the goldens: nothing else depends on them. Algorithm: Prim-like growth from the chosen cell; a binary heap (typed arrays, no allocation per cell) holds the unowned playable cells next to the claimed region, keyed by (squared distance from the chosen cell with the x seam taken the short way, `hash2(seed, cell, salt)`, cell index); the smallest is claimed, its unowned playable 4-neighbours join. The result is the K nearest CONTIGUOUS cells in distance order, a coast or another seat's land makes it reach further out, a landmass smaller than K gives all of its cells, and ties are decided by the hash of the cell (it depends on the match seed, never on array order); in open land with K = 197 the claim is exactly the radius-8 disc (a test). Cost O(K log K), no map scan: the per-cell mark lives in `state.scratch` and is cleared through the list of touched cells.

### D76 Heights: `HEIGHT_UNIT_M` and what part B decides (`src/sim/rules.ts`)
The plan's uphill factor `1 + 0.25 * dh / 32` (capped x2) needs a unit for dh. `HEIGHT_UNIT_M` is 16 m (own choice): the cap is reached at a rise of 128 units = 2048 m between two neighbouring 39 km cells, a 1000 m step costs x1.49, a 250 m step x1.12. `state.elevation` is the mean elevation in whole metres (sea clamped to 0 by `createSimWorld`), so dh in units is `floor(rise in metres / HEIGHT_UNIT_M)` from two array reads. Part B writes the choice of the incremental definition of dh (between the attacker's source cell and the target cell, no extra data) and of the depth d (BFS depth into the defender's land, kept per front cell, capped at `D_MAX_CELLS`) here, with the reasoning; this entry only records that the constants exist and what unit they are in. The constants of the conquest are already in `src/sim/rules.ts` and `docs/BALANCE.md` with the plan's values.

### D77 Troop cap and regeneration (`src/sim/economy.ts`)
N is the owned area in std-cells, cos-weighted: `warea` (the sum of the Q15 row weights of the owned sim cells, kept by `setOwner`) divided by `STD_CELL_AREA`, as a Q15 number. `N^0.75 = sqrt(N) * sqrt(sqrt(N))` with two integer roots: `s1 = isqrt(N_Q15 * 2^15)`, `s2 = isqrt(s1 * 2^15)`, result `s1 * s2 / 2^15` (floored); against the real power the relative error is below 0.1 % for N up to 40 000 std-cells (a test, and a test of the cap against `(2000 + 120 * N^0.75)`). Cap in QT = `8000 + floor(480 * N^0.75) + 3200 * cityLevels`; a tribe's is `floor(cap / 3)`; the city levels are 0 until M09 (`cityLevels()`). Regeneration per second `max((8 + 0.35 * sqrt(T)) * (1 - T / Tmax), -MAX_DECAY)` is computed in micro-troops per second from `sqrt(T) * 1000 = isqrt(T_QT * 250000)`, multiplied by `(Tmax - T)` and floor-divided by `Tmax`, then turned into micro-QT per tick (`* 4 / 10`), a tribe's positive rate times 50 %, and clamped at `-MAX_DECAY_MICRO_QT_PER_TICK`. The plan names `MAX_DECAY` but gives no number: own choice 40 troops per second (4 per tick; at the plan's cap of 2000 an owner with three times the cap loses it in about a minute). `T` above `4 * Tmax` is treated as `4 * Tmax`, where the rate is already at the clamp (for Tmax >= 2000), so every product stays below 2^53 even for a corrupt snapshot. A tick adds the rate to `troopFrac`, moves whole QT to `troops` and keeps the remainder (0 <= remainder < 10^6); when a negative sum would take `troops` below 0 it is clamped to 0 and the remainder to 0 (T >= 0 after every deduction; `deductTroops` returns what it really took). Measured, solo start disc, equator-like weight: 1200 troops reach about 90 % of the cap after 5 minutes and stay below it. Tmax = 0 gives rate 0 (no division by zero).

### D78 Gold and the start values (`src/sim/economy.ts`)
The plan says "guld från viktad mark (bas så att 500 startguld räcker en stund)". Read as: income proportional to the cos-weighted land, with the coefficient small enough that 500 start gold is a real budget. `GOLD_PER_STD_CELL_MILLI_PER_SEC` 50 (own choice) = 0.05 gold per second per std-cell: a start disc (about 49 std-cells) earns about 2.4 gold per second, so the 500 start gold equal about 200 s of income; a 400 std-cell empire earns 20 per second. No flat base income. Per tick `floor(N_Q15 * 50 / (10 * 2^15))` milli-gold (one std-cell at the equator gives exactly 5 per tick; the floor loses less than one milli-gold per tick, 0.3 % at the start disc). Gold saturates at the Uint32 maximum. A Spawn sets troops to `min(1200 troops, cap)` (the clamp only bites for a tribe on a tiny island) and gold to 500; seats start with 0 of both until they spawn, and `economyTick` skips every owner without cells. "Landandel per säte" is read as a read-out: `landShareQ15` is the cos-weighted share of all playable land an owner holds (Q15), for the scoreboard and the victory condition (M18).

### D79 State layout v3, the derived parts (`src/sim/state.ts`, `src/sim/serialize.ts`, `src/sim/territory.ts`)
New hashed and serialised arrays (appended to `STATE_ARRAY_NAMES`): `rowCos` (Uint16 per row, the committed Q15 cos table of the real world or the sine-table rows for fixture worlds; a test shows the two agree exactly for 512 rows), and per ownerId (slot 0 unowned, `ownerSlots(seats)` slots) `troops` (QT), `troopFrac` (micro-QT), `gold` (milli-gold), `capital` (cell + 1, 0 = not spawned). The header gained `players`.
