Derived, NOT serialised and NOT hashed, built by `assembleState` (also used by `deserialize`): `grid` (neighbours with the x seam, weights, the playable table, total playable weight), `agg` (cells and weight per owner) and `scratch` (an all-zero mark array). They are plain objects, not typed arrays on the state, so the registry test of M03 (every typed array of a state is registered) needs no change. `setOwner` is the ONLY function that writes `owner` during play: it keeps `agg` in step, stamps the cell and tells the outbox; a test compares `agg` with a full rebuild after random changes and after random matches. The Grid refuses (RangeError) a world whose playable weights exceed 2^32 (the real world: 2 816 830 020), which keeps every per-owner sum inside a Uint32. `deserialize` validates the new fields (`players` in 1..seats, row weights <= 32768, slot 0 empty, remainder below one QT, capital a real cell) and returns an error result, never a state the sim cannot run. `ENGINE_VERSION` 2, `FORMAT_VERSION` 3 (64 byte header, 9 bytes per cell, 2 per row, 16 per ownerId). The only other place of the `+ 1` of ownerId = seat + 1 is `ownerSlots`, next to `seatToOwner` (the source test of D52 scans for it).

### D80 Earlier tests and files touched in M04 part A (with the reason)
The golden scenario now spawns (the first turns send two Spawns per seat, about one turn in 25 afterwards one more), so the five hashes cover Spawn, the refusals and 6000 ticks of economy; `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` was re-pinned twice (D71 for the owner width alone, then here for engine version 2, the new arrays and the script) and the Node, Chromium (`e2e/sim.test.ts`, `e2e/artifact.test.ts`), worker and host tests read the same file. `tests/sim/helpers/brokenSteps.ts` is a copy of `step` and was brought in line (Spawn, `setOwner`, the economy) so that its faithful copy still equals the real step. `tests/sim/step.test.ts` used the kind name 'Spawn' as its example of an UNKNOWN kind (it was reserved for M04); it now uses 'Teleport', which will never exist. `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` follows the layout (offsets, sizes, the engine-version patch uses `ENGINE_VERSION + 1`). `tests/sim/helpers/arbitraries.ts` gained Spawn. `tests/docs.test.ts` now also checks `docs/BALANCE.md` for files it mentions. `CLAUDE.md` lists `docs/BALANCE.md` as existing. New tests: `tests/sim/rules.test.ts`, `tests/balance.test.ts` (the table equals `src/sim/rules.ts`), `tests/sim/units.test.ts`, `tests/sim/grid.test.ts`, `tests/sim/economy.test.ts`, `tests/sim/spawn.test.ts`, `tests/sim/territoryState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/economySerialize.test.ts`, `tests/sim/codecSpawn.test.ts`, `tests/sim/territoryProperties.test.ts`, `tests/sim/realWorld.test.ts`. No tolerance was changed.

### D81 Measured (Node, this sandbox, real 1024x512 world, 32 seats)
Load of the pack plus `createSimWorld` 120-130 ms; `createWorldState` 17-31 ms (copies 3 arrays, builds the grid); a Spawn (`claimDisc`, 197 cells) about 0.3-0.5 ms; `economyTick` for 32 owners about 8-16 microseconds; an empty step with 32 spawned owners about 8 microseconds; `serialize` 15 ms (4.72 MB), `deserialize` 23 ms (includes the grid and the aggregates), `hashState` 5 ms. 119 831 playable cells in 295 landmasses; their total weight 2 816 830 020 (limit 4 294 967 295). A start disc at Stockholm-like latitude has 24.6 std-cells of weighted land (cos 0.5 there), cap about 3320 troops; after 5 minutes without conquest 1200 troops have become about 3100 and 500 gold about 866.


### D82 The Attack object and its frontier (`src/sim/attacks.ts`, `src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/territory.ts`)
M04 part B. One `Attack` per (attacker, target) pair, kept in `state.attacks` in order of creation (ids 1, 2, ...; the id is also the `stamp` of the cells the attack takes). It carries the troops committed to it (`F`, QT, taken from the owner when it starts and handed back when it ends), the capture `progress` (micro-cells), the unpaid part of a cell's price (`costFrac`, milli-QT) and its FRONTIER: the target cells that touch the attacker's land. The frontier is kept in one BAG per depth (0 to `D_MAX_CELLS`); the attack takes from the shallowest non-empty bag, and inside a bag the entry that a hash of (match seed, attack id, number of picks so far) points at: a pick is O(1) (index by hash, swap with the last entry, shrink), and ties between cells of equal depth are broken by a hash, never by array order or by the order the cells were found. A first version used a binary heap keyed by (depth, hash of the cell, cell); measured, the heap was 25 to 30 % of the cost of a 100 000 cell burst, the bags are O(1) and equivalent in kind (growth ring by ring, random within a ring), so the bags stayed. The frontier is filled INCREMENTALLY and emptied LAZILY: `setOwner` (the one writer of `owner`) offers, for every cell that changes owner, the neighbours that became frontier cells to the attacks that care (the attack of the new owner on each neighbour's owner gets the neighbour; the attack of each neighbour's owner on the new owner gets the cell itself), and an entry is only checked when it is picked (the cell must still be the target's, playable, and touch a playable cell of the attacker; otherwise it is dropped). A neighbour that already touched another cell of the attacker is not offered again (it is queued already), so duplicates are rare; a purge (`FRONT_COMPACT_MIN`, then at twice the purged size) drops the stale entries. So the bags always hold a SUPERSET of the true frontier, and a test (`tests/sim/frontier.test.ts`) compares them with a brute-force recomputation over the whole map after every one of 1000 random steps of three random matches. A new attack builds its first frontier from the boundary list of its attacker (D87), sorted by cell index so that the result does not depend on the history of that list. Attacks run in an order that is a hash of (seed, tick, attack id), so no attack is systematically first. An attack ends when its frontier is empty, the border is gone (contact length 0), the attacker has no land, or its troops cannot pay for the cell it picked; what is left goes home. Alternatives considered: one heap per attack (see above); a per-tick scan of the border instead of a frontier (rejected: the work counter would grow with the front length every tick).

### D83 d, dh, the speed and the price: the interpretation (`src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/rules.ts`) - the prompt left d and dh open
SPEED. `v = K * sqrt(L) * 2F / (F + Td)` std-cells per second. `L` is the CONTACT LENGTH: the number of 4-neighbour adjacencies between a playable cell of the attacker and one of the target (for unowned land: free playable land; the coast counts for nobody), kept exactly for every pair of owners by the border index (D87); in std-cells it is `L / STD_CELL_SIDE`, so `sqrt(L std) = isqrt(L * CAPTURE_SQRT_SCALE) / 1000` with `CAPTURE_SQRT_SCALE` = 500000. `F` is the attack's own committed troops (so the speed falls as it pays), `Td` the target's troops (0 for unowned land, so the troop ratio is exactly 2 there). The ratio 2F/(F+Td) is kept in millionths, F + Td = 0 gives speed 0 (no division by zero). The cell RATE converts with `STD_CELL_AREA` (x4, not x2) and `/ TICK_HZ`: `micro-cells per tick = K_milli * sqrt_milli * ratio_micro * 4 / (10^6 * 10)`; progress is carried in micro-cells and the whole cells are taken. PRICE per sim cell in milli-QT: `BASE_COST_MILLI_QT (3000, i.e. 3 QT = 0.75 troop) * terrain% * uphill * (DIV + d) / DIV`, multiplied out and divided once so nothing is rounded early (a Plains cell at the border costs exactly 3 QT; the 0.75 is never rounded up to a whole troop, the part of a QT not yet paid is carried in `costFrac`). The defender pays no terrain term and, here, nothing at all (it only slows the attack through `Td`; see BACKLOG). d: THE DEPTH OF A CELL IN THE ATTACK'S FRONTIER, the cheapest incremental definition: a cell that touches the attacker's land when the attack starts, or that another attack's capture exposes to it, has d = 0; a cell exposed by a capture of THIS attack has the depth of the captured cell + 1. So d counts how many captures of the attack lie between the cell and land that already touched the attacker; it costs one addition per discovery and needs no distance transform. It is clamped to `D_MAX_CELLS` (80 sim cells = the proposal's 40 std-cells) when it is stored and again in the price, so it can never exceed D_MAX (unit test). Against UNOWNED land d has the same meaning (there is no defender territory to be deep in), so a solo expander pays more per cell the farther its wave has come: 1 + 80/40 = x3 at the clamp. dh: THE RISE FROM THE HIGHEST OF THE ATTACKER'S PLAYABLE 4-NEIGHBOURS TO THE CELL, in `HEIGHT_UNIT_M` (16 m) units of the mean elevation, floor; level or downhill costs no extra, so attacking the high cell of a pair is dearer than attacking the low one, and an attack comes in from its best side. Nothing is stored for it: the capture reads the (at most four) neighbours' elevations. Uphill factor `1 + 0.25 * dh / 32` capped at x2 (`UPHILL_*` constants). AMENDED IN D91 (M04 fix pass, after the audit): `d` was the depth inside ONE Attack object, so `CancelAttack` plus `Attack` rebuilt the frontier at depth 0 and dodged the surcharge (x3 at the clamp) and also made the territory grow in a rounder, faster blob (+44 % at 5 minutes when relaunching every 20 ticks). The depth a capture gives a cell is now also left in the cell (`state.exposure`) and a new attack of the same attacker starts there; see D91.

### D84 Mutual attacks, merging, cancelling, tribes and refusals (`src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/intents.ts`)
"Ömsesidiga attacker avbryter varandra" is read as NETTING: when A launches an attack on B while B is attacking A, the two forces are set against each other, both lose the smaller of the two, the one left with nothing is gone and the other goes on with the difference (equal forces end both). The lost troops are consumed (they fought). Reading it as "both end and both get their troops back" would let a 1 % counter-attack nullify any attack. A second Attack on a target the seat is already attacking ADDS its troops to the first (one attack per pair). Attacks on unowned land are never mutual. `Attack{target, pct}`: `target` is an ownerId (0 = unowned land, as the prompt says; `seat` stays a seat), `pct` 1 to 100 of the CURRENT troops; a TRIBE commits `troops / TRIBE_ATTACK_DIV` (1/20) whatever pct it names (the rule is in the sim, WHEN a tribe attacks is the bot's business in `tools/sim/bots.ts`). `CancelAttack{target}` ends the seat's attack on the target and returns its troops. Wire kinds 3 and 4, `INTENT_KINDS` append-only. Refusals (state dependent, found by `step`, counted in `state.rejects`, one `OUTBOX_REJECT` each): `REJECT_NOT_SPAWNED` (13, the seat owns no land), `REJECT_SELF` (14), `REJECT_NO_CONTACT` (15, no shared border), `REJECT_NO_TROOPS` (16, the commitment rounds to 0 QT), `REJECT_NO_ATTACK` (17); shape: `REJECT_TARGET` (11), `REJECT_PCT` (12). The places a new intent kind touches (D74's list) were all edited: `copyBody`, `withId`, `comparePayload`, the codec's `intentSize` / `putIntent` / `assertEncodable` / `Reader.intent`, the test arbitraries, `tests/sim/helpers/brokenSteps.ts`.

### D85 The phases of a tick (`src/sim/step.ts`)
1 intents (canonical order, `applyIntent`), 2 conquest (`conquestPhase`: every attack gains progress and takes its cells), 3 enclaves (`enclavePhase`: the bucket `tick % 20`), 4 economy, 5 the generator, 6 the tick. `applyIntent` is exported so that the broken copies of `step` used by the golden mutation tests apply exactly the real rules. The work counters (`state.work`, not hashed) are reset at the top of `step`.

### D86 Enclaves (`src/sim/enclaves.ts`, `src/sim/enclaveQueue.ts`, `src/sim/territory.ts`)
An ENCLAVE is a connected region of one owner X (X may be 0, free land) such that every cell next to it, outside it, is playable land of ONE other owner S > 0; sea, Peak, Ice or a second neighbouring owner next to it, or a size above `ENCLAVE_MAX_CELLS` (64 sim cells = 16 std-cells, an own choice that bounds the work of a failed fill), make it "open" and it stays. An enclave changes hands for free, all at once. "Var 20:e tick, förskjuten med hash(id)": the candidate cells (below) sit in `ENCLAVE_FLIP_TICKS` (20) buckets chosen by a hash of the CELL (the only id a candidate has) and the tick `t` checks bucket `t % 20`, so every candidate is looked at within 20 ticks of being queued and the work of a tick is its bucket, at most `ENCLAVE_PASS_BUDGET_CELLS` (4096) visited cells (the rest of the batch waits for its next pass). The buckets are game state (serialised, hashed); the per-cell membership bit is derived. CANDIDATES: a region newly enclosed by `next` when a cell goes from `prev` to `next` contains a 4-neighbour ("port") of that cell, so `setOwner` queues the ports that are not `next`'s when (a) the port's owner is not `prev` (the cell touched a different owner: it was a leak of the port's region), or (b) the non-`next` cells around the cell fall into two or more groups that contain a port, joined along the cycle of the eight neighbours by non-`next` playable cells (the change SPLITS the region), or (c) the cell touched a wall (sea, Peak, Ice, the edge of the map; a wall joins nothing), or (d) the cell was itself waiting in the queue (it hands its job to its ports). The cell itself is queued when it has at most one friendly neighbour or three foreign ones (a lone cell or a tip). A straight border queues nothing. This rule is not obvious, so it is PROVEN by test: `tests/sim/frontier.test.ts` has one test per clause (each fails when the clause is removed) and a brute-force property (random paints, spawns and attacks, then every attack cancelled and 15 enclave passes: no enclosed region of at most `ENCLAVE_MAX_CELLS` cells may remain) over 40 seeds; while it was written the property found two holes (walls were joined as if they were cells; a cell that touched the wall) that unit tests had not, and 1500 further seeds (18 751 flipped cells) run clean. A pocket that has become bigger than the cap when its check runs is not flipped (the attack that closes it can still take it at the normal price). CORRECTED IN D91: the candidate rule above missed the merged region of the NEW owner's own land (the cell is now queued whenever it has a friendly neighbour, unless it touches two different foreign owners) and the region that LOSES the cell (one of its ports is queued, for a region that was too big and is eaten down to the cap); the sentence 'the cell itself is queued when it has at most one friendly neighbour or three foreign ones' and 'a straight border queues nothing' are replaced by that, and the claim 'proven by test ... 1500 further seeds run clean' only covered the END state of 48x32 worlds, not the time an enclave survives; the property is now checked after every tick on tiny worlds and in whole 32-seat matches on the real world (D91).

### D87 Derived structures and the snapshot (`src/sim/borders.ts`, `src/sim/serialize.ts`, `src/sim/state.ts`)
DERIVED, rebuilt by `assembleState` / `rebuildAggregates` from the owner array, never serialised, never hashed: the `BorderIndex` with (1) `edges`, the contact length of every pair of owners (kept by `setOwner`, 4 updates per changed cell at most), (2) `diff`, per cell the number of playable neighbours of another owner, (3) the per-owner BOUNDARY LISTS: a cell is appended when it becomes a boundary cell and is not listed yet, never removed on the spot (swap-removal cost random writes on every capture); an entry is valid while the cell is still the owner's and still has `diff` > 0; invalid entries are skipped by the reader and purged when the list has doubled. A new attack therefore costs about the border of its attacker (`launchScanned`), never the map. Nothing stored depends on the order or the stale content of those lists (the new frontier is sorted). Cells that nobody can own take part in no adjacency (only the debug paint can give one an owner). STATE (serialised, hashed, `FORMAT_VERSION` 4, `ENGINE_VERSION` 3): `state.attacks` (the sequence number, and per attack its eight scalar fields, `picks`, and its bags exactly as they are, ascending depth) and `state.enclaves` (the 20 buckets). `deserialize` checks every count against the bytes left before it allocates, ids ascending and unique pairs, attacker/target ranges, remainders, depths and cells in range, bucket membership by hash and uniqueness, and returns an error result (`attack-range`, `enclave-range`), never a throw (hostile-bytes test, fuzz over every 7th byte of the variable part). The new fields are NOT top-level numbers or typed arrays of the state (they are objects), so the registry test of M03 needed no change; their hash coverage has its own test (`tests/sim/conquestSerialize.test.ts`: every field, every queued cell, the order inside a bag).

### D88 Calibration (rounds, constants, curves) - the solo band of the prompt
Reference scenario (`tools/sim/bands.ts`, `npm run sim:scenario -- --solo`): ONE player, no tribes, spawns on the capital of the largest country with a playable capital (Russia, Moscow), seed 1, the scripted expander attacks unowned land with 100 % whenever it touches any. Constants tuned: `CAPTURE_K_MILLI` (`BASE_COST_TROOPS` stays 3: the troop pool does not bind in the reference scenario, Moscow still holds 3 000 troops after 10 minutes). Round 0 (the plan's K = 80, measured, not counted as a round): cells per minute 458 768 1112 1495 1877 2270 2681 3095 3502 3921, i.e. 1877 after 5 minutes (inside 1500-2500) and 3921 after 10 (BELOW 5000-8000). The speed law gives `N^0.75` growing linearly in time, so N(10)/N(5) is about 2.1 and the two bands (5 min at most 2500, 10 min at least 5000) leave a window of K of about 100 to 108 on this scenario. Round 1: K = 105: 2489 / 5295 after 5 / 10 minutes (inside, but 0.4 % under the 5-minute ceiling). Round 2: K = 103: 540 958 1429 1926 2440 2979 3508 4068 4640 5225 (inside both bands with 2.4 % and 4.5 % margin; this is the curve of the final code, the K = 103 run of the first, heap-ordered frontier gave 2440 / 5206). TWO tuning rounds, the band rule of the prompt (measured value +-30 %) was NOT needed and the band stays the provisional one. Spread across starting points at K = 105 (after 5 / 10 minutes): CHN 2454/5507, KAZ 2590/5867, BRA 2459/5060, CAN 2296/4780, IND 2206/4483, USA 2138/4640, AUS 1723/3401 (an island continent); at the final K = 103: CHN 2401/5394, BRA 2408/4952, IND 2161/4364, USA 2088/4546, AUS 1700/3341. So the band is a property of the reference scenario, not of every start; a test (`tests/sim/calibration.test.ts`) holds the reference inside the band and the others inside 1000 to 3500 after 5 minutes. M09 and M12 reuse `SOLO_BAND`. AFTER D91 the reference curve is 540 958 1423 1913 2423 2961 3483 4028 4591 5171 (cells per minute, K = 103 unchanged; 2423 after 5 minutes, band 1500-2500; 5171 after 10, band 5000-8000), so the calibration stays as it was; the band is the band of the REFERENCE BOT, which attacks free land with 100 % and starts a new attack only when the old one has ended (in the reference scenario one attack runs the whole 10 minutes), not of a player who cancels and relaunches: since D91 relaunching is no lever (2355 cells after 5 minutes when relaunching every 20 ticks, 2421 every 100 ticks, against 2423 for the plain bot; before D91 3505 and 2744 against 2440).

### D89 Measured (Node, this sandbox, real 1024x512 world)
See the numbers at the end of the M04 section of `docs/PROGRESS.md` (they are logged by the tests, not asserted except through `budgetMs`): tick cost of the 12 + 20 match, the 100 000 cell burst, the work counters, `npm run sim:scenario` times.

### D90 Earlier tests and files touched in M04 part B (with the reason)
`e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` re-pinned (once for this part, after the calibration): the golden scenario now also sends Attack and CancelAttack (about one turn in four an Attack, a few with a target or pct out of range) and the rules changed (engine version 3, format version 4, calibrated K), so the five hashes of seed 1, the three of seed 2 and the adversarial ones all change; Node, Chromium (`e2e/sim.test.ts`, `e2e/artifact.test.ts`), worker and host tests read the same file. `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts`: the layout test and the golden-world size follow the format (8 bytes of attack table head, 20 bucket counts, the attacks, the queued cells). `tests/sim/rules.test.ts`: the test that pinned the plan's `CAPTURE_K_MILLI` = 80 now says "the plan's values except K, which the calibration moved to 103 (D88) and which must stay in the plan's 60 to 150". `tests/sim/territoryProperties.test.ts`: the local intent generator gained Attack and CancelAttack and one array was typed `Intent[]` (the union grew). `tests/sim/helpers/arbitraries.ts` gained the two kinds, `tests/sim/helpers/brokenSteps.ts` calls the shared `applyIntent` and the new phases, `tests/sim/helpers/worlds.ts` got an `elevationAt` option. `src/sim/scenario.ts` uses `ownerSlots` for the number of valid targets (the source test allows the `+ 1` of ownerId only in `src/sim/state.ts`). No tolerance, budget or golden PNG was changed.

## M04 fix pass after the independent audit (no visible change; the Artifact is NOT republished)

### D91 Exposure record, enclave candidates, and the tests the audit found missing (`src/sim/state.ts`, `src/sim/territory.ts`, `src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/attacks.ts`, `src/sim/serialize.ts`)
`ENGINE_VERSION` 4, `FORMAT_VERSION` 5; the golden hashes were re-pinned ONCE at the end (the new state array is hashed, the queue and the prices changed). FOUR changes of behaviour, each reproduced first and each with a test that fails when the change is taken out (mutation-checked):

1. THE DEPTH TERM CANNOT BE RESET (audit: formulas and mutation lenses, three findings). `d` is the number of captures between a cell and land that touched the attacker before (D83); it lived in the bags of one `Attack`, so `CancelAttack` + `Attack` rebuilt the front at d = 0. Chosen: option (b) of the audit, a PER-CELL RECORD in the state, `state.exposure` (Uint16, `attacker << 8 | depth`, 0 = none; ownerId is at most 255 and depth at most `D_MAX_CELLS` 80). `afterChange` writes it when a capture of an attack exposes a cell to that attack (the same depth that goes in the bag), `changeOwner` clears it when the cell changes owner, `buildFrontier` starts each cell of a new attack at the depth recorded for ITS attacker (another attacker's record, or none, means 0). It is serialised (`FORMAT_VERSION` 5), hashed, in the registry (`STATE_ARRAY_NAMES`, so the sensitivity tests cover it) and validated on load (`exposure-range`, never a throw). A continuing attack is unchanged (it uses its bags), so the calibration did not move (D88): K stays 103. Option (a) (depth 0 against free land plus a recalibration) was rejected: it changes the whole price curve of expansion and still needs a defender-depth value for owned land. Residual, accepted: (i) a cell that two attackers expose keeps the record of the one that exposed it last, so the other starts it at 0 on a relaunch (it takes two players, and only on contested cells); (ii) `CancelAttack` drops the progress fraction of a cell (it costs the canceller, not the other way round); (iii) the unpaid part of a cell's price (`costFrac`, under one QT) used to vanish at a cancel, which made cancel-per-capture about 8 % cheaper: `endNow` now charges one QT when a fraction was owed. M12a's landing has to say which depth it passes (0, or a bounded breadth-first search to `D_MAX_CELLS`): it can write `state.exposure` through the same record. Measured: the corridor (a 1-cell wide front, 80 cells) pays 477 QT in 1374 ticks with one attack and 479 / 483 / 515 QT with a relaunch every 20 captures / every 5 / after every capture (240 QT before); Moscow solo after 5 minutes: plain 2423, relaunch every 100 ticks 2421, every 20 ticks 2355 (before: 2440, 2744, 3505). Tests: `tests/sim/relaunch.test.ts`. M05, M16 and M19 must not use cancel + relaunch as a price lever; it is not one now.
2. THE ENCLAVE CANDIDATE RULE IS COMPLETE FOR WHAT IT CLAIMS (audit: frontier lens, blocker). Two holes, both found with the audit's brute-force persistence check (a region enclosed by one owner that is unchanged for more than 21 ticks): (a) a cell that goes from `prev` to `next` joins its friendly neighbours into ONE region of `next`, and it may have been the last leak of that merged region; the old rule queued the cell only with at most one friendly neighbour or three foreign ones. Now the cell is queued whenever it has a neighbour at all, except when it touches two DIFFERENT foreign owners (then the merged region touches both and cannot be an enclave: the one case where queueing is provably useless). (b) (found while verifying (a) on 32-seat matches on the real world; not in the audit) a region of `prev` that is too big for `ENCLAVE_MAX_CELLS` and is eaten down to the cap by the surrounding owner (a free pocket in an expanding empire) was never looked at again, because the ports that still belonged to `prev` were queued only for a split or a wall leak. Now one such port is queued per change (all pieces of a split are covered by the split rule). Cost: candidates per changed cell 1.1 -> 1.8, cells scanned per changed cell 46 -> 81 (mean 440 -> 1690 per tick on a 524 288 cell map), p95 tick of the 12 + 20 match about 0.22 -> 0.25 to 0.35 ms and p50 about 0.09 -> 0.11 to 0.15 ms (noisy VM) against a budget of 3.6 ms; the diagnostic bound in `tests/sim/work.test.ts` for scanned cells per changed cell therefore went from 60 to 110 (the primary bound, scanned < a hundredth of the map per tick, and the bound per candidate are unchanged). Tests: the clause tests MERGE, TWO FOREIGN OWNERS and SHRINK; 'A STRAIGHT BORDER queues nothing' became 'queues no more than the cell and ONE piece of the region it left' (the rule it pinned was the one that was wrong); a per-tick persistence property on six tiny world shapes (30 seeds x 2 modes x 450 ticks) and on a 128x64 world (2 seeds x 2 modes); `tests/sim/enclaveMatch.test.ts` (4 seeds of 32 fighters on the real world, a brute-force scan every 50 ticks, nothing unchanged across two scans). A limit that stays: a region that is bigger than the cap when its check runs is not flipped (D86).
3. THE WORK GATE CANNOT BE FOOLED BY A SCAN THAT COUNTS NOTHING (audit: formulas lens, major). `tests/conquest.perf.test.ts` now also asserts: an idle 1024x512 tick costs at most `budgetMs(0.1)` (median of 40 batches of 10 ticks, scanned 0 for 200 ticks), the same local conquest (a 64 row front) costs at most 3 times as much per tick on 1024x512 as on 256x128 (median ratio), and p50 of the 12 + 20 match is at most `budgetMs(1)`. A pass over the owner array in `conquestPhase` fails the first two (about 1.1 ms against 0.13; ratio 15 to 18) and used to pass everything. `tests/sim/work.test.ts` gained the over-budget enclave pass: a 360x360 lattice of 32 041 lone cells, no pass visits more than `ENCLAVE_PASS_BUDGET_CELLS + ENCLAVE_MAX_CELLS + 1` cells, 677 passes run into the budget, all cells flip in the end and the marks are clean (mutants: no requeue, no budget, small `touched` buffer all fail).
4. RULES THAT ONLY THE PINNED HASHES GUARDED NOW HAVE A TEST OF THEIR OWN (audit: mutation lens, majors). `tests/sim/phases.test.ts`: the first mover of two attacks that both take cells in a tick is each in 30 to 70 % of the ticks (51 % measured), the gold of a tick counts the land after the conquest and after the enclave pass of that tick, a capture that closes a pocket flips it in the SAME tick when that tick is the pocket's bucket, the pick of a bag is the entry that `hash2(seed, id, picks)` points at (rule written out in the test), and the enclave bucket depends on the seed and is not `cell % 20`. `tests/sim/economy.test.ts`: `economyTick` integrated over 6000 ticks follows the closed-form law within 0.1 % (0.01 % measured; a dropped remainder is 4 to 6 % off) and a seat reaches its cap. `tests/sim/territoryState.test.ts`: the land share is cos-weighted (an equatorial band of 64 cells has a bigger share than polar bands of 96 cells, with the hand-computed Q15 values). Mutants killed: no sort of the attack order, order by index only, economy before conquest, enclaves before conquest, economy before enclaves, pick hash without counter / id / seed, enclave bucket by `cell % 20` / without seed, `economyTick` without the remainder, unweighted land share.
5. HEADS-UP FOR M12a (audit: charter lens, major; BACKLOG). Any region of `ENCLAVE_MAX_CELLS` (64) cells or fewer that one other player surrounds flips to that player within 20 ticks, free of charge. A helicopter beachhead of 1 to 9 cells behind enemy lines is exactly that and would vanish in a second or two, and M12a's 'established' threshold of 80 cells is above the cap, so only clusters that M12a calls 'not established' survive. The exemption is NOT built here (there is no beachhead table to read yet; a speculative flag array would be hashed and serialised for nobody, and ideas go to the BACKLOG): when M12a starts, `checkRegion` must treat a region that holds an exempt cell as open (a flag array in the state, or a lookup into the beachhead table), the M12a prompt must say so and state the cap against the threshold, and it must expect an `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, re-pinned goldens and a change of the quiescence tests (`tests/sim/frontier.test.ts`, `tests/sim/enclaveMatch.test.ts`). `tests/sim/frontier.test.ts` pins today's behaviour (1, 9 and 64 cells flip, 65 stay).

EARLIER TESTS AND FILES TOUCHED BY THE FIX PASS (with the reason): `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts`, `tests/sim/conquestSerialize.test.ts`, `tests/sim/economySerialize.test.ts` (offsets and sizes follow the format: 11 bytes per cell instead of 9, the layout test also checks the exposure array); `tests/sim/frontier.test.ts` (the STRAIGHT BORDER test as described above); `tests/sim/work.test.ts` (the 60 -> 110 bound as described above); `tests/sim/helpers/randomMatch.ts` gained `denseBodies` and `denseWorld`; `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` re-pinned once, at the end (state array, queue contents, prices). No budget, golden PNG or other tolerance was touched.

## M05 Part A: the first playable conquest (the Artifact is republished by the orchestrator after part C)

### D92 The match flow is a rule of the sim, behind a config flag (`src/sim/flow.ts`, `src/sim/spawn.ts`, `src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/state.ts`)
The prompt asks for a 20 s spawn phase and 5 s of immunity and for "spawn after the phase" and "attack on own land" to be REFUSED. A refusal that only the UI knows would not survive the multiplayer of M26 and could not be counted by `state.rejects`, so both live in the sim: `SPAWN_PHASE_TICKS` 200 and `IMMUNITY_TICKS` 50 in `src/sim/rules.ts` (rows in `docs/BALANCE.md`), `phaseAt(tick)` in `src/sim/flow.ts`, enforced in `applySpawn` (a Spawn from the end of the phase on: `REJECT_PHASE` 18) and `applyAttack` (an Attack inside the phase: `REJECT_PHASE`; an Attack on owned land, target not 0, inside the immunity: `REJECT_IMMUNE` 19; an Attack on unowned land is legal then, so expansion can start the moment the phase ends). The rules only exist when the match config says so: `MatchConfig.spawnPhase` (config flag bit 4; bit 2 is left unused because `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` pins it as an unknown bit). With the flag off, which is every M03 / M04 test, the golden scenario and `npm run sim:run`, nothing changes; the golden hashes and `ENGINE_VERSION` 4 / `FORMAT_VERSION` 5 are untouched (a state with the flag set would hash differently from one without, through the header flags, as it should). The UI knows the phase from the same function through `SimView.phase`. Own choices (conservative, say if you want them different): nobody can attack during the phase, not even a seat that has already spawned (you wait for the clock; at 4x the phase takes 5 s); the immunity protects every owned seat, not only the player.

### D93 SimView, StateView, the host's frame event and ViewMirror (`src/sim/view.ts`, `src/host/protocol.ts`, `src/host/mainThreadHost.ts`, `src/host/remote.ts`, `src/host/viewMirror.ts`)
`SimView` is an interface of read-only index access (`ReadonlyNumbers`); no mutator and no writable array can be reached through it (a type test with `@ts-expect-error` pins it). Per-seat statistics are integers in the units of the sim (`troopsQt` in quarter troops, `goldMilli`, `landShareQ15` cos-weighted, `cells`, `alive`, `capital`), packed in one `Uint32Array` (`STAT_FIELDS` blocks of `seats` values) so one buffer crosses the seam. The prompt says "the snapshot carries a copy of the owner array when it has changed". A 1 MB copy per frame would be 40 MB/s of garbage at 4x, and the host already sends the changed cells; so the owner array is a copy that the PAGE keeps current from the diffs (`ViewMirror`, in place, no allocation), and the FULL copy is delivered once, by `requestView()` (the owner array and the terrain layer), and again as `fullOwner` when the outbox overflowed (D69). The per-frame light data (tick, rejects, the last refusal, the packed statistics) rides in the same event as the diff: `StateDiff.view` (a `ViewSnapshot`), not a second event, so the listener counts of the M03 host tests stay exact. `StateView` is the zero-copy variant over a live state for code in the sim's own thread (the AI driver of M09; part C). Snapshot growth is tabulated in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Snapshot growth"). Tests of M03 / M04 touched, mechanically: `tests/host/remote.test.ts` (its fake host got the new `requestView` method) and `tests/sim/codecAttack.test.ts` (the pinned list of intent kinds ends with `DebugGive`; the first five codes are asserted unchanged).

### D94 `DebugGive` (wire kind 5, `src/sim/debugGive.ts`)
`__test.give(seat, {gold, troops})` must change gold and troops "only through an intent". `DebugGive{seat, gold, troops}` (whole units, integers 0 to 1 000 000) is valid only when the match config has `debugIntents` (otherwise `REJECT_DISABLED`), refused for a seat without land (`REJECT_NOT_SPAWNED`, a gift to a seat that has not spawned would be overwritten by its start values) and for a bad amount (`REJECT_AMOUNT` 20). A troop surplus above the cap decays by the normal rule. M09 extends the intent with buildings.

### D95 The ownership texture and the territory look (`src/render/ownership.ts`, `src/render/shaders/territory.ts`, `src/render/ownerPalette.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`)
Layout as in the prompt: R + G the u16 ownerId, B flags (bit 0 playable, bit 1 water), A reserve (kept 255). The CPU copy is updated in place from the diffs; the GPU upload is the whole texture, scheduled by `Globe.render` through `flush(fxNow())` when dirty and at least 0.1 fx seconds after the last one (`uploads` counts the schedules, `gpuUploads` the real uploads through three's `onUpdate`). In test mode `advance(dt, ticks)` forces the flush so the next render shows the new owners; `run()` uses the throttled path, which is what the 10-per-second test measures. In the shader the lookup is DOMAIN-WARPED (two octaves of value noise on an integer hash, no sin, periodic over 1024 columns), the border line comes from four taps at half the line width along both screen axes (a neighbour that is another owner counts, sea never does), the inner glow from two rings of taps at 2.5 and 5 pixels, and a water cell next to land takes the owner of its nearest land neighbour (the render raster is four times finer than the sim cells; without this the coast would show a ragged untinted rim). Only the LAND albedo is changed, so ownership is drawn only where the height texture says land. Numbers (tint 0.52, glow 0.22, line 2.4 px darkened to 0.3 of the owner colour, warp 0.7 cells) are in `src/render/config.ts` and were chosen from SwiftShader screenshots only; the acceptance is a measured dE76 of the border pixel on a Mountain cell (>= 15, measured about 22 to 29). The palette is hand-picked for the six players (owner 1 blue) and greedy farthest-first for the rest, never within 30 dE76 of the own colour (the unit test states 25).

### D96 The match in the page: seats, seed, speed, clicks, keys (`src/app/match.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`, `src/app/matchKeys.ts`)
The standard match is 26 seats: you (seat 0), `MATCH_AI_NATIONS` 5 AI nations and `MATCH_TRIBES` 20 tribes (part C fills them). Default seed 1 (an address without a seed is always the same match; `?seed=` / `#seed-N` / the Dev-panel field choose another). A click is a press that moves less than 6 px (`CLICK_MOVE_PX`); a longer press rotates the globe as before. In the spawn phase a click sends a Spawn for the cell (a refusal is the sim's: sea, Peak, Ice, owned land, with a toast through `lastRejectReason`; a click on a coastal land pixel inside a water cell means the nearest playable cell). Afterwards a click on land sends `Attack{target = owner of the cell, pct = commitment}`; a click on your own land does nothing (the raw refusal is tested through `__test.submit`), on sea / Peak / Ice a toast says it cannot be conquered. Speeds: `,` and `.` step 1x / 2x / 4x and stay at the ends, space pauses and resumes the chosen speed (a stopped game stays stopped when you change speed). `[` and `]` have the aliases `-` and `+` because a Swedish keyboard needs AltGr for the brackets (AltGr arrives as ctrl + alt and is accepted). HUD numbers are interpolated between the 10 Hz ticks in the live page and exact in test mode.

### D97 Test mode and the perf report (`src/app/globeApp.ts`, `src/ui/perfOverlay.ts`)
`?test=1` / `#test` has no start screen: the match exists when `window.__test` appears, with `debugIntents` on, the HUD hidden (the six globe goldens of M02 stay as they are; `#hud` shows it) and no interpolation. The hooks are listed in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. The perf JSON gets an additive `match` section (tick, speed, the cost of `host.advance` per frame that ran ticks as mean / p95 / max, ownership uploads); the schema version stays 1 because `e2e/devpanel.test.ts` pins it and nothing was removed or renamed. The `fxClock` guard is unchanged: the match controller reads no clock, the frame loop in `src/app/globeApp.ts` passes `dt` in, and toasts live in fx seconds.

### D98 i18n: two tables, strict test mode, what stays Swedish (`src/ui/i18n.ts`, `tests/i18n.test.ts`)
`t(key, params?)` with `{name}` placeholders, `setLanguage` / `getLanguage` / `languageVersion` (a counter that cached texts compare, so a language switch rebuilds them) / `onLanguageChange`, and the formats `formatInt` (a no-break space as thousands separator in Swedish, a comma in English), `formatFixed` (the language's decimal separator, no `toFixed`) and `formatClock`. `sv` is the default; `lang=en` (`?lang=en`, `#lang-en`, the Dev-panel entry, the language button of the start screen) selects English. The keys are a type (`TextKey = keyof typeof sv`) and `en` is `Record<TextKey, string>`, so a key missing from either table or an extra one does not compile; `tests/i18n.test.ts` states the same at run time and adds what the compiler cannot see: the same `{placeholders}` in both texts, no empty text, every key written in `src` exists, every key of the tables is used (no dead strings), and no string literal with å, ä or ö in the player-facing files (comments stripped; a planted Swedish literal in `src/ui/matchHud.ts` was seen to fail it). In test mode (`setStrictI18n(true)`, set by the page when `?test=1`) a key missing from EITHER table, or a placeholder without a value, THROWS; `__test.text(key)` is the hook that proves it in the browser (`e2e/hud.test.ts` also resolves every key of both tables in the page). Outside test mode a missing key falls back to the other language and then to the key itself, so a player never sees an exception. The prompt says "every NEW string from M05 on"; the old player-facing strings that are visible during a match were moved too (the hover tooltip: class names, "Hav", "Okänt land", country names in the UI language; "Laddar världen..." and the start error). DECISION: the developer tools stay Swedish only: the Dev panel and its labels, the perf overlay and its report text, the report modal, the debug pages (`debug/data.html`, `debug/sim.html`) and the two link chips of the start page (a test pins their Swedish text). They are not part of the game; BACKLOG has the item for M23a (settings and completion) to translate them if wanted. The F3 text overlay is now hidden by default (it was visible in the live page); F3 still toggles it, so the first thing a player sees is the start screen without debug text.

### D99 The HUD: layout, test ids, leaderboard rule, per-frame cost (`src/ui/matchHud.ts`, `src/ui/hudLogic.ts`, `src/ui/hudStyle.ts`, `src/ui/uiConfig.ts`)
Layout (built in `em`, the root font follows the window width between 14 and 22 px, so 960x540 gives about 16 px and 1920x1080 22 px): banner top centre (the phase prompt and the countdown), leaderboard top right under the Dev button, status panel bottom left (gold with income, troops with the cap bar, land share, time), controls bottom right (speed, commitment), toast above them. The perf and F3 overlays own the top left corner, so the HUD keeps out of it (in perf mode the wide perf overlay can cover the banner during the first seconds; BACKLOG). Panels are nearly opaque dark (`rgba(8, 13, 24, 0.88)`) with light text; `tests/ui/hudContrast.test.ts` composes them over white and black and requires WCAG AA (4.5:1) for every text colour (the measured ratios are 6.3 to 18.3), and `e2e/hud.test.ts` reads real pixels of a day, a night and a polar ice view at both sizes (padding luma under 80, bright text pixels present). The warning at 80 % of the cap is the bar turning orange (a white mark shows the 80 % point) AND the words "Nära taket", so it does not depend on colour alone. Test ids keep the names of part A (`troops`, `gold`, `phase`, `phase-text`, `phase-seconds`, `controls`, `speed-*`, `commit`, `commit-slider`, `toast`, `start-screen`, `play`) and add `status`, `gold-rate`, `troops-cap`, `troops-bar`, `troops-warning`, `land-share`, `timer`, `leaderboard`, `leader-row`, `leaderboard-empty`, `paused`, `commit-troops`, `start-title`, `start-help`, `start-language`, `start-dev`. Because numbers are formatted (`1 234`), the raw number is in `data-value` (gold and troops in whole units, `gold-rate` in milli-gold per second, `troops-cap` in whole troops, `land-share` as the raw Q15, `timer` in seconds, `commit` in percent); `leader-row` carries `data-seat`, `data-rank`, `data-share` (Q15) and `data-you`. Leaderboard: the best five PLAYERS (you and the AI nations) by cos-weighted land share, ties to the lower seat, plus your own row with your rank when you are not among the five; the neutral tribes do not take part (`LEADERBOARD_INCLUDES_TRIBES` in `src/ui/uiConfig.ts` switches them in); nations are called "Nation N" and tribes "Stam N" until M06 gives real names. The banner counts the spawn phase and the immunity, shows the attack hint for 12 fx seconds after the immunity, and tells a player who never spawned that they missed it (the sim refuses a late Spawn, D92; there is no restart button yet, reloading the page starts a new match). Per frame the HUD allocates nothing in the steady state: texts are rebuilt only when the number behind them or the language changed (`TextCell`), the bar moves with a transform, the ranking is redone once per tick into a preallocated array, the model object is reused; `e2e/hud.test.ts` shows 500 refreshes of an unchanged state cause zero DOM mutations (about 0.003 ms each) and the live perf report carries `match.hudUpdateMs`. Clicks on every HUD panel (banner, leaderboard, status, controls) are taken by the HUD (`pointer-events: auto` on the panels, the root passes the rest through to the globe), tested with real clicks. The HUD reads time only as the `fxNow` argument of `update`; no CSS transition or animation exists (a unit test and the `fxClock` guard).

### D100 The start screen (`src/ui/startScreen.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`, `src/app/devpanel.ts`)
The first thing in the live page: a card over the globe, which turns slowly behind it (`START_SPIN_DEG_PER_S`, driven by the fx clock; the scrim is light so the globe is visible), with the title, the one-sentence promise, a large Play button (focused: Enter starts), the controls in five lines, a language button and a small Dev button. Play shows "loading" on the button for two frames before the match is built (building the world state blocks the main thread for a moment), then the screen goes away and the HUD appears already filled. The small Dev button opens the Dev panel through an event on `window` (`DEV_PANEL_REQUEST_EVENT`, `src/ui/events.ts`); the round corner button of the Dev panel stays where it is (tests of M02 pin it), so the start screen shows two ways to the same panel, which is the conservative reading of "a small Dev button". Test mode never shows the start screen (D97). The two link chips of the start page (`#probe-link`, `#debug-links`, positioned above the page wrapper) are hidden once the match starts (they would cover the HUD) and the Dev panel gained the entries `probe` (`./probe.html`), `lang-en` and `lang-sv`.

### D101 Earlier tests touched in M05 part B (with the reason)
`e2e/match.test.ts`: the HUD numbers are now formatted (`1 234`), so the two places that read the number out of the text with a digit regex (the HUD equals the sim; the live page waits for troops above 100) read `data-value` instead, and the first also states that the visible digits equal the sim value. No tolerance and no expectation about the sim changed.

### D102 AI v0 is a pure function over `SimView`; `src/sim/public.ts` is its only import (`src/ai`, `src/sim/public.ts`, `eslint.config.js`, `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`)
`think(view: SimView, memory: AiMemory, rng: Uint32Array): readonly IntentBody[]` answers with the intents ONE seat sends now (almost always none: it returns one shared frozen empty array, so a thought that does nothing allocates nothing). It reads the view, writes only its own memory and its own random stream, and has no clock. `src/ai` may import nothing from the sim but `src/sim/public.ts` (which exports `SimView`, so `../sim/view` is closed too: it holds `StateView` and `fillSeatStats`, which take a `GameState`). Proven three ways: an ESLint allow-list (a `regex` pattern: anything that is not `./x` or exactly `../sim/public`, a bare package or a dynamic `import()` is an error; the determinism rules are repeated in the same entry because a later flat-config entry replaces the option list of an earlier one), a structural scan of the real files, and a TYPE test with the TypeScript compiler (`tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`): the export list of `public.ts` is pinned name by name (adding an export is a decision that must be reviewed there), no exported function takes or returns a `GameState`, and every property of `SimView` and `SeatStatsView` is `readonly` with no writable typed array. Mutation-checked: an extra `fillSeatStats` export, a writable `owner` and a non-readonly property each fail it. `public.ts` also re-exports the intent types (type-only, `Intent`, `IntentBody`, `SpawnBody`, `AttackBody`, `CancelAttackBody`) and the constructors (`spawnBody`, `attackBody`, `cancelAttackBody`, new in `src/sim/intents.ts`), the rng functions (`createRng`, `nextU32`, `nextBelow`: the AI is HANDED its stream and these only advance that array), `isPlayableClass`, and the `AI_*` constants of `rules.ts` (`src/ai` never imports `rules.ts`). Those constants do not touch `step`, so there is no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump and no golden changes (they are in `docs/BALANCE.md`).

### D103 `SimView.contactLength` and the tick driver in the host (`src/sim/view.ts`, `src/host/viewMirror.ts`, `src/host/mainThreadHost.ts`, `src/host/aiDriver.ts`)
"Attacks the weakest NEIGHBOUR" needs to know who touches whom, and scanning the 524 288 owner cells per thought would cost milliseconds against a 0.5 ms budget. The M04 border index already holds the contact length per pair of owners, so `SimView` got `contactLength(a, b)` (the adjacency count of the sim; owner 0 = unowned land): a `StateView` reads the sim's index in O(1); a `ViewMirror` (the page's copy, where no AI runs today) scans its own copy once per `ownerVersion` with `countContacts` and caches it. A test compares the two for every pair of owners after 1000, 2500 and 4000 ticks of a real 26-seat match, and a hand-counted 4 x 2 map pins the counting rule (wrap in x, sea is no contact). The AI runs where the sim runs: `MainThreadHost` takes a `TickDriver` (`onTick(view, submit)`) and calls it BEFORE every tick with a `StateView` over the live state whose statistics were refreshed for that tick (3 us); what the driver submits goes through `submitIntent`, i.e. it is filed for T + 2 like a player's (a test proves the first AI Spawn owns land exactly 2 ticks after it was sent, and a replay test proves that a host WITHOUT the AI that is sent the recorded intents at the same ticks reaches the same state hash). `src/host/aiDriver.ts` (`AiDriver`) holds one `AiMemory` per AI seat and ONE stream `createRng(seed ^ salt)` of its own; M09 keeps this file as it is (the prompt said M09 would create it; it exists now). The AI memory and stream are not in `HostResume` yet, so a snapshot-and-resume of a match WITH the AI would diverge from the straight run (BACKLOG, M24b). The AI has no Worker issue: it only needs the view and `submit`.

### D104 What AI v0 does, and why the world is placed around you (`src/ai/think.ts`, `src/ai/place.ts`, `src/ai/memory.ts`, `docs/BALANCE.md`)
Spawn: the 25 AI seats WAIT for the human capital (`AI_ANCHOR_SEAT`) or until tick `AI_SPAWN_WAIT_TICKS` 110, then Spawn one by one `AI_SPAWN_SPACING_TICKS` 3 apart, the five nations first and then the 20 tribes (the last is down 75 to 185 ticks into the 200 tick phase). Each seat searches up to 400 seeded random places for one with a WHOLE capital disc of playable land (so a Spawn gives the full 197 cells) and no owned cell within a clearance (nations 28 cells, tribes 14; halved after every quarter of the tries down to 10), inside a square of 90 cells (about 3500 km) around the human capital for the first half of the tries and anywhere for the second half (a human on an island does not leave the AI without a capital); a Spawn that has not shown up after 6 ticks is tried again at another place. Why around the human: with places drawn over the whole map the nearest seat is about 70 cells away on average (126 000 land cells over 26 seats) and a player who expands for ten minutes would meet nobody; a gate that is meant to be played for ten minutes needs somebody to fight. Measured with a scripted human expander (`tools/sim/bots.ts`, 100 % attacks) on eight capitals and three seeds: first contact with a tribe after 36 to 109 s, with a nation after 100 to 340 s or none within six minutes in Europe and India (nearest nation 43 to 87 cells). The first design had the tribes appear at tick 0 and the nations at a seeded tick 20 to 150; the human then chose a place the AI could not know about, and two tribes placed on consecutive ticks collided (REJECT_OWNED), which is why the seats are now spaced and follow the human. Play: once per second per seat on its own tick (staggered by `seat * 3`), a seat with troops of at least `AI_EXPAND_TROOP_PCT` 50 % of the cap acts: a nation attacks the WEAKEST neighbour (fewest troops; the lower ownerId on a tie) if it has at least 1.3 times its troops (commit 50 % of its troops, jittered +-10 in steps of 5 from the AI stream), else unowned land it touches (70 %); no war during the immunity; a cooldown per target (40 ticks; a tribe 100) because a second Attack on a target adds troops to the running one and the AI cannot see the attack table. A TRIBE only expands (the sim gives it troops / 20 whatever pct says) and stops at 600 cells (`AI_TRIBE_MAX_CELLS`): without the cap the tribes of the 12 + 20 match ended with 25 000 cells, more than the nations. An attack the sim refuses can still happen: the free land an Attack wanted was taken during the two ticks of input delay (REJECT_NO_CONTACT); it is rare (seed 7, 20 minutes: 1 of about 320 intents sent; the tests bound it to 1 %). Measured on the real world (seed 1, nobody else acting): a nation holds about 1 300 to 1 700 cells after 5 minutes and 2 700 to 3 800 after 10, against 2 440 and 5 225 for the reference solo expander of the calibration (the AI waits for half the cap, the reference does not); over 30 minutes the nations reach 8 000 to 11 000 cells and eat tribes.

### D105 Earlier tests and files touched in M05 part C (with the reason)
`e2e/match.test.ts` and `e2e/hud.test.ts`: every `open(...)` of a test-mode page now carries `ai=0` (`#test_ai-0_seed-7`), because those tests were written for a match in which nobody but you acts (the seed 7 script with 197 and 382 cells and the sim hash `9db19f91`, the leaderboard with the five nations at 0, the refusal counts). The default is the AI ON (the match of the gate); the live page test at the end of `e2e/match.test.ts` runs with it on and is unchanged. The numbers and the hash are therefore unchanged (the sim does not know `ai=0`). `tests/app/match.test.ts`: the helper `session()` passes `ai: false` for the same reason (its tests are about what the human's session does); one new test states the default. `OWNER_TINT` 0.52 to 0.6 in `src/render/config.ts`: in the 4 500 km view of a full game the blue of your own land mixed with olive and mountain colours to a grey blue that was hard to tell from the sea; the border tests (dE76 >= 15 on a Mountain cell) are re-run in the full e2e project. The six globe goldens show no territory and are unaffected. The placeholder file of `src/ai` is gone (the folder is filled).

## M05 audit round (four independent audits of parts A to C; eleven findings, all fixed)

### D106 Terrain slows the capture, not only the price (`src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/sim/serialize.ts`, `src/sim/state.ts`)
Finding: terrain only raised the PRICE of a cell, paid from the committed pool, which at 40 % is far bigger than the price, so a Mountain start expanded as fast as the plains of Poland (cells after 600 ticks of the same script: Poland 517, Alps 505, Tibet 526) and the gate question "do the Alps and the Himalaya slow the conquest?" would have been answered no. Chosen: the SPEED term carries the terrain too. A captured cell of class c costs `TERRAIN_COST_PCT[c] / 100` cells' worth of capture progress (Plains 1.0, Forest 1.3, Hills 1.6, Mountain 2.5), booked as DEBT after the capture: `Attack.progress` (micro-cells) may now be negative, down to `-PROGRESS_DEBT_MAX` (1000 cells' worth, a new row in `docs/BALANCE.md`), and the next ticks' progress pays it off before the next cell can fall (`conquestPhase`: `whole` is 0 while the progress is below one cell). Option (b) of the finding, a separate `TERRAIN_SPEED_PCT` row, was rejected: one table for price and speed keeps the plan's "Mountain = 2.5" a single number and a second table would have been a second place to forget. Measured (`tests/sim/conquest.test.ts`, 100 ticks of a wide front with plenty of troops): Plains 71 cells, Forest 55, Hills 44, Mountain 28, so a Mountain front gains 0.39 of a Plains front (the test states <= 0.6, > 0.2, and Forest/Hills in between in the order of the table). The debt is signed in the snapshot: `serialize` writes `progress` as Int32 (the bytes of every non-negative value are the same as before, so `FORMAT_VERSION` stays 5) and `deserialize` refuses a value below `-PROGRESS_DEBT_MAX`. Plains-only worlds are unchanged cell for cell; any world with other classes changes, so `ENGINE_VERSION` is 5 and every sim golden is re-pinned (below).
Recalibration: the solo reference band (D88) fell out (4 201 cells after 10 minutes against 5 000 to 8 000), so `CAPTURE_K_MILLI` went 103 -> 123 (still inside the plan's 60 to 150): RUS solo now 2 430 / 5 100 cells after 5 / 10 minutes, the five other countries 1 770 to 2 210 after 5 minutes (test band 1 000 to 3 500). K 125 and 128 break the 2 500 ceiling of minute 5 (2 464 and about 2 520), 118 and 121 sit on the 5 000 floor; 123 keeps a margin on both sides.
Goldens: `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` re-pinned ONCE, with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 npx vitest run --project unit tests/sim/golden.test.ts`, because the golden world (128x64 with all terrain classes) now runs the terrain debt and `engineVersion` is part of the hash: seed 1 ticks 1 / 10 / 100 / 1000 / 6000 `5a6848b8` / `3a5673bf` / `567f0149` / `2fcad256` / `b165e0d8`, seed 2 ticks 10 / 100 / 1000 `80dbe534` / `c2281213` / `8fcbe9a2`. No PNG golden changed.
Test touched, with the reason: `tests/sim/conquestSerialize.test.ts` compared the derived field `Attack.low` of the live attack with the one the loader recounts; `low` is documented as a lower bound ("every bag below it is empty") and the live attack keeps a stale one after its shallowest bag ran empty, while the loader recounts it tight. The old numbers happened to agree for the old dynamics. The assertion now states the property: the live `low` is <= the first non-empty bag and the loaded one is exactly it. Nothing was loosened (the hash, the bags, every other field and the byte-equality of a second `serialize` are compared as before), and a debt round trip was added.

### D107 The HUD on a narrow screen (`src/ui/hudStyle.ts`, `src/ui/uiConfig.ts`, `e2e/hud.test.ts`)
Finding: at 390x844 the phase banner was a 32 px wide column (its max width was `100vw - 33em`), unreadable, overlapping the leaderboard and next to the Dev button. A media query (`max-width: NARROW_MAX_PX` = 600 px, or portrait) gives the banner `left: 8px; right: 64px` (clear of the 48 px Dev button), the text left and the countdown right on one row, and moves the leaderboard (13 em, compact rows) and the pause badge below it; the toast moves above the taller dock. e2e at 390x844 (spawn phase and after the click): the banner is at least 200 px wide and under 90 px tall, inside the screen, and intersects neither the leaderboard nor the Dev button, nor do those two intersect. Screenshots looked at (`m390-01-banner.png`, `m390-02-after-click.png`).

### D108 Territory is readable at night, and your colour is not blue (`src/render/shaders/territory.ts`, `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `src/render/ownerPalette.ts`)
Finding: your territory was blue and the tint was multiplied by the night lighting: by day blue over green or brown land read as a lake, by night the territory and its border vanished, and with a 30-minute day and the start sun 67 degrees west of the centre most of a 10-minute session is spent in the dark half. Two changes. (1) Colours: you are magenta (236, 50, 150), the AI nations red, yellow, violet, orange and teal; no player is blue or green (a unit test states dE76 >= 40 to shallow and deep sea, grass, forest, desert, rock and snow and a hue that is neither blue nor green; own colour against the other 254 entries still >= 25, measured 42.1; the 26 seats of a match pairwise >= 20.3 as before, measured 22.1). (2) Light: the territory tint stays in the albedo (so by day the terrain still shades through it), and `applyTerritory` now also leaves an EMISSIVE term in the global `territoryGlow` (display colour, weight at full night) that `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts` mixes into the lit land AFTER the lighting, scaled by `1 - day`: the interior keeps `OWNER_NIGHT_INTERIOR` 0.34 of the owner colour at brightness `OWNER_NIGHT_LEVEL` 0.62, the border line becomes a bright rim (the owner colour lightened 45 %, weight 0.9; by day it stays the dark line) and a halo of weight 0.55 within `OWNER_NIGHT_HALO_PX` 3 pixels lights the unowned land just outside the border. By day the weight is 0, so the day look and the six globe goldens are unchanged. The glow is a file-scope GLSL variable because `tests/render/shaders.test.ts` forbids our own `out vec4` text. e2e at night (sun on the far side of the block, a Mountain block): own side against unowned side dE76 39.6, rim against own 22.2, rim against unowned 51.5 (limit 15), and own against another seat 25.2 (limit 15); screenshots looked at. The old day test (border pixel against both sides on a Mountain cell, dE76 >= 15, measured 27.5 to 30.6) still passes.

### D109 The AI waits longer for you and Spawns on consecutive ticks (`src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/ai/place.ts`, `src/ai/think.ts`, `src/host/aiDriver.ts`, `src/ui/i18n.ts`)
Finding: the AI anchors its capitals around the human only if the human clicks within 11 s (tick 110); a first-time player who reads the banner gets distant nations (nearest nation 118 to 268 cells away after a Spawn at tick 150, against 23 to 25 at tick 0, so the ten-minute playtest meets nobody). Chosen values differ from the finding's suggestion (`AI_SPAWN_WAIT_TICKS` 135, spacing 2): spacing 2 made seats pick places next to a capital that was still in flight (a Spawn runs 2 ticks after it is sent, the view shows it from the third tick), which gave refused Spawns. So the DRIVER now keeps the cells of Spawns in flight (sent, not yet visible) and hands them to `think` as an optional fourth argument; `pickSpawnCell` keeps its clearance from them too. Seats can now Spawn on consecutive ticks and still never meet (test: 25 seats on 25 consecutive ticks, three seeds, every capital a full disc, 0 refusals, all capitals at least 10 cells apart), which lets the wait grow to 150 ticks (15 s) with `AI_SPAWN_SPACING_TICKS` 1: the last seat (slot 24) is down at tick 176. A human who Spawns at tick 140 gets the nearest nation within 90 cells in all of 24 matches (four continents, six seeds, no refusal). A human who waits longer than 15 s still gets distant nations (documented in `docs/PREVIEW.md` 17e; the start screen's help line now says "within 15 seconds"). The finding's test with the human at tick 150 cannot hold with any wait that also finishes before tick 200 (the AI would have to wait for tick 150 + slots), so the test uses tick 140. `AI_EXPAND_TROOP_PCT` 50 -> 40: with terrain slowing the capture (D106) and the new spawn order the weakest nation of seed 1 sat at 567 cells after 5 minutes against the unit test's 591 (three discs); across eight seeds the mean nation size was unchanged (about 1 150) but the minimum was a lottery. At 40 the mean is about 1 260 and the weakest of the 40 nations (eight seeds) is 699, so the calibration test passes with margin and is not touched. `AI_MIN_CONTACT_LENGTH` 4 (new): after these changes `e2e/ai.test.ts` (seed 7, 6000 ticks, which states 0 refusals) saw one `REJECT_NO_CONTACT`: a tribe re-attacked free land at tick 3472 with a contact of 2 cells while its own running attack took the last of them, so the front was gone when the intent ran at 3474. The driver test always allowed such races ("the price of the input delay"), but the e2e states zero, so the AI no longer expands into free land it touches along fewer than 4 adjacencies (a war needs any contact, as before; a unit test with a one-cell sliver and a ring of land, mutation checked). The match of seed 7 then runs 6000 ticks without a refusal and the eight-seed nation sizes above are with it. `tests/ai/think.test.ts` pinned the threshold as `cap / 2`; it now reads `AI_EXPAND_TROOP_PCT` (the same two probes, 8 QT below and 4 QT above the threshold).

### D110 Boundary rules for the front end and the tests that were missing (`eslint.config.js`, `tests/lint.test.ts`, `tests/boundary.test.ts`, others)
CHARTER invariant 6 ("the UI never imports step or writes sim arrays") was enforced for `src/ai` only. `eslint.config.js` now has an entry for `src/ui`, `src/render` and `src/app` with the typescript-eslint no-restricted-imports rule: only `src/sim/view.ts`, `src/sim/public.ts`, `src/sim/intents.ts`, `src/sim/flow.ts`, `src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/sim/terrain.ts`, `src/sim/time.ts` and `src/sim/state.ts` (from the state only `seatToOwner` / `ownerToSeat` as values) are importable, any TYPE import passes, dynamic `import()` of the sim is forbidden, and the exception is explicit: `src/app/debugSim.ts` (runs the scripted sim) and `src/app/match.ts` (builds the world and the host). `tests/lint.test.ts` has 14 forbidden imports in each of the three folders, the allowed ones, the exemption and the audit's `evil` example; `tests/boundary.test.ts` scans the imports and forbids assignments into sim arrays outside those two files. Mutation checked: the audit's `evil` function appended to `src/ui/hudValues.ts` fails lint and both structural tests. Tests added for the other audit findings (each mutation checked by hand): `requestView` returns copies (`tests/host/mainThreadHost.test.ts`, also the diff's light view per listener); `textureMB` counts the ownership texture (2 MiB) and the owner palette (`rgbaTextureBytes`, `Globe.info`; unit test and e2e `textureMB` 22.5 + 2 KiB); a click's meaning is a pure function (`clickMeaning` in `src/app/clickTarget.ts`: own, enemy owner, unowned, unplayable) plus a spy test that `MatchSession.attack(7)` at 65 % sends `{Attack, target 7, pct 65}` and an e2e that a real click on enemy land attacks that owner with exactly the slider's 65 % (35.2 % of the troops are left) and shrinks the enemy block; `goldPerSecMilli` is milli-gold per second (`tests/sim/view.test.ts`: over `TICK_HZ` ticks the gold grows by exactly that, player and tribe); `StatInterpolator` refreshes its previous value on every push (three pushes); `OwnershipTexture.setFromOwners` marks the texture dirty (the whole-array write after an outbox overflow); `resolveClickCell` picks the nearest of playable cells on several sides, including the row above; `takeNewReject` shows only the human's refusals (an AI seat's refused Spawn in a tick where the human sent nothing is never toasted, the human's own is). The dangling comment "the attack commits what the slider says" in `e2e/match.test.ts` was removed; the new click test covers it.

### D111 The troop surplus after a lost cell is the economy rule; the property was too strict (M05fix: `tests/sim/territoryProperties.test.ts`, `tests/sim/overCap.test.ts`)
The final verifier saw `tests/sim/territoryProperties.test.ts` fail at random (about 1 match in 3600, about 4 % of `npm run verify` runs) at `expect(s.troops[o]).toBeLessThanOrEqual(ownerCapQt(s, o))` with "expected 4720 to be less than or equal to 4713", and blamed commit be1db76 (terrain slows the capture). Reproduction (40x24, seed 46939, 5 seats, 3 players): Spawn seat 1 cell 554, Spawn seat 3 cell 0, Attack seat 1 target 4 pct 66, then 7 empty ticks. ROOT CAUSE, found by running the same sequence at 8a29cc4 and at HEAD with a per-tick trace: be1db76 is not a sim regression. Owner 4 (a tribe) sits at its full cap 4721, owner 2 takes ONE of its cells, the cap falls to 4713 and the troops stay at 4720. That surplus is the documented M04 rule (D73 and `tests/sim/economy.test.ts`, "above the cap (land lost) troops decay, at most MAX_DECAY per second, and stop at the cap"): troops are never clamped when the cap falls, the negative regeneration `(8 + 0.35 * sqrt(T)) * (1 - T / Tmax)` takes the surplus down in proportion to its size (7 QT over a cap of 4713 is about 0.01 QT per tick), `DebugGive` states the same ("a troop surplus above the cap decays by the normal rule"). At 8a29cc4 the identical match took the cell two ticks LATER (tick 12 instead of 10), outside the 10 turns the property runs, so the property saw a seat at its cap; the capture timing moved because be1db76 changed `CAPTURE_K_MILLI` (103 to 123) and the terrain debt, and the surplus is reachable at 8a29cc4 as well (the trace shows 4720 against 4713 from tick 12 there). So the sim is unchanged: no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, no golden change, no balance change (a hard clamp on every lost cell would make a lost border cell cost the defender troops it never lost, which the plan does not want).
SECOND CAUSE, found by the flake hunt at 200 times the run count (1 match in about 19 000): the refund of an ended attack. The committed troops `F` leave the owner's pool when an attack starts and go back when it ends (D82), outside the cap, and the owner regenerates meanwhile. A tribe at its cap 4703 commits 235 QT, regenerates to 4469, cancels at tick 9 and holds 4704 against the cap 4703. Same rule, same soft decay afterwards; not changed (clamping the refund would punish cancelling and would change every attack life cycle in the goldens).
THE PROPERTY, not weakened but stated exactly and per tick: troops above the cap arise only from those two events (a seat loses land and its cap falls; an attack of the seat ends and its committed troops return). So for every seat and every tick `troops <= max(cap now, troops the tick before + the troops of the attacks that vanished in this tick)`, which still catches any regeneration or economy step that lifts a seat above its cap, and the original strict `troops <= cap` at the end of the run holds for every seat that had neither event (a seat that is wiped out and respawns starts again). Both counterexamples are pinned twice: as explicit `examples` of the property (`OVER_CAP_MATCH` and `REFUND_OVER_CAP_MATCH` in `tests/sim/helpers/overCapMatch.ts`, with the shared `freshTerritoryMatch` builder) and as `tests/sim/overCap.test.ts` (exact numbers: 4721 / 197 cells before and 4720 against 4713 / 196 cells after, then 300 ticks in which the surplus only shrinks by at most MAX_DECAY per tick; 4703 full, 4468 with 235 QT committed, 4704 after the cancel, then 100 ticks of pure decay). `tests/sim/realWorld.test.ts` keeps its strict assertion: it runs 32 solo seats with no attacks, so no cap ever falls and no refund exists there (deterministic, not random). The flake hunt (random fast-check seeds, run counts multiplied through a scratch alias of `fast-check` outside the repo) is summarised in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.

## M06 Part A: the fair start, home land, regions, flags and the match setup (sim and pure logic; the menu DOM is Part B)

### D112 The fair start is ONE rule, built on the one spawn algorithm (`src/app/matchSetup.ts`, `src/app/eligibility.ts`, `src/sim/spawn.ts`, `src/sim/setup.ts`)
Every nation starts with the same troops (`START_TROOPS`), the same gold (`START_GOLD`) and a start disc of exactly K = `START_DISC_CELLS` (197) cells: the K nearest contiguous unowned land cells around its capital, taken in distance order with the hash tie-break of M04 (`claimDisc`). Nothing else spawns: the setup places every seat at tick 0 through `applySpawn` (`startSeats`), the same function an intent runs. What makes "exactly K" a promise and not a hope:
- A country (or a custom start cell) is SELECTABLE only if (a) it has at least `SETUP_MIN_COUNTRY_CELLS` (8) cells inside the region and (b) the connected land mass of its capital has at least K cells. A land mass here is a set of PLAYABLE cells (`isPlayableClass`: Plains, Forest, Hills, Mountain), 4-neighbour connected with x wrapping and y not, which is exactly what the Spawn walks through; Ice, Peak, Shallows and sea are not land. The prompt says "over land, Ice excluded"; Peak is excluded too because a Spawn cannot take it, and counting it would let a country with a K-cell land mass through whose disc then comes out short. The disc may reach over a neighbour's land (a Slovenia of 20 cells gets its 197 cells).
- A refusal carries the i18n key of its reason and the numbers of its placeholders ("för liten landmassa för en rättvis start: X av 197 celler"). The order of the checks: capital outside the region (`outsideRegion`), fewer than 8 cells (`tooFewCells`), excluded on purpose (`excluded`: the overrides' unplayable list, a merged territory, Antarctica), no capital on playable land (`noCapitalLand`: Greenland's capital cell is Ice in the 39 km grid; it is NOT moved to another cell, the rule stays "the capital's land mass"), land mass smaller than K (`smallLandmass`). 146 of the 242 countries are selectable in the world; 96 of them have fewer than 400 own cells, so a small country with a big land mass (Kenya, Thailand) is fine and a bigger one on a small island (Ireland 91, Iceland 154, Indonesia: Java 96) is not.
- The AI nations are placed AFTER you, in a seeded order of the other selectable countries, and a candidate is accepted only if its capital is at least `SETUP_CAPITAL_MIN_DISTANCE_CELLS` (17, Euclidean, x wrapping) from every capital placed so far AND a DRY RUN of the Spawn (`countDisc`, the same walk without claiming) still gives K cells on the map as it is now. Otherwise the next candidate in the order is tried ("en kandidat med färre än K startceller ersätts av nästa"). `countDisc` is `claimDisc(..., dry = true)`: the per-cell mark, not the owner array, keeps a cell from entering the heap twice, so a dry walk is identical to the real one; it is not a second spawn algorithm. If the countries run out the config is refused (`notEnoughCountries`, with `available` and `wanted`); `maxAiNations(world, config)` gives the real upper limit the menu may offer.
- The tribes are placed at tick 0 too (by the AI's own `pickSpawnCell`: a whole disc of land with clear ground near your capital, then the same dry-run check), so in a default match with 12 AI nations and 20 tribes EVERY seat holds exactly K cells at tick 0 (tests: `tests/app/matchSetup.test.ts`; every selectable country in `tests/app/fairStartAll.test.ts`). A tribe starts with the troops its cap allows (cap / 3 is below `START_TROOPS`): the "same troops" is the rule among nations; the tribes are weaker by design (M04).
- The spawn phase (20 s without attacks, 5 s immunity) stays a config option of the match (`spawnPhase`, default on): all capitals exist at tick 0 already, so it now only delays the first attack. Part B decides whether the live page keeps the full 20 s.

### D113 Home land, traits and where they live in the state (`src/sim/homeRules.ts`, `src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/serialize.ts`)
When you play a REAL country (and not in the neutral mode) the match has `config.homeRules` (config flag bit 8). Then `GameState.home` holds: the country id of every cell (a constant of the world; the ids of merged territories were replaced by their parent's in the first version, which D127 withdrew: every cell now carries its own country's id), the home country per ownerId and two trait multipliers per ownerId. In `advanceAttack` the price of a cell becomes `cost * pct * mult / (100 * 1000)` (floored once): `pct` is `HOME_COST_PCT` (50) when the cell's country is the attacker's home country (and the attacker has one) and 100 otherwise, `mult` is the DEFENCE multiplier in thousandths of the seat the cell is taken from (1000 for unowned land). So the discount belongs to the owner and to cells of that country only: another seat pays full on the same cell, the owner pays full on every other country's cell, a seat without a home land never matches cell country 0 (tests: `tests/sim/homeRules.test.ts`, mutation-checked). The speed of a capture is unchanged; only the price moves.
TRAITS from map statistics, capped at +-10 % (`TRAIT_CAP_MILLI` 100): `1000 + (share - mid) * gain / 100` thousandths, clamped. Coast share (land cells that touch water, thousandths) gives the PORT INCOME multiplier (stored per seat in the state; nothing reads it until M09), mountain share (Mountain and Peak cells) the DEFENCE multiplier. `TRAIT_COAST_MID_PERMILLE` and `TRAIT_MOUNTAIN_MID_PERMILLE` 150, both gains 50 %: a landlocked or flat country gets x0.925, a share of 350 or more the cap x1.1; the median country (coast 85, mountains 54 thousandths) gets about x0.97 and the 90th percentile (434, 542) the cap. The cap is a rule of the state: `setOwnerRules` clamps (and treats a non-integer as neutral), `deserialize` refuses a snapshot with a multiplier outside 1000 +- 100 (`home-range`), and a test runs every country of the table through the mapping.
State and compatibility: the home block (country u8 per cell, then u8 + u16 + u16 per ownerId) is serialised and hashed ONLY when the flag is on. A match without the flag has `home === null`, the same bytes and the same hash as in M05, so NO golden changed and `ENGINE_VERSION` (5) and `FORMAT_VERSION` (5) stay: an M05 snapshot still loads, and an engine that does not know the flag refuses the new snapshot at the config check. The rule is not "on" for any M05 match. A custom nation or the neutral mode has no home land at all, for every seat (a custom nation next to AI nations with a half-price home would be an unfair start; the clarification of the orchestrator's note was: home land for real countries only, both sides equal).

### D114 Regions and the playable mask (`src/data/regions.ts`, `src/sim/setup.ts`)
A Skirmish region is a union of latitude / longitude rectangles in tenths of a degree, documented in the one file `src/data/regions.ts` (Europe, East Asia, Americas, Africa and the Middle East; the world has none). A cell is inside when its CENTRE is (integer arithmetic, a test restates it in floats). The mask is applied to the match world: every playable cell outside it becomes Ice (`maskTerrain`), so the sim's own playable grid keeps every Spawn, Attack, frontier and enclave inside it; there is no second check to forget. `GameState.terrain` is hashed, so a masked world has its own map hash (`RegionAnalysis.mapHash` is undefined there and the state computes it; the world has the pack's checksum). Tests: no owned cell outside the mask after 3000 ticks of the AI at war in each of the four regions, every playable cell of a masked world is inside the mask, and a Spawn outside is refused with `REJECT_TERRAIN`. The land mass of a capital is measured INSIDE the mask (a country cut by the edge may fall below K), and a capital outside says `outsideRegion` before anything else. East Asia offers 10 countries and Europe 41 (the menu offers `maxAiNations`, not 12, there). The edges are straight lines through the stylised map, a game choice and not a statement.

### D115 Names, the neutral mode and the AI's countries (`src/app/matchWorld.ts`, `src/app/matchSetup.ts`, `src/app/inventedNames.ts`)
Names come from `public/data/meta.json`; the overrides' `neutralNames` replace the disputed ones (Kosovo, Palestine, Western Sahara, ...) in the setup's country table. The "no real borders" mode needs a custom nation (a real country with no name would be a contradiction: `neutralNeedsCustom`); the AI nations then take the same capitals (the countries are only the places) but get invented seeded names (`inventName`: syllables, never repeated in a match, the same in every language), no home land and no traits. A custom nation's flag takes its chosen colour as the field colour (`makeFlagSpec(seed, {primary})`); the flags of the AI nations come from the same generator, seeded by (match seed, country id). A configuration that arrives from an address or a stored preference is checked at run time field by field (`badField`) and answered with an error result (`setup.error.invalid` with the field name), never an exception.

### D116 Flags (`src/ui/flag.ts`, `tests/helpers/softCanvas.ts`)
