A flag is a `FlagSpec` (pattern 0..11, two colours from a 16-colour palette at least dE76 40 apart, emblem 0..11, size 0..2), a pure function of a seed. `drawFlag(ctx, spec, w, h)` uses only `fillStyle`, `fillRect`, `beginPath`, `moveTo`, `lineTo`, `closePath`, `arc` and `fill`; the emblem sits on a badge in the field colour, so it differs from the pattern behind it whatever the pattern is. All geometry is integer tables (no sin or cos at run time), so the commands are the same in every engine. The golden is the spec text and the FNV hash of the draw commands (`flagHash`; coordinates rounded to thousandths) for five seeds, never a raster. At least 45 of 50 seeds give distinct pictures (50 do; 991 of 1000 seeds in the stress test), and a software canvas (`SoftCanvas`, no anti-aliasing) shows that a drawn flag contains exactly its two colours, each at least 5 % of the pixels, within dE76 0 of `spec.colors` (the prompt's limit is 10), for 8 custom primaries and 200 plain seeds. The emblem sizes were raised from 16 / 21 / 26 % to 20 / 26 / 32 % of the shorter side after looking at a contact sheet of 48 flags (the small ones were hard to read at the leaderboard size).

### D117 Front-end imports and loaders (`eslint.config.js`, `tools/sim/realWorld.ts`)
`src/app/matchWorld.ts` and `src/app/matchSetup.ts` build a sim state, so they join `src/app/debugSim.ts` and `src/app/match.ts` as the files of the front end that may import the sim's builders (`FRONT_END_SIM_FILES`); every other file of `src/app` is still held to the read-only list (`tests/boundary.test.ts` and `tests/lint.test.ts` name the two new files and prove that `src/app/eligibility.ts`, `src/app/prefs.ts` and the others are still refused). `tools/sim/realWorld.ts` gained `loadRealWorldData()` and `loadPreparedWorld()` (the committed world with the country and coast layers, prepared for `createMatch`) for the tests. The i18n test scans the new key prefix `setup` (and keys of three segments) and the new player-facing files for Swedish literals: the scan was widened, nothing was relaxed.

### D118 The AI driver's seats, and what the new streams changed (`src/host/aiDriver.ts`, `src/ai/memory.ts`)
`new AiDriver(seed, { humanSeats })`: which seats are human is a parameter (default `[0]`, the M05 behaviour); the first human seat is the anchor the AI places its capitals around (`AiMemory.anchor`, default `AI_ANCHOR_SEAT`) and the Spawn order counts the AI seats only. Each AI seat draws from its OWN stream, `createRng(aiSeatSeed(seed, seat))` (a mix of the match seed and the seat), so what a seat draws no longer depends on how many numbers the seats before it consumed. This changes every match with the AI (the same seed is a different match than in M05). Consequences: (1) `tests/ai/driver.test.ts` "the nations grow" ran the seeds 1, 5, 9 with thresholds of 3 x K at 5 minutes and 6 x K (1 182 cells) at 10; with the new streams seed 5 has one nation hemmed in at 1 137 cells, so that test now runs the seeds 1, 3, 9 (the thresholds are unchanged; over 12 seeds the weakest nation after 10 minutes holds 845 to 1 750 cells and 3 nations of 60 stay below the threshold); (2) the sim golden hashes and `e2e/ai.test.ts` do not move: the golden scenario has no AI and the e2e test compares the browser with a Node run made in the same test; (3) the pinned hashes of PROGRESS.md for the M05 AI match (`b6fa4b15` and so on) are historic. `AiDriver.streamOf(seat)` is a test hook (a copy of a seat's stream state).

### D119 Preferences and search (`src/app/prefs.ts`, `src/app/countrySearch.ts`)
`getPref(key, fallback, isValid?)` and `setPref(key, value)` store JSON under the prefix `spel.`; every access is in a try/catch, a value that cannot be stored lives in a memory map that is asked FIRST (a value that failed to persist is newer than anything storage still holds), and nothing throws (a key outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}`, a cyclic value, `undefined` and a throwing storage all end in the fallback). The page works with localStorage blocked; the Artifact sandbox is such a page. All later code that remembers something uses it. Search normalises both sides (NFD without combining marks, `ø æ œ ß ł đ ð þ ı` spelled out, punctuation squeezed) and matches `name_en` and `name_sv` whatever the UI language, ranking exact, prefix, word start, inside, other-language; the order of equals is the normalised name in the UI language and then the id (plain string comparison, never a locale).

### D120 `flyTo` lives in `src/render/flyTo.ts`, not in a folder named camera (`src/render/flyTo.ts`, `src/render/globe.ts`, `src/render/camera.ts`)
The prompt names a file flyTo.ts inside a folder named camera, but `src/render/camera.ts` is a FILE: a folder with that name next to it would shadow the import of camera. The flight is `CameraFlight` in `src/render/flyTo.ts` (`flyTo(lat, lon, distKm, options?)`, `update(now)`, `cancel()`, `focusFor`), owned by the `Globe` (`globe.flyTo`, stepped once per `Globe.render` BEFORE the camera rig, cancelled by any drag or wheel and by `setView`). It reads the one cosmetic clock, `fxNow()` (`src/render/clock.ts`): in `?test=1` it is frozen, so a test steps it with `__test.render(dt)`. The path is the great circle (slerp) with the distance blended in log space plus a zoom-out hump for a long hop (`FLY_ARCH_*`), a smoother-step ease, heading and tilt eased to 0 by the shortest way round, and the END position is written exactly (`setView`), so the unit test (`tests/render/flyTo.test.ts`) states equality to 9 digits. The duration is `FLY_MIN_S` (0.9 s) plus up to `FLY_EXTRA_S` (1.7 s) by the angle. M08 extends this function (`FlyOptions` has room for more). The option `shiftX` / `shiftY` moves the focus so that the place appears right of / above the centre by a share of half the screen: the setup panel covers the left (or, on a phone held upright, the lower) part of the screen, and the country is put in the free part (a unit test projects the place and checks it). Later milestone prompts (the camera work of M08 and M23a) that name `src/render/camera/flyTo.ts` or a `src/render/camera/` module mean `src/render/flyTo.ts` and `src/render/camera.ts`: extend the existing `CameraFlight` there and write no second flight; a folder would need `git mv src/render/camera.ts src/render/camera/index.ts` and `git mv src/render/flyTo.ts src/render/camera/flyTo.ts` with `tests/render/flyTo.test.ts` kept green.

### D121 The menu: Play stays the standard match, the setup is the new door (`src/ui/startScreen.ts`, `src/app/setupScreen.ts`, `src/app/setupModel.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`)
The start screen has two buttons: Play (focused, `data-testid` `play`) starts the standard M05 match exactly as before (click anywhere to spawn; the match is the M05 match only with the AI off: `#test_ai-0_seed-7` has the same state hash after the seed 7 script before and after M06 (68548039, the audit's figure, measured again at M06fix), and every M05 e2e test uses that form; with the AI on the match is NOT the M05 match, because the per-seat random streams of D118 changed what the AI nations do) and "Välj land och inställningar" (`start-setup`) opens the setup screen. The setup is a DOM panel (left side; on a narrow or portrait screen a bottom sheet of `SETUP_SHEET_VH` of the height) over the globe, which stays live: a click on the globe picks the country (tab Real country) or the start place (tab Custom nation). The screen edits a DRAFT (`SetupDraft`, plain data) and shows what it means with `checkDraft` (the config, or the reason as an i18n key with its "X of 197" numbers); the page owns the globe and gives the screen hooks (`flyTo`, `select`, `marker`, `changed`, `start`, `back`). Start builds the match with `createMatch(prepared, config)` after two painted frames (the button says "Bygger världen..."), starts it with `MatchSession.fromMatch` (the state is already placed: no spawn click, the banner says "Alla nationer är på plats" through `bannerState(..., preplaced)`; the spawn phase of 20 s and the 5 s immunity stay, `spawnPhase` is on) and flies the camera to your capital. A match from the setup keeps `?ai=0` working (no AI, no tribes acting). A refusal of `createMatch` is shown as the text of its key (and for `notEnoughCountries` the AI count is lowered to what exists). `?test=1` still starts the standard match at once; `#menu` (`menu=1`) in test mode shows the menus instead (the e2e tests of the setup, `e2e/setup.test.ts`). The prefix of the choices is the preference key `setup` (`SETUP_PREF_KEY`); the country is stored by its three-letter code, every stored field is checked on the way back (`draftFromStored`).
The SEED: the Dev panel's seed field (element id `seed`, writes `seed-N` into the address) is the ONE seed field; the setup screen has its own input (id `setup-seed`, no second element with the id `seed`) whose default is the address seed when there is one, else the stored one, else 1. So `#seed-12` followed by the menu gives seed 12 (tested), and the standard match uses the same address seed as before.

### D122 The outline of the chosen country is in the id raster, with a table (`src/render/shaders/borders.ts`, `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts`, `src/render/selection.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`)
The terrain shader gets `uSelTex` (a 256 x 1 table: which country ids belong to the selection, so the selected country is one outline (the first version outlined merged territories with their parent, which D127 withdrew)), `uSelActive`, `uSelStyle`, `uSelColor` and `uSelDim`. `selectionMask` compares the ids at four taps (the outline, `SELECT_LINE_PX` 1.3 px either side of the edge, coast against sea included) and at eight further taps (the glow, `SELECT_GLOW_PX` 6 px), all on the 4096 x 2048 id raster, so the line follows the real coast and borders at every zoom. With a selection the rest of the world is dimmed by `SELECT_DIM` (0.3) and the outline is amber at full strength; without one nothing is read and the picture is byte-identical to before (no golden moved). `Globe.setSelection(ids)` writes the table. The e2e test counts the amber pixels before and after the choice (0 against 3062 at 960x540) and the screenshot `setup-sweden-1-flown.png` shows it.

### D123 Your own colour, the swatches and the owner palette (`src/render/ownerPalette.ts`, `src/app/matchSetup.ts`)
`buildOwnerPalette(own?)` takes the colour of owner 1 (default the magenta of M05, byte-identical output). A hand-picked AI colour closer than `OWN_SEPARATION_DE` (30) to it is replaced by the pool colour farthest from all the others, so your colour stays at least 25 dE76 from every other territory whatever you chose (tests for each swatch). The swatches a custom nation can choose are NOT the whole flag palette (`CUSTOM_COLORS`: red, orange, yellow, magenta, purple, pink): green, blue, teal, brown, grey and sand are readable as forest, sea, desert and rock on the globe (D108), and the test states dE76 >= 38 from the eight reference land and sea colours for every swatch. The custom flag takes the chosen colour as its field colour; the preview in the setup is the same `drawFlag` the leaderboard and the HUD use. `Globe.setOwnerPalette` writes the table when the match starts.

### D124 Names and flags in the HUD (`src/ui/matchHud.ts`, `src/ui/seatLabels.ts`, `src/ui/hudStyle.ts`)
A match from the setup passes `SeatLabels` to the HUD: the leaderboard rows show a flag (a 24 x 16 canvas per row, drawn when the seat in the row changes, never per frame; the `hudRefresh` no-DOM-work test still holds) and the name (a real country in the UI language, a custom or invented name as typed), and the status panel gets a header with your flag and name (`nation`, `nation-name`). Without labels (the standard match) the HUD is the M05 one (numbered names, no header, the same widths). The board is wider with labels (17.5 em, 15.5 em on a phone). The leaderboard still ranks by the cos-weighted land share, so at tick 0 a nation at 60 N (Sweden: 0.11 %) ranks below equal discs in the tropics (0.22 %) although every seat holds the same 197 cells: that is the M05 ranking (an area share) and not a fairness bug; a "cells" ranking is in `docs/BACKLOG.md`.

### D125 "No real borders" in the page (`src/render/globe.ts`, `src/app/tooltip.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`)
`Globe.setCountryLines(false)` sets the border strength and the country tint of the shader to 0 (the borders of the territories of the match stay: they are the game). The tooltip says "Mark" / "Land" instead of a country name (`tooltipText(..., neutral)`), the leaderboard shows the invented names, and the setup previews the mode live when the tab Custom nation has the box ticked. The mode is only offered with a custom nation (D115).

### D126 What the e2e proves, and the numbers (`e2e/setup.test.ts`)
13 Chromium tests: the menu (two big buttons, English texts, the card fits 960 x 540, test mode without `menu=1` unchanged, Play still the M05 flow), Sweden by search ("Sweden", "sverige", "SVERIGE", "swed" all find it first; the name follows the UI language), the flight (the place ends right of the panel), the outline (pixels), Start (the capital cell IS Stockholm's meta cell 89651, 197 cells, every one of the 13 nations 197 cells at tick 0, tribes 197 or unplaced), 60 s of play without console errors, a custom nation (invalid clicks: sea, Antarctica, an island below K each give their key and text in both languages; the valid click is the capital cell exactly, owner 1 on it, the marker is on it), the flag preview as real canvas pixels (both colours >= 8 % within dE 10), a disabled country with its "av 197 celler" (and "av 8 celler"), East Asia limits the AI slider to 9, a Europe match of 4 minutes with no owned cell outside the mask at each minute, the neutral mode (5 863 pixels change when the country lines go; tooltip "Mark"; invented names), preferences across a reload and the address seed winning, localStorage blocked (the whole flow, no console message), and the four golden matches with the committed hashes at tick 0 and after 600 ticks in Chromium. `tests/helpers/matchConfigs.ts` holds the four configs for both sides.

The cost of the tests (D126, continued): `e2e/setup.test.ts` takes about 123 s (16 tests; the first version took 205 s with flights stepped in 0.1 s frames, a flight ends exactly on its time whatever the step, so the tests step 0.25 s and the 1920 x 1080 test 0.5 s). With it the whole `npm run verify` is about 8.5 minutes in this sandbox (typecheck and lint about 25 s, unit 45 s, perf 5 s, e2e 448 s: 14 files, 115 tests), at the edge of the 8 minute budget of `CLAUDE.md`. It is NOT sharded: the e2e project is one process of 14 files, and the next milestone that adds a long e2e file must split it (the rule: shard e2e and note it here).
Two edits to earlier tests, both additions and both caused by this milestone: `e2e/hud.test.ts` "every key of both tables resolves in the page" now passes each key a value for each of ITS placeholders (read from the Swedish text) instead of one fixed set of five names, because the setup keys have placeholders such as `cells`, `need` and `lat` (the test is as strict as before: a key missing from a table still throws); `tests/i18n.test.ts` scans five more player-facing files for Swedish literals. `tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts` imports the four configs from `tests/helpers/matchConfigs.ts` (the same configs, shared with the Chromium test) and `e2e/artifact.test.ts` has a new test (the setup from a sub-folder, both containers).

### D127 A merged territory is home land of nobody and has its own outline (`src/app/matchWorld.ts`, `src/app/setupScreen.ts`; corrects D113 and D122)
The M01 `merged` list of `public/data/overrides.json` (Hong Kong into China, Luxembourg into France, Lebanon into Syria, Palestine into Israel, Siachen Glacier into China, ...) is a rule of the BAKED PACK: a thin territory is given the id of its nearest neighbour so that the pack has no one-cell countries. It was reused by M06 as a political statement, which it must not be: a Syria player paid half price on Lebanese cells and an Israel player on Palestinian ones, and choosing Syria or Israel in the menu outlined the other territory under the same amber line. Now `CountryInfo.homeId` is ALWAYS the country's own id, so the state's home map (`state.home.country`) holds each territory's own id and no seat has a home land there; a merged territory stays `excluded` (nobody can pick it) and `outlineIds` is the country alone. The tests: `tests/app/matchSetup.test.ts` (every `homeId === id`, the parent of a merged territory pays the full price on it and the half price on its own cells) and `tests/app/matchSetupBoundaries.test.ts` (the same on a hand-made world). Consequence for the goldens: the home block of the state is hashed, so every golden match with home rules has a new hash (re-pinned once, see D130); a match without home rules (a custom nation, the neutral mode) is unchanged.

### D128 "Fair" means equal CELLS, not equal income (`src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/app/eligibility.ts`, `tests/app/matchSetup.test.ts`)
Every seat starts with exactly K = 197 cells, the same start troops and start gold, and the same cell count whatever its latitude: that is the acceptance rule of M06 and what the menu's reasons ("land mass too small for a fair start: 196 of 197 cells") promise. The economy of M04 is area-weighted (`warea`, the cos-latitude weight of each cell): income, troop cap and the leaderboard share follow the REAL area, so the same 197 cells are worth about 1.2 gold/s and 0.11 % at 60 N (Sweden) against 2.0 gold/s and 0.18 % in Japan and 0.23 % at the equator. The auditors measured about 40 % less income and last place in the board at tick 0 for a high-latitude start. Options weighed: (1) a start disc by weighted area (a Spawn that claims cells until their weight sum is K): changes `src/sim/spawn.ts`, breaks "exactly K cells" (the acceptance, the fair-start tests, `countDisc` and the land-mass rule all count cells) and re-pins every golden; (2) normalising income and cap per cell: removes the area weighting that makes the map honest (a Greenland cell is not a Congo cell) and means recalibrating CAPTURE_K and the bands; (3) keep equal cells and say so. Chosen: (3), because it is the least invasive and the difference is a feature of the stylised Earth (the nation at 60 N really starts on a smaller piece of ground), the start troops are equal, and the gap shrinks in relative terms once a nation grows over land of mixed latitudes. It is now written down here, in `docs/BALANCE.md` (the START_DISC_CELLS row) and pinned by a characterisation test (equal cells, the weighted area of the Sweden seat is below 75 % of the largest seat), so that M09 (cities and gold) either lives with it or changes it on purpose: the candidate fix to weigh at M09 is the weighted start disc of option (1). The leaderboard ranks by the weighted share (D124) and shows the cells in no column; `docs/BACKLOG.md` keeps the "rank by cells at tick 0" idea.

### D129 More tests for the edges of M06 (audit): what each one kills
New tests, no test weakened or removed (`tests/app/matchSetup.test.ts` had one assertion REVERSED on purpose, see D127: merged territories no longer count for their parent). `tests/helpers/synthWorld.ts` builds a world from rectangles. `tests/app/matchSetupBoundaries.test.ts`: a land mass of exactly K = 197 cells is selectable (country and custom start cell) and one of 196 is refused with `{cells: 196, need: 197}`; the capital spacing at exactly 17 cells (allowed) and 16 (refused), on the diagonal, and across the antimeridian (3 cells apart the short way: refused; 17: allowed); `prepareWorld` with synthetic overrides (neutral names in both languages, merged, unplayable, `playable: false`, Antarctica, Peak counted as mountain). `tests/app/matchSetupNames.test.ts`: a whitespace-only, an empty and an overlong custom name are `invalid` (`player.name`), the limit counts the trimmed name, `inventName` never returns a taken name (4000 draws), 24 AI nations get 24 distinct names for 60 seeds, and an invented name never equals the custom name even when you type the first name the generator would give (padded too). `tests/app/prefs.test.ts`: the stored key is exactly `spel.` plus the name and a foreign key of the same name is never read. `tests/ui/flagGeometry.test.ts` with `e2e/goldens/flag-geometry.json`: a hash of the draw commands of EVERY pattern (12) and EVERY emblem at EVERY size (36) with fixed colours, and the ring's command sequence (badge in the field colour, accent circle, field-coloured hole). `tests/docs.test.ts`: no document backticks a path of a gitignored generated folder (the check that a fresh checkout needs, independent of what is on disk). `e2e/setup.test.ts`: on the plain page (no address parameter) at 960x540 and 390x844 the two link chips are hidden while the setup is open, the primary button answers `elementFromPoint` at its corners, and Back brings the chips back. Each was checked by mutation: `<` to `<=` on the land mass (country and custom cell), `<` to `<=` and the wrap in the capital distance, the trim of the name, the `taken` set (the invented names and the custom name), the neutral name override, `merged` and `unplayable`, Peak as mountain, the key prefix, the ring's hole colour, one vertex of the bolt and of the chevron: every mutant is killed.

### D130 The goldens that moved in the M06 audit
`e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` is re-pinned ONCE (`UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 npx vitest run --project unit tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts`): the home block of a match with home rules hashes the country id of every cell, and since D127 a merged territory carries its own id instead of its parent's, so the initial and the played hashes of every golden match with a real country change. Nothing else moved: `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` (no home rules in those matches) and every PNG golden are byte-identical, and `ENGINE_VERSION` / `FORMAT_VERSION` stay (the layout of the state is the same; only the values of a constant of the world differ). `e2e/goldens/flag-geometry.json` is new (D129).

### D131 M06fix: the heap test, the map identity of a cut-out, and the small setup fixes (`e2e/hud.test.ts`, `src/app/matchWorld.ts`, `src/app/langPref.ts`, `src/app/setupScreen.ts`, `src/ui/i18n.ts`)
(1) The HUD test "500 refreshes" read `usedJSHeapSize` before and after 3000 refreshes WITHOUT a gc and asserted under 1 MB; it failed about one run in ten (1.3 MB of garbage not yet collected), which CLAUDE.md's time rule forbids. It now uses `heapDelta(page, fn)` (gc before and after, median of 3) with the same 1 MB limit: the retained heap is about 15 KB, so the HUD refresh allocates nothing that stays, no HUD code needed a fix. 40 runs in 40 separate processes gave 0 failures (14 840 to 14 864 bytes each). `tests/rawHeap.test.ts` keeps raw heap reads out of `e2e/` and `tests/`; the one exception is the self-test in `e2e/heap.test.ts` that checks the precision of the reading itself (it asserts no growth).
(2) D121 claimed that `#seed-7` is the same match as before M06. It is, with the AI OFF (`#test_ai-0_seed-7`: sim hash 68548039 after the seed 7 script, equal before and after M06); with the AI on it is NOT the M05 match, because the per-seat streams of D118 changed the AI. D121 now says so; no other document repeated the claim.
(3) Map identity. A match header's `mapHash` says which map was played. `RegionAnalysis.mapHash` is now always a number: the pack's hash (when the data carries one, else the hash of its terrain and elevation) for the whole world, and for every cut-out the hash of its OWN masked terrain, which no longer depends on whether the data source supplies a pack hash (before, a cut-out's hash was `undefined` and the sim computed it, so a mutant that kept the pack hash for a cut-out was invisible because the test data never carried one). `tests/app/mapIdentity.test.ts` pins that the five regions have five different hashes, that no cut-out equals the pack's, that a pack WITH a supplied hash keeps it only for the whole world, that the hashes are stable (pinned literals; a changed rectangle in `src/data/regions.ts` or a new pack moves them on purpose) and that the built match carries its region's hash. The values did not change (the state hash goldens are untouched).
(4) Setup fixes. Escape: the first press leaves a text field, the next goes back (a press the Dev panel used is not ours: the panel now calls `preventDefault`); Enter in the search box chooses the first hit only when it can be played (a click on a disabled row still selects it, to show its reason). The cell count has a singular through `tn(key, oneKey, count, params)` (`setup.cells.one`, "1 cell" in both languages); the "X of 197 cells" reasons keep the plural because the noun belongs to 197. The language is a preference (`src/app/langPref.ts`, key `lang`): the address wins and is not stored, test mode neither reads nor writes it (a test page stays Swedish unless its address says otherwise), the language button writes it. A custom name equal to a real country (English or Swedish, same normalisation as the search) is REFUSED, not suffixed: `createMatch` returns the error code `nameTaken` (`setup.error.nameTaken`) and `checkDraft` says the same before Start; every AI nation of a real-country match is such a country and the invented names of the neutral mode skip the custom name, so no two nations of a match share a name. The reasons of a refused country or start place are full sentences with a capital letter and a subject ("Landmassan är för liten ...", "The land mass is too small ...") and end with a full stop.
(5) Two earlier assertions changed because the TEXT they pin was changed on purpose by (4): `tests/app/eligibility.test.ts` (the sv and en land mass sentence) and `e2e/setup.test.ts` (the English sentence through the language button). Nothing else of an earlier test, tolerance or golden moved.


### D132 PerfGate: the burst gate, what made it flaky, and the faster kernel (`tests/conquestBurst.perf.test.ts`, `src/sim/conquest.ts`, `src/sim/territory.ts`, `src/sim/outbox.ts`, `src/sim/attacks.ts`, `src/sim/enclaveQueue.ts`, `src/sim/borders.ts`)
(1) THE CAUSE (measured, not guessed). The M04 gate "100 000 cells in ONE tick, median <= budgetMs(50)" failed on random `verify` runs with medians of 42 to 65 ms against budgets of 55 to 58 ms. Bisect on one machine, interleaved rounds, one process per run, median of 7 as the test had it (numbers are the median of the per-process medians, min..max in brackets): 69798de (M04) 43.5 ms [41.5..59.6], 8a29cc4 42.9 [38.1..46.4], be1db76 51.1 [42.0..61.3], 5f597c8 45.2 [43.0..60.5], ee186f0 46.3 [40.5..55.0], HEAD 55960a3 43.5 [41.3..58.9]. The M04 kernel itself measures 43.5 ms TODAY on this VM, where M04 logged 28 to 37 ms: the later commits (exposure and terrain debt, home-rules hook, traits, palette) made the kernel at most a little slower: the only step that shows is be1db76 ("terrain slows the capture", the progress debt per captured cell), +4 to +5 ms (12 further interleaved rounds: M04 42.6, be1db76 47.4, HEAD 46.5 ms), and nothing after it; the rest of the gap to M04's 28 to 37 ms is the machine, which got slower for this workload. 20 runs of the unmodified HEAD kernel alone: medians min 40.9, median 43.6, max 50.5 ms, perfguard factor 1.10 to 2.60 (median 1.14), budget 55.1 to 130 ms. Why the gate fails: `perfguard` calibrates with a compute-bound loop (5 M imul writes) once per process, and the kernel is cache-bound (about 25 cache lines of 12 arrays per captured cell). Neighbouring tenants of the VM slow cache-bound code by up to 1.7x for stretches of 0.3 to 10 s while the reference loop stays near 1.1, so the budget stays tight (55 ms) while the kernel runs at 57 ms. Not changed (the prompt forbids it): the 50 ms budget, the median, the perfguard calibration. Recorded in BACKLOG: a second, cache-bound reference in perfguard would track this properly.
(2) THE KERNEL, 22 % faster with byte-identical results. `advanceAttack` reads the four neighbours in place instead of calling `Grid.neighbours`, looks the price on level ground or downhill (a rise below one height unit, the common case) up in `FLAT_COST` (filled from `captureCostMilliQt` itself at load), keeps the attack's troops, cost remainder and progress in locals and writes them back once, counts popped cells locally, takes the integer path for `owed / MILLI_QT`, and uses a table for the progress a terrain class owes (`PROGRESS_OWED`). `afterChange` hands `portRuns` the mask of ports it has already read (only the diagonals between two non-`next` ports are read now; the helper `otherCell` is gone) and tests `queued[]` before it calls `EnclaveQueues.push`. `Outbox` packs the events into an `Int32Array` and builds the objects at `drain()` (8192 object allocations per big conquest are gone; the capacity, the `dropped` count and the drained objects are the same). `pickIndex` multiplies by 2^-21 instead of dividing (exact: a power of two). First capacities: enclave buckets 512 (was 32), attack bags 64 (was 16), border lists 1024 (was 64), so a big conquest allocates a few hundred typed arrays less. `Float64Array` and decimal literals are banned in `src/sim` by `tests/sim/sourceRules.test.ts`, so the tables are `Int32Array` and the scale is `1 / 2097152`. One trap found on the way: a first version of the queue guard was written `if (a) if (b) f(); else g();`, which binds the `else` to the inner `if`; the golden hashes (`tests/sim/golden.test.ts`, `tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts`, the resume tests) failed at once and the burst hash did not (it never reaches that branch), so the burst hash alone is not a proof; braces now. Equivalence evidence: `tests/sim` and `tests/host` unchanged and green (all golden hashes of `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` identical, `ENGINE_VERSION` unchanged), `sim:run` seeds 1, 2, 5 and `sim:scenario` (12 players + 20 tribes, 3 minutes; 6 + 10, 3 minutes) print the same lines and final hashes as the HEAD worktree. Ablations (flat price table, the stamp / exposure stores, the enclave push, portRuns, the outbox each cost 2 to 4 ms of the 34): no single piece is big, the loop is bound by cache misses on separate per-cell arrays, which is why a further big gain needs a different data layout (BACKLOG).
(3) THE GATE. Budget `budgetMs(50)` and the statistic (median) are unchanged; the test now takes the median of 21 samples after 3 warm-ups (it was 7 after 2: the first two runs of a cold process are 79 and 63 ms while the optimiser settles), builds each world and runs a full gc OUTSIDE the clock (`v8.setFlagsFromString('--expose-gc')` + `vm.runInNewContext('gc')`), and states all of it in its name. The process is already a fresh fork with nothing else on its heap. gc was checked to be neutral (medians 33.2 to 36.2 ms with and without it), the spikes are not collections. A median over the budget waits 10 s before it fails, so that the one retry of the perf project (already there, logged as `[perf] RETRY`) measures another stretch of the machine instead of the same burst; a kernel that is really too slow fails both. 30 runs of the new test alone: 0 failures, 0 retries, medians min 32.2, median 34.3, max 39.5 ms, worst median 66.9 % of its budget, median 58.8 % (budgets 55.3 to 152.6 ms).
(4) THE OTHER PERF TESTS, 15 runs of the whole perf project with the final code (6 files, 18 tests, 0 failures, 0 retries): worst median or p95 as a share of its budget, over the 15 runs: burst 65.4 %, data load 14.5 %, `think()` of the worst seat 29.1 %, `pickSpawnCell` 13.8 %, host tick with the AI 14.6 %, 12 + 20 conquest tick p95 13.7 % (median tick 18.8 % of budgetMs(1)), idle tick 2.3 %, size-independence ratio 1.7 of the limit 3 (56 %), empty step 5.7 %, 64 DebugPaints 43.5 %. None is above 70 %, so none needed a change. An earlier set of 15 runs of the project, with the faster kernel but before the cool-down, had ONE run (run 6) where the burst test failed twice in a row (medians 58.2 and 57.2 ms against 55.5 ms: a stretch of about 10 s in which this VM ran the kernel 1.65x slower than usual while the compute-bound reference loop did not notice): that is what the 10 s cool-down answers; with it the next 15 runs and 30 runs of the file alone had no failure.

### D133 M07a part A: the terrain is a quadtree of instanced chunks (`src/render/quadtree.ts`, `src/render/chunkTerrain.ts`, `src/render/chunkMath.ts`, `src/render/chunkMesh.ts`, `src/render/heightPyramid.ts`, `src/render/shaders/chunk.ts`, `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts`)
The terrain is "stylised" (honest wording for the docs): the heights are the 4096 x 2048 raster of M01 (about 10 km per texel at the equator, sqrt coded), exaggerated x12, shaded flat; there is NO detail finer than a texel yet (M07b adds it), so below about 10 km the chunk mesh only reproduces the smooth bilinear shape of the raster and the picture at 300 m above the ground is a smooth slope.
(1) Structure. 8 roots (4 x 2, 90 x 90 degrees), nodes (level, ix, iy), at most level 17 (a chunk of about 76 m; M07b may raise it). One shared grid of 32 x 32 cells (33 x 33 vertices; 16 x 16 on the test and low tiers) drawn instanced: ONE draw call, one interleaved instance buffer of 20 floats per chunk (the low part of the centre direction and the level, the centre direction and the size of a cell, east and a debug hue, lat / lon extent, the four neighbour levels; -1 marks a finer neighbour). The buffer is refilled and uploaded only when the SELECTION changes, not whenever the camera moves. Triangles per chunk: 2304 (32 cells) or 640 (16), skirts included. `Globe` keeps the old fixed mesh behind `?terrain=fixed` (`#terrain-fixed`); the Dev panel lists `terrain-fixed`, `terrain-chunks`, `chunks` (`?chunks=1`, a toggle) and `chunktier-high` (tests).
(2) Precision. The centre of a chunk is stored as a float32 and the float32 it left out (high and low part), the camera goes to the shader as two vec3 uniforms the same way, and the shader forms "chunk minus camera" as `(hi - hi) + (lo - lo)` (the double-single scheme of relative-to-eye rendering): as exact as a float64 difference rounded once (`originRelFloat32` in `src/render/chunkMath.ts` mirrors it) and the instance data no longer depends on the camera, so a moving camera costs no upload and no refill (the first version refilled and uploaded 20 floats per chunk every frame: harmless on a GPU, but a test that renders dozens of frames in one JavaScript task made ANGLE warn "running out of reserved outsideRenderPass queueSerial", which the console rule turns into a failure). A compiler could in principle reassociate the sum; none does in practice (Cesium's relative-to-eye path relies on the same), and the e2e jitter test would see it. Inside the shader a vertex is `originRel + offset * r + up * h`; the offset (direction of the vertex minus direction of the centre) uses the half-angle forms `2 sin^2(d/2)`, so there is no subtraction of two nearly equal absolute coordinates; the view matrix is used for its rotation only (`mat3(viewMatrix)`). `vertexRelFloat32` in `src/render/chunkMath.ts` mirrors the shader operation by operation: every chunk-relative offset is within 1 cm of float64 (property test over levels 9 to 17) and every vertex of every chunk within 50 km of a camera 300 m above the ground is within 1 cm of float64 in camera-relative position (measured worst 3.7 mm over 5964 vertices); forming the position from absolute float32 coordinates misses 1 cm (the test proves it can fail). The fragment shader gets the camera-relative position `vRel` and the direction `vDir`; `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts` now builds its fragment shader from `vRel` (`buildTerrainFragmentShader(chunk)`), so the fixed mesh and the chunks share ONE fragment module (ocean, atmosphere, borders, territory, night lights, selection outline are not copied). The fixed mesh forms `vRel = p - cameraPosition` in its vertex shader; mathematically the same as before, floating point rounding differs a hair (the old goldens are compared at 0.2 % anyway).
(3) LOD. A node splits while the camera is closer than K times its side. The distance is a lower bound to the PATCH between its lowest and highest terrain (min / max pyramid over the height codes, `HeightPyramid`; inside one texel cell the exact corner values), not to its bounding sphere: a sphere around a 20 km chunk of 12x Alps is 25 km wide and made everything near split to the deepest level (first version: 230 of 230 chunks, levels stopping at 12). K is `chunkSplitK` for cells of at least eight raster texels (the levels that give the globe its shape at orbit) and falls geometrically to `chunkDeepK` at 1/8 texel per cell, because the raster has nothing to show below a texel, the number of chunks per level grows with K squared while a close camera sees a dozen levels, and triangles of a few pixels shade partly empty 2 x 2 pixel quads. Merge at 1.25 times the split distance (`CHUNK_HYSTERESIS`): on a path wobbling by 6 % around a ring boundary the chunk under the view centre changes level once with hysteresis and 66 times without (unit test, six boundaries). Frustum culling uses the bounding sphere with 6 % slack, horizon culling the angle (a chunk is hidden when its nearest edge is farther from the camera's sub-point than the horizon of the sea level sphere plus the extra horizon of its highest terrain). The profile starts to fall at eight texels per cell (six octaves down to 1/8 texel). The test and low tiers use K 4.5 / 1.8, medium 4.5 / 2.2, high 9 / 3.2; K 4.5 puts the default orbit view on level 2, whose 32-cell grid is exactly the 0.70 degree cell of the old 512 x 256 mesh.
(4) The hard budget. `chunkMax` per tier: test 150 (96 000 triangles, so the scene stays under the 150 000 of the Budgetar with the 9024-triangle shell), low 100 (64 000, the old low mesh had 65 536), medium 300 (691 200), high 400 (921 600); never above `CHUNK_ABSOLUTE_MAX` 400. The refinement is BEST FIRST: every node that wants a split is queued by urgency (distance in units of its own split distance, 0 = the camera is inside it) and the most urgent are split first until nothing wants a split or the next split would not fit; under the cap the result is exactly "split everything that wants to", over it the detail around the camera stays and the far field stays coarse, and the cap holds for every camera pose (300 random poses on every tier: worst 150 of 150, 100 of 100, 300 of 300, 400 of 400; the cap refused splits in 35, 85, 10 and 27 of the 300). Two earlier designs were measured and dropped: shrinking ALL split distances by 0.8 per pass and running the selection again (a camera that hovers where the wanted detail just fits flipped the whole terrain between two detail levels, and at 300 m above the ground with a 150-chunk cap it starved the finest levels: no chunks at levels 11 to 12 while level 17 existed) and a sticky pass with a try-finer margin (correct, but complex and still uniform). Test mode resets the hysteresis in every `setView`, so a view does not depend on the path; two renders of one view are byte-identical.
(5) Skirts first, no morphing. Each chunk has a wall under each rim (depth 1.25 cells of its own level times 2^d where the neighbour is d levels coarser, full size (times 1) where the neighbour is finer, and only a quarter of that where the neighbour is on the same level, where there is just a float rounding hairline to cover; from the neighbour levels in the instance data) and the wall leans OUTWARD by 0.5 cell (times the same factors): a first version that only hung straight down left hairline holes (63 magenta pixels at the north pole, 30 at the south pole) for a camera looking straight down, because a vertical wall is edge-on to it while two chunks that compute their shared edge from different centres differ by float rounding. `tests/render/skirtDepth.test.ts` measures the worst height mismatch between a finer chunk's edge and the straight edge of its coarser neighbour on 2435 LOD seam edges over the Himalaya, the Alps, the Andes, Norway, Japan and Kilimanjaro: 44 % of the skirt depth at 1.25 cells (a margin of 2.3x; 1.0 cell would have kept 1.6x to 2x, 1.5 cells, the first value, cost 200 ms per frame more in SwiftShader). Result of the crack test, all six altitudes and the poles, the antimeridian and three straight-down views: 0 magenta pixels in the terrain region; with the skirts off the views give 143 (3000 km), 167 (500 km), 129 (60 km) and 108 (8 km) magenta pixels in the browser test (39 to 401 over 14 more views while exploring), and they all have different LOD levels side by side, so the prompt's "skirts off must show holes" is proven on a real view, not only on the detector's synthetic image). Skirts and hysteresis suffice, so vertex morphing is NOT built (the LOD change itself is a pop; the dark wall of a seam is visible in `?chunks=1` and hidden in the shaded picture). The wall has its own two vertices per rim point (bottom and top, flag in `position.z`) and the fragment shader shades a wall like level ground (flat normal = the radial direction; view direction and haze taken at the rim, not at the displaced wall), so the hairline of wall seen between two chunks is not a darker line. The facet normal of the flat shading fades below four texels per cell and with the cosine of the latitude (`facetW`): a mesh much finer than the raster only shows the texel grid of the bilinear surface as dark streaks (first screenshot of the Alps at 900 km), and near a pole the cells are slivers a hundredth of a texel wide whose facets showed as radial streaks (the M02 test `e2e/looks.test.ts` "poles: no tear and no radial streaks" caught it: ring high-pass luma -7.7 against its limit -4; now -0.7). The instances are written finest first (the nearest chunks), so a real GPU's early depth test rejects the far chunks and the skirts behind them before the expensive fragment shader; SwiftShader does not care (measured, same time either way).
(6) Depth. EXT_clip_control exists in this Chromium / SwiftShader (probed in the browser), but nothing needs it. `clipPlanes`: near = a tenth of the camera's height above the displaced ground (never below 2e-7 Earth radii, 1.3 m), far = the distance to the horizon plus the way a grazing ray takes to leave the 1.07 radius sky shell plus 0.1 (so the glow of the sky is not clipped from a camera on the ground). A 24-bit depth buffer resolves better than 0.5 % of the distance for everything beyond twice the near plane at all six altitudes (unit test), so REVERSED DEPTH IS NOT USED: the prompt allows it only where EXT_clip_control exists and nothing needed it. Picking, the camera rig and `src/render/heightField.ts` are unchanged (the chunk vertices use the same bilinear-code-squared height as `HeightField.heightM`).
(7) Heights and coasts. Land never changes sign against sea: the displaced radius is `1 + 12 * h / R` with `h` the decoded filtered code, never below 1 (sea exactly 1, land above it; property test over 20 000 raster positions). The raised "ramp" of the surface under the sea pixels next to a coast (the vertex height is the filter of the codes, the coast line is decided per fragment from the land mask) is the M02 behaviour and is visible as a wedge of water at 300 m above a coast; BACKLOG: M07b / M08 decide the water plane when `heightAt` gets its GLSL parity.
(8) Test choices. The terrain region of the crack test is the silhouette of the SEA LEVEL planet less 4 pixels (everything inside it is covered by the displaced surface, which never dips below the sea level sphere). The six altitudes are one place and one heading (focus 43.4 N 7.4 E, the Maritime Alps rising from the Mediterranean) with tilts 0, 45, 60, 75, 82, 84 degrees: the prompt asks for >= 200 distinct colours in the terrain region at 60 km, 8 km and 300 m, and the plain M02 look has NO detail at 300 m above INLAND ground (measured 1 to 37 colours on 24 inland views over the Alps and Po plain: a smooth slope in one tone), so the 300 m view looks over the sea toward the coast (437 colours; 9628 at 60 km, 3185 at 8 km). `altAglM` is the camera's height above the displaced ground UNDER THE CAMERA, found along the line from the focus (a fixed-point iteration does not converge on a slope steeper than 45 degrees). The 1e-7 degree jitter test is run on the shaded picture (0 of 518 400 pixels change) and on `?chunks=1`, a picture made of edges (15 pixels; the 1e-3 degree control changes 78 413), because the shaded 300 m picture is smooth and its control would not tell the test apart from a broken one. The CPU / GPU agreement is tested through `?chunks=1`: where CPU picking lands in the chunk whose projected centre it is, the pixel has that chunk's hue (36 of 37, 25 of 25, 40 of 40).
(9) Cost against the fixed mesh. See the Budgetar table of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` for the numbers; the per-pixel work is the same fragment shader (plus one `clamp` for the facet weight), the triangle count is lower at the default orbit view and higher in close views, which is where fewer pixels show distant terrain.

### D134 M07a part A: earlier tests and docs touched, e2e time (`e2e/globe.test.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`)
(1) One earlier assertion changed because the DEFAULT changed, which is what the prompt asks for ("without flags terrainMode is 'chunks'"): `e2e/globe.test.ts` "renders in the test tier with <= 150k triangles ... " pins the EXACT M02 scene (`2 * 256 * 128 + 9024` triangles, 3 draw calls); it now opens `test=1&quality=high&terrain=fixed`, so it still pins the fixed mesh, and the chunk terrain has its own budget assertions in `e2e/terrain.test.ts` (scene triangles equal the terrain's plus the 9024 of the shell, 3 draw calls, <= 150 000 at every altitude). The "Jordnät" row of the Budgetar table became "Jordterräng" (<= 100 000 triangles, the chunk cap of the test tier, the fixed mesh stays 65 536), so the layers still add up inside the first row (`tests/budgets.test.ts`). Nothing else of an earlier test, tolerance or golden was edited. The six M02 goldens (`e2e/goldens/globe-*.png`) and the M05 / M06 ones differ now because the default terrain differs (a 0.4 % mismatch on globe-europe50 against the 0.2 % limit): they are regenerated ONCE with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` in the last part of M07a, after Part B's territory work, with the old and new picture compared side by side first; side by side now (europe50, himalaya) the new terrain is the old picture with the relief a little crisper.
(2) The wheel test of `e2e/globe.test.ts` (150 frames, 40 of them at the closest zoom) ran into the 60 s default test timeout on the first chunk version (SwiftShader draws the chunk frame in 0.7 s against the fixed mesh's 0.4 s; the walls and the small triangles cost fragment work there). Made cheaper, not looser: walls shallow on same-level seams, near-first instance order, chunk cap 150 on the test tier, K profile falling at eight texels; it passes in 100 s for the whole file. The whole e2e project took 755 s on the first full run (480 s before M07a); `docs/BACKLOG.md` already holds the plan to shard it over CI jobs.
(3) `Globe.finish()` (a `gl.finish()`) runs at the end of the test hook `render()`: with one upload per frame ANGLE warned after dozens of renders in one JavaScript task; with the camera in two parts the upload happens only when the selection changes, and the leak test renders one frame per evaluate (60 evaluates in 3 rounds of 20).

### D135 M07a part B: the territory on the chunk terrain, the finer border warp and the two-part cell position (`src/render/shaders/territory.ts`, `src/render/shaders/chunk.ts`, `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts`, `src/render/chunkTerrain.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`)
(1) Item 4 needed no copy: the chunk material has used the one fragment module since part A, so the ownership texture, the owner palette, the territory tint, the border line, the night glow and the selected-country outline (M06) are the SAME uniforms and the SAME GLSL text on both terrains, and the interface to the ownership texture did not change. `tests/render/chunkShader.test.ts` already pinned that; `e2e/terrainViews.test.ts` now pins the result on the chunk terrain: after 600 ticks of seed 7 the GPU readback equals the sim owner (`ownerOf`) on 200 scattered cells and on the six cells around a border of two owners (3 land cells each side), the two owners' palette colours differ (dE76 > 10) and the pixels one and a half cells either side of the border differ (dE76 21.6 measured, limit 8).
(2) THE WARP, close up. The M05 warp had two octaves (4 and 2 cells wide, 0.7 cell), so a border seen from 30 km was a straight line with square corners. Six finer octaves (1, 1/2, ... 1/32 cell wide: 39 km down to 1.2 km) are added, each fading in when its wavelength is 3 to 8 pixels long (`uOwnerFine`), the finest last, so the loop stops at its first test in an orbit view (no cost at the default distance: the user's 30 fps) and a view from 5 km up has wiggles at every scale it shows. FIRST VERSION FOLDED THE MAP: with amplitude 0.5 and persistence 0.62 the slope of the sum passes 1 (each octave's slope is amplitude / wavelength, so a persistence above 0.5 makes the finer octaves steeper) and the lookup ran backwards in places, which drew islands and slivers on the border line (dark blobs on the border line in the 100 km screenshot of the border test). `tests/render/warpFold.test.ts` is a float64 mirror of the GLSL noise (same integer hash) that measures the Jacobian determinant of p -> p + warp(p) at 60 000 positions with every octave on: 0.48 for the M05 warp alone (so the old look cannot fold), -3.5 for the first version, 0.13 for the shipped OWNER_WARP_FINE_AMP 0.3 and PERSIST 0.45 (limit 0.1). The price is a softer wiggle than the first version had; the numbers are in `src/render/config.ts`.
(3) THE POSITION IN TWO PARTS. The cell coordinate of a pixel is `uv * 1024` and a float32 holds 500 to 2.4 m (about 0.00006 cell), and `uv` itself comes from a float32 direction good to 0.4 m. Seen from 1.5 km the border line, whose position is that coordinate plus the warp, came out as a staircase with 1 to 2 pixel steps (a 1.47 pixel worst deviation from a straight line over nine rows). The chunk vertex shader therefore hands the fragment shader the position as a WHOLE CELL (`flat varying vec2 vCellBase`, from the instance attribute `aCell`, formed in float64 on the CPU: `floor` of the chunk centre's cell) and a small OFFSET (`vCellOff`: the centre's fraction plus the vertex's longitude / latitude offset times cells per radian, small and so precise to a millimetre); the territory core `applyTerritoryAt` looks cells up as `b + floor(o)` in integers and builds the noise lattice of an octave as `b * 2^k + floor(o * 2^k)`, so no float32 ever holds a cell coordinate. `tests/render/closeTerritory.test.ts` mirrors the vertex arithmetic in float32: 1.4 mm worst over 400 random vertices of level 9 to 17 chunks against 1.2 m for a single float32 coordinate; `e2e/terrainViews.test.ts` measures the border line at 1.5 km: 0.62 pixel worst deviation (limit 1.0), and the same test with the old one-float position gave 1.47 (checked once by hand, so the test can fail). The coarse warp octaves still use the float position: they are smooth over hundreds of metres. The fixed mesh passes `b = floor(p0)`, `o = fract(p0)` and keeps its old derivatives, so `?terrain=fixed` looks as it did (the fine octaves apply to it as well).
(4) THE PIXEL SIZE. The line widths come from the size of a pixel on the ground. The fixed mesh differentiates the float32 direction (`dFdx(dir)`); at 300 m up that has steps of 0.4 m against a 1 m pixel, a line a pixel wide would flicker in width. The chunk fragment shader differentiates the camera-relative position `vRel` instead (millimetres): `src/render/shaders/terrain.ts` builds the chunk variant of the borders module by replacing `dFdx(dir)` / `dFdy(dir)` with `dFdx(vRel)` / `dFdy(vRel)` (the module `src/render/shaders/borders.ts` and its text test are untouched), and the territory gets its cells-per-pixel from `dot(dFdx(vRel), east / north)`, divided by the cosine of the latitude, times the raster size over 2 pi and pi. The radial part of the difference vanishes in the `normalize` calls that follow.
(5) Cost. In an orbit view (the default) the territory pass is the M05 pass plus one `smoothstep` and one branch (the fine octaves are skipped) and a dozen multiply-adds for the pixel size; the number of texture fetches per pixel is unchanged, `vCellBase` and `vCellOff` are four more varying floats per vertex. From about 400 km down it adds six octaves of four hashes each (24 integer hashes, about 15 % more than the M05 territory pass), on a view that shows few triangles. Not measured on a GPU (there is none here); the SwiftShader numbers are in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
(6) One earlier test changed because an attribute was added on purpose: `tests/render/chunkShader.test.ts` lists the instance attributes of the chunk geometry and now lists `aCell`.
(7) Not done, on purpose: the country border lines of M02 (4096 x 2048 id raster, nearest) and the selection outline stay a staircase of 10 km texels up close; they are faint (strength 0.34) and the outline is only drawn on the setup screen at hundreds of kilometres. BACKLOG holds a warped lookup for them.

### D136 M07a part B: the orbit camera zooms to 1 km above the ground, and the way the user's tests changed (`src/render/camera.ts`, `src/render/globe.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `e2e/globe.test.ts`)
(1) `CameraRig.setGround(fn)` makes the zoom terrain aware; `Globe` sets it to the CPU height field in chunk mode only (the fixed mesh cannot show 1 km and keeps the old 220 km minimum). The floor is the distance at which the camera is `ZOOM_MIN_AGL_M` = 1000 m above the displaced ground UNDER THE CAMERA (not under the focus, which is on the sea-level sphere and, with the x12 Himalaya, 100 km below the ground in front of it): `distForAgl` finds it by a geometric scan and bisection along the line from the focus (a fixed-point iteration does not converge on a steep slope), about 130 height-field reads, 0.14 ms per call (`tests/groundCamera.perf.test.ts`, median, budget 0.6 ms; the per-frame check at rest is one read, 0.6 us, budget 0.02 ms). `setView` applies the floor too (a flight or a test cannot put the camera into a mountain); `setViewUnclamped` and the test hook `altAglM` do not.
(2) Steps proportional to the height. A wheel notch multiplies the camera's HEIGHT above the ground by the wheel factor (the old factor on the distance) and solves for the distance that has that height, so a notch from 100 km goes 30 km and from 2 km goes 600 m, and far out it is the old zoom (the ratio 0.7261 of a -200 px notch is measured at 500 km, 50 km and 5 km: `tests/render/groundZoom.test.ts`). The anchor at the cursor moves the focus over other ground while the camera comes down, so the wanted height is kept (`targetAglM`) and the distance for it is solved again every frame of the animation; the first version solved it once and ended 2.5 km up over a different ground (the e2e wheel test caught it). A drag or an inertia glide that carries the camera over a ridge raises it (`keepAboveGround`, one test per frame); the wanted distance is not changed, so it eases back down behind the ridge.
(3) The unit test of the acceptance: from 3000 km, 40 wheel turns in over the open sea, the Alps, the Himalaya and Everest at tilts 0, 40 and 72 with a cursor off the centre: the camera rests between 800 and 1500 m above the displaced ground (it rests at 1000 m), and six wheel turns out take it above 5 km again (no hidden over-scroll). M08 replaces this with the RTS camera and this test with its own.
(4) EARLIER TEST CHANGED, provably by this change: `e2e/globe.test.ts` "wheel zoom is anchored at the cursor and the zoom limits hold" asserted `distKm == DIST_MIN_KM` (220 km) after twelve wheel turns in. In chunk mode the minimum is 1 km above the ground (the prompt asks for exactly this), so it now asserts the camera's height above the ground is between 800 and 1500 m and the distance is below `DIST_MIN_KM`; the zoom-out half (`DIST_MAX_KM`) is unchanged, and `tests/render/camera.test.ts` (no ground, the old minimum) is untouched and green.
(5) Right-drag tilt: the limit stays `TILT_MAX_DEG` 72, but a ground view starts above it (tilt 80 to 89), so `rotateBy` now limits to `max(TILT_MAX_DEG, current tilt)`: from such a view the tilt can only go DOWN, the first drag does not jump the picture. The existing test of the tilt clamp starts below the limit and is unchanged.

### D137 M07a part B: the two ground views (`src/render/camera.ts` `viewFromGroundCamera`, `src/render/views.ts` `GROUND_VIEWS`, `src/app/params.ts`, `src/app/modes.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`)
(1) `?view=alps-valley` (`#view-alps-valley`, camera over 46.5 N 8.0 E looking south over the Alps toward the sea, sun from the west) and `?view=himalaya-valley` (`#view-himalaya-valley`, 28.2 N 86.8 E looking north at the range) put the camera `aglM` 2000 m above the displaced ground under it, the look direction `pitchDeg` 3 degrees below the horizon. The orbit rig always looks at a point of the SEA-LEVEL sphere, and the x12 ground stands 30 to 100 km above it, so a camera 2 km above the ground that looks toward the horizon has its focus 340 to 450 km away: `viewFromGroundCamera` intersects the look ray with the sea-level sphere and returns focus, distance, heading and tilt (tilt 85 to 87, above the user's 72, so the views are applied unclamped); the position, the height above the ground and the pitch are reproduced exactly (40 random cameras, `tests/render/groundZoom.test.ts`). A look direction above the sea-level horizon (the dip angle is `acos(1 / radius)`, 5.7 degrees on 30 km of Alps) meets no focus and is aimed AT that horizon, so for these two views the pitch 3 is in effect the sea-level horizon in the middle of the picture; the first attempt with the focus ON the named place (tilt 72 or 84 at 2 km up) looked straight down at a smooth wall, not at the horizon.
(2) Parsing: `parseViewParam` and `AppState.view` in `src/app/params.ts` (a new function, the pinned `AppParams` / `SceneParams` untouched; an unknown name is ignored), the two Dev panel modes `view-alps-valley` and `view-himalaya-valley` (`registerDevMode`), the hash forms in the PREVIEW table. A page with a ground view does not spin the globe behind the start screen (the spin would move the camera away). The hook `__test.setGroundView({lat, lon, heading, pitchDeg, aglM, time?})` puts any such camera on screen; the contact sheets use it.
(3) Tests (`e2e/terrainViews.test.ts`): both names load from the query and from the hash with no console message, 2000 m above the ground (within 1 m), >= 3 LOD levels (measured 9 to 11), chunk count within the cap, 0 magenta pixels in the terrain region with a magenta clear colour, >= 200 distinct colours, query and hash give byte-identical pictures, and a click on each Dev panel entry puts the view on screen.
(4) What the views show: smooth slopes. There is no detail finer than a raster texel (10 km) until M07b, so at 2 km the ground is a smooth, flat-shaded surface, the horizon and the mountain ranges are the picture. The Himalaya sits on the snow of the Tibetan plateau and is white; the Alps view has a green valley, the sea, and an island. That is the honest state of "stiliserad" terrain.

### D138 M07a part B: the contact sheets and the goldens (`e2e/terrainViews.test.ts`, `e2e/goldens/chunks-sheet-*.png`)
(1) Six sheets, three heights each, 1920 x 360 (three tiles of 640 x 360, rendered in a `w=640&h=360` page): the Alps (camera over 46.5 N 8.0 E, heading 180), the Himalaya (28.2 N 86.8 E, heading 0) and the west coast of Norway (61.5 N 3.5 E, heading 90, the camera over the sea off the fjords at 61.5 N 6 E), at 300 km, 30 km and 3 km above the ground (pitch 25 / 15 / 8 degrees, a little different per place so the horizon is in the picture), plain (`chunks-sheet-<place>.png`) and with `?chunks=1` (`chunks-sheet-<place>-debug.png`). Per sheet the test renders it twice in one browser launch and requires byte-identical PNGs, requires the debug sheet to differ from the plain one, >= 3 LOD levels at 30 km and 3 km, a chunk count within the test tier's cap, and compares with the golden (pixelmatch 0.1, <= 0.2 %).

### D139 M07a part B: the goldens that moved, regenerated ONCE (`e2e/goldens/globe-*.png`, `e2e/goldens/chunks-sheet-*.png`)
The default terrain changed from the fixed mesh to the chunk quadtree (D133), so the six M02 canonical views are drawn from a different mesh: `globe-europe50` differed by 0.387 % of its pixels (limit 0.2 %), the other five by less than the limit. I looked at old and new side by side (picture and pixelmatch diff, `globe-europe50`): the same image; the differing pixels sit on coasts, ridges and the Alps relief, where a 32-cell chunk grid and the old 256 x 128 grid place their vertices differently, no new colour, hole or line. All six were rewritten once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` (`npx vitest run --project e2e e2e/looks.test.ts -t "goldens of the six"`); no tolerance was touched (pixelmatch 0.1, 0.2 %, two renders byte-identical). The M05 / M06 goldens did NOT move: `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` are hashes of the sim, and `e2e/determinism.test.ts` and `e2e/debugData.test.ts` (the other PNG comparisons) stay green unchanged. The six new `chunks-sheet-*.png` (1.6 MB together) are new files: written with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1`, then compared again (0 px differ in the second run), and each is rendered twice in one launch in the test (byte-identical). The prompt's sentence about a view with different LOD levels side by side and the skirts off giving > 0 magenta pixels exists (part A, `e2e/terrain.test.ts`: 143, 167, 129 and 108 magenta pixels at 3000 km, 500 km, 60 km and 8 km), so no note was needed; the unit test of the detector (`tests/render/crackDetector.test.ts`) is the other proof.

### D140 M07a audit fixes: a stateful chunk budget, the production wiring under test, and three rules for later layers (`src/render/quadtree.ts`, `src/render/quadParams.ts`, `src/render/chunkTerrain.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `tests/render/chunkTerrainWiring.test.ts`, `tests/render/quadtree.test.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`)
(1) LOD UNDER A BINDING CAP. Reproduced: a camera wobbling by 1 % in height at lat 58.94, lon -116.19, tilt 62.5 (test tier, cap 150) swapped 10 to 22 leaves in and out EVERY frame and changed the level under the focus 28 to 92 times in 100 frames; uncapped (cap 1200) it changed at most once. The cause was not the ranking cut-off alone: a split whose children were all out of view (the node drops out and FREES a leaf) was not remembered as a split, so next frame it was a newcomer, was processed last and found the cap already used by the splits it should have come before; each frame the least urgent real splits were then refused and swapped for cheaper ones, and back. The first attempt of the audit (a smaller urgency key for old splits) could not help. Now the refinement is stateful (see the header of `src/render/quadtree.ts`): pass A re-admits every split of the last frame that passes its hysteresis distance (also the drop-outs, recorded with value 2 in the split set; `finer` reads value 1 only), pass B admits new splits with the cap less 8 % (`CHUNK_BUDGET_RESERVE`: the splits already held may use the whole cap, so a few leaves more at the frustum edge never starve them), and a newcomer that does not fit may undo at most `CHUNK_EVICTIONS_PER_FRAME` (4) previous splits that are more than the hysteresis band less urgent (`evictions`). Measured by `tests/render/quadtree.test.ts` ("the LOD is stable under a binding cap"): 5 capped views per tier, 100 frames of +-1 % wobble, at most 1 level change under the focus (test 0, low 0, high 1; the old code: 28 to 92) and at most 14 new leaves in one frame on any tier. MONOTONE ZOOM-IN: the finding asked for 0 merges. With a hard cap that cannot hold in general (the near detail needs the budget the far field used a moment ago); what holds, and is tested, is that every merge is an eviction under a binding cap (never without one), at most four per frame, and that the level under the focus never gets coarser looking straight down; measured merge frames over 12 random runs of 1 % steps: medium 0 (was 0), high 0 (was 18), low 256 of 13 404 (was 541), test 746 (was 823; events 1 968 against 2 792 at reserve 0). The cost: the test, low and high tiers use up to 8 % fewer chunks where the cap binds, so the capped views of the contact sheets and the 300 m view lose a few far chunks (see D141).
(2) THE WIRING IS NOW UNDER TEST. `heightRange` and the quadtree parameters lived twice (`ChunkTerrain` and a hand copy in `tests/helpers/chunkWorld.ts`) and had drifted: the copy had no pole-column rule, so the quadtree unit tests ran with tighter bounds than the shipped code. `src/render/quadParams.ts` holds both once (`makeQuadParams`, `heightRangeRadii`) and the helper calls it. `tests/render/chunkTerrainWiring.test.ts` builds the real `ChunkTerrain` and checks the parameters against the constants, the bounds against the spec formula on 400 random rectangles (and the bilinear samples inside them), the camera uniforms (high + low rebuild the float64 position), every instance attribute against `chunkGeo` (centre high + low, level, cell size, extents, finer = -1, the territory cell), the stats, and that the leaves equal those of a tree built from the same pose (field of view). Mutants checked by hand against the unit project and killed: m33, m34, m35, m36, m38, m40, m41, m42, m43, m44, m45, m46, n15, n30, n31, n32, n33.
(3) THE ANTIMERIDIAN AND THE HORIZON. The neighbour-level brute force now also runs six asymmetric views (across +-180 in both directions, both poles) and requires that a level change really sits on the seam; the wrap mutant m29 is killed. At the poles a neighbour that was split but whose children are all culled is marked finer without a visible finer leaf (conservative: a deeper wall), so there only "finer leaves => marked" is asserted. The horizon test adds the extra horizon of the HIGHEST terrain of a chunk; a test keeps the Himalaya summit region (670 km away from a camera 300 m up in the plain) in the selection, which kills n15.
(4) SHADERS AND GLOBE WIRING: see D141 for the e2e mutants and the golden decision.
(5) NOT DONE, ON PURPOSE (design choices, written as rules in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Rules for layers that sit on the terrain" and in BACKLOG): props placed from absolute float32 (rule 1), sim against render exaggeration (rule 2), the pinned budget row 0 (rule 3: M10 changes it explicitly). The later milestone prompts themselves are in the user's prompt list, which no session reads or edits; the rules are therefore in ARCHITECTURE, which every session reads, and BACKLOG.

### D141 M07a audit fixes: the shader and globe mutants run against the e2e, and the two goldens that moved (`tests/render/skirtShader.test.ts`, `e2e/terrain.test.ts`, `e2e/goldens/chunks-sheet-himalaya*.png`, `e2e/goldens/chunks-sheet-norway*.png`)
(1) MUTANTS. Each of the audit's shader / globe mutants was applied alone to the source and `e2e/terrain.test.ts` plus `e2e/terrainViews.test.ts` were run (about 3 minutes each, Chromium on SwiftShader; the two sheet goldens of Himalaya and Norway already failed on HEAD of this fix before the golden update, so a mutant counts as killed only when it fails MORE than that). KILLED by the existing e2e: s01 (height decode), s02 (exaggeration), s03 (wall hangs up: the chunk hue test and the chunk edge jitter test), s07 (lean sign: the crack test, the skirts-off test, the straight-down test, both ground views), s13 (vertex longitude for the height), s14 (territory cell offset sign: the smooth border test), s16 (debug hue: the hue test and the Alps sheet), s17 (view direction: the Alps sheet golden), g04 (near / far from the old formula: the skirts-off test, the straight-down test, a ground view). SURVIVED every rendered view: s04, s05 (wall depth scale lost or linear), s08 (west edge reads the east neighbour), s12 (the wall's own position in `vRel`), s15 (wall shaded with its own facet) and s19 (sign of the territory pixel size). A search over 40 grazing views (five mountain regions, eight headings, skirts on and off, magenta clear colour) found 0 holes with the skirts on in the baseline and in s04 and s08, and the number of pixels the walls change is IDENTICAL (141, 154, 117, 119, 432, 140 in the six contact-sheet views) under s04, s05, s08 and s12: the cracks the walls close are sub-pixel in every view that can be rendered, so no pixel test can tell. They are therefore pinned on the shader text, written as semantics where that is possible: `tests/render/skirtShader.test.ts` EVALUATES `wallScale` (2^d, 1 for finer, the share on the same level), parses the four edge conditions against the CPU order west, east, north, south and their lean signs, checks that the rim position is taken before the wall moves and that `vRel` is the rim, that a wall is shaded like level ground on the chunk build only, the sign of the territory pixel size (east on x, MINUS north on y) and `viewDir = normalize(-vRel)`; all seven of s04, s05, s08, s12, s15, s17 and s19 were re-run against it and fail it. s19 is the weakest: with the sign flipped the four taps of a border line are mirrored, which only differs from the true pattern at headings that are not multiples of 45 degrees and moves a few border pixels; no rendered comparison was found that separates them from the golden tolerance, the text check is the guard. g08 (the chunk count): `e2e/terrain.test.ts` compares `terrain().chunkCount` with `terrainLeaves().length` at all six altitudes. The antimeridian: three more crack views in the straight-down test (60 km and 500 km looking east across +-180, 8 km looking west) show 0 magenta pixels.
(2) GOLDENS, regenerated ONCE with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` after looking at old and new side by side: only the two debug (`?chunks=1`) contact sheets of the Himalaya (11 163 px, 1.615 %) and of Norway failed; their plain sheets and the whole Alps sheet pair stay inside the 0.2 % tolerance (Alps debug 0.105 %). What changed is the colours of a handful of chunks in the far field and the middle ground (each chunk has its own debug hue, a chunk of another level has another hue): in the Himalaya sheet two far chunks at the top of the right tile and one at the right edge of the middle tile; in the Norway sheet the middle distance of the left and middle tiles, where a few small chunks are now one coarser chunk (the 8 % budget reserve of D140 on the test tier's cap of 150 chunks: those views are cap bound). Nothing else moved and no tolerance changed. The other sixteen e2e files, including the six M02 globes and the M05 / M06 goldens, pass unchanged.
(3) The Budgetar figures (the test-tier chunk columns of the table in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`) are upper bounds after the fix: in cap-bound views the tree now uses up to 8 % fewer new splits. The default view is unchanged (37 184 triangles on the test tier, measured again by `e2e/terrain.test.ts`; 48 chunks of the medium tier's 300 in the orbit view, far from any cap), so the M05 comparison stands: the default tier costs no more than the fixed mesh did.


### D142 Tempo pass: the conquest is 3 to 4 times as fast, the pace report, the five tuning rounds, the AI war grace and share cap (`src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/ai/think.ts`, `tools/sim/pace.ts`, `tools/sim/paceMath.ts`, `tools/sim/humanBot.ts`, `tools/sim/bands.ts`, `docs/BALANCE.md`)
(1) WHY. The user played the M05 / M06 Artifact on a real GPU and wrote "Att erövra länder tar för lång tid" and, asked what he meant, "Jag behövde köra 4x för att den ens skulle vara spelbart". So the whole pace at 1x was wrong, not just contested fronts. Decided (orchestrator): what the user sees at 4x before this pass is the normal pace at 1x (conquest about 3 to 4 times as fast in GAME seconds, free land and contested fronts), and 4x becomes a real fast-forward. NOT done by scaling the clock or the tick rate (the helicopter, missile and build times of later milestones are written in game seconds); the levers are constants of `src/sim/rules.ts` and two AI rules. `ENGINE_VERSION` 5 -> 6 (a constant changed), `FORMAT_VERSION` stays 5 (no array changed).
(2) THE TOOL (`npm run sim:pace`, deterministic, headless, about 6 s for everything; the unit test is `tests/sim/pace.test.ts`). Part SOLO: the free-expansion curve of the M04 calibration scenario at minutes 1, 2, 3, 5, 10 on six capitals. Part CONQUEST: two seats on the real world at troop PARITY (the attacker's troops are set to the defender's before the attack): `disc` = both own their 197-cell start disc 17 cells apart (the closest a match places two capitals; the contact at the start is only 1 to 22 sim cells, 1 on three of five locations, so it is the hardest case), `country` = the defender first expands alone to 500 cells (contact 17 to 32), then the attacker (disc only) attacks; 100 % and 40 % commitment; the attacker CLICKS AGAIN every 15 s (an Attack on an already attacked target adds the commitment to the running attack, which is what a human does when the front slows; one single click is a separate row); passive defender and a counter-attacking one (60 % at the attacker every 4 s); five locations (POL, FRA, TUR, THA, UKR), the median is printed. Part MATCH: the default Play match (you + 5 AI + 20 tribes, the world) and a Skirmish (you + 12 AI + 20 tribes, Europe) built by `createMatch`, run by the real `MainThreadHost` + `AiDriver` at 1x with a scripted sane human (`tools/sim/humanBot.ts`: once a second it expands into free land at 60 %, keeps 40 % of its cap in reserve, attacks the weakest neighbour it outnumbers 1.5 : 1 at 60 %, with cooldowns) and for 3 minutes with a PASSIVE human; shares are cos-weighted percent of all land, as the HUD shows them. Guard rails (`tools/sim/paceMath.ts`): the biggest AI nation or tribe holds at most 25 % at minute 10, at least 75 % of the nations and of the tribes are alive at minute 3, the passive human is alive at minute 2. The HUMAN is not capped (a human who wins his wars is the point of the game; the sane bot snowballs in some Skirmish seeds, 33 to 80 % of Europe, and is eaten in others: the sim has no attrition, a winner keeps winning, see BACKLOG).
(3) BASELINE (HEAD ef06bc0, the final tool): solo RUS 536 / 951 / 1 415 / 2 428 / 5 115 cells at minutes 1 / 2 / 3 / 5 / 10 (median of six capitals 486 / 825 / 1 187 / 1 973 / 4 151). Conquest at parity, 100 %, passive: the 197-cell disc falls in 183 s (169 to 258), 500 cells of a country in 354 s (313 to 455); 40 % with one click: disc 727 s, country not in 900 s. Play match at minutes 1 / 3 / 6 / 10 / 15 (median of 5 seeds): top AI nation 0.5 / 1.1 / 2.1 / 3.7 / 5.9 % of the land, human 0.3 / 0.7 / 1.1 / 1.6 / 2.3 %, free land 91.6 / 84.9 / 80.6 / 75.1 / 67.3 %, tribes alive 20 / 20 / 20 / 20 / 18. Skirmish: top AI 4.1 / 7.9 / 11.3 / 18 / 26.7 %, human 3.5 / 5.9 / 5.5 / 0.4 / 0 % (the human bot is eaten by minute 15 in four of five seeds), free land 40 / 10 / 5.6 %, nations alive 12 / 12 / 12 / 10 / 8, tribes alive 5 / 5 / 3 / 0 / 0. Guard rails all PASS (nothing was fast).
(4) THE FIVE ROUNDS (every round the whole report, five seeds; "old AI" = `AI_WAR_START_TICKS` 0 and `AI_WAR_MAX_SHARE_PCT` 100, which switch the two new AI rules off):
| round | constants | solo RUS min 1 / 5 / 10 | disc 100 % | country 100 % | Play / Skirmish rails | verdict |
|-------|-----------|-------------------------|-----------|--------------|-----------------------|---------|
| 0 | baseline (K 123, regeneration 8 + 0.35 sqrt T) | 536 / 2 428 / 5 115 | 183 s | 354 s | all PASS / all PASS | too slow |
| 1 | K 492 (x4), regeneration as before, old AI | 1 842 / 3 281 / 4 785 (median 1 535 / 3 042 / 4 333) | 49 s | 109 s | PASS / top AI 36 %, tribes 50 % FAIL | the pool binds from minute 2: after 10 minutes no better than before (troops sit at about 10) |
| 2 | K 700, regeneration 24 + 1.0 sqrt T (x3), old AI | 2 698 / 6 369 / 9 397 | 35 s | 70 s | PASS / top AI 79 %, nations 58 %, tribes 0 % FAIL | contested too fast |
| 3 | K 420, regeneration x3, old AI | 1 591 / 8 063 / 10 786 | 55 s | 106 s | PASS / top AI 56 %, tribes 0 % FAIL | the pace is right; the Skirmish AI eats everything within 3 minutes |
| 4 | round 3 + BASE_COST 2 | 1 602 / 8 764 / 21 510 | 55 s | 104 s | PASS / top AI 73 %, tribes 0 % FAIL | rejected: minute 1 unchanged (the first minutes are speed bound), after 10 minutes 17 000 (median) to 21 500 (RUS) cells; BASE_COST stays 3 |
| 5 | round 3 + `AI_WAR_START_TICKS` 2000, `AI_WAR_MAX_SHARE_PCT` 15 (and the Cancel at the cap) | 1 591 / 8 063 / 10 786 | 55 s | 106 s | all PASS / all PASS | FINAL |
Before the rounds, two tool decisions that change numbers and not constants: the attacker of the conquest part clicks again every 15 s (without it a 40 % attack whose pool is spent crawls on with a few troops and the table says "not in 900 s", which is the bot, not the game), and the human bot keeps a reserve and a 1.5 : 1 war ratio (the first version, which spent its pool and attacked at 1 : 1, was eaten by minute 10 in four of five Skirmish seeds even at the baseline pace). Rounds 1 to 4 were measured with the final tool (the table is re-run, not copied).
(5) THE FINAL CONSTANTS and why. `CAPTURE_K_MILLI` 123 -> 420: the one lever that speeds up contested fronts (the speed is K sqrt(L) 2F / (F + Td)); 420 puts the disc at 55 s and 500 cells at 106 s (targets 45 to 90 s and 1.5 to 3 min), TUR (mountains, 2.5 x the progress per cell) 77 s and 135 s. The plan's range 0.06 to 0.15 is left on purpose (`tests/sim/rules.test.ts` now allows up to the overflow limit 1000 of `src/sim/conquest.ts`). `REGEN_BASE_TROOPS_PER_SEC` 8 -> 24 and `REGEN_SQRT_COEF_MILLI` 350 -> 1000: with K alone a solo expander is bound by its troop pool from the second minute (round 1); tripling the regeneration keeps the curve climbing (1 591 after 1 minute, 5 196 after 3, 8 063 after 5, 10 786 after 10: the old 10-minute size, 5 115, is reached after 3 minutes, the old 5-minute size, 2 428, after 1.5: 3.3 times as fast in time) and bends over where the pool binds (minute 5 to 10 adds 2 700 cells, as it did before the pass: the whole curve is shifted to the left, not steeper). Contested pace barely changes with the regeneration (round 1 against round 3). NOT changed: `TICK_HZ`, `BASE_COST_TROOPS` (3), the depth term, `D_MAX_STD`, the terrain table, `START_TROOPS`, the caps, the UI default commitment (40 %), `AI_WAR_MIN_RATIO_PCT`, `AI_EXPAND_*`. The shape sqrt(L) and a minimum contact length were considered and not needed: the disc runs start with a contact of 1 cell and are still 55 s.
