Page and probe: `groundHeightAt` matches the GLSL height within 0.017 % of the local relief on 600 points (492 land) over the Himalaya, the Alps and a coast (`e2e/rts.test.ts`); Node and Chromium agree to 1e-6 m, not bit for bit (two builds of V8 have their own sin and cos).
(6) THE INPUT STACK (`src/app/input.ts`). A stack of modes over plain event records; the BASE mode is what `globeApp.ts` did inline in M05 and M06 (a click that moves less than `CLICK_MOVE_PX` is a click on the match, a longer press grips the camera, right/middle/ctrl/shift turns it, hover shows the tooltip). Later modes push themselves and see pointer, wheel and key events first; a mode that used a press keeps that pointer until its release. `Escape` pops the top mode; with an empty stack it calls the pause handler (`setPauseHandler`, a no-op until M18). A right click without a drag (movement <= `CLICK_MOVE_PX`) calls the context handler (`setContextHandler`, a no-op until M20a) exactly once with `{x, y, cell, lat, lon}` of the ground under the cursor (nothing when the ray misses the globe). Reserved keys: 1 to 6, E, H, F, Escape (`RESERVED_KEYS`; `matchKeyAction` binds none of them); only F (fly to your own capital) and Escape do anything. In `globeApp.ts` Escape and F are taken only inside a match and not when the setup screen, the Dev panel or a text field already used the key (`defaultPrevented`, `typingInField`), and Escape is only `preventDefault`ed when it popped a mode. Pointer Events with `touch-action: none` on the canvas (as before); `attachDomInput` is the only DOM adapter. TWO FINGERS: the first finger is the left button; the second turns the drag into a gesture on the midpoint: pan (the midpoint is gripped), pinch (`zoomAt` by the log of the distance ratio over `WHEEL_ZOOM_RATE`: spreading zooms in), twist (a clockwise twist on the screen turns the heading the other way, about the gripped point: the map follows the fingers). The scripted gesture test (`tests/app/input.test.ts`): a pan keeps the midpoint's ground within 1 px, a pinch of x2 leaves 0.50 of the height, a twist of 30 degrees turns the heading by -30.1 degrees.
(7) FLIGHTS WITH AN ALTITUDE (`FlyOptions.altAglM`, `rts`). The M06 path is untouched (its tests are unchanged and green). A flight with `altAglM` is planned on the camera MODEL (ground position, height, heading, tilt: `modelFromPose` / `poseFromModel`) in three phases whose windows come from `planGroundFlight`: the camera RISES to at least `FLY_ARCH_RATIO` 0.4 times the distance flown (and never under either end), MOVES along the great circle while it is high, and DIVES to the end height; the rise and the dive share 60 % of the time in proportion to their log spans and the move 40 %, and the duration is the longest of the classic one, the one that keeps the log-rate of the height under `FLY_RHO_MAX` 2.4 e-folds per second (4 % of the height per frame at 60 fps; the acceptance is 5 %) and the one that keeps the speed of the move under 2.4 heights per second. From orbit to 300 m: 14.6 s; 30 km to 300 m: 6.0 s; 450 km to 5 km over 320 km: 5.9 s; a 12 km hop from 300 m to 2 km: 4.9 s (it rises to 4.8 km first). Why not interpolate the orbit state as the classic flight does: the camera passes through the x12 mountains on the way down and the clearance lifts it by kilometres (the AGL-stepping problem of D154 (1)); why not move while descending: over a cliff of slope 8 the ground under a camera 300 m up moves it 2.4 km for every 300 m sideways, so the dive is vertical. The end pose is solved exactly (`cameraLookingAt`: the point at the centre of the screen, the heading and the tilt asked for at the point, the lowest distance that is `altAglM` over the ground under the camera); a height under 300 m is raised to 300 m (the negative acceptance: 50 m, 0 and -10 m all end at 300 m and never go lower on the way); tilt is kept in 20 to 80. After the flight the camera holds the height it arrived at (the RTS camera); a grab ends the flight and keeps the height it has. All time is `fxNow()` (the flight clock of M06): a frozen clock freezes the flight, and `durationS` is honoured to the frame. MEASURED (60 fps in fx steps, `tests/render/flyToGround.test.ts`): orbit to 300 m over the Himalaya, the Alps, the Pacific and the Andes: worst step 4.1 % of the height, worst turn 0.4 to 0.9 degrees, lowest height 300.0 m, ends at 300.00 m. A flight is also planned from 5 km over the Alps to 3 km over the Himalaya (it rises above 1 500 km on the way).
(8) BOOKMARKS. `window.__test.bookmark(name, durationS?)` and the Dev panel ("Bokmärke: Alperna / Himalaya / Anderna / din huvudstad", custom `run` actions that close the panel again) fly to `BOOKMARKS` of `src/render/views.ts` (Alps 46.0 N 7.7 E, 50 km up, tilt 55, heading 330; Himalaya 28.0 N 86.9 E, 90 km, tilt 55, heading 20; Andes 32.65 S 70.0 W, 60 km, tilt 55, heading 270: a first guess of 12, 40 and 30 km put the camera against one snow slope that fills the screen) or, for the capital (`F`), to the player's capital at `CAPITAL_BOOKMARK` (450 km, tilt 45). The numbers are first guesses for the look of the x12 relief; the user judges the feel. New hooks: `flyTo`, `bookmark`, `flightActive`, `cameraMix`, `holdAgl`, `pickGround`, `groundHeightAt`.
(9) Part B (the performance scene, the report, the six goldens, PREVIEW) is D156.

### D156 M08 part B: the performance scene, its report, the six height goldens, where the camera looks (`src/app/perfScene.ts`, `src/ui/perfSceneReport.ts`, `src/ui/perfSchema.ts`, `src/render/gpuTimer.ts`, `e2e/perfScene.test.ts`, `e2e/rtsHeights.test.ts`)
(1) THE SCRIPT. `?scene=perf` (`#scene-perf`, Dev panel "Prestandascen") draws the orbit view once, measures the display's refresh with 70 EMPTY animation-frame callbacks (the first 10 skipped; nothing is drawn and the fx clock stands still, so the interval is the display's, not the scene's), and then flies five phases over the Himalaya in fx time: ORBIT (21 500 km, rests 3 s), CONTINENT (a classic flight to 3 500 km, tilt 50, 4 s, rests 2 s), VALLEY (a ground flight to 3 000 m above the ground at 30.0 N 90.0 E, tilt 78, rests 3 s), GROUND (the same place at 300 m, rests 3 s) and CRUISE (13 km north at 300 m, rests 1 s). The ground flights are timed by `planGroundFlight` (D155 (7)), not by the script. The page has no menu and no match in this scene, the mouse is OFF while it runs (a touched camera would spoil the numbers; the base mode is attached when the run is over) and the perf overlay is on by itself (`isPerfScene`): its top line says what is going on and its button is the one button "Kopiera rapport" of M02, now with the scene section in the report. Flown on the real height field at 60 fps in fx steps (`tests/render/perfSceneFlight.test.ts`): 2 086 frames, 34.8 fx seconds (orbit 181 frames, continent 363, valley 713, ground 474, cruise 355), the worst frame moves the camera 4.23 % of its height (the start of the cruise: 13 km in 5 s at 300 m is 2.4 heights per second, the bound of the plan), the worst turn is 0.40 degrees, the lowest height 300.0 m, it ends at 300.00 m. A display of 30 fps takes the same script (the fx step is capped at 0.1 s per frame): the steps double, 8 % of the height at the worst.
(2) WHERE IT LOOKS (screenshots, not guesses). The prompt says "300 m AGL over the Himalaya". At 28.2 N 86.8 E (the M07a valley view) and at the first guess 27.4 N 86.6 E the picture at 300 m and at 3 km is ONE SLAB: a white or brown wall that fills the screen, because the relief is 12 times exaggerated (a 20 degree slope is a 78 degree wall) and the camera sits against the massif. Nine screenshots of candidates at 300 m and 3 km (tilt 45 to 78, headings 0, 90, 180, 270, in the high tier through `?chunktier=high`) showed that the edge of the Tibetan plateau at 30.0 N 90.0 E, tilt 78 (the RTS limit is 80), heading north has a horizon with the range behind it, snow ridges in the middle and rock below. That is the place of the valley, the ground and the cruise phases and of the six goldens. The continent phase stays over the range (28.0 N 86.9 E). Recorded for the user's eye: at 300 m the ground is a close-up of one slope (the stylised flat shading of M07b, 1 km texels and the ridged octaves), and a camera at tilt 60 against a steep slope sees only that slope; the camera is right, the picture is what a 300 m camera over the x12 mountains is.
(3) THE REPORT IS AN ADDITIVE EXTENSION OF THE M02 SCHEMA. `schemaVersion` stays 1: the report gets an optional section `scene` the way M05 added `match`; a reader checks for the section. Fields (`PerfSceneSection`): `completed`, `quick`, `durationS` (the sum of the frame intervals), `fxDurationS`, `frames`, `truncated` (the recorder holds 14 400 frames), `frameMs` and `fps` (mean, p95, max), `maxTriangles`, `maxDrawCalls`, `maxChunks`, `minAglM`, `canvas` (CSS size, drawing buffer size, the pixel ratio the renderer uses, the window's `devicePixelRatio`), `refresh` (measured?, samples, median interval, Hz), `cadence` (the bin edges `PERF_CADENCE_EDGES_MS`, the counts, and the shares of frames that lasted about 1, 2, 3 and 4 or more refresh intervals), `cappedHint`, `cpuMs` (the JavaScript time of the update and draw submission per frame), `gpuMs` (the timer query, below) and `phases` (frames, mean, p95, median, maxima of triangles and chunks, the end height). The top-level `renderer`, `vendor`, `tier`, `viewport.dpr` and `userAgent` of M02 are in the same report (the renderer string, tier and DPR the prompt asks for). The JSON Schema is `PERF_REPORT_SCHEMA` in `src/ui/perfSchema.ts` with a 60-line validator of the subset it uses (no new package): `tests/perfSchema.test.ts` validates the plain, match and scene reports, and rejects thirteen mutations (a missing field, a string for a number, a null from a NaN, a negative count, a fraction for a count, a verdict outside the enum, an unknown key, an empty phase list, a wrong kind, a missing phase field, a histogram that is not an array, a missing `devicePixelRatio`); `e2e/perfScene.test.ts` validates the report of a real run and the JSON that "Kopiera rapport" shows.
(4) THE QUESTION THE EXTRA FIELDS ANSWER (the user's feedback: "steady 30 fps", M05, and a strong GPU). A GPU that is merely slow gives frame times that move with the work (orbit is cheap, 300 m over the x12 Himalaya is dear); a frame-rate cap or a vsync lock gives the same multiple of the display's refresh interval in every phase. `cappedVerdict` (`src/ui/perfSceneReport.ts`): `unknown` without a measured refresh or a phase with at least 10 frames; `none` when the median frame is under 1.25 refresh intervals; `likely-capped` when every phase with at least 10 frames has its median within 8 % of the SAME whole multiple (2 or more) of the refresh interval; `unclear` otherwise (slower than the display, medians that differ between the phases). Tested on series that must and must not be called capped (a 30 fps lock on 60 Hz with very different work per phase; 20, 33 and 52 ms phases; one phase off the multiple; 72 fps locked on 144 Hz). It cannot tell a cap from a GPU that happens to be exactly twice too slow in every phase; the GPU time below separates them. `GpuTimer` (`src/render/gpuTimer.ts`) wraps `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` (one `TIME_ELAPSED` query around each draw, a pool of 6, polled once a frame, a disjoint event drops the queries in flight); where the browser offers it the report has the GPU milliseconds per frame, and a locked verdict then also says how little of each frame the GPU was busy ("busy 6.2 ms of each 33.3 ms frame"). It is absent in SwiftShader (`gpuMs.available` false) and tested against a fake WebGL2 context (`tests/gpuTimer.test.ts`). The `cpuMs` is the JavaScript time of the frame. If the report says `likely-capped` and a refresh of 60 Hz, the fix is not in the renderer: a browser or driver setting (vsync, a battery or power mode, a hidden-window throttle, or an Artifact frame that the page does not control).
(5) THE TEST-MODE RUN (`__test.runPerfScene({dt, maxFrames})`). The shortened script (flights of 1 fx second, rests of 0.2, no probe) is run frame by frame with a real draw in each. `Globe.finish()` (what `render` calls) only FLUSHES in Chromium: 54 draws in one task left a backlog that cost the next animation frame 24 s ("Running out of reserved queue serials" in the console) and the test that clicked a button after the run timed out. `Globe.sync()` (a one-pixel `readPixels` of the default framebuffer, test mode only, `preserveDrawingBuffer` is on there) waits for the GPU, which also makes every logged frame time include the drawing. At 320x180 in the test tier: 38 frames in 12 s wall (about 300 ms a frame under SwiftShader), `maxChunks` 149 (the cap of the tier), `maxTriangles` 104 384, `maxDrawCalls` 3, the 300 m phase ends at 300.0 m; the assertions are `maxChunks >= 12` and `maxTriangles >= 20 000` as the prompt says (the orbit phase draws 46 chunks and 38 464 triangles, so the numbers do prove that the flight came down: the e2e test also requires that the ground phase drew more than the orbit phase) and nothing about wall time. The live page on SwiftShader (400x225, a smoke run polled for 7 minutes, no console message) ran the probe and reached the ground phase (about one frame a second, the fx step capped at 0.1 s); it is far too slow to finish in a test, so the live end of the script (the overlay saying KLAR, the mouse coming back) is covered by the unit test of the script and by the quick run, not by a live run.
(6) THE SIX GOLDENS (`e2e/rtsHeights.test.ts`): `e2e/goldens/rts-height-orbit.png`, `rts-height-continent.png`, `rts-height-region.png`, `rts-height-30km.png`, `rts-height-valley.png`, `rts-height-300m.png`, 480x270 each, the test tier, over 30.0 N 90.0 E with the sun fixed at 40 E: orbit (21 500 km, straight down; the camera is 21 433 km over the ground), continent (3 000 km over the ground, tilt 45), region (300 km, tilt 55), 30 km (tilt 66), valley (3 km, tilt 78), 300 m (tilt 78); the RTS views are put there by `flyTo` with an altitude and a duration of 0 (the real RTS pose, which then holds its height: the test asserts the height within 3 % and that `holdAgl` is set). All six are rendered twice in one browser launch and must be byte-identical, differ from each other in more than 20 % of the pixels, and match their golden (pixelmatch 0.1, at most 0.2 %). They are NEW files: no earlier golden moved in M08 (`verify` ran green with the goldens as M07b left them, D155 (9)).
(7) NOTED IN THE SCREENSHOTS, NOT PART OF M08 (BACKLOG): at 3 km tilt 70 over the Alps the shading shows saw-tooth edges along the facets and at 300 m a streaky "hair" pattern; both are the flat facet shading and the mottling of M07b seen from very close; the colour tiles of the class texture (about 10 km) show as blocks from 100 km to 30 km.
(8) `npm run verify` IS NOT BACK UNDER 8 MINUTES, AND THAT IS STATED, NOT HIDDEN (the first task of M08 in D154 (8)). Measured at the end of M08 on the 4-core sandbox: typecheck 21 s, lint 21 s, unit 1:14 (154 files, 1 722 tests), perf 15 s (10 files, 32 tests), e2e 10:27 (23 files, 184 tests), e2e-serial 14 s (4 files, 7 tests), `verify` 12:09 in all and `verify:fast` about 1:20. The e2e stage's test time sums to 30:27 over its three workers; by file (seconds): setup 211, shading 199, terrain 185, looks 140, hud 138, lodPop 135, terrainViews 133, globe 125, artifact 89, devpanel 74, match 71, setupFix 59, detailSheets 51, detail 44, rts 39, ai 31, debugData 30, perfScene 28, rtsHeights 19, determinism 9, smoke 8, sim 5, negative 4. M08's own files cost 86 s of that (rts 39 in part A, perfScene 28 after the cuts below, rtsHeights 19); the rest is earlier milestones and no file dominates: the largest, setup, is 7 % of the total. Getting from 30:27 to the 18 minutes of test time that 8:00 needs (3 workers) means taking 40 % out of tests that each prove something the prompts asked for; the options listed in D154 (8) (every 12th pixel in the shading scans: about 65 s of 199; the detail contact sheets rendered once: about 20 s; fewer path steps in `e2e/lodPop.test.ts`) add up to about 2 minutes of test time, 40 s of wall time, and each one removes statistical power from a rule test (the shares have wide margins, the sample counts would drop to a quarter): that is not worth it and was not done. What the rule of CLAUDE.md says is "shard e2e and note it here": (a) the e2e stage now prints the test time per file after it runs (a vitest JSON report next to the default one, `test-results/verify-e2e-times.json`, `slowestFiles` in `scripts/verify.mjs`, tested), so the next milestone starts from numbers; (b) `node scripts/verify.mjs --shard i/n` passes vitest's `--shard` to the unit, e2e and e2e-serial stages (the perf stage is never split, typecheck and lint have no tests to split; `shardOf` refuses a malformed value instead of running everything; tested in `tests/verifyScript.test.ts`; `vitest run --project unit --shard 3/40` ran 4 of 154 files). Three shards on three machines of this size run in about 5 to 6 minutes each. NOT DONE: wiring the three shards into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (a matrix of `node scripts/verify.mjs --shard ${{ matrix.shard }}/3`, `timeout-minutes` kept at 20 per job, `tests/ci.test.ts` updated to match): CLAUDE.md warns that pushing `.github/workflows/*` is often refused, and the orchestrator owns the push; a GitHub runner has 2 cores and would take about 20 minutes for the whole `verify` in one job, so the matrix is advisable before the first CI run that matters (BACKLOG). The perf scene's own tests were cut where they only cost time: the test-mode run uses fx steps of 0.34 s (21 frames instead of 38, 7 s instead of 12) and the live-page test waits for the first scene frame only (19 s for the whole file alone).
(9) THE OVERLAY IN THE SCENE. While the scene runs the overlay text is rewritten once a second (a time rule, not every 15 frames: at 1 fps on a software renderer 15 frames is 15 seconds), the text box only ever grows (the button under it does not jump), the box scrolls when the window is lower than it (`max-height` 100 % of the window minus 16 px: at 320x180 the button was 344 px below the visible area), and the report's section is built at most once a second (it sorts copies of the recorded frames). The e2e test of the live page dispatches the click on the button: Playwright's stability check never passes at one frame a second with a text that changes every frame; a person on a real GPU clicks a box that has been still since the run ended (the text stops changing when `done`).

### D157 M08 audit pass: the wheel zoom is a dolly, the grip cannot jump, the input stack passes clicks to modes (`src/render/camera.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `src/app/input.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`, `tests/render/rtsZoom.test.ts`, `tests/render/rtsFollow.test.ts`)
(1) THE BLOCKER, RE-REPRODUCED AND FIXED. One wheel notch at tilt 55 to 67, 680 m over the Andes threw the camera 5 875 km (the audit's real-input case; `tests/render/rtsZoom.test.ts` and `e2e/rts.test.ts` reproduce it, the unit tests fail on the first build with the same teleports). Two causes, both fallbacks. (a) The anchor correction of the wheel and the grip of a drag intersected the cursor ray with the sphere of the held ground point and, when the ray missed it, took the NEAREST point of that sphere (right for the globe's limb, wrong for a ground point): the rig then turned by the angle to a point thousands of kilometres away. (b) A camera BELOW the sphere of a ridge it looks up at meets that sphere from the inside, where a cursor ray above the ridge exits hundreds of kilometres away. Fixed in `CameraRig.surfaceHit`: the grabbed surface is the sphere while the camera is outside it and the TANGENT PLANE at the held point when it is inside; a ray that misses, or meets the surface at less than `RTS_GRIP_MIN_COS` 0.1 (about 6 degrees, scaled by the weight squared), holds nothing and the step is skipped (a grip does not follow the cursor to the horizon, where one pixel is worth all the ground); one correction turns the rig by at most `RTS_ALIGN_MAX_RANGES` 1 times the camera's range to the held point (and never under `RTS_ALIGN_MIN_M` 2 km of arc). The globe's own drag (the sea-level sphere, nearest point at the limb) is untouched: its M05 behaviour is pinned byte for byte by the test that compares the stack with the bare rig. The globe-regime wheel anchor skips a step whose cursor ray misses the sphere.
(2) THE ZOOM IS A DOLLY (the three zoom majors share one cause). The first build solved "the distance along the line from the sea-level focus at which the camera is H metres over the ground" again every frame of a zoom, from a focus the anchor itself moved, with the height follow running on top: over relief the roots of that equation jump (the camera bobbed by kilometres 1.7 km for a 62 m notch), the loop did not converge on steep ground (23 of 1 080 notches never settled, one dived to the floor and stayed with a stale hold), and the follow slid the camera after the anchored turn (up to 200 px of drift). In the RTS regime (a ground, a picker, the camera under 4 000 km over the ground) the wheel is now a DOLLY: the notch asks for `exp(delta * WHEEL_ZOOM_RATE)` of the height (accumulating over quick notches), `solveDolly` finds ONCE, along the cursor ray, the first point at which the camera is that high over the ground (flat-ground steps, the first crossing found by scanning the overshooting bracket in eighths and bisecting; about 1 ms of CPU a notch with its frames, `tests/rtsCamera.perf.test.ts`), the camera eases along the ray (`ZOOM_TAU_S`) and the zoom ENDS: the last 1 % is taken in the same step, the height reached becomes the held height (a zoom with nothing to travel, at the floor, still takes the camera and holds its height), the follow is off while it runs. Moving along the cursor ray leaves the look direction alone, so the ground point under the cursor stays exactly under it: worst 2.0 px over 360 random notches over Himalaya, Alps and Andes at 3, 30 and 300 km (the residual is the roll the rig does not have, put back by one small turn about the planet's centre against the point picked when the zoom began; the tilt that a zoom from the globe must ease into 20 to 80 degrees uses the same correction). A ray flatter than 1 / `RTS_DOLLY_MAX_TRAVEL` 8 (7 degrees: the horizon, the sky) or one whose solution travels more than 8 times the change of height (a camera looking down a valley wall, a zoom out against a slope behind it: the Himalaya valley view needed 15.7 km for a 65 m notch) goes straight up or down instead (`zoomKind` 'vertical'; 5 of 357 notches in the sweep, the point under the cursor then slides, which is better than a flight of kilometres). A zoom whose goal is above `RTS_ZOOM_MAX_AGL_M` 5 000 km is a globe zoom (the e2e of the globe scene found it: twelve huge notches out must reach `DIST_MAX_KM`). A zoom that eases the tilt on its way (from the globe, the tilt 7 to 20 degrees) turns the ray it travels along and would stop short (the same e2e: 13 km instead of 300 m): when it ends more than 0.3 % (2 m) off its goal the way left is solved once more from where the camera is, at most `RTS_DOLLY_MAX_FIXES` 6 times, never per frame. The globe regime keeps the M07a zoom (distance to the sea-level focus, solved each frame): it is above 4 000 km, where relief is 2.5 % of the height and the equation has one root. A grab (left drag) ends a dolly under way and holds the height it had reached. `CameraRig.zoomKind` ('ray', 'vertical', null) exists for the tests.
(3) THE FOLLOW IS PINNED (`tests/render/rtsFollow.test.ts`): a step per frame is at most 1 - exp(-dt / `RTS_FOLLOW_TAU_S`) of the gap and at least half of it, a settled 300 m hold is within `RTS_FOLLOW_EPS_M` 0.05 m, a camera nobody touched does not follow, and a zoom (also one with nothing to travel) and a drag set the held height; checked by four mutations (an instant snap, an epsilon of 50 m, the zoom's and the drag's hold removed), each failing the file. The follow is on the ground at the position the camera has: the ground is static, so the rig's rest stamp (state unchanged, nothing to do) is exact; a test that changes the ground under a resting camera uses a ramp the camera is dragged over.
(4) THE INPUT STACK PASSES CLICKS TO MODES (`src/app/input.ts`). The first build gave a pushed mode no way to take a click, a right click or a long press: M10 placement, M13b deploy, M15b targeting and M20 would each have re-implemented click-versus-drag and still could not stop the M05 spawn / attack. Now `InputMode` has optional `click`, `context`, `longPress` and `tick`; the base mode only REPORTS them (`stack.dispatchClick / dispatchContext / dispatchLongPress`: the pushed modes topmost first, then the base's own action only when the stack is empty), `stack.take(e, mode)` claims a pointer mid-gesture, and a touch held for `LONG_PRESS_S` 0.5 fx seconds (uiConfig; `stack.tick(fxNow())` once per frame from `renderFrame`) is a long press: the modes may take it, else it is a context command at that place, so touch has a path to the M20a resolver. The resolver runs only with an empty stack (M20a's own rule; it could not know the depth before). CONSERVATIVE DEFAULT, said here: a pushed mode that does not implement `click` swallows the click (nothing happens, the M05 spawn / attack does not run under a placement or a pause mode); a mode that wants the base's action as well calls `stack.base.click?.(x, y, e)`. `InputStack.key` refuses ctrl and alt for every key but Escape (Ctrl+1 is a browser tab). The page's keydown adapter moved into `attachDomKeys` (every reserved key goes to the stack, nothing else; `isReservedKey`, no hard-coded F / Escape in `src/app/globeApp.ts`); M20a's hotkeys.ts takes it over and its grep test must name `src/app/globeApp.ts`, `src/app/main.ts`, `src/app/devpanel.ts` and `src/app/setupScreen.ts` (ARCHITECTURE). New test hooks `__test.onContext(fn)` / `onPause(fn)` let `e2e/rts.test.ts` watch the real page's wiring: a real right click gives one command whose cell is `__test.pick(x, y).cell.index`, Escape with an empty stack gives one pause call.
(5) TEST HOLES, FILLED, each with the mutation that survived the first build now failing: the waypoints of the performance scene as literals (orbit 21 500 km, continent 3 500 km, 3 000 m, 300 m, the 13 km cruise, every place in a Himalaya box, the fx duration freezing when the run ends: six of the audit's mutations killed; W12 ("afterFrame records while probing") is an EQUIVALENT mutant, `idx < 0` already refuses every frame during the probe, and the direct test of it stays); `HeightField.pick` against a 20 m reference march over 1 200 grazing rays (the audit's `clear * 4` step now fails: a ray 0.87 m under the surface 25 m before its hit); cancel and lost release (`dragEnd` in `pointerCancel` and in the lost-release guard, pointer ownership of a move, the touch hover, a foreign pointer-up); the follow (3); the stack (4). The input test's wheel case is no longer the only wheel test: `tests/render/rtsZoom.test.ts` holds the mountain cases.
(6) VERIFY AND CI. `npm run verify` is 12 minutes here and the budget of 8 is NOT met (D156 (8)); CLAUDE.md said CI runs three shards, which `.github/workflows/ci.yml` does not. The conservative choice: the sentence in CLAUDE.md now says the budget is not met and the matrix is not wired, and the task stays in Next of PROGRESS. Wiring it changes `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, which the orchestrator pushes and a token often refuses (CLAUDE.md), so it is left to the user's decision and not smuggled into this pass.
(7) NOT DONE, NOT FINDINGS: the vertical fallback of (2) does not keep the point under the cursor; the perf test of the notch has a budget of `budgetMs(2)` (measured 0.85 ms with its 30 frames).


### D158 CamFix: the whole globe in view when zoomed out, the eased tilt, the window-dependent maximum distance, and a camera over mountains that M08 had already smoothed (calm over the Alps, a dive of up to 79 % at the Himalaya wall; wording corrected in CamFix-2) (`src/render/fit.ts`, `src/render/camera.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `tests/render/fit.test.ts`, `tests/render/fitCamera.test.ts`, `tests/render/mountainCalm.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/mountainRun.ts`, `tests/helpers/silhouette.ts`, `e2e/zoomOut.test.ts`)
(1) WHAT THE PROMPT GOT WRONG, found while reproducing (screenshots first, as asked). (a) Its screenshot script zoomout.mjs (a scratch file outside the repo) set only the Playwright viewport; in test mode the canvas keeps its 960x540 unless the address has `w` and `h`, so its 9:16 and 21:9 pictures are a 960x540 canvas cut by the viewport (the globe "far to the right" in 9:16 is the 16:9 picture cropped) and its mouse sat off the canvas centre in the wheel runs (the "heading -15 degrees, latitude 24" of its 21:9 log). The 16:9 pictures show the real fault; the new e2e opens its pages with `w` and `h`. (b) The offset of the globe's centre is `atan(sin t / (d + cos t))`, not `d - cos t` (the centre of the planet is BEHIND the focus: the camera is at distance d from the focus and d + cos t from the centre): 7.85 degrees at 42 000 km and 72 degrees, not 8.6 (`tests/render/fit.test.ts` pins it, and the whole extent formula against a camera that projects the real outline of the sphere). (c) "in 9:16 the horizontal half field of view (8.6 degrees) is smaller than the globe's angular radius (8.7 degrees), so the globe does not fit even centred" is wrong: the angular radius of the disc at 42 000 km is asin(1 / 7.59) = 7.57 degrees (the distance to the planet's CENTRE is the focus distance plus one radius), the disc fits a 9:16 window centred with 12 % to spare (it fills 88 % of the half-width). What 42 000 km got wrong in 9:16 was everything tilted, and in a window narrower than 0.46 the disc does not fit at all. The two real faults the user saw are the ones the prompt names under (1) and (2): the tilted globe is off centre and cut (at 42 000 km and 72 degrees in a 16:9 window the far edge of the disc is at 1.08 half-heights from the middle, and the whole globe sits low in the picture) and the maximum distance ignores the window.
(2) THE GEOMETRY (`src/render/fit.ts`, pure). The rig looks at a focus F on the sea-level sphere from distance d (Earth radii) at tilt t. In the camera frame the planet's centre is at (0, -sin t, d + cos t): the disc is a cone of half angle `a = asin(1 / c)`, `c = sqrt(d^2 + 1 + 2 d cos t)`, whose axis is `e = atan2(sin t, d + cos t)` below the view axis. Its extent: the far edge at `tan(a + e)`, the near edge at `tan(a - e)` (the focus is always inside the disc), the sides at `sin a / sqrt(cos^2 e - sin^2 a)` (the plane x = k z that touches the cone); as fractions of the half-height and half-width (`discExtent`, `discFits`, `discFill` for the tilt-0 case, `fitDistKm` its inverse). `tests/helpers/silhouette.ts` projects the real tangent circle of the sphere with `CameraRig.project` and `tests/render/fit.test.ts` compares the two for random windows, distances, tilts and headings (to 1e-3 relative). The window enters through `narrowHalfTan(aspect)`: tan of half the field in the narrowest direction (vertical for aspect >= 1, horizontal below), the aspect clamped to at least `FIT_ASPECT_MIN` 0.25 and a canvas with no size (NaN, 0) is the reference 16:9.
(3) THE MARGINS, CHOSEN. The disc must fill at most `FIT_FILL_SAFE` 0.92 of every half-extent (8 % free on every side; the acceptance asks for 5 %). At the maximum distance it fills 63 % of the narrowest half-extent (acceptance: 55 to 80 %): `DIST_MAX_KM` is 32 000 km (was 42 000, which is 50 % of a 16:9 half-height) and is exactly the maximum of every window at least as wide as high; a narrower window gets the distance with the SAME fill in its narrow direction (`maxDistKm(aspect)`: 62 000 km at 9:16, 88 000 km at 0.4), continuous and monotone in the aspect, never above `DIST_ABS_MAX_KM` 150 000 (the ceiling binds below aspect 0.25). `DIST_MAX_KM` stays as the 16:9 constant because the old tests name it for their 960x540 rigs (camera.test, rtsZoom.test, e2e/globe.test.ts): they pass unchanged, none was edited. The rig holds `maxKm` from its width and height (`setViewport` recomputes it and brings a distance beyond it, and the target of a zoom under way, to it; `rig.maxDistKm`), and every clamp that used the constant uses it: `setView`, the update loop, the wheel (`zoomAt` in both regimes), the distance solver of the globe zoom. `setViewUnclamped` and the flights are not clamped (they never were); `flyTo` ends in `setView`.
(4) THE EASED TILT (`maxTiltForDist`, `tiltCapAt`, `shownTiltAt`). A pure function, no state: the camera SHOWS `min(the user's tilt, the limit)` where the limit is 90 (nothing) up to `FIT_TILT_FREE_KM` 8 000 km, falls with a smoothstep of the logarithm of the distance, and is 0 from the distance where the disc fills `FIT_FILL_SAFE` at tilt 0 (20 247 km at 16:9, 40 014 km at 9:16). 8 000 km is where the RTS camera of M08 ends (its zoom stops at 5 000 km of height, a camera at 80 degrees tilt and 8 000 km is 4 700 km up): the RTS regime never sees the limit, a test scans every state with a height under 5 000 km and tilt up to 80 and the limit is above the tilt in all of them. WHY NOT THE GEOMETRIC LIMIT (the prompt's `maxTiltForDist` = the largest tilt at which the disc still fits with a margin): it is 0 where the disc just fits (nothing can be tilted without cutting it) and GROWS with the distance (25 degrees at 32 000 km, 16:9), so it is not monotone, and at the maximum distance it would leave the globe's centre 27 % of a half-height off the middle of the picture, while the acceptance asks for the centre within 3 %. The eased limit is monotone and continuous (Lipschitz in ln(distance), tested on random pairs and a fine sweep), is 0 wherever the disc fits with the margin (so there the whole disc is in, centred: tested over random windows, tilts, headings and distances against the projected outline, margin >= 5 %), and never exceeds the geometric limit from the point where the disc fits (tested). The user's tilt (`view.tilt`) is not touched: the pose uses the shown tilt (`CameraRig.pose()`, `positionOf`, so the picking (`rayAt`, `pickGround`, the pick round trip within 1 pixel tested at 8 000 to 40 000 km, six window shapes), the anchor of the wheel, the clearance and the AGL all use the same pose), and zooming in again gives the tilt back (tested: 60 degrees out to the maximum and back, within 1 degree, six window shapes). `cameraPositionFor(view, aspect = 16:9)` and `distForAgl(..., aspect)` follow (the old test that keeps `cameraPositionFor` in step with the pose passes at 30 000 km and tilt 89 for the 16:9 rig). A right drag at a far distance starts from the shown tilt and stops at the limit (no dead zone, no stored tilt that cannot be shown); a drag that only turns the heading leaves the tilt alone.
(5) FLIGHTS PLACE THE CAMERA BY ITS POSE (`freePose`). `setPose` (the ground flights of M08 put the camera at arbitrary poses on their arch, tilt 60 at 12 000 km) must come out as placed: after `setPose` the shown tilt is the view's own tilt until the next thing the user's hands do (`zoomAt`, `dragStart`, `rotateBy`, `yawBy`) or a hook (`setView`, `setViewUnclamped`) does. The first build had no such flag and the 5 flight tests (`tests/render/flyToGround.test.ts`, the bookmarks) failed with steps of 7 to 37 % of the height per frame at the arch. Consequence, said here: a flight that ENDS far out at a high tilt (nothing does: the bookmarks end at 450 km or lower, the perf scene's orbit is a `setView`) shows that tilt until the user's first input, which eases it (a pop of the size of the difference).
(6) THE FEEL, MEASURED (60 fps in fx steps, six window shapes, `tests/render/fitCamera.test.ts`): one wheel notch out or in from 3 000 to 30 000 km moves the camera at most 2.9 % of its distance and turns the look direction at most 2.2 degrees in a frame (M08's bound for flights is 5 % and 3 degrees; a wheel notch is not a flight, but it is inside both); a spin of five notches at once (the zoom itself then moves the camera by a sixth of its distance in its fastest frame) changes the shown tilt by at most 12.8 degrees in a frame at 60 fps (it unwinds in a fraction of a second): fast zoom, fast tilt. The slope of the ease is 145 degrees per e-fold of the distance at its steepest (smoothstep over 0.93 e-folds at 16:9); a longer ease was not possible (it has to be over where the disc fits, and the RTS camera needs the first 8 000 km).
(7) A WHEEL STRAIGHT OUT WITH THE CURSOR AT THE CENTRE (acceptance 3): from 3 000 km at tilt 60 (and 0 / 40 / 72, headings 0 / 90 / 200, 6 000 km start, six window shapes, with the real height field as ground and picker, and without a ground) the globe ends centred (the middle of the outline within 3 % of the half-extent: measured 1e-15 over 72 runs), the heading changes at most 1 degree (measured at most 0.005 degrees, the latitude at most 0.004: the wheel's anchor is the focus itself) and the user's tilt is still what it was. With the cursor elsewhere the anchored zoom rotates the focus to keep the point under the cursor (as before); the globe is centred anyway at the maximum, because at tilt 0 the planet's centre is on the view axis.
(8) RESIZE. `setViewport` recomputes `maxKm` and the end of the ease; a window that gets narrower than it was allows a farther zoom, one that gets wider (taller to wider) brings a distance beyond the new maximum to it (tested). A DEFAULT start view at 21 500 km is unchanged in every window (it is below the 32 000 km of the narrowest allowed maximum); in a tall window it is a globe larger than the window (BACKLOG: start at the distance where it fits).
(9) CAMERA OVER MOUNTAINS: MEASURED FIRST, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT M08 ALREADY FOLLOWS THE HELD HEIGHT (calm over the Alps; at a fast pan against the Himalaya wall it dips to 79 % under the held height at 3 km and 29 % at 20 km, see CamFix-2 at the end of this file) (`tests/helpers/mountainRun.ts`, `tests/render/mountainCalm.test.ts`; numbers in docs/PROGRESS.md "Measurements CamFix"). The script: the real detailed height field, the rig as `Globe` builds it, fx steps of 1/60 s, inputs as the page gives them; the camera placed by `viewFromGroundCamera` (the same call in every version) at 0.3, 1, 3 and 20 km over the Alps (46.0 N 7.7 E) and the Himalaya (28.4 N 86.7 E), looking north at 35 degrees below the horizon; six pans of 500 px (a fast 625 px/s and a steady 312 px/s), then eight wheel notches. It was also run in a git worktree of `ec44223` (the commit M08 started from, which IS version 9: the prompt names the two as if they differed). RESULTS. v9 (the globe camera keeps its distance to the sea-level point while the ground goes by): over a pan the camera's height over the ground leaves its held height by 10 to 100 times (over the Alps it ends 30 to 46 km up and goes to -2 200 m, inside the mountain, from a hold of 300 m; over the Himalaya it ends 66 to 78 km up), nothing returns to the held height, the zoom is monotone. M08: the pan keeps the held height (ends within 0.5 % of it every time), the dip under it is 0 to 26 % of the hold, 79 % at the Himalaya wall at 3 km (the follower's lag over a slope of 4.7), never under 300 m; of the terrain's roughness slower than a second 46 to 64 % reaches the camera (at 300 m, where the clearance rules, 57 to 92 %), of the shake faster than a sixth of a second 26 to 34 % (a camera that copies the ground has 100 % of both); the wheel never reverses (0 direction reversals of the camera's radius in all 6 runs, v9 the same: the height over the ground is not a hunting measure, a straight line over bumpy ground has a bumpy height). So the zoom of v9 was not the hunting of the user's report; the pan was (a camera that ends 25 km up in the Himalaya is "väldigt jobbig"), and M08's follower had already removed it. PINNED (named tests; two mutations run and killed): `the held height is followed, not copied` (hfShare < 0.70 and shake share < 0.45 at 3 km and 20 km, both places, both speeds; mutation "no smoothing" = `RTS_FOLLOW_TAU_S` 1e-9: all 8 fail, plus the dip test), `the clearance of 300 m is never undercut` (mutation "clearance removed" = `liftToClearance` returns false: 3 of 4 fail), the dip thresholds, the zoom (no extra reversal, end within 0.5 %), and a control (the same script with no picker, the v9 camera, ends 10 to 20 times off and goes under the ground). TRIED AND REJECTED: a look-ahead (the follower aims at the higher of the ground under the camera and the ground it will be over in 0.25 s at its present speed, which takes the lag out of a constant slope): the dip at the Himalaya wall fell from 79 % to 32 % and at the Alps from 8 % to 1.5 %, but the shake share rose from 0.33 to 0.64 to 0.75, the direction reversals from 7 to 53 and from 18 to 143 and the largest acceleration tenfold (the velocity estimate and the maximum of two rough samples are noise); a camera that dives a little at a wall is calmer than one that shivers all the time. Reverted; the idea is in BACKLOG with the numbers. Not changed: the follower, its constants, the clearance, the 300 m feel (M08's tests stand untouched).
(10) GOLDENS AND SIM. No golden moved (every golden view is at most 21 500 km and tilt 0, below the 32 000 km maximum of any window, and `maxTiltForDist` is the tilt itself below 8 000 km); `ENGINE_VERSION` and the sim goldens are untouched (nothing of `src/sim`, `src/ai`, `src/host` changed).
(11) NOT DONE, SAID HERE: (a) the start view in a tall window (BACKLOG); (b) a smoothing of the shown tilt in time (it is a function of the distance, so a quick spin of the wheel unwinds the tilt as fast as it zooms; a rate limit would make the pose depend on the history and every test hook that sets a view would need a reset); (c) the camera at 300 m hugging the ground still copies 57 to 92 % of its roughness (the clearance is a hard rule and the 300 m feel was not to change); (d) windows narrower than 0.25 or wider than 4 are treated as 0.25 and 4 (the disc then does not fit at the maximum distance).

### D159 AutoTier-1: the default tier follows the graphics card class (`src/render/gpuClass.ts`, `src/render/quality.ts`, `src/render/globe.ts`, `src/ui/gpuTip.ts`, `tests/gpuClass.test.ts`, `e2e/gpuTip.test.ts`)

Why: the user's real report (`#scene-perf`, version 10) came from a school HP ProBook with Intel UHD graphics: 68.8 ms per frame on `high`, GPU bound. The default is now chosen by the card, once at start.

(1) The table (ordered, first match of the renderer string wins; the vendor string only when the renderer matches nothing): software (SwiftShader, llvmpipe, WARP, Basic Render Driver) -> software; Intel Arc -> capable; other Intel -> integrated; Radeon RX / Pro / VII -> capable; Radeon "Graphics" / Vega N Graphics / 780M style (Ryzen APU) -> integrated, because the APU string carries no model and the part is a fraction of a discrete card; other AMD / ATI, NVIDIA, Apple (M series, ANGLE Metal) -> capable; Mali, Adreno, PowerVR, VideoCore, Immortalis, Xclipse -> integrated; anything else -> capable.

(2) Unknown = capable: a wrong guess toward the strong side is corrected by the adaptive resolution of AutoTier-2 and costs one slow first minute; a wrong guess toward the weak side takes picture quality from a player with a good card for good.

(3) Order of choice: `?test=1` (always `test`) > `?quality=` / `#quality-` (source `param`, or `dev` when the address hash carried it, which is what the Dev panel writes) > the pref `quality` (a valid tier; nothing writes it yet) > class (integrated / software -> `low`, source `auto`) > `medium` (source `default`). The automatic tier is NEVER written to prefs. Test mode reports source `test`, an addition to the five values of the prompt.

(4) The renderer is read in one place (`readGpuStrings` in `src/render/globe.ts`). Because the tier decides the context's antialiasing, a throw-away canvas is probed once before the Globe exists (`probeGpuStrings`, context released at once). `resolveQuality` stays pure (the class is a parameter).

(5) The tip shows only when the class made the choice (`tierSource` `auto`): with an explicit `?quality=` the text "the game chose low" would be untrue. In test mode only the hook `?gpu=<string>` (query only; the hash grammar cannot carry it) shows it, with `menu=1`; the tier stays `test`, so no golden changes. The e2e pages that open the live page under SwiftShader mostly pass `quality=low` already, so they never show it; the live `#scene-perf` e2e now runs on `low` under SwiftShader (it only checks that the run completes).

(6) `.start-root` got `align-items: safe center` so that a card taller than the window (now possible at 960x540 with the tip) scrolls from its top instead of being cut off.

### M09a: structures, part A of M09 (`src/sim/structures.ts`, `src/sim/structureRules.ts`, `src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/sim/public.ts`, `tests/sim/structures.test.ts`)

1. **State and hash.** The structure table is game state: it is in the hash (`foldStructures`) and the snapshot (FORMAT_VERSION 6, a block after the home rules, always present) and ENGINE_VERSION is 7. Both golden files (`e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json`, `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json`) were re-pinned ONCE for this, with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1`, because the hash layout changed; the golden scenario now also sends Build, Upgrade and Delete (most are refused; the run builds structures, a test checks it).
2. **Free list.** A slot is free when its owner is 0. `highWater` slots were ever used; a Delete pushes the id on a recycle stack and the next Build pops it, else takes slot `highWater`. Only the live records, `highWater` and the stack are serialised (a restored table allocates exactly as the straight run), the per-cell index (a Uint16 per cell, id + 1) and the per-owner counts are derived and rebuilt in `assembleState`; a test compares the rebuilt index with the incremental one.
3. **Intents.** `Upgrade` and `Delete` carry `structure` (the table id) because `id` is already the intent id. Wire kinds 6, 7, 8 appended. Reason codes 21 to 38 in `src/sim/intents.ts`. The order of the checks in `checkBuild`: type, cell, Peak, ownership, Mountain / Hills / Forest rules, Port coast, occupied cell, spacing, fallout, Silo needs a finished own Factory, room in the table, purse. Peak comes before ownership so the reason names the real cause.
4. **One implementation.** `checkBuild(source, goldMilli, seat, type, cell)` is the rule; the public `validateBuild(view, seat, type, cell)` passes `view.stats.goldMilli[seat]`, the sim passes `state.gold[owner]` through a small adapter (`StateBuildSource`). The fallout layer is `SimView.falloutUntilAt(cell)` (a method, zero until M14; M14 backs it with a per-cell array). A `ViewMirror` shows an EMPTY structure table until the M10 snapshot extension: UI code that calls `validateBuild` on a mirror before M10 would see no spacing or Factory.
5. **Spacing interpretation.** `SPACING_CELLS` 16 is a Euclidean distance in sim cells (dx wrapping, no latitude weight): a structure closer than 16 is refused, exactly 16 is fine. `SPACING_SCOPE` 0 = the same type, any owner (the reading of the prompt); 1 = same type same owner; 2 = any type. Unfinished structures count. Delete frees the spot at once.
6. **Prices.** Next price = floor(base * min(12, 2 + n) / 2) with n = structures of that type the seat owns (unfinished ones included, so two Builds in one tick cannot both get the cheap price). An upgrade to level L costs base * L. An upgrade takes the type's build time (own choice, the prompt gives none); while it runs the structure keeps its old level and cannot be upgraded again. Delete refunds nothing; the cooldown is per seat, 300 ticks. Gold is taken when the intent runs.
7. **Provisional prices measured (the paragraph the prompt asks for).** Solo scenario (`npm run sim:scenario -- --solo`, seed 1, Russia, an expander that spends nothing; probe in the scratchpad): the seat starts with 500 gold, so the first City (400) is affordable at tick 0 and the first Factory (500) after 900 gold have been earned in total, at 64 s (City + Factory), a second City (600) at 102 s, a third (800) at 140 s. Income per minute in the solo run: about 350 in minute 1, 1040 in 2, 1800 in 3, 2550 in 4, 3250 in 5 (it grows with the land). After the tempo pass (D142) conquest is fast, but gold income is unchanged and still keeps up with these prices: the first City is "within one to two minutes" (it is instant), a City plus Factory within about 1 minute. No base price was moved, and no M04 band was touched.
8. **Phase.** `structuresPhase` runs after the economy: finished upgrades raise the level. It costs O(highWater) only when structures exist; the perf test (12 seats, 300 structures) measures it against the empty-step budget.
9. **Left for M09b, and how the table fits it.** Effects read finished structures (`tick >= readyTick`, level from `level[id]`); the City level sum for `cityLevels()` and the Port pair cache iterate the live slots or the per-owner counts. Conquest needs a hook where a cell changes owner (`setOwner` in `src/sim/territory.ts`) that calls a `structures` transfer / destroy by `byCell`. Until then a structure keeps its recorded owner when its cell is conquered (not reachable from a normal match before M09b ships, since nothing yet gives the conquered cell's structure to the new owner).

### D160 AutoTier-2: adaptive resolution (`src/render/adaptive.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `src/render/globe.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`, `tests/render/adaptive.test.ts`, `e2e/adaptive.test.ts`)
1. **What it does.** A pure regulator (`AdaptiveResolution`) turns frame times into a scale of the DRAWING BUFFER (ladder 1, 0.85, 0.72, 0.6, 0.5 times the tier's pixel ratio). `Globe.setRenderScale` changes only the buffer (`setPixelRatio` and `setSize(w, h, false)`, once per change): the CSS size, the camera rig, the projection and picking work in CSS pixels and never see it; the terrain LOD depends on the camera distance and field of view, not on pixels (no pop; screenshots of one view at 1.0 / 0.72 / 0.5 differ only in sharpness). The owner texture, the HUD and the overlays are untouched.
2. **Which clock.** The frame time fed in is the GPU time of the draw call (`gpuTimer`, `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`) when the browser has the extension, otherwise the interval between animation frames. Reason: fewer pixels only help a GPU-bound frame; a CPU-bound or compositor-bound frame (a long interval with a short GPU time) must not be punished with a blurrier picture. The fx clock (`src/render/clock.ts`) is for effects and is stepped by `min(dt, 0.1)`, so it cannot measure a slow frame. In the performance scene (which reads the same timer queue for its report) the regulator, when forced on with `adapt=1`, uses the interval instead of draining the queue.
3. **Numbers.** Window 30 frames, the statistic is the MEDIAN (a spike from a tab switch, a shader compile or a texture upload cannot move it; frames over 1000 ms are not fed at all); the first 90 frames are ignored; `ADAPT_DOWN_MS` 30 (a 30 fps frame is 33.3 ms: 30 leaves a little margin, and two windows in a row are needed), `ADAPT_UP_MS` 18 (a 60 Hz display delivers 16.7 ms; the gap 18 to 30 is the hysteresis: the biggest step up of the ladder multiplies the pixel count by at most 1.44, so a load that read under 18 ms reads under about 26 ms one step up, still under the 30 ms limit, and the regulator does not swing); two slow windows in a row to go down (about 2 s at 30 fps), four fast ones to go up, at least 2 s of frame time between two changes; a window between the limits resets both counts. All of it is in `src/render/config.ts`; the unit tests use a cost model `time = a + b * scale^2` and find the highest scale that fits.
4. **When the ladder is not enough.** After scale 0.5, the chunk density goes ONE step down (`Globe.setChunkDensity(ADAPT_DENSITY_SCALE)` = 0.7: the split distances of the quadtree times 0.7, the octave law follows, the mesh and MSAA are untouched) and is logged (`console.info`, which the e2e console check ignores); it comes back only when the scale is 1.0 again and the frames are fast. DEVIATION from the prompt: it asked for "the next tier's `chunkMax` / `chunkSplitK`"; the tier below `low` does not exist and `medium` has the same split constant as `low` (4.5), so that step would be a no-op on exactly the machines that need it; the existing density lever with a fixed 0.7 works from every tier. The ladder of scales is measured in "Measurements AutoTier-2" (pixel-bound against vertex-bound).
5. **Switches.** On in the live game, off in test mode and in the performance scene (`#scene-perf` and `?perf=1` measure the raw speed) unless `?adapt=1` / `#adapt-1`; `?adapt=0` / `#adapt-0` is always off; the Dev panel has `adapt-on` / `adapt-off` (hash, reload, like `ai-on`); the test hook `__test.adaptive` (`enable`, `feed`, `simulate`, `info`) runs it in test mode with a synthetic GPU. The report gets the additive field `adaptive` (`enabled`, `scale`, `scaleMean`, `scaleMin`, `steps`, `densityReduced`; schemaVersion stays 1) and F3 a line.
6. **Cost.** Per frame: one `push` (a few additions and a comparison; the median sort of 30 numbers runs once per window, in preallocated typed arrays, no allocation); the GPU timer is enabled only when the regulator is on in the live page. No extra GL synchronisation: the timer results come out of the queue a few frames late, which only delays the first reaction by a window.
7. **Mutants.** `tests/render/adaptive.test.ts` runs the same scenario on a regulator with one rule broken and requires the check to fail there: "mean instead of median" (spikes), "no warm-up", "no hysteresis" (one window both ways), "no chunk-density step".
8. **Not changed.** No golden, no earlier test, no sim, AI or host code, no tier's mesh / MSAA / DPR cap, `ENGINE_VERSION` and the hashes.


### M09b: structure effects, conquest of structures, the AI build move (`src/sim/structureEffects.ts`, `src/ai/build.ts`, `src/host/aiDriver.ts`, `tools/sim/scenario.ts`, `tests/sim/structureEffects.test.ts`, `tests/ai/build.test.ts`, `tests/sim/economyScript.test.ts`)

1. **Effects are a derived cache.** City levels, the fixed gold (City, Factory) and the Port gold (with trade) per owner live in the `StructureTable` (`effCityLevels`, `effFixedMilli`, `effPortMilli`), never hashed. They are recomputed when the table changes (`effDirty`: add, remove, transfer, level up, embargo) and when a structure finishes (`effNextTick` = the earliest future `readyTick` / `upgradeReadyTick`), so the trade pairs are worked out on a change only. A restored state starts dirty and recomputes the same numbers (a test compares a restored run with the straight one). The port trait (M06, +-10 %) is applied at read time to the whole Port income, so it needs no invalidation.
2. **Numbers.** Factory and Port pay the same at every level (the 10x limit of the prompt: a level-3 Port would break it); only the City grows with its level. The Factory bonus is 25 % of that City's income, once per City however many Factories. Trade: the distance is the integer floor of the Euclidean distance in sim cells; a pair with an embargo still occupies one of the 3 partner slots (it pays 0), which is the conservative reading of "zero under embargo". Gold is floored once per tick (`floor(milli per second / 10)`), at most 0.9 milli-gold per tick (under 0.5 % of the smallest trade value).
3. **Embargo flag.** The prompt says the flag exists; it did not. `StructureTable.embargo` ((seats + 1)^2 bytes, kept symmetric by `setEmbargo`) is game state, hashed and serialised (FORMAT_VERSION 7). Nothing sets it yet (diplomacy is out of scope); the tests do.
4. **Defense Post.** `advanceAttack` collects the target's finished Posts once per attack per tick (only when the target owns one) and multiplies the cell price by 5/2, floored, for cells within 12 cells (distance squared <= 144, so exactly 12 counts) after the home / trait adjustment. A Post pays for the owner of the land, not for the attacker.
5. **Conquest.** `changeOwner` calls `structureCellChanged` when a structure stands on the changed cell: City / Factory / Port go to the new owner one level lower (min 1, an upgrade in progress is lost), Silo / SAM / Post are removed (their id is recycled, no Delete cooldown). The enclave flip, Spawn and DebugPaint go through the same function, so they are covered. A cell freed to NO_OWNER destroys its structure. The spacing rule is not re-checked after a transfer.
6. **Re-pin (ENGINE_VERSION 8, FORMAT_VERSION 7).** The state hash now contains the embargo flags and the rules changed (cap and gold from structures), so `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` were re-pinned once with UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 through `tests/sim/golden.test.ts` and `tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts`. Two older tests were moved because their arithmetic knew the byte layout: `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` (two sizes, +25 bytes for 4 seats) and `tests/sim/homeRules.test.ts` (the offset of the trait block from the end, +25).
7. **AI build move.** Order of need: Factory when `(factories + 1) * 2 <= cities`, a Port (from the second City, one per 3), a Post (from the fourth City, one per 4), else a City; an Upgrade of the lowest idle City when it is cheaper than the next City. SAM and Silo are never built (no effect before M14). If the wanted type finds no place (a Port without a coast cell in the window) a City is tried in the same think. Nations only, never a tribe; one request per think. It runs after `think` in `AiDriver` (so `think` and its tests are untouched), draws from the seat's own stream, and the `build: false` option of the driver keeps it off. Tuning round 1: the prompt's example gate "troops >= 50 % of the cap" never opened (the AI spends its troops on a long attack and a City is bought with gold), so no City was built in 10 minutes; the gate is 0 now (`AI_BUILD_CITY_TROOP_PCT`), and a Port without a place falls back to a City. Round 2: a cities-only probe (no Factory, Port, Post) gave 11 511 cells, no better than the mix, so no further ratio or price change was made.
8. **Which view the AI gets.** `MainThreadHost` hands the driver a `StateView` over the live state, which has the real structure table; a `ViewMirror` (the page) still shows an empty table until the M10 snapshot extension, so the AI must run in the sim's thread (it does).
9. **AI state blob.** `AiDriver.serialize()` writes int32 words: a magic, the intent counter, the Spawns in flight, and per AI seat its memory words (`memoryWords`) and its four random words. `restore(blob)` before or after the first tick applies it (all or nothing, checked against the seat layout). The test serialises at tick 1000, resumes a host with a restored driver and reaches the same hash at tick 2000 as the straight run, and a driver without the blob does not.
10. **DEVIATION: the economy target (b) was not reached.** Asked: AI v0 with building has land in [100 %, 150 %] of the band's upper limit (14 000 to 21 000 cells) after 10 minutes. Measured (Russia, seed 1): AI v0 without building 12 791, with building 12 568, factor 0.90. Buildings raise the troop cap (+800 per City level) and the gold, but in this scenario the land is limited by the speed of the front and the attack rhythm, not by the troop pool, so no ratio or price helps (the cities-only probe: 11 511). Per the M04 rule the test interval is the measured value +-30 % and the factor is in BALANCE.md. A lever outside this slice (the AI's troop commitment, or the capture speed) would be needed; it is in BACKLOG. (a) holds: the build-free scripted expander is inside the M04 band.
11. **Provisional prices unchanged.** A first City is affordable at tick 0 with the 500 start gold; with the gate at 0 the AI builds its first City within the first seconds after its Spawn.

### CamFix-2: the shown-tilt limit has a speed, the right drag keeps the stored tilt, the start view fits the window, and the mountain words say what was measured (`src/render/camera.ts`, `src/render/fit.ts`, `src/render/config.ts`, `src/app/globeApp.ts`, `tests/render/camFix2.test.ts`)

(1) THE SHOWN TILT HAD NO SPEED. CamFix showed `min(tilt, cap(distance))` as a pure function, and a flight (`setPose` = free pose) showed the raw tilt, so grabbing a flight at 20 000+ km snapped the picture to the eased tilt in one frame, and a spin of five wheel notches changed it by up to 12.8 degrees. Now the rig keeps `capNow`, the limit as it is: it moves toward its goal (the eased limit, or 90 while the pose is free or under 8 000 km) by at most `FIT_TILT_RATE_DEG_S` = 132 degrees per second of the fx clock (M08's 2.2 degrees per frame at 60 fps; a camera at 21 500 km moves 2.9 % of its distance for 2.2 degrees), stepped in `update` with the same sub-steps as the rest. The shown tilt is `min(user tilt, capNow)`: only the CAP is rate limited, so the user's own tilt (a right drag at low height) is never delayed. `setView`, `setViewUnclamped` snap the cap to its goal (a view set by hand is as it was), `setPose` puts it at the pose's own tilt (the picture is as placed, and the eased limit slides in from there when the user grabs). The final value of a still picture is unchanged. Test: every tenth frame of a flight from 21 500 km to the Himalaya is grabbed (wheel and drag), then no frame changes the shown tilt by more than 2.2 degrees or the position by more than 2.9 % (16 of 25 new tests fail on the code before).
(2) THE RIGHT DRAG. The globe branch of `rotateBy` recomputed the stored tilt from the shown one on any vertical movement, so "zoom out, turn, zoom in" lost the user's tilt (60 became 0). The stored tilt now changes by `dy * ROTATE_DEG_PER_PX`, clamped to 0 .. `TILT_MAX_DEG`. This provably changes one earlier test: `tests/render/fitCamera.test.ts` "far out, a right drag UP from a tilt that is eased ..." asserted the old rule (the stored tilt jumps to the eased cap); its three assertions on the stored tilt now state the new rule and one keeps "never shown above the cap". The price: a drag UP far out stores a tilt that is not yet shown (no dead zone is created: the stored value moves by exactly the drag, and shows when the distance allows).
(3) THE START VIEW. `fitViewDistKm(distKm, aspect)` = `min(maxDistKm, max(distKm, tiltEaseEndKm))`: the identity for every window of aspect 1 and more (so the 16:9 goldens do not change), farther out for a tall one. Used by `src/app/globeApp.ts` for the start view only. NOT done: the `earth` canonical view (a golden contact-sheet view in 16:9) and `PERF_ORBIT_KM` (the perf scene's host has no aspect) are left as they were; BACKLOG.
(4) THE MOUNTAIN WORDS (D158 (9) and PROGRESS, PREVIEW 17aa): "calm" overstated it. Measured: calm over the Alps (dip 8 % at 3 km, 26 % at 20 km), but at a fast pan against the Himalaya wall the camera dives to 79 % under the held height at 3 km (632 m) and 29 % at 20 km. The pin "under 85 %" stays as it was (a guard against worse, not a claim of calm); the test is renamed so that it does not claim calm. The look-ahead that would improve the dip was tried and rejected in D158 (9).

### CamFix-2 regression: the free shown-tilt limit clamped a stored tilt above 90 degrees (`src/render/camera.ts`, `tests/render/camFix2Wheel.test.ts`)

CAUSE. `e2e/rts.test.ts` "wheel all the way in stops 300 m above the ground" failed over open sea after CamFix-2 (rest 360 479 m up, tilt 83.6, instead of 300.0 m, tilt 62.4). CamFix-2 showed `min(tilt, capNow)` with `capNow` at `TILT_CAP_FREE_DEG` (90) at every distance of 8 000 km and less, where CamFix showed the stored tilt as it is. A stored tilt is the angle at the sea-level focus and is above 90 for a camera that is under the sea-level sphere: the last step of a 14-notch dolly over open water lands a hair low (agl -1.5 km), `viewFromPose` then reads the pose from the far side of the sphere (distance 11 078 km, tilt 150), and the 90 degree clamp showed that camera as a different pose (thousands of kilometres away). The dolly went on from there, used up its `RTS_DOLLY_MAX_FIXES` and rested 360 km up. Before CamFix-2 the same camera was shown as stored, `keepAboveGround` lifted it straight up and it rested at 300.0 m. The cap sliding, the held anchor branch and `pitchIntoLimits` were NOT involved (`capNow` stays at 90 in the whole run).

FIX. `CameraRig.shownOf(tilt)`: the limit is in force only below `TILT_CAP_FREE_DEG`; at 90 (every distance up to `FIT_TILT_FREE_KM`, and a free pose) the shown tilt is the stored one, as before CamFix-2. `shownTilt` and `positionOf` use it; `setPose` starts the limit at `min(tilt, 90)`. Nothing changes where the limit binds (the eased distances, the rate), so no earlier test, tolerance or golden moves. `tests/render/camFix2Wheel.test.ts` is the e2e experiment on the real height field (14 notches of -1500 at the cursor (520, 280) from 4 000 km at tilt 30, 60 frames of 0.5 s, open sea and the Himalaya): it fails on the code before the fix (open sea 360 479 m) and passes after.

NOT FIXED (older than CamFix-2, BACKLOG): over open water a long dolly can still end below the sea-level sphere for a moment and fly out and back (before CamFix-2 the same run went to 5 898 km and back); it ends right only because `keepAboveGround` lifts it.

### M10a: the City on the terrain, placement, build bar and toasts (slice 1 of M10; `src/render/structures.ts`, `src/render/structureModels.ts`, `src/host/structureSnapshot.ts`, `src/app/placement.ts`, `src/ui/toast.ts`)

1. **Slice, not the whole milestone.** Asked by the orchestrator: only the City with infrastructure that the other five types plug into without a redesign. The registry (`STRUCTURE_MODELS`), the layer (one mesh per modelled type), `BUILD_ITEMS` of the bar and `InputTarget.build(type)` for the digit keys are those plug points. A type without a model is not drawn and its key stays unbound (the AI builds Factories, Ports and Posts; they are in the table and the state, not in the picture, until their slices).
2. **The snapshot carries the table only when it changed.** `ViewSnapshot.structures` is always present in `requestView` and in a frame's diff only when `structureSignature` (a 32-bit fold over the used slots and the delete cooldowns) differs from the last diff that carried it. A table of a few hundred structures is a few kilobytes; a signature pass per frame is one loop over `highWater`. The page replaces its copy whole (`MirrorStructures.adopt`) and the renderer rebuilds its instance buffers only when `structuresVersion` moved. `buildProgress` is not stored: it is `(tick - buildTick) / (readyTick - buildTick)` (`structureProgress`), evaluated in the shader from the interpolated tick, so a construction costs nothing per frame on the CPU.
3. **Placement without an instance matrix.** The instance position is a float32 hi / lo pair and the camera-relative sum is formed in the shader (the terrain chunks' own rule, ARCHITECTURE rules for layers (1)); the frame (up, east, north) comes from the instance's direction. Float64 positions and heights stay on the CPU (`StructureLayer.instances()`), which is what the height test reads: `heightM` equals `exaggeration * groundHeightAt` within 1 m (rule (4): a foundation uses the full ground height; the model's rim reaches 0.22 units below it so that it never floats over the coarser distant surface).
4. **Scale policy.** The model's radius is `max(true size, STRUCT_SCREEN_FRAC * camera distance)` up to `STRUCT_MAX_SCALE` x the true size (0.011 and 70: about 11 px of radius at 960 x 540 from 400 to 2 500 km, a speck from orbit). It is shader maths per instance. No icon-quad LOD and no night lights in this slice (BACKLOG).
5. **DEVIATION: the instance cap follows triangles, not 500.** The orchestrator's slice and the original prompt say 500 instances in the test tier; the City has 200 triangles, so 500 would be 100 000 triangles against the row of 15 000 that ARCHITECTURE rules for layers (3) promised. The cap per type is `STRUCT_TRIANGLE_BUDGET[tier] / triangles of the model` (test 15 000 = 75 Cities, low 30 000, medium 60 000, high 120 000); what does not fit is counted in `props().dropped`. The Budgetar row says `<= 15 000` triangles and `<= 10` draw calls (the slice uses 1, 2 with the ghost). The icon LOD (BACKLOG) is what lets 500 instances fit later.
6. **The ghost is guidance only.** `validateBuild` on the snapshot is the same `checkBuild` the sim runs; the click sends the intent and the sim validates again. A click on a red ghost sends nothing and shows a toast with the key `toast.build.<reason>` (`buildRejectKey`); the mode stays on. The mode leaves on a non-Shift green click, Escape (the stack pops before the pause handler is asked), a right click, a long press and the key of the type again.
7. **i18n key names.** The build texts are `hud.build.*` and `toast.build.*`: `tests/i18n.test.ts` finds literal keys by a fixed list of prefixes, and the existing test was not touched.
8. **Golden.** One new golden, `e2e/goldens/structures-city.png` (UPDATE_GOLDENS=1, once, after looking at it: a close-up of a finished City on the Bavarian plain, 2.6 km south of it, 1.1 km up, afternoon sun). No earlier golden changed: the structure layer draws nothing until a structure exists, and the goldens have none.
9. **Test hook for a clock set back.** `__test.setView({time})` sets the fx clock, also backwards; the pop-in then read a negative age and drew nothing. A negative age now shows the finished model (a real frame loop never turns the clock back).

### M10-2: the Factory and the Port (slice 2 of M10; `src/render/structureModels.ts`, `src/render/structures.ts`, `src/ui/buildBar.ts`, `e2e/structuresFactoryPort.test.ts`)

1. **Two registry entries, nothing else.** `STRUCTURE_MODELS` gets `STRUCT_FACTORY` (a hall with an owner-coloured roof, two chimneys, a tank and an annex, about 140 triangles) and `STRUCT_PORT` (an owner-coloured disc, a pier, a yellow gantry crane, a warehouse and three containers, about 120 triangles), both on the City's pale rim and owner-coloured disc, so the base reads the owner from far away. Both are well under the 250-triangle allowance, so the per-tier instance cap (triangle budget / model triangles) is at least 60 in the test tier. Key 2 and 3 and the buttons are the same `build(type)` hook and `BUILD_ITEMS` plug point as the City; the price is the sim's escalating `buildPriceMilli` (Factory 500, Port 400 base). No toast key was missing: M10a already carries `toast.build.*` for every reject code; only the button texts (`hud.build.factory*`, `hud.build.port*`, and a per-type too-poor tooltip) are new.
2. **The Port's pier points to the sea.** A model may say `seaward: true`: the layer then sets the instance's yaw from the neighbouring cells (a 5 x 5 window around the cell, `groundHeightAt <= 0` counts as sea, weighted 1/distance) instead of the pseudo-random yaw; without a sea neighbour it falls back to the pseudo-random yaw. It costs 24 height samples per Port on a table rebuild, never per frame. The ghost uses the same yaw. `DrawnStructure` (and `props().list`) gain `yaw` so a test can sample the water ahead of the pier.
3. **The ghost shows the model of the type being placed.** M10a drew the City's shape for every ghost; `setGhost` now rebinds the ghost's vertex attributes to the type's buffers (shared, no copy) and its size.
4. **Build bar layout.** The bar wraps (`flex-wrap`, a max width); in the narrow layout it moves to the right of the gold panel and grows upward, so six buttons fit 540 x 960 (the e2e adds stand-ins for keys 4 to 6 and checks the box and a screenshot).
5. **One earlier test line changed.** `tests/render/structureModels.test.ts` said `modelledTypes()` equals `[STRUCT_CITY]`; a registry that grows breaks that line by design, so it now says the City is the first entry (`toContain` for the new types lives in `tests/render/structureModelsFactoryPort.test.ts`). The City's own geometry asserts are untouched. The slice with Silo, SAM and Post makes the same one-line change; keep either.
6. **Golden.** One new golden `e2e/goldens/structures-factory-port.png` (UPDATE_GOLDENS=1, once, after looking at it): a sheet of three close-ups side by side (a City, a Factory and a Port of one Rome-start match, each camera 4.6 km south and 2 km up, the middle 320 px of each shot), so that the three silhouettes can be compared in one picture; one structure per shot because the cells are about 39 km apart. No earlier golden changed.
### M10-3: Silo, SAM site and Defense Post (slice 3 of M10; `src/render/structureModels.ts`, `src/ui/buildBar.ts`)

1. **Silhouettes.** Silo: the only LOW model with a thin white missile standing on a dark pad; SAM site: launcher tubes plus a big dish on a mast; Post: the only slim tower, with a flag, inside a wall ring. All on the City's plate (pale rim, owner-coloured disc). True radii 560 / 520 / 450 m (City 700). Triangles are 250 or fewer each (unit test), so the per-tier instance cap is at least 60 of each in the test tier.
2. **The dish turns in the shader.** A vertex with `aTint >= 2` is rotated round the model's y axis by `uSpin` before the instance yaw; its real tint is `aTint - 2`. The angle is ONE scalar uniform per frame (`fxTime * 0.9 rad/s mod 2pi`, `STRUCT_DISH_RAD_PER_SEC` in `src/render/config.ts`; cosmetic, never the sim's RNG); wrapping on the CPU keeps the sine precise in a long session, which a raw `uTime * speed` in the shader would not on a GPU. The instance's random yaw is the per-instance phase. The e2e test compares two frames one whole sun day (1800 fx s) apart, because the sun moves with the fx clock: the same light, another dish angle (SAM 6975 pixels differ, a Post 0).
3. **The ghost wears the type's model.** `setGhost` swaps the shared position / colour / tint attributes and `uSizeM` when the type changes (no copy, no new mesh: still one ghost draw call).
4. **Silo locked state.** The sim rule "a Silo needs a finished own Factory" is read through `hasFinishedFactory` (new export in `src/sim/public.ts`, one line in the pinned list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`), not copied into the UI. The button is dimmed with `data-state="locked"` and the tooltip `hud.build.needFactory`; it still starts the placement mode, so the ghost shows the same reason in red and a click shows `toast.build.noFactory`.
5. **Texts.** The Swedish name of the Silo button is "Missilsilo" because the i18n parity test counts texts equal in both tables (a bare "Silo" would be a ninth). No `toast.build.*` key was missing: M10a already had one for every reason code.
6. **Build bar layout.** Six buttons do not fit a row beside the HUD panels: the bar wraps (max 23 em wide, centred between the Gold and Speed panels in landscape; right-aligned, narrower than the window minus the Gold panel in portrait); the hint and the toast stack sit above it (their `bottom` values moved: 17 em for the toasts in landscape, 23 em / 30 em in portrait). An e2e test pins every button inside the window at 960x540 and 540x960.
7. **Existing test touched.** `tests/render/structureModels.test.ts` asserted `modelledTypes()` equals `[STRUCT_CITY]`; it now asserts the City is among them (provably caused by the new entries, nothing else in the test changed).
8. **Golden.** One new golden, `e2e/goldens/structures-military.png` (UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 once, after looking at it): a three-panel sheet (Silo, SAM on a Mountain ridge cell next to a Peak, Post), each panel a crop of its own 3 km close-up centred on the structure's base, HUD hidden. No earlier golden changed (`structures-city` still matches).

### M09c: level effects of Factory and Port, the AI economy target (`src/sim/rules.ts`, `src/sim/structureEffects.ts`, `src/ai/build.ts`, `tests/sim/structureLevels.test.ts`, `tests/ai/richUpgrade.test.ts`)

1. **Level effects.** Before, an Upgrade of a Factory or a Port wasted gold. Now Factory gold is 2.0 / 4.5 / 8.5 gold/s, its City bonus 25 / 30 / 35 % and its reach 20 / 22 / 24 cells; Port own gold 2.0 / 4.5 / 8.5 and its trade share 100 / 110 / 120 % of the trade value. The gain per upgrade is set so that price / gain is 280 to 340 s (Factory 1000 / 1500 gold, Port 800 / 1200), about a new City's 267 s, inside the asked 90 s to 6 minutes; the payback test reads the incomes from the real effects cache. A City upgrade (800 gold, 1.5 gold/s, 533 s, plus 800 troop cap) is left as it is (BACKLOG).
2. **The 10x limit (15 gold/s) needed two new rules.** A level-3 Port with 3 far partners would earn 8.5 + 3 * 3.3 * 1.2 = 20.4: `PORT_INCOME_MAX_MILLI_PER_SEC` = 13 000 clamps one Port (14.3 with the +10 % trait). A Factory's bonus on up to 8 level-3 Cities in reach would be 9 gold/s on top of 8.5: a Factory boosts at most `FACTORY_BONUS_MAX_CITIES` = 3 Cities (a City takes the best Factory in reach with a free slot, highest level then lowest id, Cities in id order; a scratch array, never hashed), worst 13.2 gold/s. The old cap test (a level-1 Port: 11.9 / 13.09) still passes unchanged because the level-1 numbers did not move; the new worst cases are in `tests/sim/structureLevels.test.ts`. The old tests of `tests/sim/structureEffects.test.ts` are untouched and pass.
3. **AI economy target (b), M09b deviation closed.** Levers: `AI_EXPAND_TROOP_PCT` 40 -> 37 (the gate for acting), and the AI upgrades when rich (`AI_BUILD_RICH_PCT` = 100: the purse pays the wanted structure AND an upgrade, then the lowest finished City / Factory / Port is upgraded first). Both together: 14 274 land cells after 10 minutes in the solo scenario (Russia, seed 1), factor 1.02 of the band's upper limit; the M09b result was 12 568 (0.90). Two rounds were used in the sense of the prompt; the neighbouring values are in BALANCE.md. M04's bands are unchanged; the build-free expander is still inside them. `tests/sim/economyScript.test.ts` now asserts the asked [1.0, 1.5] instead of the M09b measured +-30 % (that interval was only a stand-in for the unmet target).
4. **Build and Upgrade only where nobody is next door.** Two refused intents appeared (REJECT_BUILD_NOT_OWNED, REJECT_STRUCT_NOT_OWNER) because the intent runs 2 ticks after the AI looked and a border cell or structure may be conquered by then. `src/ai/build.ts` now takes only cells with no 4-neighbour of another owner (sea and free land are fine, a Port stands on a coast) and upgrades only such structures. This is a behaviour change of the build move only (not of `think`).
5. **Upgrade validation.** The AI uses the table conditions the sim's own `checkUpgrade` tests (finished, idle, below the top level, purse); `validateUpgrade` is not in `src/sim/public.ts` on this branch (another track adds it), so nothing was added to `src/sim/public.ts` but the constant `AI_BUILD_RICH_PCT` (pinned in `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`). The economy script counts 0 refused intents.
6. **Pace.** Raising the AI's expansion lowers the human share in `tests/sim/pace.test.ts`: 43.2 % before, 25 -> 29.6 (fails the floor 30), 33 -> 30.4, 37 -> 40.6 %. The test is chaotic in the exact value; 37 has a margin of 10 points. The test was not touched.
7. **Re-pin.** No byte layout changed (ENGINE_VERSION and FORMAT_VERSION stay), but the rules and the AI changed the hashed state: both golden hash files were re-pinned ONCE with UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 through the existing golden tests.


## M10-4 (selection panel, range rings, night lights, far icons)
