## Fix-3A2: audit 2 (radar reach, navigation, AI limits, MatchView) and the M12 preparation

Audit 2 (four reviewers) of the pushes v17 to v21; every finding was checked against HEAD 04eaba8 first. None was moot after Sim-Levels2 (the AI rules `richUpgrade`, `1 + Cities * 5`, the district rules and the `cityLevelTarget` cap all still exist; the "level 1 to 10" wording of the audit just reads "up to AI_CITY_LEVEL_CAP" now). Headless slice, no Chromium, no golden file touched (`git diff e2e/goldens` is empty).

1. **LZ reason codes get their own range (fit[5]).** `LZ_REASON_BASE = 64` in `src/sim/rules.ts` (row in `docs/BALANCE.md`); `LZ_WATER` .. `LZ_NO_ROUTE` are 65..72 (`LZ_OK` stays 0), above the highest `REJECT_*` code (44). `LzReason` is the union `0 | 65..72`; `LZ_REASON_NAMES` became a `Record` keyed by the code, so `LZ_REASON_NAMES[lz.reason]` in the debug page keeps working unchanged. The nav fingerprint hashes routes, not reasons, so the code change cannot move it, and no hashed state uses LZ codes. **Deviation:** the prompt also asked for toast texts in the reject key map; `src/ui` was off limits for this slice, so `rejectKey(65..72)` answers the generic `reject.other` ("Avvisat (kod 65)") instead of the WRONG text of a REJECT code, which is the bug that mattered. M12a adds the eight `lz.*` texts together with the Launch toast (BACKLOG). Test: `tests/sim/lzCodes.test.ts` (disjoint ranges, names, `rejectKey`).
2. **`Los.sync` is memoised (fit[4]).** `sync(src)` returns after ONE comparison when the structure table object, `src.tick`, `highWater`, `liveCount` and the extra-site version equal those of the last full scan (`syncScans` counts scans for the tests). Sound because the sim edits the table only inside `step`, between two ticks, and a SAM that finishes building only does so as the tick passes `readyTick`, which is in the key. A caller that edits a table by hand inside one tick (tests, tools) calls the new `Los.invalidate()`. **Test change, justified:** `tests/sim/los.test.ts` "lazy rebuild" upgrades a SAM (`level[id] = 2`) and transfers it at one fixed tick with equal counters; two `los.invalidate()` lines were added there (no assertion touched). Perf case in `tests/los.perf.test.ts`: highWater 820, 480 `exposure()` calls per tick, median 0.13 to 0.3 ms against `budgetMs(0.5)` (without the memo about 1.3 to 3 ms and 5280 scans for 11 ticks). Hashes unchanged.
3. **A* heuristic is a lower bound now (sim[4]).** The old east-west part used the width of the row being expanded, which overestimates when the goal is nearer a pole; the measured worst cost ratio on an OPEN synthetic world with 16 nav rows was 1.55 (documented bound 1.3). New `heuristic` in `src/sim/nav/route.ts`: the 8-neighbour lattice bound `min(D,V) * sqrt(w^2 + h^2) + (D - min) * w + (V - min) * h` at the narrowest row width `w` between node and goal, and it also prices an excursion to a narrower row beyond them (rows walked twice, the great-circle bend), stopping as soon as more rows cannot help. Every step costs at least its lattice length (factor >= 1), so it holds for every path. A first try with a plain Euclid bound was a bound too, but it was so loose that the 1.3-weighted search wobbled in the 4:1 test fixtures of `tests/sim/nav.test.ts` (three fixture tests failed); the lattice bound keeps all of them green untouched. Proof by search: `tests/sim/navExact.test.ts` runs Dijkstra with the same step costs on 24 random 32 x 16 nav worlds (both modes, lambda 0 and 3, a SAM site and a Post present), on an open world for every pair of rows, and on the real 256 x 128 nav grid (60 seeded pairs): every route is a legal path and costs between 1.0 and 1.3 times the optimum (measured worst: random worlds 1.02, open world 1.16, real grid 1.16; the old heuristic 1.55 on the open world). **Fingerprint:** `PINNED_HASH` in `tests/sim/nav.test.ts` 4680ed7e -> abb0fadd, because routes on the real world changed (equal or cheaper, not hashed state); nothing else pins routes. Perf unchanged (`tests/nav.perf.test.ts`: median 0.08 ms).
4. **Radar mask honesty (sim[2]).** Text only. The `LOS_MASK_SLACK_GM` comment, its `docs/BALANCE.md` row and this entry now state the measured numbers instead of "zero one-sided errors": zero false-hidden cells on the eight fixture sites, but 47 of 786 376 cells (0.006 %) in 17 of 300 random real-world sites (5.7 % of sites) are hidden by the mask and visible on the exact line (worst: the mask asks 1060 game metres where the line needs 205). The M11a owner decision stands (accept; a finer mask grid is the alternative C of M11a, in `docs/BACKLOG.md`). No code or number changed.
5. **Unlocked nav and LOS behaviours, now locked (sim[3], mutation[9], mutation[5]).** New tests with mutants killed (below). Not locked, moved to BACKLOG: the LZ score weights and the `>=` boundaries, the priority of ENEMY_CITY_NEAR against TOO_HIGH, an enemy City across the date line, `landReachable` / `landLabel` (no caller) and the A* tie rule.
6. **Packed MatchView and AI limits (mutation[7], mutation[8]).** `tests/sim/matchViewPack.test.ts` and `tests/ai/limits.test.ts`, mutants below.

### M12 preparation: Los/Nav access inside `step` (fit[3]; DECISION only, nothing built here, M12a builds it)

Problem: `step(state, turn)` reaches neither `Los` nor `Nav` (built outside the state, from a `SimView`), and the occluder layer `maxElev` is not in `SimWorld` at all, while M12a needs inside the hashed step the occluder height for the flight altitude, `planRoute` at LaunchHeli and the landing-zone check, and M12b needs per-tick SAM visibility.
- Option 1: an optional `maxElev` in `SimWorldInput` / `SimWorld` (NOT part of `mapHash`, so every golden stays) plus a lazily built, never hashed `Los` / `Nav` on `GameState`, the pattern of the derived caches in `structureEffects` (`effCityCount`, `districtCounts`): rebuilt on demand from the state, dropped by `deserialize`, invisible to `hashState`.
- Option 2: a `SimEnv { los, nav }` that the host passes into `step`.
- **Chosen: option 1.** Reasons: `step` keeps its two-argument signature, so `MainThreadHost`, the worker protocol, `serveHost`, `MatchSession`, `createMatch` and every fixture stay as they are; a deserialized match needs no hook (the first query rebuilds, exactly as `Los.sync` already does); replay and save/load cannot disagree about an environment the host forgot to pass; the derived-cache pattern is already reviewed twice. Option 2 touches all of those call sites at once and makes "a host without an env" a state the sim must handle. Cost of option 1: the sim package holds one more lazy object per match, and `maxElev` must be loaded where `SimWorld` is built (`createWorldLoader.loadSim` already reads it). Because `Los.sync` is memoised per (table, tick, counters) now (item 2), the 480 queries per tick of M12b cost one scan. M12a must keep all of it out of `hashState` and `serialize`, and must call `Los.invalidate()` only if it edits a structure table by hand inside one tick (the sim itself never does).
## Fix-3B (audit 2: visible faults of the render, app and UI)

1. **Modal keys (feel[0], fit[1]).** `keysBlockedByModal()` in `src/app/globeApp.ts` (a modal is open, or the match is over) ends the match-key listener early and is the new fifth argument of `attachDomKeys` (`modalOpen`): only Escape is handed to the stack. A focused dialog button keeps its own Space and Enter: that is the menu's key, not a match key (the e2e takes the focus away before pressing). Mutants: listener guard removed -> `modalKeys` pause and result cases fail; `attachDomKeys` argument removed -> the result-screen case fails (digit 1 starts placing).
2. **Structure priority (feel[1], fit[0]).** `StructureLayer.rebuild` first collects the candidates in id order. If everything fits (every type under its cap, the sum under the triangle allowance) nothing changes (same order, same buffers, goldens untouched). Otherwise the candidates are ranked: the local owner (`setLocalOwner`, set at match start) first, then, only when the camera is lower than `STRUCT_RANK_NEAR_ER` (0.5 Earth radii: higher up a model is under the icon threshold anyway), by squared distance to the camera, then by id. The models are given out in that order under the same cap and allowance; every candidate also gets a far icon (the icon cap is the sum of the type caps, at most 4096), so nothing vanishes. `dropped` now means "left without a model". The camera is not an input of the rebuild: the ranking uses the camera of the last `frame`; a ranked layer re-ranks when the camera has moved more than a quarter of its altitude (`STRUCT_RERANK_MOVE_FRAC`), never per frame (a unit test counts the rebuilds of a still camera: 0). One toast `toast.structuresSimplified` per match the first time `dropped > 0`. Measured with a unit scratch run of the layer (camera 1.05 Earth radii from the centre, 7 meshes visible = 6 model types + icons; `renderer.info` counts the same visible meshes): 0 structures 0 calls / 0 triangles before and after; 40 structures 7 calls, 6 566 model + 80 icon triangles before and after; 150 structures, test tier: 7 calls before and after, models 14 896 -> 14 946 (both under the 15 000 allowance), icon triangles 182 -> 300 (+118, the dropped ones); low tier 150: 7 calls, 24 600 + 300 before and after (nothing dropped). No allowance number was touched. The old assertions of `tests/render/structureBudget.test.ts` ("the cut is by id") still hold because that scene has no local owner and a far camera.
3. **Open city (feel[2]).** Checked on HEAD (Look-2): at level 9 with five districts, 960x540 / 1366x768 / 540x960 had 1 / 1 / 0 of 5 districts in the free part after the flight. `cityFitFlight` (`src/app/cityViewMode.ts`, pure) raises the end altitude (`CITY_VIEW.flyAltM` is now the floor, `flyMaxAltM` 14 000 the cap) until the City plus a ring of `ringExtentM` 2 400 m fits `fitMargin` 0.85 of the free part (the window minus the measured panel box, under the 64 px status bar), and shifts the look-at point (heading 0, so east is right) so that the City stands in the middle of the free part. After the change 5 / 5 / 5 are inside (portrait: the lowest district at y 308 of a sheet starting at 318; the vertical factor 1.4 is the foreshortening of the near side). The flight time is unchanged (fast profile, at most 1.8 s). When the camera already stands within `flyNearM` and the City is on screen no flight starts (unchanged: the player chose that view).
4. **Escape cancels the flight (feel[3]).** `cityFlightOn` marks the open-city flight; closing the view (Escape or any `closed` callback) cancels it when it still runs, a drag or the wheel clear the mark. Mutant: removed cancel -> `cityOpenFit` Escape case fails.
5. **Layout (feel[6]).** `.setup-head` wraps and the title does not (`white-space: nowrap`, buttons wrap under it); in narrow or portrait windows the spectator bar sits at `MATCH_PANELS.spectatorTopNarrowEm` (below the 5-row leaderboard). Mutant: old CSS -> three of six `fixLayout` cases fail (header at 960x540 and 1366x640, spectator at 540x960). Screens looked at: setup 960x540, spectator 540x960.
6. **Far icons (feel[4]).** `iconHalfPx` (`src/render/iconScale.ts`): half size = clamp(a third of the on-screen spacing of two structures, `ICON_HALF_PX_MIN` 3.5 px, `ICON_HALF_PX` 5 px); the spacing is the smallest `SPACING_CELLS_BY_TYPE` (3 cells) times the cell height times 0.65 (typical latitudes), about 76 km; the vertex shader repeats the clamp (a test pins the source text). No new draw call, no new geometry. Looked at: 14 cities 3 cells apart at 3000 km (full-size separate icons), 8000 km (about 3 px half size, separate; the first try with a 1.5 px floor shrank a lone City at 14000 km below what `e2e/structuresSelect.test.ts` needs to see, 12 changed pixels against more than 25, so the floor went 1.5 -> 2.5 (16 pixels, still short) -> 3.5; a per-icon nearest-neighbour spacing would let a lone structure keep 5 px, see BACKLOG), 15000 km (3.5 px half size, 7 px dots instead of 10 px discs, neighbours partly overlap: a territory of 197 cells is about 17 px wide there, so single icons cannot be told apart whatever their size; they stay dots instead of a blob). Mutant: share 1/3 -> 1 fails two `iconScale` tests.
7. **Draw-call ceiling (mutation[13]).** The city scene (terrain, shell, stars, City model, ring, icons, districts) measures 7 draw calls in all three window sizes; `e2e/cityOpenFit.test.ts` asserts <= 10. `tests/budgets.test.ts` asserts that the per-layer draw-call rows add up to at most 16 (they add up to 14 now) and none exceeds 10, so the ceiling of 100 in row 0 is no longer the only guard. A layer that adds draw calls has to touch that number on purpose.
8. **Mutants (mutation[11]).** Mutant -> test that kills it: `modelRadiusM` without `closeMul` -> `lookFormulas` (30 km); ring lift sign `lat - ...` -> `lookFormulas` (2 tests); ring lift without cos(lat) -> `lookFormulas` (2 tests); pole clamp removed -> `lookFormulas`; cos floor 0.05 -> 0.0001 -> `lookFormulas`; `otherCityStyle` always 'new' -> `lookFormulas`; shift-click gating without `wantsShiftClick` -> `inputShift`; flight `durationS` not winning over `fast` -> `flyToFastDuration`; city fit east sign -> `cityFit`. Two mutants cannot be killed and are equivalent: dropping `!e.ctrlKey` and dropping `e.button === 0` from the shift-click expression change nothing, because the very next condition already sends Ctrl and the right and middle buttons to the turn branch first (the guards are belt and braces). The "closeMul fall-off start", `districtRadiusM`, the district cut distance and the `cityBand` clamp items of the finding were not pinned in this slice (see BACKLOG Fix-3B).
9. **`__test.give` (feel[7]).** The sim ignores a gift above `DEBUG_GIVE_MAX` (1 000 000) silently; the test hook now throws with that reason, documented in the `TestHooks` comment and in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
10. **Meaningless or answered after Look-2 / Sim-Levels2.** None of the nine findings was void; feel[2] needed the check on HEAD first (done, see 3). Goldens: none changed (see the final report for the e2e files that were run).

11. **One existing assertion changed, because the change causes it.** `e2e/cityView.test.ts` asserted that after "Oppna stad" from 2600 km the camera stands below 2600 m (the old 1500 m end altitude with slack). With the fit the flight ends at about 3.6 km at 960x540 (and higher in a portrait window), so the bound is now 5000 m (the test still proves a flight happened: it starts above 5000 m); nothing else of that test moved.
12. **A second existing assertion changed for the same reason.** `e2e/lookCities.test.ts` asserted an end altitude of 1500 m +- 150 and the City at the screen centre after the fast open-city flight; both are what Fix-3B changes on purpose. It now asserts at least 1450 m and below 5000 m, the City left of the panel and within 80 px of the middle of the free part (the flight is still at most 1.8 s and still ends in the same number of frames).
## Fix-3C: docs that lied, test holes in app and UI, hygiene (audit 2, lenses fit and mutation; headless)

1. **Each finding checked against HEAD 04eaba8 first.** fit[2] (PROGRESS), fit[7] (ARCHITECTURE), fit[8] (BACKLOG, M18-2 point 7) were all still true and are corrected: Done rows for every slice since M10-4, finished passes moved to a section "Archive" at the end of `docs/PROGRESS.md` (text kept, two stale boxes ticked with a note), Known issues M11a now says "accepted by the owner" (M11b point 10), Next points at M12a and M20a-lite and at audit 3 after five pushes. mutation[6] was true (no test on the Play win path). mutation[2] app part and mutation[10] were true. fit[9] true. fit[10] true (313 158 B gz total, main chunk 263 782 B at Fix-3C; the 311.9 kB of the audit was measured with Vite's own gzip).
2. **`STRUCT_LEVEL_SCALE` is left in `src/render/config.ts` (marked unused in ARCHITECTURE).** Removing it means editing `tests/render/structureModels.test.ts`, and the render side belongs to another builder. BACKLOG has the line.
3. **The Play win choice moved to `winForPlay(test)` in `src/app/match.ts`** (the globeApp.ts change is one call plus two import edits). `MatchOptions.win` became `WinConfig | undefined` so the call can pass `undefined` in test mode. Behaviour is unchanged: the plain Play match gets `defaultWinConfig('world')` outside test mode (commit 2dd505a), test mode none.
4. **`cityTotals` caps the Barracks sum at `BARRACKS_REGEN_PCT_MAX`** (the owner-wide cap the sim applies in `src/sim/structureEffects.ts`). A single panel therefore shows at most the cap; the true owner sum over several Cities is also capped, so the panel never over-promises. Chosen over a label change because the number is what the player compares with the sim.
5. **Bundle guard** in `tests/portable.test.ts` (it reuses the build the file already makes): total gzipped JS at most 329 000 B (measured 313 158 B, +5 %) and the largest chunk at most 277 000 B (measured 263 782 B, +5 %). The old acceptance "<= 300 KB gz" has been missed since the city view and the match flow; the caps pin the present size so growth fails a test. Raise them only with a line here. Lazy loading of the city view and the match panels is a BACKLOG proposal.
6. **`PLAYER_FACING` in `tests/i18n.test.ts`** got the 14 files of the finding; the scan found no Swedish literal. The district icon literal `#0d1830` is `UI_COLORS.field` now (same value, no visible change).
7. **Mutants** (each applied by hand to the one line, the named test file run, the line restored). `tests/ui/fix3c.test.ts` (UI) and `tests/app/winPath.test.ts` (app):

## Fix-3 integration (orchestrator, after merging Fix-3A1, Fix-3A2, Fix-3B and Fix-3C)

The full `npm run verify` of the merge found two e2e expectations that the merged slices made stale; both were corrected, none weakened:
1. `e2e/viewshed.test.ts`: `NODE_FINGERPRINT` 4680ed7e -> abb0fadd. Fix-3A2 made the A* heuristic admissible, which changes real routes (DECISIONS Fix-3A2 point 3); Chromium returned abb0fadd, equal to the new `PINNED_HASH` in `tests/sim/nav.test.ts`, so Node and Chromium still agree.
2. `e2e/rts.test.ts` ("the capital bookmark and F ..."): the test pressed Escape with an empty stack, then F. Since M18-2 that Escape opens the pause menu, and since Fix-3B (audit 2, feel[0]) the match keys are dead behind it, so F correctly did nothing. The test now presses Escape a second time (closes the menu) before F; every assertion is unchanged.
## M12a-1: helicopters in the sim, part 1 of 3 (the access to Los / Nav from `step`, the tables, ProduceHeli, the fleet cap)

Everything below that the plan did not fix is PROVISIONAL (balance in M19). Part 1 has no visible change: the economy gets a new sink (200 gold per helicopter) and a new cap, and nothing flies yet.

1. **Los / Nav inside `step` (option 1 of "M12 preparation", built as decided).** `SimWorldInput.maxElev` / `SimWorld.maxElev` is optional and a copy; it is NOT in `mapHash` (a world with and without it has the same header hash and the same state hash; `tests/sim/stateAccess.test.ts`). `GameState.env` (`SimEnv`, `src/sim/env.ts`, no runtime imports so `src/sim/state.ts` can hold it) keeps `maxElev` and the lazily built `Los` / `Nav` plus one reused `NavView` of the state. `src/sim/access.ts` builds them at the first question from the state's own `elevation`, `rowCos`, `terrain` and the layer; `exposureOf` / `planRouteOf` / `scoreLzOf` are the public queries asked of a state (a test shows a state answers exactly like a Los / Nav built by hand from the same arrays, and that a step function asking before every step runs the same match: one `Los.sync` scan per tick however many questions). Nothing of it is hashed or serialised; `deserialize(bytes, { maxElev })` builds a fresh environment (without the option the first question throws `MissingOccluderError`, a wrong-length layer is a `header` error), and `MainThreadHost.resume` takes `MainThreadHostOptions.maxElev` for the same reason. A world without the layer plays on and only fails at the first Los / Nav question, with a message that names `maxElev`. `loadSim` already returned the layer; `tools/sim/realWorld.ts` and the test worlds (`tests/helpers/synthWorld.ts`, `tests/helpers/miniWorld.ts`) now pass it. **Not done here (the other track owns `src/app`):** the two explicit field lists in `src/app/globeApp.ts` (the `sim:` objects at the `createWorldLoader` call sites) still omit `maxElev`; part 2 must add `maxElev: world.sim.maxElev` there, or a live match throws at its first LaunchHeli.
2. **The tables are designed for all of M12a (`src/sim/heliTable.ts`).** Helicopter table: 64 slots, owner 0 = free, free list = `highWater` + the `recycled` stack (a removed id is reused LIFO, hashed and serialised like the structure table). Fields: `owner`, `state` (own names: `HELI_BUILDING` under production, `HELI_PAD`, `HELI_LIFT`, `HELI_CRUISE`, `HELI_APPROACH`, `HELI_HOVER`, `HELI_UNLOAD`, `HELI_RETURN`, `HELI_DOWNED`; part 1 uses the first two), `factory` (the home Factory's structure id), `readyTick` (BUILDING: the tick production ends) and `stateTick` (the tick the state began, for the timed states), `x` / `y` Q8 cells (the centre of cell (cx, cy) is cx * 256 + 128; `cellCentreQ8`), `alt` whole GAME metres (the LOS occluder unit), `hp` tenths, `load` troops, `fuel` Q8 cells (full = `HELI_FUEL_MAX_Q8` = 180 * 256), `mode` (`ALT_MODE_*`), `target` (landing zone cell + 1, 0 = none), `routeLen` / `routeIdx` and a FIXED route buffer of `HELI_ROUTE_CAPACITY` 64 waypoints per helicopter (`route[id * 64 + k]`, the simplest deterministic choice; 16 KB; part 2 refuses a longer route with NO_ROUTE; only `routeLen` cells are hashed and serialised). Beachhead table: 64 slots, `owner`, `seed` cell, `landTick`, `status` (`BEACH_CUT_OFF` 1, `BEACH_ESTABLISHED` 2), a new landing takes the LOWEST free slot (no stack: one scan of 64). Both tables are in the hash (`foldHelis`, after the structures, before the match block) and in a new "air block" of the byte format between the home block and the structure block (so the structure and match blocks keep their positions from the end); `deserialize` range-checks every field (error `air-range`; a queued or parked helicopter needs a live Factory of its owner, a queued one the production time the rules set; the recycled ids must be exactly the unused slots).
3. **ENGINE_VERSION 13 -> 14 and FORMAT_VERSION 11 -> 12, ONCE; both golden hash files re-pinned ONCE.** `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` (every hash moves: the header carries the engine version and the air block folds into every state hash; the scenario script also got a ProduceHeli line, last in the list so earlier picks stay, which makes the 6000-tick golden contain produced, parked, queued and scrapped helicopters and a non-empty recycle stack: seed 1 reaches 2 live and 4 used slots, seed 2 four of each) and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` (the four built matches). The worker test (`tests/sim/worker.test.ts`) reads the same file and passes; the Chromium comparison is the orchestrator's `verify`. Old snapshots are refused as before (`format-version` / `engine-version`). Earlier tests changed because this change provably causes the difference: `tests/sim/codec.test.ts` (the unknown kind byte 12 is now 13: kind 12 is ProduceHeli), `tests/sim/codecAttack.test.ts` (the kind list gets ProduceHeli), `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` and `tests/sim/homeRules.test.ts` (the layout, the golden world size and the offset from the end of the home block grow by the 16 bytes of the empty air block), the version pins in `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts` and `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/helpers/brokenSteps.ts` (the faithful copy of `step` runs `heliPhase`), `tests/sim/lzCodes.test.ts` (the LZ codes now map to their own `toast.lz.*` texts instead of `reject.other`; the disjointness assertions are untouched), the `SimView` mock of `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` (`helis` added), and the pinned export list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`.
4. **ProduceHeli (`src/sim/heliRules.ts`, `heliPhase.ts`).** Intent `ProduceHeli{seat, factory}` (wire kind 12, one varint). The reasons in the order they are checked: `REJECT_PHASE` (18, the start phase of a match with a spawn phase), `REJECT_HELI_NO_FACTORY` 45 (no such id, a free slot or another type), `REJECT_HELI_NOT_OWNER` 46, `REJECT_HELI_FACTORY_BUILDING` 47, `REJECT_HELI_QUEUE_BUSY` 48 (one at a time per Factory), `REJECT_HELI_FLEET_CAP` 49, `REJECT_HELI_NO_GOLD` 50; an id outside 0..4095 is refused by the intent shape as `REJECT_STRUCT_ID`. 200 gold is taken when the order is applied, production takes `HELI_BUILD_TICKS` 120 (12 s), the helicopter becomes `HELI_PAD` in the step that runs AT tick `readyTick` (the same convention as a finished upgrade: visible one tick later), standing at its Factory's cell centre with `HELI_HP_TENTHS`, a full tank and no load. Fleet cap = `HELI_FLEET_BASE` 2 + the sum of the levels of the seat's FINISHED Factories (an unfinished one adds nothing; provisional), clamped to `HELI_FLEET_MAX` 12 (Sim-Levels2 lets Factory levels reach 999); a helicopter under production counts. A seat that loses a Factory can end up over the cap: its helicopters stay, only new orders are refused. `checkProduceHeli` is THE implementation; the sim's `applyProduceHeli` and the public `validateProduceHeli(view, seat, factory)` both call it (`tests/sim/heliProduction.test.ts` runs the public wrapper on a `StateView` against the sim for the phase, owner, factory and gold reasons). `SimView.helis` is a read-only `HeliView` (owner, state, factory, readyTick, position, hp, load, fuel, `countOf(ownerId)`) and `fleetCapOf` / `fleetCountOf` take a view and a SEAT (like `validateBuild`). The page's copy is `ViewSnapshot.helis` (sent in the full view and in a frame's diff only when `heliSignature` changed) read through `MirrorHelis`.
5. **Factory conquered, deleted or destroyed (PROVISIONAL).** The helicopters QUEUED or PARKED (`HELI_BUILDING`, `HELI_PAD`) in that Factory are scrapped at once, a queued production is lost without a refund, `OUTBOX_HELI_SCRAPPED` (a = id, b = state) is pushed for each. The hook is `scrapFactoryHelis`, called from `structureCellChanged` (conquest by Attack, DebugPaint, the enclave flip, cell freed) and from `applyDelete`. Airborne helicopters (part 2) are NOT touched: their `factory` field is only where they left from, part 2 must not trust it (RTB goes to the NEAREST own Factory). The conquered Factory itself goes to the new owner one level lower as before, with an empty queue. Tested: conquest, destruction by a freed cell, Delete, the other Factory's helicopter and an airborne one survive, no refund.
6. **Outbox.** `HELI_QUEUED` 6 (a = id, b = Factory), `HELI_PRODUCED` 7 (the same), `HELI_SCRAPPED` 8 (a = id, b = state) are pushed. Declared for parts 2 and 3, not pushed yet: `HELI_LAUNCHED` 9, `HELI_LANDED` 10, `BEACHHEAD_FORMED` 11, `BEACHHEAD_REVERTED` 12, `HELI_DOWNED` 13 (b = cause, 1 = out of fuel).
7. **Texts.** `rejectKey` -> `structureRefusalKey` -> `airRefusalKey` (`src/ui/buildReasons.ts`) gives the six ProduceHeli codes and the eight LZ reasons 65..72 their own `toast.heli.*` / `toast.lz.*` keys (sv and en in the same commit; `tests/ui/heliReasons.test.ts`, `tests/i18n.test.ts`). No other UI change, no rendering. The AI v0 ignores helicopters (unchanged).
8. **Cost.** `tests/heli.perf.test.ts` (median of 200 steps after 50 warm-ups, 1024x512, 12 players, 300 finished structures, 12 helicopters in the table): the step costs the same with and without helicopters (a few microseconds either way; budget `budgetMs(4)` and at most `budgetMs(0.5)` above the world without), `heliPhase` alone about 1 us (budget `budgetMs(0.05)`), one `checkProduceHeli` (it scans the structure table twice) about 2 us (budget `budgetMs(0.2)`). The phase returns at once when the table is empty.

### M12a-1 hooks for part 2 (flight) and part 3 (landing, beachhead)
- Launch: check with a new `checkLaunchHeli` in `src/sim/heliRules.ts` (same pattern: `HeliSource`-based, wrapped by a public `validateHeliLaunch`), pick the helicopter with `state.helis.state[id] === HELI_PAD && factory[id] === factoryId`, route it with `planRouteOf(state, seat, factoryCell, lzCell, mode)` and rate the LZ with `scoreLzOf(...)` (both already callable from the intent code; the reason of a bad LZ is the `LZ_REASON_BASE + n` code, already mapped to texts), store the waypoints in `helis.route[id * HELI_ROUTE_CAPACITY + k]` with `routeLen` / `routeIdx = 0`, `target = lz + 1`, `mode`, `load` (taken from `state.troops`), `state = HELI_LIFT`, `stateTick = state.tick`, push `OUTBOX_HELI_LAUNCHED`.
- Flight: extend `heliPhase(state)` (already phase 5b of `step`, early-returns on an empty table); positions are `x` / `y` Q8 cells (wrap x with `width * 256`), speed from `AIR_MODE_SPEED_MILLICELLS_S[mode]`, fuel counts down in `fuel` (Q8 cells), altitude in `alt` (game metres; the Los occluder height is `losOf(state).terrain.occGm[cell]`), the timed states use `stateTick`. Out of fuel: `HELI_DOWNED` and `OUTBOX_HELI_DOWNED`.
- Recall: a new `RecallHeli` intent needs a wire kind 13 (append to `INTENT_KINDS`, `src/sim/codec.ts`, `validateIntent`, `comparePayload`, `copyBody`, `withId`, `applyIntent`) and a new reject code above 50 and below 64 (51..63 are free; LZ reasons start at 65) with a text in `src/ui/buildReasons.ts` `airRefusalKey`. Adding a kind changes no table layout; behaviour that changes the hash of a golden scenario needs the usual one-time golden update.
- Landing: `state.beachheads.add(ownerId, seedCell, state.tick, BEACH_CUT_OFF)`, `OUTBOX_HELI_LANDED`, `OUTBOX_BEACHHEAD_FORMED` / `REVERTED`; `serialize` / `hashState` / `deserialize` already carry the beachhead table, so part 3 adds no field. A cut-off cluster's cells must not count in the troop cap, regeneration and gold land: those are `src/sim/economy.ts` / `src/sim/structureEffects.ts` reads of `state.agg` and the owner array.
- Anything new that needs fields must still fit the tables above; if one does not, that is a second ENGINE / FORMAT bump and a second golden update, to be avoided.

### M12a-1 mutant table (one changed line each; every row was seen RED before the test was kept, the file restored afterwards)

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|
| the fleet count leaves out helicopters under production | heliProduction: REJECT_HELI_FLEET_CAP ... counts |
| the queue check removed | heliProduction: REJECT_HELI_QUEUE_BUSY |
| production ends one tick early (`tick + 1 < readyTick`) | heliProduction: price and time are tick-exact |
| the price is not taken | heliProduction: REJECT_HELI_NO_GOLD (exact price empties the purse) and the price test |
| the spawn-phase gate dropped | heliProduction: REJECT_PHASE |
| the fleet cap not clamped to 12 | heliProduction: clamped to HELI_FLEET_MAX |
| an unfinished Factory adds to the cap | heliProduction: clamped ... and an unfinished Factory adds nothing |
| conquest does not scrap the helicopters of the Factory | heliProduction: the golden scenario exercises ... scrapping, and the conquest test |
| Delete does not scrap them | heliProduction: through the sim: a scrapped helicopter's id goes to the next order |
| the free list ignores the recycle stack | heliProduction: a removed id is reused first (LIFO) |
| the route buffer is not cleared when a helicopter is removed | heliProduction: through the sim ... the route buffer of the old one is cleared |
| the air tables left out of `hashState` | heliProduction: every field of both tables is in the hash |
| route cells / beachhead status left out of the fold | heliProduction: every field of both tables is in the hash |
| `deserialize` does not check the Factory of a queued or parked helicopter | heliProduction: deserialize refuses a value no rule could have produced |
| `maxElev` leaks into the map hash | stateAccess: the map hash does not depend on it |
| `losOf` builds the occluder layer from the mean elevation | stateAccess: the ridge matters (a ridge whose mean is 20 m and max 5200 m) |
| `deserialize` forgets the layer handed in | stateAccess: a deserialized state answers the same questions |
| no error without `maxElev` | stateAccess: a world without maxElev plays on, and the first question throws |
| a new `NavView` per question | stateAccess: building them changes neither the hash nor the bytes (one reused window) |

Two rows were first NOT killed and were fixed in the tests before this table was written: the ridge of the access test had a mean high enough to occlude without the layer (now mean 20, max 5200), and nothing pinned the reuse of the view (now `navViewOf(s)` is asserted identical twice).
## M20a-lite: hotkey registry, help overlay, command resolver, top bar (UI and app only; `src/sim` untouched)

1. **One keyboard listener.** `src/app/hotkeys.ts` holds a `HotkeyRegistry` (entry: `id`, `keys`, `labelKey`, `scope`, `run`). Three scopes: `global` (F2, F3: work on every screen and in a text field), `match` (space, `,`, `.`, `[`, `]`, `-`, `+`, `?`: dead behind a modal, in a text field and with ctrl / meta; AltGr passes), `stack` (the RESERVED keys 1 to 6, E, H, F, Escape: handed to `InputStack.key`; only Escape passes behind a modal, Fix-3B point 1). `register` throws when a non-`stack` entry takes a reserved key, when a `stack` entry takes anything else, and on a duplicate id or key, so no other code can bind the reserved keys (the CLAUDE.md rule is now enforced). `attachHotkeys` is the one `keydown` listener of the game (once per page, from `src/app/main.ts`); the match scene registers its entries with an AbortSignal, `registerPageHotkeys` registers F2 for every scene. E and H are `hidden` entries (reserved for M13, not listed in the help). `matchKeyAction` and `attachDomKeys` stay (their unit tests are unchanged) but the page no longer calls them.
2. **Documented exceptions to "no keydown elsewhere"** (`tests/app/hotkeyGrep.test.ts`, which also fails when an exception loses its listener): `src/app/devpanel.ts` (Escape closes the open panel, Enter in its fields; F2 itself is a registry entry that asks the panel through `DEV_PANEL_REQUEST_EVENT`), `src/app/debugViewshed.ts` (arrow keys of a debug map), `src/app/setupScreen.ts` (Enter in the search field, Escape back to the menu: the setup owns the keyboard before a match), `src/ui/matchPanels.ts` (the Tab trap of a dialog, on the dialog's own element), `src/app/main.ts` (the M00 spike scene's F3 box). The prompt named only the debug pages; the setup screen, the dialog trap and the spike were listed in the M20a note of ARCHITECTURE already.
3. **Help overlay.** `src/ui/helpOverlay.ts` renders the non-hidden entries (`hotkeys.listed()`), so the list cannot drift (`e2e/m20aLite.test.ts` compares the DOM rows with `__test.hotkeys()` both ways). Opening it pushes a `help` input mode on the stack: Escape pops it (no pause menu), a click or right click closes it, the reserved keys are swallowed behind it. `?` is a `match` entry, so a modal keeps it out. It is a small card (24 em wide), opaque, centred; looked at in 960x540 and 540x960, both languages.
4. **Command resolver** (`src/app/commandResolver.ts`): a table of rules `{id, priority, triggers, applies, resolve}`; the first rule by priority that applies decides. Rules today: `build-own` (10, primary, a structure type is being placed), `deselect-other` (20, primary and context, a structure is selected: the selected structure's own cell keeps it, another own structure reselects on a left click, anything else deselects), `attack` (100: land that is not yours attacks its owner, own land does nothing, sea / peak / ice and "not spawned" are `invalid`, the spawn phase does nothing). Hooks for later: M13b `registerCommandRule({id: 'launch-heli', priority: 15, triggers: ['context'], applies: Factory selected and land target, resolve: ...})`, M15b the same for a Silo with a target mode; they need no change in the resolver or the selection mode (only a `case` for the new command kind in `runContext` of `globeApp.ts`). **Two deviations from the prompt text.** (a) The placement mode and the selection mode keep handling their own LEFT clicks (D157 unchanged), so `build-own` and the primary half of `deselect-other` are modelled and unit-tested but only the `context` trigger is wired live (`runContext` in `globeApp.ts`). (b) Before this slice a right click with an EMPTY stack did nothing (`setContextHandler` was a no-op) and with a structure selected nothing took it; now an empty-stack right click is an attack on land (the same `sess.attack` as the left click) and with a structure selected it deselects. The prompt said "idag: avmarkerar" for the latter; the code did not.
5. **Top bar.** The old `status` panel (testid `status` and every id inside it kept) moves from bottom left to the top left and gains a win row: the leader's share against the land condition NOW (`MatchView.requiredPct`, overtime curve included, `leaderSeat`, `holdSeat`, `holdTicksLeft`; `src/ui/winMeter.ts`, pure, tested against a real packed MatchView). The leader's share is the Q15 `landShareQ15` the leaderboard shows (the cos-weighted share of all land; the sim's land condition compares weighted area over total weight, the same measure, not re-derived here). The row is hidden when the match has no land condition or has ended. Texts put the figures first (`{pct} av {need} · {name}`, `{secs} s kvar ({name})`) so an ellipsis cuts the nation name, not the numbers. The dock now only holds the controls; the selection panel sits at the bottom left (`bottom 0.6em`, narrow `11em`) because nothing is under it any more; the banner, the paused badge, the leaderboard and the spectator bar sit below the bar (`TOPBAR` in `src/ui/uiConfig.ts`: 5.4 em wide, 8.6 em narrow; the wide bar measures about 4.2 em). The spawn banner became one row (text and countdown side by side, as it already was on a narrow window) and the leaderboard moved to the LEFT on a narrow window: older e2e tests (goldFit, cityView and others) click the spawn place at a fixed canvas point, (508, 205) at 960x540, and the new positions would have covered it. The label of the win row has the class `hud-win-label`, not `hud-label`: `e2e/hud.test.ts` lists the `.hud-label` texts of the status panel in document order and must not change.
6. **10 Hz write gate** (`src/ui/hudGate.ts`, `HUD_MIN_INTERVAL_S` 0.1): `MatchHud.update` returns early unless 0.1 fx seconds passed since the last write, or the speed, the commitment or the language changed (a click must not wait), or the clock went backwards. Ticks do NOT open the gate (at 4x the sim ticks 40 times a second). Test mode turns the gate off (`throttle: false`): its HUD numbers are the sim's and the e2e tests read them right after `advance`. The "0 mutations at rest" test (`e2e/m20aLite.test.ts`) therefore rests on the per-value caches (`TextCell`), which already skipped unchanged values; the gate itself is unit-tested (`tests/ui/topBar.test.ts`: 10 s at 60 fps gives 80 to 101 writes).
7. **Test additions that touch older files.** `tests/i18n.test.ts`: the prefix `hotkey` joins `KEY_LITERAL` (otherwise the dead-key check cannot see the new keys; this only widens the check). `GlobeTestHooks`: `hotkeys()`, `helpOpen()`, and `matchInfo()` gained `winFlags`, `leaderSeat`, `requiredPct`, `holdSeat`, `holdTicksLeft`. New test-mode param `win=1` (the default land condition in test mode, so the win meter can be tested; `winForPlay` is unchanged).
8. **Goldens.** No golden PNG changed: the globe goldens are taken with the HUD hidden; the only shots with the HUD (`hud=1`) are looked at, not compared.
9. **Not done (BACKLOG "M20a-2")**: pooled nation labels, front arrows (need the host snapshot's attacks), minimap, alerts, tactical view, touch (M20b).
10. **Known failure on the base, not caused by this slice:** `e2e/rts.test.ts` "the capital bookmark and F need a spawned capital ..." fails on commit 51e30b3 itself (run in an unmodified copy of the base): Escape with an empty stack opens the pause menu since M18 and Fix-3B makes F dead behind it, the test still expects F to fly. The test is left as it is (it must not be weakened); it needs the orchestrator's decision (press Escape twice, or drop the Escape step).

## M20a-lite integration (orchestrator)

The full `npm run verify` of the merge of M12a-1 and M20a-lite found one stale e2e expectation that was a real layout fault: `e2e/matchEnd.test.ts` ("the pause menu does not cover the spawn banner") failed because M20a-lite moved the spawn banner below the top bar (top `TOPBAR.wideEm + 0.4` em, about 2.9 em high) while the pause menu card still started at the old 6.5 em and covered the countdown. The fix is in the code, the test is unchanged: `MATCH_PANELS.pauseTopEm` is now `TOPBAR.wideEm + 3.8` and the new `pauseTopNarrowEm` (`TOPBAR.narrowEm + 3.8`) applies in the narrow / portrait media block of `src/ui/hudStyle.ts`.
## M12a-2: helicopters in the sim, part 2 of 3 (LaunchHeli, the flight, fuel, RecallHeli, the return)

Everything below that the plan did not fix is PROVISIONAL (balance in M19). Part 2 has no visible change; it gives the helicopters a flight and gives the player's troops a new place to be (in a helicopter) for a while. Landing and beachhead are part 3: the landing here is a placeholder.

1. **Intents and reasons.** `LaunchHeli{seat, factory, lzCell, troops, altMode}` is wire kind 13, `RecallHeli{seat, heli}` kind 14 (append only). The launch names the FACTORY (the lowest-id helicopter in `HELI_PAD` at it flies; one Factory is what the player selects), not a helicopter. New codes 51 `REJECT_HELI_NO_PAD`, 52 `REJECT_HELI_BAD_TROOPS`, 53 `REJECT_HELI_NO_ROUTE`, 54 `REJECT_HELI_NOT_FLYING`; the other reasons are reused: 18 (`REJECT_PHASE`, the start phase), 45 (not a Factory), 46 (not the seat's Factory or helicopter). **A bad landing zone is refused with the `scoreLz` reason itself (65..72)**, which is what the prompt's "LZ_INVALID + reason" comes to; there is no separate 55 code (51..63 stay mostly free). Order: phase, Factory, owner, pad, troops (1 to `HELI_MAX_TROOPS` and at most the seat's whole troops), zone, route (`REJECT_HELI_NO_ROUTE`: none, shorter than two waypoints, longer than `HELI_ROUTE_CAPACITY`, or a world without the occluder layer). RecallHeli: an unknown id or a helicopter that is not lifting, cruising, approaching or hovering is NOT_FLYING (a helicopter already returning is too: a second recall changes nothing), another seat's is NOT_OWNER. The shape is checked by `validateIntent` first: troops must be an integer 0..2^32-1 (`REJECT_AMOUNT`), `altMode` 0 or 1 (`REJECT_SHAPE`), `lzCell` in the grid (`REJECT_CELL`), `heli` 0..63 (`REJECT_ID`). **One implementation:** `checkLaunchHeli` / `checkRecallHeli` in `src/sim/heliRules.ts`; the sim's `applyLaunchHeli` / `applyRecallHeli` and the public `validateLaunchHeli(view, nav, ...)` / `validateRecallHeli(view, ...)` call them (the zone and the route come through a `LaunchProbe`, so the UI answers from the Nav it built on its snapshot and the sim from its own). A world without `maxElev` REFUSES a launch (NO_ROUTE) instead of throwing `MissingOccluderError` inside `step`. `tests/sim/heliFlight.test.ts` runs the public wrapper against the sim for seven cases. Exports pinned in `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`.
2. **The flight (`src/sim/heliFlight.ts`).** `LIFT` for `HELI_TAKEOFF_TICKS` 20 (the launch tick and the 19 after it; the altitude climbs linearly to the floor of the cell; `CRUISE` begins in the step at launch tick + 20), `CRUISE`, `APPROACH` (the last leg, within `HELI_APPROACH_CELLS` 5 of the zone), `HOVER` for `HELI_HOVER_TICKS` 30 (the placeholder runs in the step at hover start + 30), `RETURN`, `PAD`. Positions are Q8 cells; per tick the helicopter moves `round(speed * 256 / (1000 * TICK_HZ))` Q8 (Low 54, High 77, i.e. +0.45 % and +0.26 % over the exact speed; the carry of a step over a corner goes on to the next leg), the direction of a leg is `isqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy)` with the components truncated toward zero, so no tick moves more than the step, x wraps over the date line (the shortest way, `tests/sim/heliFlight.test.ts` crosses the seam). Fuel: the Q8 distance flown; lifting and hovering cost none; at 0 the helicopter is `HELI_DOWNED` (`OUTBOX_HELI_DOWNED`, b = 1), stays 50 ticks, is removed, its load is LOST. `ENGINE_VERSION` 14 -> 15 ONCE; `FORMAT_VERSION` unchanged (the tables of part 1 were enough: the airborne states and routes are already serialised; `deserialize` accepts them).
3. **Altitude and clearance.** `HELI_CLEARANCE_GAME_M` = 100 game metres. alt = max(`groundGm` + AGL, `occGm` + 100) of the cell under the helicopter (the two arrays of `losOf(state).terrain`, so it is exactly `EXAG * mean + AGL` against `occluder + clearance`). The altitude goes toward the highest floor of the cell and the next `HELI_LOOKAHEAD_CELLS` 4 cells along the heading, by at most `HELI_VERT_GM_PER_CELL` 4000 game metres per cell flown (one limit for up and down), and is never below the floor of its own cell ("the floor wins"). Measured over the Alps (the 34 Alps flights of the 102 of the 100-flight test, 17 Low and 17 High, in the box 43.5-47.5 N, 5.5-15.5 E): at the highest ground the route crossed, alt is 328 to 5552 game metres above `EXAG * mean + 300` (mean 2711, Low mean 2497); the largest lift anywhere on an Alps flight is 20724 m (Low) and 16336 m (High); the highest altitude of any flight is 39208 game metres (the `air-range` check of `deserialize` allows 65535). The rule is therefore far from the old `mean + 300`: over a ridge the occluder is 8 * (mean + 0.6 * (max - mean)), so the helicopter is where a SAM's mask says the ridge is, not above the average ground. Descents are rate-limited, so a helicopter stays high for a few cells after a ridge (the cost is nothing; M12b's visibility reads this `alt`).
4. **Routes.** The route of a launch is the `planRoute` result of the check, stored in the helicopter's buffer (`routeIdx` starts at 1; waypoint 0 is the Factory). The way home is planned when it begins (`planRouteOf` from the cell under the helicopter to the nearest own FINISHED Factory, in the flight mode of the mission). PROVISIONAL: if that cannot be planned (the cell is closed to the nav grid) the helicopter flies the straight line; the floor clears the ground but the line may cross a Peak cell (BACKLOG). The nearest Factory is by squared Q8 distance, ties by the lowest structure id.
5. **RecallHeli and the lost Factory (PROVISIONAL, simple rule as the plan asks).** Recall turns a lifting, cruising, approaching or hovering helicopter for the nearest own Factory at once; the load stays aboard and goes back to the seat when the helicopter sets down on a pad (`HELI_HOME`, the helicopter's `factory` field becomes that Factory, fuel full). A Factory lost while a helicopter is airborne changes nothing for it: the mission is flown, then the way home goes to the nearest REMAINING own Factory; with none it is scrapped at the moment it turns for home (`OUTBOX_HELI_SCRAPPED`, b = state) and its load goes back to the seat ("scrapped on arrival" of the plan is read as "when there is nowhere to go"). A helicopter whose target Factory is lost on the way home looks for the nearest remaining one at arrival, else is scrapped. Tested: two Factories (the nearest wins, not the home one nor the lowest id), no Factory (scrapped, troops back), the home Factory removed in the air, a Factory conquered in place under a lifting helicopter.
6. **Troops (no troops created or lost).** LaunchHeli takes `troops` * 4 quarter-troops from the seat at once; they ride in `load`; they go back at `setDown` (a pad) or `scrap`; a downed helicopter loses them. `tests/sim/heliFlight.test.ts` runs a whole mission, a recall and the two scrap cases with the economy off (`heliPhase` + the tick counter only) and asserts the seat's troops plus the helicopter's load are the same figure on EVERY tick, and exactly the starting figure at the end. Troops returned to a seat above its cap decay like any surplus (no special case).
7. **THE LANDING PLACEHOLDER and the hooks for part 3 and M13.**
   - Where: `landingPlaceholder(state, id)` in `src/sim/heliFlight.ts`, called from exactly one place, `flyHelis`, in the step in which the hover ends. Today it pushes `OUTBOX_HELI_LANDED` (a = id, b = the troops it carries), and calls `startReturn(state, id, 0)`. The load is still aboard.
   - Part 3 replaces the BODY: set `HELI_UNLOAD` (the state exists, `stateTick` = now; 10 ticks, so `flyHelis` needs an `s === HELI_UNLOAD` branch before `HELI_HOVER`'s neighbour), conquer the zone `t.target[id] - 1` and its neighbours with `t.load[id]` troops (the M04 cell price, x2 for an enemy cell), form the beachhead with `state.beachheads.add(ownerId, zoneCell, state.tick, BEACH_CUT_OFF)` + `OUTBOX_BEACHHEAD_FORMED`, set `load` to 0 (and put a failed landing's load on the floor), then `startReturn(state, id, 0)`. `HELI_LANDED`'s b may then change meaning (cell instead of troops): `src/sim/outbox.ts` documents it.
   - Functions part 3 uses: `scoreLzOf`, `losOf`, `planRouteOf` (`src/sim/access.ts`), `startReturn` / `scrap` / `setDown` (module-private today: export them if a second file needs them), `state.beachheads`, `src/sim/conquest.ts` for the cell price. Fields: `helis.target` (zone cell + 1), `load`, `stateTick`, `mode`.
   - M13 reads: `SimView.helis` (`state` HELI_*, `x` / `y` Q8 cells (divide by `HELI_POS_ONE`), `alt` game metres, `load`, `fuel` Q8 cells, `hp` tenths), the events `HELI_LAUNCHED` 9, `HELI_LANDED` 10, `HELI_DOWNED` 13, `HELI_RTB` 14, `HELI_HOME` 15, and `validateLaunchHeli` / `validateRecallHeli` for the zone picker and the buttons. `maxElev` is already in the two `sim:` lists of `src/app/globeApp.ts` (done in the M20a-lite merge), so a live match can launch; a state restored WITHOUT the layer refuses every launch as NO_ROUTE (it does not throw).
8. **Measured flight times** (real 1024x512 world, `tests/helpers/heliFlights.ts`: 3 regions x 2 modes, routes 31 to 83 cells, an enemy SAM site in each box): the outbound flight from launch to hover takes on average 26.2 s Low and 21.0 s High (the 100-flight run, 102 flights, route mean 54.2 cells; the 40-flight run 30.0 s and 22.4 s, route mean 60.4); the speed measured over cruise is 98.8 % to 100.4 % of the table value; the largest step of any tick is 1.018 x the speed (limit 1.05); max distance from the start is at least 100 % of the straight distance to the zone but only 43 % of the polyline (routes around mountains and SAM sites are up to 2.3 times the straight line, so the plan's "at least 80 % of the route length" cannot hold for a winding route and is tested against the straight line: DEVIATION); all 102 came home in the 100-flight run and 41 of 42 in the 40-flight run (one 83-cell route ran dry on the way back, which the plan allows: 95 % are required). A 60-cell raid takes 28.6 s + 2 s take-off Low and 20 s + 2 s High one way (asserted at least 15 s); 60-cell routes in the tests took 150 ticks and more.
9. **Cost.** `tests/heliFlight.perf.test.ts` (median of 200 after 50 warm-ups, 1024x512, 12 players, 300 structures, 12 helicopters cruising): the flight phase 49 us on a loaded machine and 25.8 us on a quiet rerun (budget `budgetMs(0.5)` = 1280 us at the measured calibration factor 2.56), a whole step 43.6 us against 17.7 us without them (extra budget `budgetMs(0.5)`). **`SimView.helis` while all 12 fly:** the signature changes in every step (250 of 250), the page transfer (signature pass + 10 `slice` copies of at most 12 entries each) costs 8.6 us per frame (budget `budgetMs(0.1)`), far below anything the perf report shows, so the table is NOT changed to a cheaper transfer (a changed-rows or tick-counter scheme would add state for no visible gain).
10. **Goldens (recomputed ONCE).** `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json`: every hash moves because the header carries `ENGINE_VERSION`. The golden scenario world now carries `maxElev` (= the mean elevation; not part of the map hash, so `mapHash` and the world hash are unchanged) and the script got a LaunchHeli (about one turn in 2) and a RecallHeli (one in 5) line, last in the list (picks above stay); nearly all are refused, and exactly one launch is accepted in the two pinned seeds (seed 1, tick 5998), so the 6000-tick hash holds a helicopter in the `HELI_LIFT` state. `tests/sim/worker.test.ts` and the Chromium page replay the same file. Earlier tests changed because this change provably causes the difference: `tests/host/resume.test.ts` (a restored host is handed the scenario world's `maxElev`: without the layer it refuses the golden run's accepted launch and diverges, the hazard of any restore that forgets the layer), the version pins in `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts` and `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts`, the unknown kind byte in `tests/sim/codec.test.ts` (13 is LaunchHeli now, 15 is unknown), the kind list in `tests/sim/codecAttack.test.ts`, the "unknown code" 51 in `tests/ui/heliReasons.test.ts` (55), and the pinned export list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`.

### M12a-2 mutant table (one changed line each; every row was seen RED before the test was kept, the file restored afterwards)

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|
| the step is twice as long | heliFlight: flies at the mode speed; heliFlightReal |
| no clearance (floor = ground + AGL only) | heliFlightReal / 100: clearance, AGL floor |
| the occluder replaced by `mean + clearance` (the old formula) | heliFlightReal: clearance over the Alps |
| the altitude limiter wins over the floor | heliFlightReal: clearance |
| hover one tick short / take-off one tick short | heliFlight: Hover lasts / TakeOff lasts (tick-exact) |
| the load is not returned on landing | heliFlight: a recalled helicopter sets down ... |
| the troops are not taken at launch | heliFlight: an accepted launch takes the troops |
| fuel is not spent | heliFlight: running out of fuel |
| Downed removed a tick early / Downed keeps its load | heliFlight: running out of fuel ... |
| Recall of a returning helicopter allowed / Recall does nothing | heliFlight: RecallHeli reason codes |
| the troops purse is ignored / the owner check dropped | heliFlight: BAD_TROOPS / NO_FACTORY, NOT_OWNER |
| the return goes to the first Factory, not the nearest | heliFlight: the return goes to the NEAREST own Factory |
| no scrapping without a Factory | heliFlight: scrapped when it turns for home |
| x is not wrapped / no shortest way over the seam | heliFlight: a flight over the date line |

Two rows were first NOT killed and the tests were added before this table: the nearest-Factory rule (one remaining Factory proves nothing) and the wrap (a self-consistent unwrapped x still arrives; the test now asserts 0 <= x < width * 256 on every tick).
## M13a-1: the helicopter is visible (render and UI; src/sim untouched)

1. **One merged model, one draw call.** `buildHeli()` is a single geometry of 106 triangles with a part code per vertex; the layer is one `InstancedBufferGeometry` mesh of capacity 64 (the sim table). Rotors turn in the vertex shader from ONE uniform (fx time x 24 rad/s wrapped on the CPU); nothing is rewritten per frame. Draw calls: +1 when at least one helicopter is drawn (0 otherwise), measured with the layer on and off in `e2e/helis.test.ts`; the table row of "Budgetar" and `tests/budgets.test.ts` get the layer's own row (<= 7 000 triangles, 1 call); the sum of the layer rows rises from 14 to 15 draw calls, the test allows 17 (16 + this one) and row 0 (100) is untouched. Triangles at the maximum: 64 x 106 = 6 784.
2. **The helicopter shares the structures' scale policy.** The model is built in a unit disc and scaled by `HELI_MODEL_SCALE` (0.11) FACTORY units (77 m), under the same `uPxK` / `uClose` / `uMaxScale` policy with the Factory's 700 m as the size. That keeps a fixed share of the Factory at every zoom, so the pad offset (`HELI_PAD_OFFSET`, a free corner of the plate) stays beside the Factory instead of sliding into the hall. Cost: far away a helicopter is as big as a Factory scaled down by 0.11, a few pixels (a far icon is BACKLOG).
3. **A parked helicopter stands on the pad corner, not at the cell centre.** The cell centre is inside the 350 m hall of the Factory model. The offset is in model units added in the shader, so the row's position (what the test compares to the sim within 0.5 cell) is untouched. The Factory model itself is NOT changed (no golden moves).
4. **Height.** `aglM = max(0, alt - GAMEPLAY_HEIGHT_EXAG * meanElev(cell))` metres above `groundHeightAt` (rule 2 of the layer rules, `radiusAboveGround`); a parked one is 0. M12a-1 leaves `alt` 0 for a parked helicopter; if M12a-2 defines flight altitude differently this is the one line to change. Without `meanElev` (a test world) `alt` is taken as the height above ground.
5. **Every state is drawn, only `HELI_BUILDING` is not.** Downed ones stand with still rotors (`aInfo.z`). Heading comes from the movement between table versions; a recycled slot starts with a hashed heading. No interpolation between snapshots (BACKLOG).
6. **Toasts without the outbox.** The page only receives the reject events of the outbox, so `HeliWatcher` compares the seat's counts (finished / under production) between table versions (`helisVersion` moves only when a new table came): finished up and building down = produced, building down and total down = scrapped. It does nothing between versions and allocates nothing per frame.
7. **Texts.** `sel.heli.*`, `hud.fleet`, `toast.heli.produced`, `toast.heli.scrapped` in a block at the end of both tables; the reason of a dimmed button reuses `toast.heli.*` through `rejectKey`. The fleet number is written n, slash, cap without a key (identical in both languages; the language-parity test caps identical texts).
8. **Hooks.** `__test.helis()`, `__test.heliSpin(angle | null)`, `__test.heliLayer(on)`; documented in ARCHITECTURE.md. No golden changed: the layer draws nothing without helicopters and the globe goldens have none; the fleet group only adds a "0/n" to the HUD, which the goldens do not show (HUD hidden).
9. **Not done**: effects, the H camera, the scenario slice, a far icon, interpolation, a leak test (see BACKLOG "M13a-1 leftovers").

## M12a-3: helicopters in the sim, part 3 of 3 (the landing, the beachhead, the cut-off cluster)

Everything below that the plan did not fix is PROVISIONAL (balance in M19). Part 3 replaces the landing placeholder of part 2 and gives a landing real consequences: a helicopter carries troops behind the enemy line, takes up to nine cells, and the foothold lives 45 s unless it grows or gets a City.

1. **The landing (`src/sim/heliLanding.ts`, `performLanding`).** `HELI_HOVER` ends into `HELI_UNLOAD` (`HELI_UNLOAD_TICKS` 10, the state of the table that part 1 reserved; `RecallHeli` is refused in it, `NOT_FLYING`); in the step in which the 10 ticks are over the cells are taken and the helicopter turns for home empty. The order: the zone `target - 1` (when it is the helicopter's own land, or an owner `landingBlockedBy` names: no landing, `HELI_LANDED` b = 0, the load flies home as in part 2), then N, E, S, W, NE, SE, SW, NW (`landingCell`, x wraps over the date line, a row above the first or below the last is skipped), at most `BEACHHEAD_LANDING_CELLS` 9. A cell the helicopter owns already or that is unplayable is skipped (not counted) except the zone, which must be taken. Price per cell: `captureCostMilliQt(class, 0, 0)` (an Attack cell at depth 0 on level ground: terrain included, the uphill term is 0 because nobody stands next to it), x `LANDING_ENEMY_COST_PCT` 200 / 100 for a cell that belongs to somebody else (the plan's "x2"), then the home trait (`adjustedCostMilliQt`) and the defender's Post or Walls exactly as `advanceAttack` does it; the anti-snowball discount does NOT apply (as the prompt says). Paid from the load (4 quarter troops per troop) with the same fractional carry as an Attack (`owed = frac + cost`, whole QT out, the rest carried); a cell the rest of the load cannot pay for ends the landing. The unpaid fraction of the last cell is paid rounded up when troops are left (as `CancelAttack` does), so a landing never makes a troop and never hides a price. **A zone that cannot be taken** (the load pays for no cell, or the cell is unplayable) FAILS: no cell changes, no row, the load is gone, `HELI_LANDED` b = -1. **The rest of the load** is `launchAttack(owner, the owner the zone had, rest)` when the two have contact after the landing (an Attack from the beachhead under the ordinary rules), else it goes back to the seat. `HELI_LANDED` is now `a = helicopter, b = cells taken (1 to 9)`, `-1` failed, `0` nothing to land on (it was `b = load` in part 2; no reader existed: `HeliWatcher` of M13a-1 compares table versions). `OUTBOX_BEACHHEAD_FORMED` (a = slot, b = seed) follows. `state.match.conquered` counts the cells. Cells taken by the landing carry stamp 0 and no exposure depth (BACKLOG).
2. **The altitude of a helicopter that unloads is the hover altitude** (it does not descend to the ground in the simulation): the flight property of part 2 ("never below occluder + clearance on any tick of a flight") and M12b's visibility questions then hold for the whole mission, and `tests/sim/heliFlightReal.test.ts` and its 100-flight sibling stay green unchanged. A first version descended to the ground in the 10 ticks; the 40-flight test caught 42 clearance violations and the descent was removed. A renderer can draw the descent from the state change.
3. **The hook for relations.** `src/sim/public.ts` has no `relation` query yet (M18c-1 adds it), so `landingBlockedBy(owner, targetOwner)` exists and always returns false. It is asked for the zone's owner (a blocked zone: no landing, the load flies home) and for every neighbour (a blocked neighbour is skipped). The launch check does not ask it (BACKLOG): the refusal comes at the landing, which costs the player a flight but cannot lose troops.
4. **The beachhead row.** `state.beachheads.add(owner, zone, landTick, BEACH_CUT_OFF)` at the landing (landTick = the tick of the step that took the cells), lowest free slot; a full table (impossible at 12 helicopters and 450 ticks) just makes the cells ordinary land. Rows are checked in `beachheadPhase` at the start of `heliPhase` (a row outlives its helicopter), at age = 10, 20, ... ticks: `scanCluster` (breadth-first, **8 neighbours** because a landing's NE, SE, SW and NW cells touch the zone only diagonally; the owner's playable cells; x wraps; bounded by `BEACHHEAD_ESTABLISH_CELLS` 80). **Established** = the search reaches 80 cells or meets a City (any City on the cluster, finished or not): the row is freed and the cells are ordinary land. Otherwise **exactly `BEACHHEAD_REVERT_TICKS` 450 ticks after the landing** (an evaluation tick because 450 is a multiple of 10) every cell of the cluster goes to owner 0 (`changeOwner`: structures on it are destroyed, Attacks lose their contact and end), `OUTBOX_BEACHHEAD_REVERTED` (a = slot, b = seed) is pushed and the row is freed. A cluster that the owner grows by an ordinary Attack (the rest of the load) is part of the search; at 80 cells it is established, which is the way to keep a foothold. A seed the enemy takes moves to the first of its eight neighbours (N, E, S, W, NE, SE, SW, NW) that is still the owner's, else the row is freed and whatever is left becomes ordinary land (a hole: an enemy can free a foothold by taking the seed with its neighbours; BACKLOG if it matters).
5. **Merging (PROVISIONAL, the simplest rule).** When the search from row i meets the seed of another row of the same owner, the two are one cluster and the YOUNGER row (later landing, then the higher slot) is freed; the joined cluster reverts at the OLDER one's time, unless it has become established.
6. **The economy leaves a cut-off cluster out (cached, derived, not hashed).** `ownedStdAreaQ15` (the one place the troop cap, the regeneration and the gold land read the area from) subtracts `cutOffWeightOf(state, owner)` = the Q15 cos-weighted area of the owner's cut-off clusters (fewer than 80 cells; a City counts only when its row is next checked and freed). The cache lives on `BeachheadTable` (`dirty`, `cutWeight`), is set dirty by `changeOwner` while a row exists (one comparison per captured cell), by `add`, `remove` and `rebuildIndex` (a restored state starts dirty) and is rebuilt by the first read: a pure function of the cells and the rows, so a restore and a straight run answer alike (the snapshot test asserts the figure after the restore and the hash on every 15th tick through a revert). Between changes a read is one array access. Two clusters that already touch are counted once (the second search treats the first one's cells as walls), so no cell is subtracted twice before the merge at the next check. Structures on cut-off cells still pay their structure gold; the land share of the win rule counts the cells (both BACKLOG).
7. **The enclave rule does not flip a cut-off cluster (the exemption of "M04 audit 5").** `checkRegion` (`src/sim/enclaves.ts`) treats a region as OPEN when it holds the seed of a cut-off row of its owner or one of the eight cells around it (`holdsBeachhead`; nine mark reads per row, only when a row exists). Without it a landing of 1 to 9 cells in the middle of one enemy's land is an enclave and flips free of charge within 20 ticks (the test "returns to neutral EXACTLY ..." fails with the line removed). **Consequence, accepted:** after a cluster is established (row freed) an established cluster of at most 64 cells that ONE other owner surrounds completely is an ordinary enclave and flips; only clusters that touch sea, a mountain, a second owner, neutral land, or have more than 64 cells survive (BACKLOG, M19).
8. **Elimination (M18-1).** A player's cut-off cells count as land: the player is not eliminated while the row lives; when the cluster returns to neutral in the step at landTick + 450 the match phase of the SAME step sees no cell and flags the elimination (`tests/sim/heliLanding.test.ts`: a player whose Factory cell is taken after the landing).
9. **ENGINE_VERSION 15 -> 16 ONCE** (`FORMAT_VERSION` unchanged: both tables were enough; the derived cache is not stored). `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` recomputed once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` (every hash moves with the engine version; the scripted 6000-tick runs never reach a landing, so nothing but the version moved them). The worker test replays the same file. Earlier tests changed because this change provably causes the difference: `tests/sim/heliFlight.test.ts` (the mission test: hover to return is now hover + `HELI_UNLOAD_TICKS`, and `HELI_LANDED` b = 0 on the seat's own land; the troop conservation assert is unchanged), the version pins in `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts` and `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts`. Note for the merge with M18c-1: both tracks bump the engine version and recompute both golden files; the orchestrator sets the final values.
10. **Measured.** A real Alps landing (factory at sim cell (548, 131), zone (593, 121), 600 troops, Low mode, an enemy ring of nine cells; the numbers are ticks after the launch step): +1 `HELI_LIFT` at (548.5, 131.5) alt 0; +22 `HELI_CRUISE` alt 2932 game metres; +51 alt 9204; +101 alt 2804; +151 alt 5600; +201 alt 11860; +231 `HELI_APPROACH` at (588.7, 121.7) alt 2708; +254 `HELI_HOVER` at (593.5, 121.5) alt 1644, fuel 131 of 180; +284 `HELI_UNLOAD`; +293 the nine cells are taken (`HELI_LANDED` b = 9, `BEACHHEAD_FORMED` b = the zone cell 124497); +294 `HELI_RETURN` empty (the rest of the load went back to the seat: no enemy cell was left to attack); +501 still returning (fuel 87), one beachhead row. CPU (perf project, median of 200 after 50 warm-ups, 1024x512, 12 players, 12 helicopters in the air, 4 cut-off beachheads): a tick of the helicopter phase with the cut-off cache rebuilt EVERY tick 34 us (budget `budgetMs(0.5)` 753 us), the check of all four rows at once (four bounded searches) 11 us, the flight phase alone 25 us (unchanged from part 2).
11. **The bundle guard moves.** The total gzipped JavaScript of `tests/portable.test.ts` was 329 471 B after this part (cap 329 000 B at Fix-3C, +5 % over 313 158 B; the growth since is the helicopter code of M12a-1, M12a-2, M13a-1 and M20a-lite plus this part's ~2 KB). The cap is raised to 335 000 B (+1.7 %); the main-chunk cap (277 000 B) did not move. This is the procedure the test names (a line here).
