## M18c-1: diplomacy in the sim and the AI (headless; the interface is M18c-2)

Provisional choices, all numbers in `src/sim/rules.ts` and `docs/BALANCE.md` ("Diplomacy (M18c-1)").

1. **Model.** A relation per pair of PLAYER seats (never a tribe, never the neutral owner): war (default), truce (`TRUCE_TICKS` 2400 from the acceptance, then war again) or alliance (until somebody breaks it). Symmetric: both directions are stored and hashed. Proposals live in a table of `PROPOSAL_CAPACITY` 32 (sender, receiver, treaty kind, last tick), at most one per DIRECTED pair, `PROPOSAL_TTL_TICKS` 600 long; a treaty that starts drops every proposal between the two (both directions). A pair that has a truce or an alliance cannot be proposed the same again (AT_PEACE / ALLIED); a truce is renewed by a new proposal once it has run out (or upgraded to an alliance at any time); a running truce cannot be prolonged.
2. **Time is tick exact.** `expireDiplomacy` runs at the START of every step, before the intents: a truce accepted at tick T is a truce for the steps T .. T + 2400 - 1 and war from the step T + 2400 on (an Attack in that very step is allowed); a proposal made at T can be answered in the steps T .. T + 599. `DiplomacyView` (what the page and the AI read between steps) is tick aware in the same way, so a truce whose end tick has come already reads as war before the sweep.
3. **Intents.** Wire kinds 15 to 20 after RecallHeli (14): `ProposeTreaty{to, treaty}` (the field is `treaty`, not `kind`, which is the discriminator), `AcceptTreaty{from}`, `DeclineTreaty{from}`, `BreakTreaty{with}`, `ImposeEmbargo{target}`, `LiftEmbargo{target}`; all seats are SEATS. Reason codes 80 to 93 (`REJECT_DIPLO_*`): the free interval above the landing-zone codes 65 to 72, as the orchestrator asked. `tests/sim/lzCodes.test.ts` said "every LZ reason is above the highest REJECT code" (true while the highest was 54); it now excludes the `REJECT_DIPLO_` block from that one inequality (disjointness still holds for the others, and `tests/sim/diplomacy.test.ts` pins the block as 80 to 93, 14 distinct values, none equal to another REJECT code). `REJECT_PHASE` is reused for the spawn phase (immunity is not a diplomacy phase). Check order in every intent: match over, spawn phase, sender (a player, not eliminated), the other seat (not itself, a player, not eliminated), then the intent's own rule.
4. **One implementation.** `checkPropose` / `checkAnswer` (Accept and Decline share it) / `checkBreak` / `checkImpose` / `checkLift` in `src/sim/diplomacy.ts` read a `DiploCheckView` (the shape of a `SimView`); the public `validate*` pass the page's view, the `apply*` pass a thin view of the live state. `tests/sim/diplomacy.test.ts` runs every reason through both and compares them.
5. **The attack ban.** `applyAttack` refuses an Attack on a truce or alliance party with `REJECT_DIPLO_TREATY_BOUND` (free land is never bound), after the SELF check and before the contact check; a treaty that starts cancels the attacks between the two (both directions, troops back to the attackers, taken cells stay). The helicopter landing of the other sim track must ask `atWar(state, ownerA, ownerB)` before it takes land (BACKLOG).
6. **Win conditions stay individual.** An ally of the leader can win or lose alone; there is no shared victory in this version (BACKLOG). Alliance effects: only the trade (point 8).
7. **Betrayal.** `BreakTreaty` (truce or alliance) is war at once, sets the breaker's cooldown (`TREATY_BREAK_COOLDOWN_TICKS` 1800: it cannot PROPOSE, it can still answer) and counts in `treatiesBroken` of the breaker; a truce that runs out costs nothing. Both sides count the treaty in `treatiesMade` when it starts. `MatchView` gets `treaties`, `betrayals` and `relations` (truces and alliances the seat has now) per seat, appended to the packed array (the page reads them from `SimView.match`).
8. **Embargo and alliance trade.** `ImposeEmbargo` is free and one-sided to impose; the trade of the two seats' Ports is 0 for both while ANY of them has one on the other (`embargoBy` keeps who put it on; two embargoes need two lifts; the old symmetric flag of `StructureTable.embargo` stays the thing the trade reads and `syncEmbargo` keeps it equal). Raw flags set by `setEmbargo` (old tests) have no owner and load and hash as before. Allied Ports trade at `TRADE_SAME_OWNER_PCT` as if they had one owner (the "same owner" rate, half of the open rate); nothing else is shared. The lift "by an accepted proposal" of the prompt was not built (BACKLOG).
9. **Elimination** (`clearDiplomacyOf`, called by `winPhase` when it flags a seat): the seat's treaties end (war, no cooldown, no betrayal), its proposals in both directions go, its embargoes are lifted; the statistics stay. **A test bound moved:** the D143 check in `tests/ai/driver.test.ts` allowed 0 refused intents in a Skirmish match and 1 in Play; it now allows 1 in both. Treaties change who fights whom, so the Skirmish trajectories moved and seed 3 meets the rare "target flipped whole in the 2 ticks of the delay" race once (an Attack refused with NO_CONTACT at tick 2682, not a diplomacy refusal); the strict REJECT_NO_ATTACK check beside it is unchanged, and the AI now neither starts nor cancels an attack against a seat it has an open proposal with (an acceptance in the pipe would end the attack or refuse the new one: that was the first failure of the same test, seed 2, a refused CancelAttack).
10. **State, hash, serialisation.** `GameState.diplo` (a `DiplomacyTable`) and `MatchState.treatiesMade` / `treatiesBroken` are state: a diplomacy block after the match block (sparse lists of pairs, embargoes and proposals, the cooldowns, the two statistics arrays, and the block's own byte length as the last word so the reader finds the tail from the end and a wrong district count is still a size error), hashed by `foldDiplomacy` (one fold of every field; `tests/sim/diplomacy.test.ts` changes each field once). The reader checks pairs (two distinct players, ascending, relation 1 or 2, a truce that has not ended and is not longer than the rules allow, no end tick on an alliance), embargoes, cooldowns and proposals (players, no duplicate directed pair, a tick in range): `'diplomacy-range'`. **ENGINE_VERSION 15 -> 16 and FORMAT_VERSION 12 -> 13, once**; `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` recomputed once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` (the scenario has no diplomacy, so every hash changes only because the hash now folds the empty diplomacy tables and the header carries version 16). The helicopter landing track raises the engine (and perhaps the format) on its own branch: the ORCHESTRATOR sets the final values and regenerates both golden files when the tracks are merged (the comments at `ENGINE_VERSION` and `FORMAT_VERSION` say so). Tests that hard-code byte offsets from the end of the bytes (`homeRules`, `districtsState`, `fix3a1Match`, `win`, `structures`, `serialize`) got the diplomacy block (16 + 12 per ownerId when empty) in their arithmetic; `tests/sim/structures.test.ts` computed its offset from the end of the structure records without the match block and only hit the right word by luck, it now computes it properly. `tests/sim/codec.test.ts` and `tests/sim/codecAttack.test.ts` list kinds 15 to 20 (the first unknown kind is 21), `tests/sim/matchViewPack.test.ts` the three new seat blocks.
11. **The page's copy.** `ViewSnapshot.diplomacy` (null unless `DiplomacyTable.version` moved since the last diff that carried it; always in `requestView`), `MirrorDiplomacy` (a table filled by `adopt`, read through `DiplomacyReader` against the mirror's own tick), `ViewMirror.diplomacy` / `diplomacyVersion`. `SimView.diplomacy` is a required field: `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` builds a SimView stub by hand and got the field.
12. **The AI** (`src/ai/diplomacy.ts`, `diplomacyMove` called by `AiDriver` after `think` and `buildMove`; deterministic, the only draw from its own stream is the 30 to 60 tick pause before an answer, drawn when it first SEES a proposal): it answers a truce from a stronger proposer or when an attack runs between it and at least two other players (either way); an alliance from a proposer at or below 25 percent of the land against a leader above 30 percent that is at war with both; otherwise it declines, and it declines everything from a seat that broke a treaty against it in the last 3000 ticks (seen as a relation that went to war before the truce was due or from an alliance, and not by its own break; remembered in `betrayedAt`, `lastRel`, `lastUntil`). It proposes (on its think rhythm, from `AI_WAR_START_TICKS`, at most one per 600 ticks, none while one of its own is open or its cooldown runs): a weak AI (land below half the median of the living players) a truce to the strongest neighbour that attacked it; any AI an alliance to the strongest player that is not the leader when a leader holds more than 40 percent. An AI above 40 percent itself breaks its alliances one by one. `think` never attacks (or cancels against) a treaty party or a seat it has an open proposal with. Every intent goes through the same `validate*` first. The memory has its own words (`diplomacyWords`, kept apart from `memoryWords`, whose layout and test are untouched); the AI-state blob magic is now 'AIS' + 2.
13. **Measured behaviour** (real world, 10 minutes = 6000 ticks, 6 AI nations and 20 tribes, no human, seeds 1 to 5): per match about 2 proposals (10 in 5 matches), 1.4 declines (7), 0.6 accepts (3 treaties), 0 breaks, 0 refusals at the sim. In the standard match with a human (seat 0 spawned in Europe), over 6 seeds, 24 truce proposals by the human at 60 s intervals from 3.5 minutes were accepted 3 times (12 percent: only when the human is the bigger, or the AI fights two others) and 18 alliance proposals 0 times (no nation holds 30 percent of the land in 10 minutes, so the alliance rule never fires; it is the rule of the prompt). The economy: `npm run sim:scenario -- --solo` has no AI and does not change; `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` (passive human, the AI at war) stays inside its guards. The AI is quiet on purpose: the knobs are `AI_TRUCE_ACCEPT_WARS`, `AI_ALLY_*` and `AI_WEAK_MEDIAN_PCT`.
14. **Bundle size.** The gzipped JavaScript went from 328 174 B (the base commit, cap 329 000) to 334 052 B (+5.9 kB: the sim code, the codec, the AI and 28 strings), so the total cap in `tests/portable.test.ts` is now 338 000 (the main chunk cap 277 000 is untouched). The merged tracks may need a new look at it.
15. **Not done** (BACKLOG "M18c-1 leftovers"): the interface, shared victory, lifting an embargo by a proposal, the AI's use of the embargo, the landing ban for helicopters.

## Round 16 integration (orchestrator: M12a-3 landing merged with M18c-1 diplomacy)

Both sim tracks raised ENGINE_VERSION on their own branch (16). The merge sets the final values: **ENGINE_VERSION 17** (M12a-3 is 16, M18c-1 is 17) and **FORMAT_VERSION 13** (M12a-3 added no state arrays; M18c-1 added the diplomacy block). Both golden hash files were recomputed ONCE with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` through `tests/sim/golden.test.ts` and `tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts` on the merged tree (the branch values could not be combined by hand). The version-pin tests (`districtsState`, `fixSim`) follow. `tests/portable.test.ts` total gzip cap: both tracks raised it (335 000 and 338 000) and the page now also carries the M13b launch preview (Los, Nav, planRoute, scoreLz): the cap was set to the measured size of the merged build plus about 3 % (see the line in the test).
## M13b: deploy mode, landing zone preview and route preview (render, UI, app; src/sim untouched)

1. **No `SimHost.query`; plain functions on the page's own Nav (deviation, the prompt allows it).** The original prompt wanted `SimHost.query('previewLaunch')`. The slice prompt let me choose the variant that repeats the fewest rules, and `validateLaunchHeli(view, nav, ...)` already exists and is the very `checkLaunchHeli` the sim runs, so `previewLaunch` in `src/app/launchPreview.ts` is a thin function over it plus `planRoute` (route, ETA, fuel) and `isExposed` (the colour). The page builds ONE Nav per session (`createLos` + `createNav` on the world's elevation layers and the mirror's terrain, the way `src/app/debugViewshed.ts` does; about the cost of the sim's own, built at the first Skicka-helikopter click). M24a's WorkerHost needs no change for this; if the Nav must move into the worker later, `LaunchPreviewer` already takes an async `query(req) => Promise<PreviewResult>`, which is the whole interface. No `src/sim` file changed (`git diff src/sim` is empty).
2. **The throttle (`LaunchPreviewer`).** At most ONE query out; at most one per `DEPLOY.previewIntervalS` (0.2 s, 5 Hz) on the fx clock; a newer wanted request REPLACES the pending one (nothing queues); an answer whose request is no longer the wanted one (the cursor moved, a slider moved, the mode ended) is dropped and counted; `current()` never returns the answer of another request, so the marker is amber (waiting) rather than wrong. Answers arrive as Promises, so a marker is never green in the frame of the move. A click does NOT wait for the preview: `decide(cell)` asks the sim's `validateLaunchHeli` at that moment.
3. **Parity (`tests/app/launchPreview.test.ts`).** 35 cells on a synthetic 512x128 world (every LZ reason 65..72 incl. a sealed nav block for NO_ROUTE, BAD_TROOPS three ways, NO_PAD, NOT_OWNER, NO_FACTORY, valid in both modes): the sim's own `LaunchHeli` through `step` on a deserialised twin, `validateLaunchHeli` and `previewLaunch` give the same code; the same `troops` goes to all three. NO_GOLD is a ProduceHeli code (a launch has no price), covered as the production check the Factory button shows (`validateProduceHeli` against the real ProduceHeli with an empty purse).
4. **Deploy mode.** `createDeployMode` is an InputMode pushed OVER the selection mode: Escape pops it only (the Factory stays selected, nothing else opens), a left or a right click (a touch tap or a long press too) on a valid zone sends `LaunchHeli{seat, factory, lzCell, troops, altMode}` and pops itself, a click on a refused zone shows the reason as a toast (`rejectKey(code)` -> `toast.lz.*` / `toast.heli.*`) and stays. The button, the troop slider (1 to `HELI_MAX_TROOPS`, default `DEPLOY.troopsDefault` 1) and the Low / High switch (default Low) live in the Factory panel (`src/ui/deployBox.ts`, inside the panel's helicopter box so no layout of its own). The slider is not clamped to the purse: the sim says BAD_TROOPS, which the panel shows in words.
5. **Resolver rule and key.** The rule `factory-deploy` (priority 15, trigger `context`): a right click with a Factory selected opens the deploy mode with that Factory chosen, anywhere on the map. It does NOT send straight away (the player would not see the verdict and the route first). Key L (`deploy` in the registry, scope match, label `hotkey.deploy`) does the same from the keyboard and toggles. `tests/app/commandResolver.test.ts` changed in two places because this rule provably changes the table: the extension-point probe rule now has priority 14 (it sat at 15, the same as the new rule, and the test wants it IN FRONT) and the expected id lists include `factory-deploy`.
6. **The layer (`src/render/deployLayer.ts`, 3 draw calls).** The route is a `Line` drawn twice from one geometry: dim (alpha `DEPLOY.style.dimAlpha`, no depth test, so it shows through a ridge) first, bright (depth test) over it; hi / lo float32 positions made camera-relative in the shader like every layer; vertices are the route's waypoints cut into pieces of at most 1.5 cells (so a long leg follows the curve of the globe) at ground height plus the flight mode's AGL (`AIR_MODE_AGL_M`), with an exposure value per vertex (0 hidden, 1 seen by an enemy sensor) that the fragment shader mixes from green to red. Lines are one pixel wide (WebGL has no wide lines; a ribbon is BACKLOG). The marker is one mesh: a ring and a pole in tangent-plane model units whose size is the larger of 1.2 % of the camera distance and 12 km; colour by state (amber waiting, green valid, red refused), no depth test. The buffers are written only when the answer changes (5 Hz at most); `frame` allocates nothing. "Helicopter layer + deploy layer <= 4 extra calls" (1 + 3) is pinned in `tests/budgets.test.ts` and measured with the layer on and off in `e2e/deploy.test.ts`. Two budget tests changed because the layer is new: the sum of the layer rows in "Budgetar" is now allowed 18 (was 16 and 17), justified by the new row (3 calls).
7. **Helicopter list and news.** `ownHeliRows` lists the seat's helicopters that are not under production (state, troops, fuel percent, Recall when `validateRecallHeli` is 0); `MissionWatcher` compares the table between versions (the page gets no outbox events other than rejects) and says `toast.heli.landed` when a hovering helicopter leaves HOVER and `toast.heli.lost` when one becomes DOWNED. BEACHHEAD_REVERTED has no toast: the beachhead table is not in `SimView` in this branch (BACKLOG "M13b-2").
8. **i18n.** Keys `deploy.*`, `heli.*` (list, states), `hotkey.deploy`, `toast.heli.landed`, `toast.heli.lost` in a block at the end of both tables; `tests/i18n.test.ts` scans the prefixes `deploy` and `heli` too now (it listed the prefixes it knew; the keys would have been reported dead). Colours and sizes: `DEPLOY` in `src/ui/uiConfig.ts`.
9. **Size caps raised** (`tests/portable.test.ts`, "Raise them only with a line in DECISIONS"): the deploy UI (`launchPreview`, `deployBox`, `deployMode`, `DeployLayer` and its shaders) added about 8 to 10 kB gzipped (3 %). Measured gzipped JavaScript: main 280 318 B (cap 277 000 -> 294 500) and total 335 273 B (cap 329 000 -> 352 000), each plus 5 %. CORRECTED in Fix-4B (audit 3, finding 2.5): the first version of this line said the page "now carries `createLos`, `createNav`, `planRoute`, `scoreLz`" because of the preview and that a lazy `import()` of `launchPreview` would take them back out. That is wrong: since M12a-1 `step()` itself imports them (`step` -> `heliFlight` -> `access` -> `los` / `nav`), the sim runs on the main thread, and the shared `view` chunk that holds them is a static import of main (modulepreloaded in `index.html`), so they are on the first load with or without the preview. A lazy `launchPreview` would save about 1.6 kB gzipped (the whole deploy UI with layer and shaders about 8 to 10 kB), not 16 kB, and the idea is dropped. The real candidates if the first load ever matters are in BACKLOG "M13b leftovers" (the English string table, the sim chunk until Play is pressed, the share of three.js). The caps were raised in steps (329 000 -> 338 000 -> 352 000, main 277 000 -> 294 500), each to the measured value plus 3 to 5 %, so the guard only forces a line here; a per-chunk or first-load cap would say more than a measured-plus-percent ratchet.
10. **Not done**: the pulsing beachhead ring (no beachhead data in the view), the exposure plan view on key E, a SAM variant, an ETA per helicopter in the list (the view does not carry the target). See BACKLOG "M13b-2".


## Round 16 integration, part 2 (orchestrator)

1. **The "landed" toast waited for the wrong state.** `MissionWatcher` (src/ui/heliInfo.ts, M13b) announced MISSION_LANDED when a helicopter left HELI_HOVER, which was right with the part-2 placeholder (the hover led straight to the return). With the real landing of M12a-3 the hover ends in HELI_UNLOAD and the cells are taken 10 ticks later, so the toast came before the beachhead existed (and a recall from the hover would have raised it as well). It now fires when the helicopter leaves HELI_UNLOAD. `tests/app/deployMode.test.ts` (the watcher test) follows and gains a recall case; `e2e/deploy.test.ts` now asserts that the zone cell belongs to the player after the toast (the assertion M13b had left as an `it.skip`).
2. The first full `npm run verify` of the merge had three 20 s screenshot timeouts (deploy, globe settle, setup 1920x1080) while three auditors, a builder and the verify shared the 4 cores (load average 13 to 18). The globe and setup tests pass alone; the deploy test failed alone for the reason in point 1.
## M18c-2: diplomacy in the interface and a livelier AI negotiator

1. **What it is.** The interface of M18c-1: a panel (key D, the top-bar button "Diplomati", Escape closes), cards for incoming proposals, toasts for every answer, badges in the leaderboard, treaty columns on the result screen, and a gate on the attack click. Nothing in the sim changed except the AI's temperament (6 below); the UI only sends the six treaty intents and reads `SimView.diplomacy`. Files in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("M18c-2").
2. **No rule is repeated.** Every button state is the answer of the `validate*` function (`buildDiploRows`); the reason code of a refused button is the tooltip (`diplomacyRefusalKey`), and a click on a refused button shows the same text as a toast (a touch screen has no tooltip). The only UI-side reading of a rule is `attackBlock` ("relation is not war" for a click on a player's land); the sim still refuses the intent with the same code if it were sent (`tests/ui/diploModel.test.ts` checks both), so a UI that is wrong can never break the rule, only show the wrong toast.
3. **The break asks twice** (like Delete): the first click arms the button (it says "Bekräfta: bryt avtal", the row shows the warning with the cooldown time `TREATY_BREAK_COOLDOWN_TICKS` (3:00) and the traitor mark), the second one within `DIPLO.confirmSeconds` (6 s on the fx clock) sends `BreakTreaty`; any other button, closing the panel or the time disarms. After a break the panel shows the cooldown line (the sim's `breakCooldownLeft`) and your own traitor mark (`MatchView.betrayals`).
4. **Answers are toasts, also silence.** `DiploWatcher` compares the proposal table frame by frame: a new proposal to you is `toast.diplo.incomingTruce` / `incomingAlliance` (and the signal and the card), a proposal of yours that is gone is accepted (the relation is a treaty), expired (its last tick has passed) or declined. An expired incoming proposal just disappears. The first look only learns the table.
5. **Where things sit.** The button is in the second row of the top bar (the row that holds the win meter and the fleet gauge) with a 1.4em height, so the top bar keeps its height (the e2e overlap tests of M20a-lite stay green); the 44 px touch target is an invisible `::after`. The cards are top left under the top bar on a wide window and top right on a narrow one; the panel is a centred overlay (z 33, below the help overlay) and is an input mode, like the help overlay. The leaderboard row got a fifth grid column for the badges (always present, empty when there is none, so the columns do not jump). The panel writes the DOM only where a node changed; the e2e test counts 0 mutations at rest with the panel open.
6. **AI temperament (ENGINE_VERSION 17 -> 18 ONCE; `FORMAT_VERSION` unchanged; both golden files recomputed once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1`).** The prompt: the AI accepted a human truce in about 12 % of the cases and an alliance never in 10 minutes. New rules (no new randomness; the pause before an answer is still the only draw): (a) a TRUCE is accepted when the AI fights `AI_TRUCE_ACCEPT_WARS` (2) players, OR fights `AI_TRUCE_OTHER_WARS` (1) player other than the proposer and the proposer is not the leader (land share below the largest), OR the AI's own share is below `AI_TRUCE_MEDIAN_PCT` (100 %) of the median of the living players; the old rule "the proposer has more land" is gone (it accepted a truce from the leader without a reason and declined one from a nation in trouble). (b) an ALLIANCE: the leader threshold `AI_ALLY_LEADER_MIN_PCT` 30 -> 25 percent (the proposer's maximum 25 % and "both at war with the leader" stay). (c) the AI that is weak (below `AI_WEAK_MEDIAN_PCT` of the median) OR fights `AI_TRUCE_ACCEPT_WARS` players proposes a truce to the seat whose attacks it has seen in the most think rounds (`AiMemory.hits`, counted in `observe`; at least `AI_TRUCE_MIN_HITS` (2) rounds; a tie goes to the stronger), instead of "the strongest neighbour that attacked it" (one look). `hits` is the third thing in `diplomacyWords` (word count 2 + 5 * seats; the blob magic is unchanged: M18c-1 is not released). Constants in `src/sim/rules.ts`, rows in `docs/BALANCE.md`, the three new ones exported through `src/sim/public.ts` and pinned in `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`.
7. **Measured** (`npx tsx tools/sim/diploMeasure.ts`: the real world, seats: a scripted fighter "human" + 6 AI nations + 8 tribes, 10 minutes, 4 seeds (1 to 4); the human proposes a truce to one living AI every 300 ticks from tick 2400, round robin, 47 to 48 proposals in all). Before (the M18c-1 rules): 19 of 48 accepted (40 %), 36 treaties made in all, the AIs made 5 proposals. After: 25 of 47 accepted (53 %), 52 treaties in all, the AIs made 10 proposals, 2 of them to the human. Of the proposals where the new conditions held (the AI fights a party other than the human or is below the median) 24 of 24 were accepted (by construction); the 22 declined ones were AIs at peace with everybody and at or above the median: the "no" is kept for a nation that has no reason to stop. The target of the prompt (about 40 to 60 percent when the conditions match) is met without a second tuning round (the limit was two; one was used). The old 12 % came from the weaker human of the original test; the scripted human here is a fighter that grows, so the "before" is 40 %, not 12 %: what moved is the share of AI-AI treaties (36 -> 52) and the AI's own proposals (5 -> 10).
8. **Tests that changed because this change provably causes it:** `tests/ai/diplomacy.test.ts` (the truce rows: one war with another party now accepts, a leader asking while the AI holds the median is still declined, below the median accepts, an exact 25 % leader is no leader and 30 % is one, the grudge test now uses a position where the AI is below the median; the proposal test is extended by the "most frequent attacker" test), `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts` and `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts` (the ENGINE pin 17 -> 18), `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts` (the three exports), `tests/i18n.test.ts` (the key scanner knows the prefix `diplo`). `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` and the `--solo` band are unchanged and green (the economy does not touch diplomacy).
9. **Mutants shown** (each makes a named test fail, then the source was put back): the truce button ignoring `validateProposeTreaty` (4 tests of `tests/ui/diploModel.test.ts` fail), the watcher never saying "expired" (the watcher test fails), `attackOrExplain` sending the attack anyway (`e2e/diplomacy.test.ts` "attack click": no `reject.diplo.treatyBound` toast), `BreakConfirm.click` always confirming (`e2e/diplomacy.test.ts` "break": the first click already broke the treaty, `data-armed` stays 0).
   Also: `e2e/goldens/result-screen.png` re-pinned once (two more columns, "Ingångna avtal" and "Brutna avtal": the only golden this change moves; the top bar, structures, city and districts e2e goldens with `hud=1` stay green).
10. **Not done** (see BACKLOG "M18c-3"): the AI's embargo, shared victory, chat, tributes, a declaration of war with notice, a history of answers.

## Fix-4A1: audit 3, helicopter, beachhead and treaty rules (ENGINE 19)
Source: audit 3 findings [0.0], [0.1], [0.2], [0.6], [1.10], [1.1] to [1.4] against HEAD c672abe. All were still open at HEAD (checked by reading the code and by the new tests, each of which fails on the old code or on its mutant).

1. **[0.0] Zombie nation.** `winPhase` calls `scrapOwnerHelis` (heliPhase.ts) in the tick of the elimination: every helicopter of the seat (queued, parked, airborne) is removed and its load is lost (the seat has no land to return it to). `performLanding` also does nothing for an eliminated owner (HELI_LANDED b = -1), which guards a state restored from before the rule.
2. **[0.1] Cut-off cluster.** Chosen: no new per-cell state (a landing mark would change FORMAT, and the other builder owns the deserialize guards). A landing whose taken cells touch (8 neighbours) land the owner already held is ordinary land at once and creates NO beachhead row, unless that land is itself the cluster of a live cut-off row (then the row check merges them as before: the existing merge test still passes). Second guard: a cut-off cluster that contains the owner's capital cell is established at its check. Known remainder: a bridging landing between a probation cluster and a small main body can still revert the main body if neither holds a City or the capital; unreachable for a nation that has a capital, noted here.
3. **[0.2] Treaty ban.** Only tests (the guards existed): neighbour skip for truce and alliance, partner cells keep their owner, 6 cells taken, `conquered` = 6; page-side `validateLaunchHeli` refuses a partner's zone with `REJECT_DIPLO_TREATY_BOUND` and agrees with the sim.
4. **[0.6] Tables.** New codes (the range 55 to 63 was reserved for the helicopter milestone; 55 is left alone because `tests/ui/heliReasons.test.ts` pins it as "other"): `REJECT_HELI_TABLE_FULL` 57 (ProduceHeli with all 64 helicopter slots used; the per-seat cap stays `REJECT_HELI_FLEET_CAP` 49) and `REJECT_HELI_BEACHHEAD_FULL` 58 (LaunchHeli when rows in use + helicopters already on a mission >= `BEACHHEAD_CAPACITY`; refused before the departure, no troops drawn). Texts `toast.heli.tableFull` / `toast.heli.beachheadFull` (sv, en, last in the tables). The page-side `validateLaunchHeli` has no beachhead table (`LaunchProbe.beachheadFull` answers false there; BACKLOG). Table sizes and FORMAT unchanged. Comments fixed; `64 * BEACHHEAD_ESTABLISH_CELLS` is now `BEACHHEAD_CAPACITY * ...`. `applyProduceHeli`'s `id < 0` branch returns `REJECT_HELI_TABLE_FULL` instead of REJECT_NONE.
5. **[1.10] Alliance trade.** The M18c-1 decision (item 8, allied Ports at the same-owner half) is REVERSED: an alliance cost income. `TRADE_ALLIANCE_PCT = 125` (BALANCE row); `tradeMilliPerSec(dist, sameOwner, embargo, allied)`. The cap `PORT_INCOME_MAX_MILLI_PER_SEC` (13 gold/s per Port, so 15 gold/s for ten level-1 Cities still holds with the +10 % trait) applies after the sum, so no Port exceeds it. Test `allied Ports trade ...` in `tests/sim/diplomacy.test.ts` changed on purpose (`allied < full` became `allied > full` and the exact 125 %).
6. **Engine.** ENGINE_VERSION 18 to 19 once. Tests that pinned 18 (`tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts`, `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts`) follow. `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` recomputed once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` through `tests/sim/golden.test.ts` and `tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts` (the header hash carries the engine version; no scripted run reaches a changed rule path except through it). Node == worker is covered by the unchanged worker tests of verify:fast.
7. **Mutants (each made the named test fail, then the source was restored; script not committed).**

`npm run verify:fast` once at the end: typecheck and lint green, unit 2693 passed; the 3 failures were docs-only (BALANCE source column, backticked file names) and were fixed and re-run as single files (tests/docs.test.ts, tests/balance.test.ts, tests/sim/rules.test.ts: 40 passed). verify:fast was not repeated whole (shared cores).
## Fix-4A2: guards of the save parser, exact curve pins, the AI's diplomacy in constructed positions (audit 3)

1. **Finding 0.3 (44 surviving guard operands).** `tests/sim/fix4a2Forged.test.ts` has one test per guard of the air block and the diplomacy block of `deserialize`: a valid blob with ONE field patched to the first illegal value (byte patch, so the guard answers before the checksum; the neighbouring legal edge value answers `checksum`, which separates a guard from the checksum). Offsets come from `tests/helpers/saveLayout.ts`. Measured with a script that removes one `||` operand of each guard line (85 operands in the air block, `readDiplomacy` and the new checks): 67 killed, 18 survive. The 18 are DEFENSIVE guards whose removal another guard answers with the same error code for every blob a test can build: the count bounds of pairs, embargoes, proposals and beachheads and their `> end` twins (a forged count shifts the next field, and the closing length word or a range check refuses the shifted read), `recycledCount > highWater` (implied by `live + recycled === highWater`, live >= 0), `routeLen > HELI_ROUTE_CAPACITY` (the 65th route word is always another record's field, which is out of the cell range), the first `p + EMPTY_AIR_BYTES > end` (implied by the minimum-size check), the second recycled-id loop (a duplicate is already refused by the first loop), and the length-word comparison inside `readDiplomacy` (the same word is read first by the tail check). They stay in the code as bounds protection before a read; no test can tell them from their neighbours.
2. **Finding 0.4: three real gaps closed, FORMAT_VERSION and ENGINE_VERSION unchanged.** `stateTick` and `landTick` after the saved tick, and a `readyTick` more than `HELI_BUILD_TICKS` ahead, are refused (`air-range`); the structure embargo flag must equal the OR of the two `embargoBy` directions (`diplomacy-range`; refused rather than derived, because deriving changes the state and the checksum refuses it anyway); an eliminated seat that owns land, a helicopter, a treaty, an embargo or an open proposal is refused (`match-range`). Every real save loads as before (the golden scenario, the heli and diplomacy fuzz, all unit tests). Only one assumption of an existing test changed because of this: `tests/sim/structureEffects.test.ts` "a state with structures and embargoes round-trips" set the trade flag directly with no imposer, a state no rule makes; it now sets `embargoBy` too.
3. **Finding 0.5: `no-occluders`.** `deserialize` refuses a blob with a helicopter in LIFT..RETURN when `options.maxElev` is missing (`step` would throw `MissingOccluderError`). Tests that deserialized an airborne state without the layer now pass it: `tests/sim/heliProduction.test.ts` (round trip, forged table; the old `env.maxElev` is null assertion became "the layer is the one handed in") and `tests/sim/worker.test.ts` and `tests/host/remote.test.ts` (the golden scenario has helicopters in the air at tick 6000, so its snapshots are loaded with the scenario's layer).
4. **Finding 1.0: exact curve values.** `tests/sim/levelCurveTable.test.ts` pins `satPermille` and the twelve curves at the levels 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 500, 999 with literal tables (made once by a separate Python script from the formula) and with a BigInt reference at every level 1 to 999. Mutants: floor to ceil or round in `satPermille` and in `lift`, `level - 1` to `level`, 1000 to 1024 in either divisor, the level-0 guard of one curve, a wrong base, a swapped extra, a table that starts at 2 or ends one early: 13 of 13 killed.
5. **Finding 1.5: the AI's diplomacy.** `tests/ai/diplomacyPositions.test.ts` and `tests/ai/diplomacyThresholds.test.ts` (constructed positions, real sim rules). Operator mutation of `src/ai/diplomacy.ts` (133 mutants): 60 survived the first file alone, 32 survive all new files plus the old ones. The 32 are equivalent or unobservable: loop bounds `o < view.players` to `<=` (the index `players` is a tribe and `livingPlayer` is false), `>= 0` sentinels that only differ at tick 0 (the AI never thinks before AI_WAR_START_TICKS), the insertion sort `>` to `>=` (equal values), `livingPlayer`/`strongest`/guard chains where the alive flag and the eliminated flag imply each other, and `validateBreakTreaty` behind the alliance check. Named audit mutants: A38 (`lastUntil` not stored) killed by "a truce is remembered with its end tick" and "a truce that simply runs out is no betrayal"; A31 (grudge ignored for the alliance partner) by "the alliance goes to the second strongest, unless it broke a treaty"; A14 (alliance without war with the leader) by "declined when the AI itself is allied with the leader"; A20 (replies to proposals for others) by "no answer and nothing drawn from the stream"; A33 (think gate) by "it speaks only on its think tick"; A32 and the phase guard by the ended-match and unspawned cases; A25 (open proposal blocks nothing) by "nothing while it has an open proposal of its own"; A43 (`src/ai/think.ts` cancels while a proposal is open) by "an attack is not cancelled while a proposal is open". A06, A07, A35 (the AI's own break counted as a betrayal): the own-break guard is reachable only for a SECOND ally who breaks within two think rounds of the AI's own break, and there it hides a real betrayal; killed by pinning exactly that behaviour, and the proper fix (remember which seat was broken with) is in BACKLOG because it changes AI play. No bug fix was needed for A38 (the memory is stored); `git diff e2e/goldens` is empty.
6. **Finding 1.9: payback bands.** Next to "payback strictly increases" every payback test (Factory, Port, City, Market) now has an upper band about 10 percent above today's deliberately long values (Factory L2 1629 s, L3 3650 s, L4 7782 s, L6 18182 s, L10 108696 s; Port 1231 / 3077 / 6154 / 17391 / 52632 s; City L2 2133 s ... L30 2.4 Ms; Market L2 1067 s, L3 2667 s) and a floor of 90 s. The long values stay the design (a soft cap through the price base x L, Sim-Levels2 item 4); a regression to a steeper price, or a gold curve that collapses, now fails. The refusal pin of `tests/ai/driver.test.ts`: no refusal with a `REJECT_DIPLO_*` code (>= 80) in the Skirmish and Play runs, so the allowance of one refusal cannot hide a diplomacy refusal.
7. **Finding 0.7 (partly).** `tests/sim/fix4a2Misc.test.ts`: `dirtyTrade` after BreakTreaty and after an elimination, the `diplo.version` bump and the two-sided flag of Impose/Lift (5 of 5 mutants killed). The mutants in the heli files (landing cadence, free quarter troop, `match.conquered`, established cluster, look-ahead, Recall in HOVER) are left for after Fix-4A1 (BACKLOG).
8. **Finding 2.10: one layout helper.** `tests/helpers/saveLayout.ts` replaces the tail arithmetic in `districtsState`, `fix3a1Match`, `win`, `structures`, `homeRules` and `diplomacy` tests (eight sites); the next block in front of the checksum changes the helper only. No assertion changed.
9. **Findings not done:** 1.7 (page-side heli/diplomacy transport tests) is a slice of its own; see BACKLOG "Fix-4A2".

## Fix-4B: the beachhead is shown and explained, the deploy mode stops making garbage, the test holes of the app and UI layer (audit 3)

Base commit c672abe (M18c-2). Every finding was checked against HEAD first (the audit ran against 719b212).

1. **[2.1] The beachhead (still open at HEAD, done).** `SimView.beachheads` is a read-only `BeachheadView` (`src/sim/view.ts`, exported by `src/sim/public.ts`, the four new names pinned in `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`): per slot `owner`, `seed`, `landTick`, `status`, and `cellsOf(slot)`, the cells the cluster of the row holds now (the bounded cluster search of `src/sim/beachheadCluster.ts`, which leaves the scratch marks clean; the cell count is not a column of the sim's table, so the host computes it). The host side follows the helicopter pattern (`src/host/beachheadSnapshot.ts`, `src/host/beachheadMirror.ts`, `ViewSnapshot.beachheads`, `ViewMirror.beachheads` / `beachheadsVersion`): the table is sent only when its signature (rows plus cell counts) moved; the signature costs one search per row and nothing without rows. `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` got one line (its hand-made `SimView` needs the new field; nothing was weakened). The page: a pulsing ring in the owner colour in the SAME ring mesh and draw call as the selection ring (BEACH_RING_MAX 3 rings, a ring of `BEACH_RING_RADIUS_CELLS` 2.2 cells round the seed, every vertex on the ground; the index buffer is ordered beachhead rings first, then selection, then range, so one contiguous draw range shows any combination; the pulse is one uniform from the fx clock; no new draw call, `e2e/cityOpenFit.test.ts` still counts 7), a chip in the second row of the top bar ("Brohuvud 11/80 celler, 34 s kvar" with a tooltip that says what to do; written at most once a second, hidden without a row), and two toasts. The toasts are made from the table like `MissionWatcher` (the page gets no outbox): a row that vanished with a neutral seed cell REVERTED; a row that vanished while the seed is still ours is ESTABLISHED unless another row of ours remains (then it was a merge and nothing is said); a seed taken by somebody else says nothing. The sim has no "established" event; that is the cost of not touching the sim (BACKLOG "Fix-4B leftovers"). PREVIEW 17ao and 17as are rewritten (the landing takes 1 to 9 cells, the rest of the load attacks on, an isolated beachhead under 80 cells without a City returns after 45 s, how to make it established, the ring, the chip, the toasts). A 10-troop landing in `e2e/beachhead.test.ts` took 11 cells (the landing plus the attack of the rest of the load), so the e2e asserts "1 to 79", not "1 to 9".
2. **[2.6] Garbage per frame: bigger than the audit said, measured, fixed.** `e2e/deployAlloc.serial.test.ts` reads V8's own heap figure after every frame (`Runtime.getHeapUsage` over CDP; `performance.memory` is bucketed and reads a constant per frame, the first try) at 320x180 in three states of one match. Before: the deploy mode with a resting cursor allocated 155 to 156 kB a frame against 18 to 21 kB for a selected Factory (two runs: baseline 43.0 / 37.8 kB, selected 18.0 / 21.0 kB, deploy 156.1 / 155.2 kB), so about +137 kB a frame: the audit's strings and literals are tens of bytes, the pick (`pickGroundAt`: the ray, the hit, the cell object, run by `DeployMode.tick` every frame) is the bulk. After: baseline 35.4 / 38.7 kB, selected 20.9 / 17.2 kB, deploy 17.6 / 19.2 kB, so deploy over selected is -3 / +2 kB (the noise of the reading); the test allows 12 kB, and the render itself allocates 20 to 40 kB a frame (three.js, the ownership flush), which is outside this slice (BACKLOG). The changes: (a) `DeployMode.tick` picks only when `DeployTarget.cameraMoved()` says so (`CameraRig.poseChanged(out)`: seven numbers compared, no allocation; a pointer move still picks at once); (b) `LaunchPreviewer` compares the four numbers of a request (`sameRequest`) instead of building `requestKey` strings (`requestKey` stays exported for logs; `want()` is a plain assignment); returning to a request whose answer is still held shows it at once, as before; (c) `heliInfoOf`, the marker key and the rows are compared as integers; the deploy block's model is one object rewritten in place; `ownHeliRows` reuses its row objects and is asked only when the tick, the table version or the Factory moved; (d) `DeployBox.update` compares its numbers one by one (no `sig` array and no `join`) and the list the same way; (e) `HeliLayer.recordDrawn` (default true, so the unit tests are unchanged) is switched off by the page outside test mode, so the table rebuild makes no list. Mutant R1 (always record) survives by design: the list is unobservable except as garbage. Deviation from the Time rule in `CLAUDE.md` ("heap asserts use heapDelta"): the test measures ALLOCATION per frame, which a gc-before-and-after retained-heap reading cannot see, so it reads V8's exact figure after each frame and asserts a difference between states of one page (a raw read of the page's own memory number is what `tests/rawHeap.test.ts` forbids and what the test does not use).
3. **[1.6] The app layer: checked against HEAD.** P07 to P16 (previewer), R05 to R08 (resolver), K17 to K20 (hotkeys) and M05 / M11 / M12 (deploy mode) were TEST HOLES, the code was already right (the failed query frees `inflight`, `mode` is in the request, `clear()` bumps the epoch and resets the throttle, the ETA rounds up, a freed slot plans nothing, own sensors are skipped, the Factory rule is `context` only, `quiet` is not prevented, `listed()` drops the hidden keys, L and D carry `noRepeat`, F2 is guarded, a touch move and a long press are not taken). They are pinned now (`tests/app/launchPreview.test.ts` appended, `tests/app/appFaults.test.ts`). ONE real fault, found by the first test: `attachHotkeys` guarded itself with a module-wide flag, so only the first target in a process ever got the listener (a fake `EventTarget` in a test never did, which is why the function had no unit reference); it is a `WeakSet` of targets now (the page has one target, so nothing changes there). The test failed before the fix (`expected [] to equal ['x']`). The previewer's own comparison note: with string keys a request that came back after another one found its held answer again; the number compare keeps that (a test pins it).
4. **[1.7] Transport and pure UI functions** (`tests/host/heliTransport.test.ts`): `snapshotHelis` -> `MirrorHelis` for every field and slot, `heliSignature` moves for each of the ten fields (two slots each) and the totals, `snapshotDiplomacy` -> `MirrorDiplomacy` (relations with end ticks, embargoes, cooldowns, proposals, and a newer table REPLACES the older one), every code of `diplomacyRefusalKey` has its own key (14 codes, 14 keys, both tables), `heliStateKey` names every state, `padHeliOf`, `ownHeliRows` (fuel percent, Recall), `heliPanelInfo` (queue per Factory, rounded up, at least 1 s), `MissionWatcher` (first look, a reused id, once per version) and `HeliWatcher` (first look, growth alone is no news).
5. **[1.8] `cityFitFlight` computed** (`tests/app/cityFitProject.test.ts`): a pinhole projection of a ring through the camera the flight returns; the ring must lie inside the free part (strictly, at 960x540, 1366x768, 540x960, a panel exactly a third wide, a window 200 px high, a wide window with a bottom sheet, tilts 0, 25, 50), the City must stand in the middle of the free part (within 4 % of its size), and where the fit decides the altitude the ring must fill 0.32 to 1 of the free part (the vertical case at most 0.6). Two things came out. (a) A REAL, small fault, fixed: the vertical centring was first order (`-sy D tanHalf / cosT`); the exact solution is `-sy D tanHalf / (cosT - sy tanHalf sinT)` and the first order left the City 46 px (5 % of the window) off the middle of a bottom sheet on a phone; after the fix the lowest district of `e2e/cityOpenFit.test.ts` stands at y 260 of a 318 px free part instead of 308 (all five inside, both before and after). (b) A FINDING, left alone on purpose: `needD` counts `ringExtentM` against HALF the free span (`fw / (width / 2)`), so the ring that really fits is `ringExtentM / 2` of plain ground (1 200 m); a ring of the full 2 400 m would overflow the free part up to 2x (-272 px at 960x540) in a plain pinhole picture. The real five districts do fit (`e2e/cityOpenFit.test.ts`, which projects the district CENTRES; the structure scale policy enlarges the models with the distance, which is what the factor 2 stands for), the number is calibrated, and fixing the formula would double the end altitude of every open-city flight (3 593 -> 7 186 m at 960x540, 5 202 -> 10 404 m at 1366x768) for a change nobody asked for; the test pins the formula's own geometry with a ring of `ringExtentM / 2` and says so (BACKLOG "Fix-4B leftovers").
6. **[2.0] `PLAYER_FACING` discovers its files** (`tests/i18n.test.ts`): every TypeScript file under `src/ui` and `src/app` except the developer tools (the Dev panel and `src/app/modes.ts`, its registry of `?` modes: Swedish only, D98; the debug, perf and probe files; the report) and the tables (`src/ui/i18n.ts`); the old list stays as a floor the discovery may not fall below, and a test shows that a Swedish literal in a file nobody listed is found, in all three quote styles, and a comment is not. The first run found only `src/app/modes.ts`, a developer tool, now on the explicit list; no player-facing file hid Swedish text.
7. **[2.5] The gzip text** of M13b (item 9 above) is corrected in place, and `docs/BACKLOG.md` "M13b leftovers" lists the real candidates.

## Fix-4C: docs that lied and were too large (audit 3 findings 2.8 and 2.9; docs only, no source change)

1. **Checked against HEAD c672abe first.** Both findings were still open: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` was 199 kB with rows up to 4.4 kB (the Read tool refuses it in one call), its "Aktuella versioner" line said ENGINE 14 / FORMAT 12 while the code says ENGINE_VERSION 18 (`src/sim/state.ts`) and FORMAT_VERSION 13 (`src/sim/serialize.ts`), and `docs/PROGRESS.md` listed M12a and M20a-lite as next although both are merged.
2. **The split.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` keeps ONE row per module (path, a cut purpose, a cut API list, a link in a fourth column); the full rows were moved verbatim, in the old order, to `docs/arch/<area>.md` with areas sim, ai, host, render, ui, app, data, tools, tests. The long heading line of the module table is in `docs/arch/history.md`; the sections Sim flow, Test hooks, Test layout, Chunk terrain against the fixed mesh and Fix-UI additions were moved verbatim into the sim, tests, render and ui files. Nothing was deleted: the index (about 40 kB) plus the area files hold every old character (the index rows are cut copies of the moved ones). The row area is decided by the first path of the row, so a row that spans two areas lives in the area of its first file. New milestone text goes into the area file, the index row stays short.
3. **Tests** (`tests/docs.test.ts`): the backtick-path and gitignored-folder checks now also run over every file in `docs/arch/`; new tests cap `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` at 46 000 bytes and 1 300 characters per line, cap module rows at 450 characters with a link to an existing area file, require every area file to start with "# Architecture detail" and to be linked from the index, and compare the version line with the constants in the code. Mutant check (reasoned, not run against a rebuilt file): the OLD 199 kB file fails the size cap, the line cap and the row cap; the old "ENGINE_VERSION 14" line fails the version test; a new area file the index does not link fails the link test.
4. **CLAUDE.md** Read order point 3 now names the index and `docs/arch/` (115 lines, the limit is 200). `docs/CHARTER.md` is untouched.
5. **PROGRESS.md.** Done rows added for M12a-1/2/3, M13a-1, M13b, M18c-1/2, M20a-lite, Fix-3A1/3A2/3B/3C (merge SHAs from `git log --merges`) and a row "Fix-4 (pågår)"; the finished passes were moved out of Now to the end of the Archive word for word; Next now names M12b, M13a-2, M20a-2, M18b, M18d, M18e, M13c, M14 onwards and audit 4 after five more pushes.

## Audit 3 downstream decisions (for M12b, M14, M17, M20b)

Audit 3 findings 2.2, 2.3 and 2.4. All four were checked against HEAD c672abe and are still open (no `navOf(state)` accessor on `StateView`, no `hostile(a, b)` predicate, `SimView.helis` unfiltered, `LaunchPreviewer` not generic). No code is changed here; each point says what the named milestone's prompt MUST do.

(a) **The AI needs the state's own Nav (before M17).** `scoreLz`, `planRoute` and `validateLaunchHeli` take a `Nav` that the caller builds, and `src/ai` may only import `src/sim/public.ts`, so the AI cannot call them. DECISION: `StateView` (the AI's view inside the sim thread) gets a read-only accessor `navOf(state)` returning the `Nav` the state owns (`GameState.env`, built once per match); a `ViewMirror` (the page) returns `null` because it has no terrain table of the sim. The AI treats `null` as "no helicopter planning". M12b MUST add the accessor (and its line in the pinned export list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`) before any AI helicopter move; M17 must not start without it.

(b) **Hostility is a relation, not "another seat".** `planRoute`, `scoreLz` (`ENEMY_CITY_NEAR`) and the route preview now treat EVERY other seat as hostile. DECISION: one predicate `hostile(a, b)` (true unless the relation is truce or alliance, read from `SimView.diplomacy`) is added to `NavView` / `LosSource` and used by `planRoute`, `scoreLz`, the preview and M12b's SAM rule: an ally's SAM is not avoided and does not shoot at an ally's helicopter. M12b MUST add the predicate and these four uses, with a test per use that fails when the predicate is replaced by "other seat".

(c) **Helicopter visibility is per viewer.** `SimView.helis` is the global truth and the layer draws everybody's helicopters, so an AI's helicopters are visible to the human and the reverse, and low-altitude stealth has no effect. DECISION: filter at the `SimView` boundary with a per-viewer visibility mask: the host computes ONCE per tick, for each viewer, the exposure of the viewer's sensors at each helicopter's cell and flight level (own helicopters always visible), and both the `ViewMirror` and the AI's `StateView` read that mask instead of the raw table. M12b MUST add it (and the test that a low helicopter behind a ridge is absent from the enemy's view and present in the owner's).

(d) **The page's channels.** `SimHost.query` was replaced in M13b by plain functions plus an injected async query inside `LaunchPreviewer`; CHARTER invariant 6 and M15b step 1 still read as if `SimHost.query` existed. DECISION: a builder does not change `docs/CHARTER.md`; this block is the decision and BACKLOG has the line "proposed CHARTER change, for the user". The page has no outbox events (it only gets tables), so the one-tick facts that M14 / M15a need (detonation, cut-off) and M20b's alarms are lost unless handled. DECISION: `ViewSnapshot` gets a capped, drained `events` array (`kind`, `tick`, `a`, `b`) filled from the outbox, rather than keeping a table row alive for the effect length (about 50 ticks). M14 MUST add it and its cap; M15b step 1 MUST make `LaunchPreviewer` generic (`<Req, Res>` plus a key function) before it adds a second previewer.

## M12b: air defence against helicopters and raid statistics (ENGINE 20, FORMAT 14)

Headless slice; decision (b) of "Audit 3 downstream decisions" is done here, (a) and (c) are left to M17 (BACKLOG "M12b").

**Rules.**
1. `hostile(a, b)` (`src/sim/hostile.ts`): false for the same party, a truce and an alliance, true otherwise. It reads the optional `diplomacy` window of the view (`NavView.diplomacy?`; the state's own `StateNavView` carries a `DiplomacyReader` on the live table). A view without it (a fixture, an old test view) treats everybody as hostile, which is the war default, so the nav fingerprints and every LZ / route test are unchanged. Used by `planRoute` (the sensors it avoids and counts), `scoreLz` (`ENEMY_CITY_NEAR`), `previewLaunch` (the red waypoints; the ONE line touched in `src/app/launchPreview.ts`) and the guns. The guns read the live table every tick, so a treaty signed or broken in mid-flight acts from the next tick; a route that was planned before is not re-planned (a flight keeps its line).
2. Defense Post: `POST_AA_DPS` 15 = 15 tenths of a hit point per tick on the helicopter's 1000, within `POST_AA_RADIUS_CELLS` (= `POST_RADIUS_CELLS` 12, no new number), both flight modes, stacking: one Post fells a full helicopter in 67 ticks (6.7 s), two in 34. The pad repairs hit points (`setDown` sets `hp` to `HELI_HP_TENTHS`; before M12b nothing could hurt one).
3. SAM site: a hostile, airborne (LIFT to RETURN, not PAD / BUILDING / DOWNED) helicopter is a valid target when the owner's layer detects the cell (`Los.detected`, Low only above the mask, High in range) AND the site's own mask lets it fire (`Los.fireControl`, the range is `samRadiusCells(level, mountain)`, the existing level curve; the hit chance is flat). A site fires when `tick >= salvoTick` (the first as soon as a target is valid) at the lowest-id valid target, sets `salvoTick = tick + SAM_SALVO_PERIOD_TICKS` (90) and rolls `samRoll(seed, tick, samId)` < `SAM_HIT_PCT` (55): three nested `splitmix32Mix`, no PRNG state. The nominal Low / High altitude of the LOS maths (ground + 300 / 3000 m) is used, as the route planner does, not the exact flown altitude (a flown Low helicopter is at or above the nominal one over ridges, so the sites are slightly pessimistic for the attacker only over a crest).
4. Order independence: every decision of a tick comes from the state at the start of the phase (helicopters in ascending id; each site picks the lowest valid id); only then salvos are spent, rolls made and kills and damage applied (a SAM kill wins over Post wear in the same tick). `airDefensePhase(state, reverse)` visits the structures backwards for the test; the hashes are equal tick by tick over 600 ticks and several kills.
5. Spatial hash: `AaIndex` (counting-sort layout in typed arrays, 32-cell buckets, x wraps), rebuilt each tick that a helicopter is airborne (a scan of the structure table up to its high-water mark), kept in `state.env.aa` (derived, never hashed). Cost: 42 us median (12 helicopters, 40 structures, the budget is `budgetMs(0.5)` = 1092 us at the sandbox factor 2.18).
6. State: `StructureTable.salvoTick` (u32 per row, reset on add / remove, kept by a conquest; serialised as the 9th word of a structure record, so `BYTES_PER_STRUCTURE` 32 -> 36, FORMAT 13 -> 14; hashed; `deserialize` refuses a clock on anything but a SAM or more than one period ahead of the saved tick: `structure-range`). ENGINE 19 -> 20 once; `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` recomputed once with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` (the Node run and the worker run read the same files; `tests/sim/worker.test.ts` is unchanged).
7. `HELI_DOWNED` causes in the event's `b`: `HELI_DOWN_FUEL` 1 (as before), `HELI_DOWN_SAM` 2, `HELI_DOWN_POST` 3. The load is lost (`downHeli` sets it to 0), nothing is painted, no beachhead.

**Deviations from the prompt.** (1) One line in `src/app/launchPreview.ts` (decision (b) names the preview colour; the prompt forbids `src/app`): the order of the two wins, the line is minimal. (2) The toast texts were NOT added: the page sees the helicopter table, not the event, so it cannot tell fuel from SAM from Post; adding unused keys would fail `tests/i18n.test.ts`. The existing toast "En helikopter sköts ner." stays (BACKLOG "M12b"). (3) Test helpers changed because the change provably causes the difference: `tests/helpers/saveLayout.ts` (`BYTES_PER_STRUCTURE` 36) and `tests/helpers/heliFlights.ts` (the three enemy SAM sites of the M12a-2 flight-physics property now hold fire, `salvoTick = 0xffffffff`, because they were there to shape routes and the property is about flight physics, not survival). `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts` gets the ten new exports of `src/sim/public.ts` (an appended block); the version pins of `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts` and `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts` move to ENGINE 20 / FORMAT 14. (4) The CLI is a sub-command of `sim:scenario` (`heli-raid ...`), not a new npm script.

**Raid statistics** (`raidStats`, 50 seeded trials, flight mode Low unless stated; `tests/sim/airDefense.test.ts` asserts the three targets; no constant was tuned, so zero of the two allowed rounds were used):

| Fixture | Low | High | SAM kills (Low) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `alps` (real map, no air defence; seeds 1, 2, 3 all give 50/50) | 50/50 = 100 % (target >= 80 %) | not run | 0 |
| `plains-sam` (two level-1 SAM sites that cover the zone and all of the last 12 cells) | 1/50 = 2 % (target <= 30 %) | 8/50 = 16 % | 49 |
| `ridge-sam` (ridge between zone and site) | 50/50 = 100 % (98 points above the plains, target >= 30) | 20/50 = 40 % | 0 |

A raid succeeds when the load comes down: a landing that took at least one cell, or a beachhead formed. A raid that is not shot down flies on to the pad in the replay but the statistics stop at the landing. `ridge-sam` Low is never covered because the 2000 m ridge hides the whole approach from the site, High is seen over it (range only) and dies 60 % of the time (more than one salvo during the hover).

**Mutants** (each applied alone to the code, `tests/sim/airDefense.test.ts` run, every one failed except the last, which is equivalent):

| no `fireControl` (the site's own mask) | "fire control is the SAM's OWN mask" |
| every helicopter counts as High | Low behind the ridge, and the 50-trial statistics |
| guns shoot allies (`atWar` check removed) | "treaties silence the guns" |
| route: `o === seat + 1` instead of `hostile()` | route test and preview test |

| destroyed site: index keeps removed ids | survives: equivalent, the index test `type` already excludes a removed row (`type` is 0) |

(The first version of the hit-rate test imported `SAM_HIT_PCT` for its window, so the 70 mutant passed; the test now holds the literals 0.52 and 0.58 of the plan, found by this table.)

## M18b-1

Federations part 1 of 3: the baked provinces and sub-nations ("delnationer"). Headless; no sim, ai, render, ui or app change; e2e/goldens untouched.

1. **Source.** Natural Earth `ne_50m_admin_1_states_provinces.geojson` (2.3 MB; the 10 m file is 12 MB) at the same commit `ca96624a56bd078437bca8184e78163e5039ad19`, fetched from the allowed host raw.githubusercontent.com (it needed `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` in this sandbox; the bake cache is gitignored). The 50 m file has 294 provinces for nine countries only: RUS 85, USA 51, IND 36, IDN 33, CHN 31, BRA 27, CAN 13, AUS 9, ZAF 9. Other countries have no provinces and can never be federations (the 10 m file would add more, backlog). `public/data/meta.json` is NOT changed (its file list would change its hash), so the admin-1 file is named in CREDITS.md and here.
2. **Pack.** `public/data/federations.bin.gz` (27 kB gz): header, country table, province table (id, country index, key = iso_3166_2 else name, English name, cells, centroid cell, tiny flag), sub-nation table (id, country index, name, cells, centroid, province ids), u16 province raster, u8 sub-nation raster. The format is in the comment of `src/data/federations.ts`. manifest.json gets `federations`, checksums.json a new row `packs.federations`; all old rows are byte-identical (the diff of both files has only added lines), `bake:check` also parses the pack. `npm run bake -- --only=federations` rebuilds just the new pack and the two index files from the committed sim pack; a full `npm run bake` adds it too.
3. **Province raster.** Polygons are filled on the sim grid (cell centre rule of `fillPolygon`); a province cell must lie on a land cell of the province's own country. Gaps (narrow borders, coast differences) are filled by a breadth-first flood from the painted cells in index order with the neighbour order west, east, north, south, inside the same country; cells no flood reaches (islands without a polygon) go to the nearest province centroid of that country. Every land cell of a country with provinces has exactly one province.
4. **Candidates.** `FEDERATION_MIN_AREA_CELLS` = 2000 (1.6 % of the 124 869 land cells without Antarctica) AND provinces in the file. Measured land cells: RUS 23782, CAN 13859, USA 9019, CHN 7721, BRA 5737, AUS 5645, IND 2256, (KAZ 2653 and ARG 2258 have no provinces), IDN 1229, ZAF 924. Result: Russia, Canada, USA, China, Brazil, Australia, India (a 4 % line would have dropped India).
5. **Sub-nations.** Provinces with at least one cell are merged: while the smallest group has fewer than `FEDERATION_MIN_PROVINCE_CELLS` (300, about 1.5 times the fair start K = 197) cells or there are more than `FEDERATION_MAX_SUBNATIONS` (12) groups, the smallest group joins its smallest land neighbour (ties: longer shared border, then lower id); a group with no land neighbour (Hawaii) joins the nearest group by province centroids. Names: the biggest province name, "A and B" for two, "A Region" for three or more (English only). Ids are global, 1 to 64, in country order, biggest first.
6. **Report** (`npm run bake:federations`, pasted):

```
RUS Russia: 23782 cells, 85 provinces -> 12 sub-nations
    1 Sakha Republic (4978 cells, 1 provinces)
    2 Krasnoyarsk Krai (3998 cells, 1 provinces)
    3 Khabarovsk Krai Region (2372 cells, 5 provinces)
    4 Irkutsk Region (1758 cells, 7 provinces)
    5 Arkhangelsk Region (1641 cells, 3 provinces)
    6 Khanty-Mansi Autonomous Okrug Region (1489 cells, 4 provinces)
    7 Kirov Region (1474 cells, 31 provinces)
```
