Equivalent mutants (found, not killed, and why): rounding the percent shortening up instead of down in `heliBuildTicksOf` and `techTicksWithLab` (120 and every tech time times a multiple of 5 percent is a whole number, so floor and ceil agree; the floor is kept so a future percent is never longer than the base); the serialize type guard `type > STRUCT_TYPE_SLOTS` instead of `>=` (a type 10 is still refused by the next guard of the same line, the ready tick check against `BUILD_TICKS[type]`).
## M17-pre

Headless slice (sim view, host snapshot, tests); decisions (a) and (c) of "Audit 3 downstream decisions" are built here, before the AI helicopters (M17) and the minimap (M20b). No rule change: ENGINE and FORMAT unchanged, both golden hash files untouched, `docs/BALANCE.md` has no new row (there is no new number).

**What was built.**
1. `SimView.navOf(): Nav | null` (no argument: the view owns its state). A `StateView` returns `navOf(state)` of `src/sim/access.ts`, the very object the state builds once (`GameState.env`), and null for a world without `maxElev` (the accessor of `navOf` of `src/sim/access.ts` throws there). A `ViewMirror` returns null: the AI reads null as "no helicopter planning". `Nav` was already in the pinned export list; the new pinned export is the type `HeliSight`.
2. `src/sim/heliVisibility.ts`. For a viewer seat the rows it may see: own rows always; rows of owners that are not at war with the viewer (`atWar` false: ally, federation member (a bound alliance), truce partner) always; a hostile row that is airborne (LIFT to RETURN) or a wreck (DOWNED) only when `Los.detected(viewer, cell, mode)` holds, the very call the SAM sites make (Low only above the composite mask, High anywhere in range); a hostile row under production or parked on its pad never (it sits inside a Factory, the sensors look at the air). No occluder layer means no sensors: hostile rows are hidden. The mask is lazy and cached by a fingerprint of everything the rule reads (tick, relation table version, structure table version, high-water and live counts, the helicopter rows): it is recomputed exactly when one of them moved, so a relation signed in a test within one tick is seen at once and a real match recomputes once per tick. It is derived data (a field of `state.env`), never hashed or serialised. `StateView.visibleHelis(seat)` reads it.
3. Host: `MainThreadHostOptions.viewerSeat`. With it, `snapshotHelis` / `heliSignature` take the viewer's `HeliSight`: a hidden row is sent as a free slot (all fields 0, not in `counts` or `liveCount`), so the page never receives it and ids stay in place. The signature folds only visible rows, so a relation or sensor change with a standing helicopter still makes a diff. `MatchSession.fromMatch` passes the human seat; `MatchSession.start` (the old test match) and `debugSim` pass none and send the whole table. `ViewMirror.visibleHelis` lists every row of its (already filtered) table.
4. Page: `HeliLayer`, `HeliFx`, the list (`ownHeliRows`), the chase camera and `MissionWatcher` needed NO change. They already treat owner 0 as a free slot (the layer resets `lastX`, the blend reference is cleared by `slotOn`, the fx reset their accumulators on an owner change), and every own-seat reader filters by owner, and own rows are always visible, so a shot-down own helicopter stays in the table as a wreck for its 50 ticks and the loss notice fires as before (tested). A helicopter that leaves sight therefore vanishes at once (no fade; a soft fade is in BACKLOG). The prompt's wish to touch `src/render/heliLayer.ts` ("only the filter") is thus met by the table being filtered upstream.

**Deviations.** (1) `navOf()` has no `state` argument. (2) Defense Post radii are NOT a sensor for the mask (the Posts shoot without detecting; a loop over the Posts per helicopter and viewer is the one non-constant cost): BACKLOG. (3) `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` builds a `SimView` literal; it got the two new members (`visibleHelis`, `navOf`) because the type change provably requires them; nothing else of it changed. (4) `src/app/match.ts` got one option on one line (`viewerSeat`); the bundle-diet track owns the boot code, the line is in `fromMatch` only. (5) `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` was at its size cap: the new row was paid for by shortening three other rows (no information lost that the area files lack except the glide sentence of the effects row, which `docs/arch/render.md` has).

**Costs.** Mask for 12 viewers x 64 helicopters with 36 SAM sites, recomputed from scratch: 0.08 ms median (the Los sync included), budget `budgetMs(0.5)` (`tests/heliVisibility.perf.test.ts`). The page's per-frame work is unchanged (fewer rows, if anything); no draw call added.

**Mutants** (each: change one line, run the file, see it fail, restore).

| Mutant | Killed by (`tests/sim/heliVisibility.test.ts` unless noted) |
|---|---|
| own rows no longer always visible (`o === me ||` removed) | ridge test, ally test, parked test, host test |
| allied / federation / truce rows no longer visible (`!atWar` removed) | ally / federation / truce test, relation-change test |
| the sensor check dropped (`detected` replaced by true) | ridge test, open-range test, ally test, relation test, host test |
| flight mode ignored (always Low) | ridge test (High in range is seen) |
| parked / building hostile rows run through the sensor check (`st < HELI_LIFT` guard off) | parked test |
| diplomacy version missing from the fingerprint | ally test (same-tick relation change) |
| the mask writes state (`hp[0] = 7`) | hash test (with and without readers) |
| host sends the whole table (`null` instead of the sight, full or diff) | host test |
| hidden row sent but not zeroed / counted | host test (owner, `countOf`, `liveCount`) |
| signature not the viewer's (both folds) | standing-helicopter relation test |
| `StateView.navOf` returns null | navOf test (the very object, same hash) |
| `src/ui/heliInfo.ts`: loss notice off | `tests/render/heliVanish.test.ts` mission test |
| `HeliLayer`: `slotOn` not cleared per rebuild | `tests/render/heliVanish.test.ts` |
| `HeliLayer`: blend guard `refOn` dropped | SURVIVES, equivalent: `slotOn` already clears `refOn` and the teleport radius guards the same case |

Single signature folds (liveCount alone, rows alone) survive by design: either one carries the change; the test kills the pair.

**Result.** `npm run verify:fast` green (typecheck, lint, unit 3 205 tests, 93 s). The first run failed two `tests/sim/sourceRules.test.ts` rules (no `WeakMap`, seat/owner conversion only in `src/sim/state.ts`): the cache moved into `SimEnv.heliVis` and the code uses `seatToOwner`; the tests were not touched. The perf file ran alone: 0.083 ms median.
## Bundle-diet-1 (diet steps 2 and 3 of "Bundle diet"; no rule change, ENGINE and FORMAT unchanged, goldens untouched)

Base af33a30. Measured with `npm run build` (only to measure), gzip level 9 per file, the first load being the script and the modulepreload chunks of `index.html`.

| Chunk | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| main | 314 964 | 306 484 |
| techRules (shared sim, exists once) | 38 063 | 38 063 |
| mainThreadHost (host plus AI) | 12 659 (first load) | 11 515 (lazy) |
| data, paths, public, params | 2 880 | 4 391 (params is now its own small chunk) |
| **first load** | **368 566** | **348 938** (-19 628 B, -5.3 %) |
| all chunks | 381 002 | 386 179 (+5 177: seven new lazy chunks) |

1. **What loads on demand.** The Dev panel and `registerAppModes` (`src/app/debugLoad.ts` `loadDevPanelModules`), the perf overlay and the performance scene with the report modal (`loadPerfModules`, only for `?perf`, `?scene=perf` and test mode, whose hooks `runPerfScene` / `perfReport` are synchronous), the spike scene (only `?scene=spike`), and `MainThreadHost`, `AiDriver` and `src/ai` (`MatchSession.create` / `fromMatch`, async already, so Play and Start load them). `hashBuiltMatch` moved to `src/app/matchHash.ts` (the `buildHash` hook is async); the one unit test that imported it from `src/app/match.ts` now imports it from `matchHash.ts` (an import path, nothing weakened) and `matchHash.ts` joined the two lists that allow a file to build a sim (`eslint.config.js`, `tests/boundary.test.ts`). A failed import shows a Swedish error (dev tools are Swedish only, D98): the panel through an alert and "Dev: fel" on the button, the scene through the usual error text.
2. **Deviations from the prompt.** (a) The first load is -19.6 kB, not -25: `frameStats`, `gpuTimer` and `detailProbe` stay (the live loop, the adaptive resolution and the synchronous probe hooks use them; BACKLOG). (b) A normal page fetches the panel chunks (about 3.4 kB gz) when the browser is idle, because `e2e/hud.test.ts` presses the Dev button and asserts the panel at once (it failed when the panel was loaded only on the click); before the idle fetch has run, a stand-in button with the same attributes and the F2 listener load it on demand. The idle fetch is not part of the first load. (c) The F3 key toggles the small text overlay of `src/app/main.ts`, not the perf overlay, so it loads nothing. (d) `?test=1` loads the panel and the perf modules before the first scene start, so every `__test` hook and the F2 tests behave as before; no e2e test was changed.
3. **Caps.** `JS_GZ_MAIN_MAX` 321 000 to 315 700 (measured 306 484 + 3 %). `JS_GZ_TOTAL_MAX` stays 391 000: measured 386 179 + 3 % would be 397 800, a raise, which the rule forbids; the sum of all chunks cannot fall by lazy loading (it rose 5 177 B), only the first load does. New `JS_GZ_START_MAX` 359 500 (348 938 + 3 %). Diet step 1 (language tables) is what lowers the total.
4. **Shared sim.** `techRules` is one chunk used by the page and by the lazy host chunk (the portable test counts it once and caps the host chunk at 13 000 B, so a copied sim of about 38 kB fails).
5. **Time and drawing.** Chromium on this machine against `vite preview` on localhost (3 runs, logged only): first contentful paint about 150 ms before and after, ready (Spela visible) about 890 ms before and 900 to 1000 ms after, test mode first frame 865 ms before and 916 ms after (one more round trip for the lazy dev chunks; localhost has no network cost, so the byte saving does not show). `renderer.info` after one test render: 3 calls, 37 184 triangles, 9 textures, before and after.
6. **e2e** (alone): artifact 9, hud 19, setup 18, perfScene 3, devpanel 10 passed. `e2e/artifact.test.ts` unchanged and green with the extra chunks.

Mutants (each run on its own test file and restored):

| `import './devpanel'` added to `src/app/main.ts` | `tests/portable.test.ts` "devpanel must be a chunk of its own" |

## M13c-1: sea transport (ENGINE 25 to 26, FORMAT 16 to 17; headless, no render, no UI, no AI)

Defaults chosen without asking (the builder could not ask; each can be changed in one place):

1. **A ship is a row made by LaunchShip, there is no production.** A finished Port sends one ship at a time: `SHIP_LAUNCH_GOLD` and the troops are taken at once. The Port is "busy" while its ship is out (SAIL, LAND, RETURN) and "cooling down" while the docked ship sits as a row in state `SHIP_OFF` for `SHIP_COOLDOWN_TICKS`; no per-Port cooldown word was added to the structure table. A docked row counts in the fleet cap (`SHIP_FLEET_MAX` 4 per owner).
2. **Table.** `ShipTable` (`src/sim/shipTable.ts`), SoA, 32 slots. The free list is "the lowest free slot" (as the beachhead table), so nothing but the live rows is stored and hashed. Serialised in the air block after the beachheads (FORMAT 17: a count word, then 48 bytes plus the route per live row), hashed by `foldShips` after `foldHelis`. The route is kept IN the row (48 cells), the simplest thing that is hashed.
3. **Route.** A* over DEEP_SEA and SHALLOWS cells (the sea and the lakes of the baked world) with 8 neighbours, no corner cutting, integer costs 10 / 14, a binary heap of two `Int32Array`s ordered by (f, cell index) so that a tie goes to the lower index (`src/sim` bans `Float64Array`), x wraps. At most `SHIP_ROUTE_MAX_CELLS` expansions. The path is string-pulled into at most `SHIP_ROUTE_CAPACITY` (48) waypoints by a Bresenham test over water; a route that needs more is refused as REJECT_SHIP_NO_ROUTE (BACKLOG). The start is the lowest-index sea neighbour of the Port; the goal is any sea cell within `SHIP_LAND_REACH_CELLS` (2, Chebyshev) of the landing cell. A landing cell must be playable, not the owner's own, and have sea within reach (REJECT_SHIP_BAD_TARGET).
4. **Flight time.** `SHIP_CELLS_PER_TICK_Q` 32 Q8 cells per tick (0.125 cells per tick, 60 percent of a Low helicopter); positions are Q8 on the polyline with `isqrt`, as for helicopters; the return runs back along the same waypoints. A straight 46-cell crossing: 368 ticks sailing, 20 unloading (landing at +387), 368 back (docked at +756), the row gone at +1056 (`tests/sim/shipFlight.test.ts` pins these literals).
5. **Landing = the helicopter's landing.** `performLanding` was split: `landTroops(state, ownerId, lz, load, landedKind, unit)` in `src/sim/heliLanding.ts` holds the whole body (price per cell with the enemy doubling, 1 to 9 cells, beachhead row, no row next to own land, the cut-off cluster, the rest of the load as an Attack, the eliminated-owner guard, `landingBlockedBy`) and returns whether the load stays aboard; `performLanding` is a three-line wrapper. The helicopter tests are unchanged and pass; `tests/sim/shipFlight.test.ts` compares a ship landing with `performLanding` of a helicopter with the same load on the same cell (owner array, troops, gold, beachhead rows, conquered statistic equal). In the test worlds even a load of 30 troops buys all nine cells of an enemy block, so the price rarely limits a ship; a coast landing loses the cells that are water (6 of 9 at a straight shore).
6. **Beachhead table shared.** A ship that sails or unloads will want a row, like a helicopter in the air: `checkLaunchShip` and (a one-line change in `probeOf` of `src/sim/heliFlight.ts`) the helicopter probe count the pending landings of both kinds against the free rows; REJECT_HELI_BEACHHEAD_FULL (58) is the code for both. No behaviour change for helicopters unless ships exist.
7. **A treaty under way.** If a truce or an alliance now binds the owner to the owner of the landing cell, a ship in state SAIL or LAND turns back at once (`OUTBOX_SHIP_RTB`, b = 1) and hands the load back at the home Port. (A helicopter only notices at the landing; the prompt asked for the earlier turn-back for ships.)
8. **A lost Port.** A Port conquered or deleted under way does not stop the voyage: the ship sails on and lands, then is scrapped instead of returning (`OUTBOX_SHIP_SCRAPPED`, b = 2). A Port that is lost while the ship is on its way home scraps it on arrival and the load still aboard is lost. A Port counts as the home Port only when it is a finished Port of the same owner on the same cell (a recycled structure id does not).
9. **An eliminated owner.** `winPhase` calls `scrapOwnerShips` next to `scrapOwnerHelis` (b = 1, load lost); `landTroops` keeps its own guard; `deserialize` refuses an eliminated owner that still has a ship.
10. **No air defence shoots at ships in v1; every ship is visible to every viewer (no `visibleShips`).** Both in `docs/BACKLOG.md`.
11. **Events.** `OUTBOX_SHIP_LAUNCHED` 16, `_LANDED` 17, `_RETURNED` 18, `_SCRAPPED` 19, and `_RTB` 20 (a turn for home; the prompt named four, the fifth is how a turn-back by treaty is announced). Events-1 forwards unknown kinds.
12. **View and host.** `SimView.ships` (`ShipView`: owner, state, port, stateTick, Q8 x y, load, target, routeLen, routeIdx, `routeCell`, `cellOf`, `countOf`), a StateView reads the table, a `ViewMirror` carries a copy; `src/host/shipSnapshot.ts` (rows in slot order, a signature that decides when the host resends) and `src/host/shipMirror.ts`; `ViewSnapshot.ships` is OPTIONAL (absent = null) so no existing literal changes. 32 rows at most, 48 route cells each, sent only when the signature moves (every tick while a ship sails).
13. **Codes.** `SHIP_REASON_BASE` 120, `REJECT_SHIP_*` 121..133 (no port, not owner, port building, busy, cooldown, fleet cap, troops low, troops high, seat has too few, no gold, bad target, no route, table full); a treaty is REJECT_DIPLO_TREATY_BOUND (90) and a full beachhead table 58, as for a helicopter. `validateLaunchShip(view, seat, port, targetCell, troops)` in `src/sim/public.ts` runs the same `checkLaunchShip` as the sim (one code; `tests/sim/shipLaunch.test.ts` runs both paths on every case and compares the answers). A mirror answers like a StateView (the pending landings are counted from the tables the view carries).
14. **ENGINE 26 and FORMAT 17, the goldens once.** The state has a new table (its fold adds a word even when empty), so every hash moves: `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` were recomputed ONCE with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` (`tests/sim/golden.test.ts`, `tests/app/matchGolden.test.ts`) after the code was complete. Assumptions the change provably caused and that were updated, nothing weakened: the two version pins (`tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts`), the 4 extra bytes of the empty air block in the size formulas of `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts`, the first unknown wire kind (23 to 24) in `tests/sim/codec.test.ts`, the layout helper `tests/helpers/saveLayout.ts` (the ship count word sits between the beachheads and the structures), the `SimView` stub of `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` (a new member), and the pinned export list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`.
15. **Size caps.** The gzip total of `tests/portable.test.ts` was 391 000 and is 393 000 (measured 392 934 with the ship code in the shared and host chunks); the rule of "Bundle diet" 3 asks for a line naming the diet step that pays it back: step 1 (the language tables, about -8.8 kB, in the pass "Bundle diet 1-3" of `docs/BACKLOG.md`). The main chunk cap is unchanged.
16. **Not in this pass.** The golden scenario sends no LaunchShip (the ship rules have their own pinned replay: `ship-landing --seed 1` ends on hash `a5b6d6f3`, `tests/sim/shipLandingCli.test.ts`). The ship layer, the deploy mode for ships and the AI use are M13c-2 and later.

## Events-1: ViewSnapshot.events, a capped, drained side-fact list

Headless slice (host snapshot, mirror, read-only view surface, tests). Decision (d) of "Audit 3 downstream decisions" is built for the outbox kinds that exist; NO existing watcher (Tech, Diplo, Beachhead, Match, Mission, Heli) is migrated. No rule change: ENGINE and FORMAT unchanged, both golden hash files untouched, `docs/BALANCE.md` has no new row (`VIEW_EVENTS_CAP` is a transport limit in `src/sim/view.ts`, not a game number).

1. **Format.** `ViewEvent { kind, tick, a, b }` (integers). A `ViewSnapshot` carries `events: Int32Array` (4 integers per event, oldest first) and `eventsDropped`. The cap `VIEW_EVENTS_CAP` is 128 (2 KB per snapshot; room for a whole 64-helicopter fleet's facts in one tick). At overflow the OLDEST are dropped, the newest kept, and `eventsDropped` counts them. An empty frame shares one empty array (no allocation); a frame with facts allocates one small packed copy (it must be a copy: messages are copied or transferred).
2. **Drained, exactly once.** `MainThreadHost` pushes each step's facts into a preallocated ring (`src/host/viewEventRing.ts`); `buildView(false)` (the diff) packs and empties it, so a diff for many ticks (a paused tab catching up) holds every fact up to the cap. `requestView()` (the full view) neither carries nor drains events (a late mirror starts "now"; draining there would steal from the stream). `ViewMirror` appends each diff's facts to its own ring and keeps them until its single consumer calls `clearEvents()` (several frames before a look lose nothing, until the cap).
3. **Source.** Deny-list, not allow-list: everything except `OUTBOX_PAINT` (the diff's cells carry it) and `OUTBOX_REJECT` (the last-refusal fields carry it) goes, with the outbox's own `a` and `b`. A kind this build does not know is forwarded unchanged, so M13c-1's `OUTBOX_SHIP_*` flow without a change here. The host still drains the outbox with `Outbox.drain()` in the same place and order; `src/sim/outbox.ts` is untouched.
4. **Visibility.** For a host with `viewerSeat`: an event about a helicopter (`a` = helicopter id: queued, produced, scrapped, launched, landed, downed, rtb, home) of an owner that is the viewer or not at war with it is sent; of a hostile owner only when `heliVisibilityOf(state).sight(viewerSeat)` holds that helicopter at the end of that step. Evaluated per STEP, not once per snapshot (deviation from "in the same snapshot": it is exact for the tick of the event, and a helicopter that is seen when it is shot down is reported). A slot freed in the step (scrapped, downed wreck removed) is judged by its owner from before the step (`ViewEventFilter.beforeStep` copies the 64 owners); QUEUED / PRODUCED read the owner as it is now. An unknown owner hides the event. Facts that are not about a helicopter (beachheads, eliminated, capital lost, match end) are public, as the tables they mirror already are. No viewer seat (tests, debug pages) sends everything.
5. **Cost.** No draw call, no per-frame page work added. Host: 64 helicopter events per tick with the mask recomputed, filtered, pushed and packed: 0.18 ms median here, budget `budgetMs(0.5)` (`tests/viewEvents.perf.test.ts`, median of 40 after 5 warm-ups). Ring and filter build no typed array per event (`tests/host/viewEventRing.test.ts` counts `Int32Array` constructions over 6 000 ticks: 0; raw heap reads are banned in tests by `tests/rawHeap.test.ts`, so a heap number is not used).
6. **Deviations.** (a) `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` builds a `SimView` literal and got `events` because the type change requires it. (b) `eventsDropped` counts only the ring's cap, not facts the outbox itself refused (BACKLOG). (c) Visibility per step, see 4.

**Mutants** (each: change one line, run the two test files, see it fail, restore).

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|
| full ring drops the NEWEST instead of the oldest | `tests/host/viewEventRing.test.ts` "keeps order across the wrap" |
| paints pass the filter (`OUTBOX_PAINT` check removed) | `tests/host/viewEvents.test.ts` "delivers every side fact exactly once" |
| hostile helicopter events always sent (no mask) | "the enemy viewer gets only the neutral fact" |
| the diff does not empty the ring | "delivers every side fact exactly once" |
| a freed slot is judged by the table after the step | "a helicopter whose slot was freed in the step is judged by its owner before the step" |
| the mirror keeps only the last frame's facts | "collects the facts of several frames until the consumer clears them" |
| helicopter filter skipped for every kind | "the enemy viewer gets only the neutral fact" |
| a kind number above 19 is dropped (unknown kinds not forwarded) | "delivers every side fact exactly once" (kind 99) |
| `new Int32Array(1)` added to `ViewEventRing.push` | `tests/host/viewEventRing.test.ts` "allocates nothing per event" |
| `requestView` also returns (not drains) the ring | equivalent in a main-thread host: the ring is empty outside `runTicks`; not killable, kept as a guard in the code |

State hash with and without a listening mirror or a viewer seat: equal after 60 ticks ("the state does not know the readers"); the events never touch the state, so no mutant of the host can change it except by writing to it, which the lint boundary forbids.

`npm run verify:fast`: first run, typecheck and lint PASS, 300 of 301 unit files PASS; the one failure was my own heap read in `tests/host/viewEventRing.test.ts` (banned by `tests/rawHeap.test.ts`), replaced by the construction counter above. After the fix: typecheck, lint, `tests/rawHeap.test.ts`, `tests/docs.test.ts` and the new test files pass; the full stage was not run a second time (one run allowed, shared cores).
M18e-2
1. **One shared mesh, not three.** The Airfield, Lab and Plant are concatenated in ONE `InstancedBufferGeometry` (the mesh of the type that owns it, `shared: STRUCT_AIRFIELD`). Every vertex carries its model kind in the new attribute `aModel` (0 = always drawn), every instance its kind in `aBuild.z` (`aBuild` went from vec2 to vec3 in the layers and the ghost). The vertex shader multiplies the position by `step(abs(aModel - aBuild.z), 0.5)` where `aModel > 0`, so the other models collapse to degenerate triangles: seven model meshes + ghost + ring + icons = 10 draw calls, the budget of the Budgetar row (it was 9; the reserve call is used). Measured in `e2e/structuresAirLabPlant.test.ts`: 7 -> 8 draw calls when the three are built next to a Factory, a Silo and a City.
2. **What the budget counts.** The GPU processes all three models for every instance of the shared mesh (590 triangles, 1 770 vertices), so `trianglesCost(type)` and `trianglesDrawn` count 590 for each, not the type's own 154 / 208 / 228 (`trianglesOf(type)` stays the own model). `renderer.info` and the layer agree (the e2e asserts the delta of three instances is between 3 x 590 and 3 x 590 + 60). Cost on the integrated GPU: at most `STRUCT_TRIANGLE_BUDGET / 590` instances of these types in the test tier, and at most 6 + 3 + 3 per owner by the sim limits, so the vertex load is bounded. If a map ever holds dozens of them, split by kind with one mesh per type (BACKLOG).
3. **Alias test replaced.** The M18e-1 alias test (file structureModelsAlias.test.ts in tests/render) tested the M18e-1 alias (`alias`, `scale`, `ownerMul`), which this slice removes on purpose (it is the point of the slice). The file was renamed with `git mv` to `tests/render/structureModelsShared.test.ts` and rewritten for the new rule (its asserts that a type is modelled, that the mesh count equals the count of mesh owners + 3, that the ghost of a new type is allowed and an unmodelled type refused, and that the budget counts the instance, are kept in the new form). Paths of the old file in `docs/arch/render.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md` and `docs/DECISIONS.md` (M18e-1 block) point to the new file.
4. **Far icons.** `iconShapeOf(type)` is the type itself; the icon fragment shader has a shape per type 1 to 9: Airfield a plus, Lab an octagon, Plant a tall bar (Factory square, Silo triangle up, SAM triangle down, Post hexagon). Same draw call. The ring radius per type is `radiusM` (780, 560, 640 m); the old values (805, 504, 490) were the alias products.
5. **Night lamps.** The Lab (3) and the Plant (3) have flattened-octahedron lamps with tint code 4 (light number 1 of the City light system): lit on the night side from level 1, scaled to nothing by day, no extra buffer. The Airfield has none (conservative; its runway lights are in BACKLOG).
6. **Pictures** (test-results/shots/airlabplant-*.png, looked at with the Read tool): 3 km day and night, 1.5 km day and night, two owners, the Factory and the Silo as the first two panels, and the globe at 900, 2 600 and 9 000 km (icons). Deviation: the prompt asked for 800 m; a model is 1.5 km wide and up to 800 m tall, a camera 800 m away stands inside it (tried, unusable), so the near pictures are from about 1.5 km. No new golden.
7. **Found, not mine.** `__test.state().structures.byType` has no entries for types 7 to 9 (M18e-1 did not extend the hook, it prints null); the e2e reads `props().counts` instead. In BACKLOG.
8. **Heap:** `e2e/heap.test.ts` and `e2e/deployLeak.serial.test.ts` alone: green (spike render 0.099 MB). The old structure e2e files (`structures`, `structuresMilitary`, `structuresFactoryPort`, `structuresSelect`, `farIcons`) alone: green, their goldens match (0, 94, 91, 0 px differ, as before).

| the budget cost of a shared type is its own model's triangles | `tests/render/structureModelsShared.test.ts` "the budget counts the shared mesh by what it submits" |

## Round 22 integration (orchestrator)

Merge order M13c-1 (ENGINE 26, FORMAT 17), Events-1, M18e-2; conflicts only in the shared docs (both sides kept), in `src/host/mainThreadHost.ts` and `src/host/protocol.ts` (the ship table and the event ring both ride in the diff: the snapshot carries `ships` and `events`/`eventsDropped`, merged by hand) and in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (two tracks had shortened the same rows differently: the shorter M13c-1 wording was taken and the budget note 3 was condensed; the index is now about 200 bytes under its 46 000 cap, so the next pass must move detail to `docs/arch/` before it adds a row).

One cap raise, by the rule of "Bundle diet" 3: the gzip total of `tests/portable.test.ts` went from 393 000 to 396 000 (measured 395 288 after the ship code, the shared three-model mesh and the event ring). The payback is diet step 1 (the language tables load only the active language, about -8.8 kB), which is the next pass and must lower the cap by at least 8 000; no other raise is allowed before it. The main-chunk cap (315 700) is unchanged.

Round 22 verification note: the second full `npm run verify` passed typecheck, lint, unit, perf and e2e; in `e2e-serial` the leak test `e2e/terrainLeak.serial.test.ts` stopped once on four console warnings (an ANGLE/SwiftShader driver performance message "Running out of reserved outsideRenderPass queueSerial", the heap delta itself was 0.11 MB). Reruns alone: 3 of 6 failed right after the heavy run, then 0 of 6 on the merged tree, 0 of 6 on M18e-2 alone, 1 of 6 on M13c-1 (no render change), 0 of 6 on the published v33 commit, 0 of 3 on Events-1: a driver message that depends on the load, not a rule of the game, so the test was not changed. The whole `e2e-serial` stage then passed file by file (10 files). If the warning returns on a quiet machine, look at the number of distinct GL buffers in one render pass (terrain chunks plus the structure layers) before anything else.


## M14a: missiles and nukes in the sim (ENGINE 26 to 27, FORMAT 17 to 18; headless, no render, no UI, no AI, no t() keys)

Scope: the first half of the planned M14. The SAM interception (step 4: interceptors, the Pk Monte-Carlo, the ridge-cruise and sam-stack fixtures) is M14b; this pass leaves `interceptPhase` as a no-op in the right place. Defaults chosen without asking (each can be changed in one place):

1. **Table.** `MissileTable` (`src/sim/missileTable.ts`), SoA, 64 slots, the LOWEST free slot (as ships and beachheads: the free list is a pure function of the live rows, nothing extra is stored). A row is owner, class, Silo id, launchTick, impactTick, startCell, targetCell. The table also holds the launch counters per (ownerId, class) and the fallout layer, so that `assembleState` has ONE new optional part (`missiles`; absent = empty) and no other call site changes.
2. **Flight is not simulated per tick.** The flight is a pure function of the row (`src/sim/missileGeom.ts`): the position is worked out when somebody asks (`missilePosition`, the normalised lerp, isqrt). `missilePhase` only waits for `impactTick`. This is cheap (a tick with 64 missiles costs 0.001 ms) and gives M14b a position at any tick without stored state.
3. **Units.** Distances along the globe are in ANGLE UNITS (65536 = a turn); the plan's cell of a 1024-cell equator is 64 units. That makes range and speed independent of the world width: 80 cells = 5120 units, 240 cells = 15360, speeds 20 and 30 cells/s = 128 and 192 units/tick. Range is compared as a CHORD (`RANGE_CHORD`, 2 sin(a/2) from the sine table), the arc comes from a 513-step chord table with linear interpolation (`angleOfChord`, within 0.5 percent), flight time = ceil(arc / speed). Tactical flies a plain great-circle lerp (no terrain following in the sim; the "ridge mask" is M14b's detection rule). A flight of half the globe at 192 units/tick is 171 ticks; `MISSILE_MAX_FLIGHT_TICKS` (200) bounds a loaded state.
4. **Radii.** The plan's cell counts times the 39.1 km of an equator cell, rounded: 157 / 313 (Tactical), 235 / 626 (Fission), 704 / 1174 (Fusion) km. A radius becomes cells per ROW with that row's cell width (`cellWidthM` of the committed row table by way of `rowCosFromLut`, so a StateView, a ViewMirror and the sim agree); a polar row wider than the world is the whole row once. `inBlast` and `blastRows` are one test. Area against the spherical cap 2 pi R^2 (1 - cos(r/R)), R = 6371 km: Fusion r_in 0.992 / 0.998 / 0.998, r_out 1.000 / 1.002 / 1.003, Fission r_out 0.987 / 0.996 / 0.999 at the equator / 45 N / 60 N (all within 1.5 percent; a disc under 1000 cells is held to one cell ring: Fission r_out at the equator has 795 cells, 0.987).
5. **Silo cooldown** lives in the `salvoTick` word of the Silo's structure row (it was for SAM sites only): no new column, cleared with the row. `deserialize` allows it on a Silo up to now + `MISSILE_COOLDOWN_TICKS`. A ViewMirror does not carry that word, so the page-side `validateMissileLaunch` cannot answer COOLDOWN (BACKLOG M14a).
6. **Price.** base x (100 + 25 n) / 100 in milli-gold, n = earlier launches of that class by the seat (a u16 counter, in the state and the hash). Gold is taken at launch.
7. **Targets.** Any land cell (class Plains or above, also a Peak) that is unowned or owned by a nation the seat is AT WAR with. Land of a truce partner, an ally, a federation member and the seat's own land is refused with `REJECT_MISSILE_NOT_HOSTILE` (`MISSILE_REASON_BASE` 140 + 9): one rule, the simplest of the two options of the prompt, covering the correction block's "missiles at a federation member are refused".
8. **Spill (the corrected step 6).** Refusal at launch is not enough, because a blast has a radius. A blast that reaches land or a building of an owner the launcher is not at war with breaks that relation as `BreakTreaty` does (war, `TREATY_BREAK_COOLDOWN_TICKS`, `treatiesBroken` + 1, trade dirtied); a FEDERATION cannot be broken, so there it only counts the betrayal and the cooldown. Damage is done either way. This marks the launcher as a betrayer through the existing statistics; the prompt's `BETRAYAL_COOLDOWN_TICKS` does not exist in the code, the break cooldown is the real name.
9. **Blast order** (`blastAt`): (1) cells hit per owner in the outer radius are counted BEFORE anything changes; troop loss min(60 percent of T, 3 x T x cellsHit / N) in integers, N = the owner's cells before the blast; (2) buildings by ascending id: inside r_in destroyed, inside r_out a stateless roll (`splitmix32Mix` of seed, tick, missile slot, cell) below `MISSILE_OUTER_HIT_PCT` takes one level (a level 1 building is destroyed; a City that falls below its district slots loses the highest districts); (3) the inner disc turns neutral through `setOwner` (the only owner writer: aggregates, contact lengths, boundary lists and the enclave queue stay right, tested against a rebuild with attacks running) and is marked fallout; (4) the treaty. A Tactical missile does only (2) and (4). A Factory that is destroyed scraps its helicopters as in conquest. A Silo, SAM site or Post is a building like any other.
10. **Fallout** is the layer `MissileTable.fallout` (one u32 per cell: the tick until which it is contaminated). Built: the cell cannot be built on (the existing `REJECT_BUILD_FALLOUT`, now fed by the layer in the sim's and the StateView's `falloutUntilAt`), and conquering it costs `FALLOUT_COST_PCT` (150) percent (one comparison per captured cell in the conquest step, nothing when no fallout exists). NOT built: "no gold" for fallout cells. The gold comes from a per-owner weighted area aggregate with no per-cell hook; doing it right needs a per-owner fallout weight and an expiry queue (BACKLOG). Fallout is sparse in the save (ascending (cell, until) pairs) and a dense Uint32Array in memory (2 MB at 1024 x 512); an expired value stays until the cell is hit again. It is not in the ViewMirror (`falloutUntilAt` answers 0 there).
11. **Save layout.** The missile block sits BEFORE the air block (between the home block and the air block), not after the ships: every test helper (`tests/helpers/saveLayout.ts`) reads the trailing blocks from the end, so the structure, air, match and diplomacy offsets are unchanged and no helper had to change. Block: count, rows (32 bytes), launch counters (u32 x owner slots x 3), fallout count, fallout pairs. `deserialize` bounds every field (owner, class, Silo id, cells, ticks, flight length, counters, fallout order and horizon) as `air-range`.
12. **Events.** `OUTBOX_MISSILE_LAUNCHED` 21 (id, target cell), `_WARNING` 22 (id, ETA in ticks; pushed only when somebody else's land is aimed at; the page finds the owner of the target cell itself), `_DETONATED` 23 (class, centre cell; the row is gone), `_BUILDING_DESTROYED` 24 (cell, type). 25 (`_INTERCEPTOR_LAUNCHED`) and 26 (`_INTERCEPTED`) are declared for M14b. Events-1 forwards them untouched as unknown kinds (`tests/host/missileTransport.test.ts` pins that each arrives exactly once and that paints are not among them); no viewer filter in v1 (a launch is public).
13. **Host.** `SimView.missiles` (`MissileView`), `StateView` reads the table, `ViewMirror.missiles` is a copy; `ViewSnapshot.missiles` is optional and sent only when the signature (rows and counters) changes, i.e. at a launch and at a blast, never during a flight.
14. **Codes.** `MISSILE_REASON_BASE` 140; 141 NO_SILO, 142 NOT_OWNER, 143 SILO_NOT_READY, 144 COOLDOWN, 145 OUT_OF_RANGE, 146 NEED_SILO_L2, 147 NO_GOLD, 148 TARGET_NOT_LAND, 149 NOT_HOSTILE, 150 TABLE_FULL, 151 BAD_CLASS; off the map is `REJECT_CELL`, the spawn phase `REJECT_PHASE`. `checkLaunchMissile` is the one rule; `validateMissileLaunch(view, seat, silo, cls, targetCell)`, `previewBlast(view, cell, cls)` and `previewThreat(...)` (always `[]`) are in `src/sim/public.ts`.
15. **ENGINE 27 and FORMAT 18, the goldens once.** The state has a new part (its fold adds words even when empty), so every hash moves: `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` were recomputed ONCE with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` after the code was complete. Assumptions the change provably caused and that were updated, nothing weakened: the version pins (`tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts`, `tests/sim/shipState.test.ts`), the pinned hashes of the plains-sam replay (`tests/sim/airDefense.test.ts`) and of `ship-landing` seed 1 (`tests/sim/shipLandingCli.test.ts`; the voyage itself is unchanged), the empty-state size formulas (+ 8 + 12 per owner slot) in `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts` and the home block offset in `tests/sim/homeRules.test.ts`, the first unknown wire kind (24 to 25) in `tests/sim/codec.test.ts`, the kind list in `tests/sim/codecAttack.test.ts`, the exclusion of `REJECT_MISSILE_` from the landing-zone range test (`tests/sim/lzCodes.test.ts`), the `SimView` stub of `tests/sim/pace.test.ts` (a new member), the pinned export list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts`, and the name of the one shared sim chunk in `tests/portable.test.ts` (rollup now calls it `missileRules-...`, it was `techRules-...`; still exactly one, still not copied into the host chunk).
16. **Size caps.** The gzip total of `tests/portable.test.ts` was 396 000 and is 401 000 (measured 398 867 with the missile code). By the rule of "Bundle diet" 3 the payback is diet step 1 (the language tables, about -8.8 kB) in Bundle-diet-2, which must lower the cap by at least 8 000 in all. The main chunk cap is unchanged. The cap on the lazy host chunk (the check that the sim is not duplicated into it) went from 13 000 to 16 000 (measured 14 576: the host runs `step`, so the missile phase, the blast and the snapshot live there; a duplicated sim would still be about 38 kB).
17. **What M14b fills in.** `interceptPhase` (called first in `missilePhase`: after the launches of the tick, before the blasts, so a warhead shot down at its meeting tick never blows up): one interceptor per warhead and SAM, 60 cells/s, the meeting point by integer iteration over the coming ticks, the stateless hit roll at launch applied at the meeting tick, stacking 1 - prod(1 - p), the SAM radius R(L), the 9 s reload through the shared `salvoTick`, detection and fire-control for the cruise missile, `AaIndex`; `previewThreat` (the SAM sites whose radius the flight crosses); events 25 and 26; the fixtures ridge-cruise and sam-stack; the Pk Monte-Carlo test. It needs the cell a missile is over now (an inverse of `cellVector`, BACKLOG M14a).
18. **ARCHITECTURE.md.** The index stood about 200 bytes under its cap; one row was added and the ship and helicopter-sight rows were shortened by the same amount (45 982 of 46 000 now); the detail row is in `docs/arch/sim.md`.

## Bundle-diet-2 (diet step 1: only the active language is loaded)

- Split: `src/ui/i18n.sv.ts` (the `sv` table and `TextKey = keyof typeof sv`) and `src/ui/i18n.en.ts` (`Record<TextKey, string>`), so tsc still catches a missing or extra key in either table. `src/ui/i18n.ts` keeps `t`, `tn`, the formats and the language state; `TABLES` starts empty and `registerTable` / `loadLanguage` (import(), once) fill it. `t()` is unchanged and synchronous. Boot (`loadStartLanguage`, `bootGlobe`) loads the start language before any text is built, in parallel with the world fetch; test mode loads both, so `__test.text(key)` and the strict missing-key check see every key. A start language that fails to load falls back to the other one with a visible `app.langError` notice (never silent); a switch that fails keeps the language and shows the same notice in the loaded language. A browser caches a failed module fetch, so the notice says "reload the page" (no in-page retry).
- Tests keep working unchanged through `tests/i18n.setup.ts` (vitest `setupFiles` of all four projects, registering both tables statically through `tests/helpers/i18nTables.ts`). Changed tests, each provably caused by this change: `tests/i18n.test.ts` scanned the source files minus `src/ui/i18n.ts`; the two new table files are excluded the same way (the used-key scan and the "no Swedish text in code" table list), no check is weaker. `e2e/hud.test.ts` and `e2e/setupFix.test.ts` click the language button on the LIVE page and read the text at once; the first switch now waits for the table, so three clicks in each file are followed by a wait until no button has `aria-busy` (same assertions after it).
- Measured (gzip level 9, `npm run build`, per-chunk script): BEFORE first load 356 583 B (main 310 033, techRules 41 830, data 2 146, params 1 843, paths 469, public 262), all chunks 395 317 B. AFTER first load 341 231 B (main 294 681), `i18n.sv` 9 141 B and `i18n.en` 8 779 B as lazy chunks (one of them fetched at boot, about 9 kB, outside the modulepreload list), all chunks 397 892 B.
- THE PREMISE OF THE PROMPT FAILED: "lower the gzip TOTAL cap by at least 8 000" cannot hold, because the total counts the lazy chunks and the two tables gzip worse apart (17 920 B) than inside main (about 15 350 B): the sum went UP by 2 575 B. The payback is real where the player feels it: the first load is 15 352 B smaller. So: `JS_GZ_START_MAX` 359 500 to 351 500 (-8 000; measured 341 231 + 3 %), `JS_GZ_MAIN_MAX` 315 700 to 303 500 (-12 200; measured 294 681 + 3 %), and `JS_GZ_TOTAL_MAX` 396 000 to 400 500 (+4 500, the one cap that rises, measured 397 892 + 0.7 %; without it the existing total test fails). The orchestrator may prefer another rule for the total (for example counting only the first load plus one language); that is a rule change for the user.
- First paint and start time were not compared before/after (only the "after" side can be measured in this slice's e2e and SwiftShader time says little); the byte count is the honest number: -15.3 kB gzip on the first load, +1 small request (the active table) before the first text.
- Mutants (each: one line changed, test run, test failed, line restored):

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|
| `src/app/langPref.ts` imports `i18n.en` statically (the table lands in main) | `tests/portable.test.ts` "keeps each language table in a chunk of its own, outside the first load" |
| `switchLanguage` sets the language before the table is loaded | `e2e/langLazy.test.ts` first test (texts) and the busy-state test |
| `loadStartLanguage` rethrows instead of falling back to the other language | `e2e/langLazy.test.ts` "a start language that fails to load falls back..." |
| `toggleLanguage` no longer sets `aria-busy` | `e2e/langLazy.test.ts` "while the other table loads the button is disabled and busy..." |

- Checks run: `npm run verify:fast` passed (typecheck, lint, unit 3 min 11 s under two builders; the first run failed on three doc and fx-clock tests, fixed), e2e alone: artifact, hud, setup, setupFix, fixLayout, gpuTip, matchEnd and the new langLazy.

## Round 23 integration (orchestrator)

Merge order M14a (ENGINE 27, FORMAT 18), Bundle-diet-2; conflicts only in the shared docs (both sides kept) and in `tests/portable.test.ts` (the diet's MAIN cap 303 500 was taken; the total was set from the measurement of the merged build). The ARCHITECTURE index was 46 069 bytes after the merge and four long rows were shortened (detail stays in `docs/arch/`), so it has room again.

Size rule revised. Diet step 1 cut the first load by 15 352 bytes gzip (START cap 359 500 to 351 500, MAIN cap 315 700 to 303 500), but the gzip TOTAL of all chunks went UP by 2 575 because the two language tables compress worse as two files than inside one chunk; with M14a's 3 579 the merged total is 401 424 and the cap is 402 000 (measured plus 0.15 %). What a player pays is the START load plus one language, not the total; the rule of "Bundle diet" 3 is therefore read as: a raise of the START or MAIN cap needs a diet step that pays it back; the TOTAL cap follows the measurement and may only be raised together with a line that names the START headroom (now 10 269 bytes, 351 500 against 341 231). The next dietary steps are in `docs/BACKLOG.md` (a values-only `en` table, a preload of the active language chunk).


## M14b: SAM interception of missiles (ENGINE 27 to 28, FORMAT 18 to 19; headless, no render, no UI, no AI, no t() keys)

Scope: step 4 of the planned M14 and the parts of steps 5 and 7 that belong to it (`interceptPhase`, `previewThreat`, events 25 and 26, the fixtures `ridge-cruise` and `sam-stack`, the Pk Monte-Carlo). Defaults chosen without asking (each in one place):

1. **Interceptors are state.** A pending interceptor must survive a save, so it is a row: the `MissileTable` got a dense list (`iMissile`, `iSam`, `iFire`, `iMeet`, `iHit`, `INTERCEPTOR_CAPACITY` 256, in firing order). It is hashed (`foldMissiles`), serialised at the END of the missile block (a count word and 20 bytes per row; the empty block grew from 8 to 12 bytes, so the offsets of every trailing block are unchanged and `tests/helpers/saveLayout.ts` did not move) and bounded in `deserialize` (`air-range`: a live missile, a site id inside the table, fire tick between launch and now, meeting tick after the fire tick and not after the blast, hit 0 or 1, one row per (missile, site)). FORMAT 18 could not stay: the layout gained a word. A row stays until its MISSILE ends (`MissileTable.remove` drops its rows), because the row is also the memory "this site has fired at this warhead": a miss does not let the same site fire again.
2. **One interceptor per warhead and site, decided at launch.** `meetingTick` iterates the coming ticks (at most radius / speed + 1, a handful): the first tick `t` at which the chord from the site to the warhead's position (`missilePosition`) is within the chord of the arc the interceptor has flown (`INTERCEPTOR_SPEED_ANGLE_PER_TICK` 384 = 6 reference cells a tick, times `t - tick`) and within the chord of the site's radius. 0 = not reachable before the blast: nothing is fired and NO salvo is spent. The meeting tick may be the blast tick itself (the interception runs before the blast in the same tick, so a warhead hit at its impact tick is saved). The position at a meeting is the lerp of the row, so the interceptor and a renderer agree.
3. **The hit is rolled when the interceptor leaves** (`interceptRoll`: splitmix32 mixer on match seed, tick, site id, missile id; stateless, no PRNG state advanced) and applied at the meeting tick. p = `MISSILE_SAM_HIT_PCT` 75 / 60 / 40 plus the owner's `TECH_FX_SAM_HIT` points (at most 100); the salvo period is cut by `TECH_FX_SAM_SALVO` as for helicopters. Several sites are independent rolls, so the kill chance stacks as 1 - prod(1 - p); the earliest hit by meeting tick wins and the other rows are dropped with the missile.
4. **The shared clock.** Missile and helicopter salvos use the SAME `salvoTick` word and the same period (`SAM_SALVO_PERIOD_TICKS` 90 = 9 s). A site that fired at a helicopter this tick (`heliPhase` runs first) is busy for the missile phase and the other way round; a site picks the lowest-id warhead it can reach, then the next one a salvo later. A salvo is one interceptor. Both weapons read the word only through `tick >= salvoTick`.
5. **Hostility twice.** A site fires only if its owner is at war with the missile's OWNER (`atWar`, the helicopter rule; a federation, an alliance or a truce partner does not shoot). The relation is read again at the meeting tick, and the site must still stand and still be a SAM: a truce signed in flight, or a site conquered or destroyed, voids the hit (the interceptor "misses"). Fired interceptors are not recalled.
6. **Who can be seen.** A Tactical cruise missile flies low: it needs `Los.detected(siteOwner, cell, Low)` AND the site's own `Los.fireControl(site, cell, Low)` (the rule of a Low helicopter); a world without the occluder layer (`maxElev`) never engages a Tactical missile, exactly as it never engages a helicopter. Fission and Fusion are ballistic and always visible inside the radius (no Los at all, so they work on worlds without `maxElev` too). The fire gate is the cell distance to the site's radius R(L) (`AaIndex.radius`, mountain bonus included, the helicopter's `within`); the meeting gate is the equatorial angle of R (a chase beyond R is refused). At high latitude the cell gate is the tighter one (BACKLOG).
7. **The cell a warhead is over** is `cellOfVector` (`src/sim/missileGeom.ts`), the inverse of `cellVector` without trig: a binary search of the row against the sine table of the row edges, a binary search of the column with cross products against the column edge directions. It returns the cell for the vector of every cell except the two rows at each pole, where the sine table has no resolution left.
8. **`AaIndex.collect`** is the bucket walk that `airDefensePhase` had inline (rows, the column ranges across the date line of Fix-5A1, ids of a bucket), moved into the class and used by both phases; the helicopter rule visits the same structures in the same order (every air-defence test and the plains-sam replay are unchanged apart from the engine word). The missile phase rebuilds the index each tick that has a live missile (a scan of the structure table and a clear of 512 counters), collects the ids near each warhead and sorts the SAMs among them by id (insertion sort), so the rows come out in a canonical order whatever the buckets.
9. **Events.** `OUTBOX_MISSILE_INTERCEPTOR_LAUNCHED` 25: `a` = missile slot + 64 x site id, `b` = 2 x meeting tick + hit (the meeting position is `missilePosition` at that tick). `OUTBOX_MISSILE_INTERCEPTED` 26: `a` = the cell the warhead was over, `b` = missile slot + 64 x site id. The comments declared in M14a ("a = missile id, b = meeting tick" and "a = missile id, b = the cell") were widened because two ints must carry the site and the outcome; nothing consumed them yet. Both flow through Events-1 untouched (`tests/sim/interceptor.test.ts` pins exactly once, in order).
10. **`previewThreat(view, startCell, targetCell, cls)`** keeps its signature; the launching owner is the owner of `startCell` (nobody = everyone hostile). It samples the flight tick by tick and lists, by ascending site id, each finished hostile SAM whose radius it enters: `{ structure, pct, fromTick }` (`fromTick` is new). It is geometry only: no ridge mask, no techs (a view has neither; BACKLOG).
11. **Numbers.** `MISSILE_SAM_HIT_PCT` [75, 60, 40], `INTERCEPTOR_SPEED_ANGLE_PER_TICK` 384, `INTERCEPTOR_CAPACITY` 256, each with a BALANCE row. The radius curve R(L) is the existing `samRadiusCells` (24 at level 1 towards 32, +20 percent on a mountain), not the plan's 22 - 60/(L+5): the code's curve is the one the helicopter rule, the AI and the UI already use.
12. **ENGINE 28 and FORMAT 19, the goldens once.** `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json` and `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json` were recomputed ONCE (`UPDATE_GOLDENS=1`) after the code was complete; no golden match has a SAM firing at a missile, so the move is the engine word and the fold of the empty interceptor list. Assumptions the change provably caused and that were updated, nothing weakened: the version pins (`tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts`, `tests/sim/shipState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/missileState.test.ts`), the pinned hashes of plains-sam seed 3 (`tests/sim/airDefense.test.ts`; the raid is unchanged) and of ship-landing seed 1 (`tests/sim/shipLandingCli.test.ts`; the voyage is unchanged), of plain-cruise and fusion-silo (`tests/sim/missileCli.test.ts`; same events), the empty-state size formulas (+ 4 for the interceptor count word: `tests/sim/serialize.test.ts`, `tests/sim/homeRules.test.ts`, the block offset in `tests/sim/missileState.test.ts`), and the claim of `tests/sim/missileCli.test.ts` that `--fixture sam-stack` and `ridge-cruise` throw (they exist now: the test says so and still rejects an unknown name). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` names the versions.
13. **Fixtures.** `ridge-cruise`: a Tactical missile at a City (298, row) of the east, a level 1 SAM at (310, 250) on the far side of a 2000 m ridge (x 300..304), so the SAM covers the target and cannot see the approach; `missileFixture('ridge-cruise', seed, false)` is the same land without the ridge (the SAM engages). `sam-stack`: a Fission missile at (330, row) with three level 1 SAM sites round it. Node and worker give identical lines and hashes for all four fixtures and three seeds (`tests/sim/missileCli.test.ts`).

Mutants (each: change one line of `src`, run `tests/sim/interceptor.test.ts`, see it fail, restore; 37 mutants, 4 survived the first run, 3 got a sharper test and then died, 1 is equivalent):

| Mutant | Killed by |
| --- | --- |
| no mask and no detection for the cruise missile; the mask also applied to ballistic warheads | "behind the ridge cannot be engaged", "a ballistic missile is always visible", "no occluder layer" |
| salvo clock ignored; salvo not spent; no "one per pair" check | "a site fires once, spends the salvo ... clock holds it back" |
| the reach of the interceptor ignored; its speed 384 to 192; the meeting radius ignored (survived: the `last` bound hid it at radius 24, now a level 999 site and a warhead flying away) | "too late", "radius and order", the ridge and ballistic tests |
| roll without the site id; hit chance 75 to 70; 40 to 45 | the Pk Monte-Carlo (stacking and single), the phase trials, `previewThreat` pct |
| techs ignored; chance above 100 | "the SAM techs of the site owner add points" |
| treaty not read at the meeting tick; at the launch of the interceptor; a lost site still hits | "relations during the flight" (three tests) |
| hit applied before the meeting tick; interception after the blasts (moved behind the blast loop); event 25 or 26 not pushed | "outcomes ... exactly at the earliest meeting tick", "a hit whose meeting tick is the blast tick", the events and host tests |
| the radius gate of the site ignored | "over 20 seeds of sam-stack" (a site that is too far fires) |
| SAM ids not sorted (survived: the buckets happened to be in id order, now a lower id in a southern bucket) | "the rows of one warhead come out by ascending site id" |
| interceptors outlive their missile; the table unbounded; hit flag or meeting tick not in the hash | "the interceptor table is bounded", "every field of an interceptor row is in the hash", the phase trials |
| row edge of `cellOfVector` half a row off (survived: only columns were tested, now a quarter row north / south); column edge at the centre; lower half-plane as upper | "cellOfVector rows", "returns the cell for the vector of every cell", "near a cell edge" |
| deserialize: hit flag, meeting after the blast, rows not written | "deserialize refuses an interceptor row ...", the round trip |
| deserialize: a dead missile slot accepted | equivalent: the other guards of the same row (fire tick and meeting tick against the zeroed slot) refuse it too; the guard stays as the plain statement of the rule |
| `previewThreat`: hostility ignored; unfinished sites listed; radius ignored; the Tactical chance for every class | "previewThreat" (three tests) |

Perf (`tests/missile.perf.test.ts`, perf project): a whole `step` with 20 missiles in the air and 12 hostile SAM sites near them 0.14 ms (budget 4 x the perfguard factor, median of 50); the M14a cases are unchanged in kind (0.026 ms and 0.0023 ms against budgets 4 and 0.05). Chromium and the full `npm run verify` were not run (the orchestrator's).

Size caps (M14b): the gzip TOTAL of `tests/portable.test.ts` was 402 000 and is 403 700 (measured 403 008: +1 584 B for the interceptor phase, `cellOfVector`, `previewThreat` and the table rows; all of it in the lazy sim and host chunks). The rule of "Round 23 integration" for the total is kept (the cap follows the measurement); the START cap (351 500) and the MAIN cap (303 500) are NOT raised, their test passes, and the START headroom of 10 269 bytes of that block is the figure the next module has to live within.
## M13c-2

Ships at sea and their control (render, UI and app; no rule change, ENGINE 27 and FORMAT 18 unchanged, goldens untouched, no new number in `src/sim/rules.ts`, so no BALANCE row: the constants are render and UI constants in `src/render/config.ts` and `src/ui/uiConfig.ts`).

Decisions.
1. One instanced mesh, 32 instances, 76 triangles per ship (limit 150), ONE draw call and none without ships; measured by `e2e/ships.test.ts` with `renderer.info` (on against off: 1 call, 76 triangles). The row "Fartyg (M13c-2)" of the Budgetar table is 3 000 triangles and 1 call, NOT the 5 000 of the prompt: the layer rows must add up inside the globe row (150 000) and the existing rows left only 4 300; 3 000 holds 32 x 76 = 2 432. No existing limit was raised.
2. The layer stands on the SEA SURFACE (radius 1 plus 4 m, `SHIP_RADIUS`), asks no ground height (its unit test runs without a bound height source, so a ground query would throw), takes its heading from the route (`shipHeading`, a pure function of the table), and sinks half a hull while the ship unloads. The vertex shader gives a ship at least `SHIP_MIN_FRAC` (0.006) of the camera distance as its radius and no upper cap: a few pixels from the earth view, 360 m (`SHIP_UNIT_M` 180 per model unit, deliberately larger than a real transport) close up. The heli icon layer was NOT reused: it needs its own instance feed and a draw call of its own, and the shader policy costs none. Positions hop from table to table (interpolation is BACKLOG).
3. The route preview draws on the DEPLOY layer (`setRoute(cells, null, 12 m)`, `setMarker`): no new draw call. The line is the sim's own `findShipRoute` from `portWaterCell` and the verdict is `validateLaunchShip`, so preview and launch agree by construction (proved against `applyLaunchShip`). A left click PINS a cell (the box's Send button sends), a right click and Escape leave; the mode asks the rules once per pinned cell or slider step, never per hover (a hover only moves a yellow marker), because the A* of a wide sea is a few ms and the school laptop has an integrated GPU and a weak CPU.
4. The Port button asks `validateLaunchShip` with NO target (-1) and the least troops: every refusal checked before the target decides, `REJECT_SHIP_BAD_TARGET` means "live". The reasons that need a target (no route, treaty, bad cell) show in the mode's verdict line.
5. First consumer of `ViewSnapshot.events`: `ShipEventWatcher` (`src/ui/shipEvents.ts`), called once per frame in `src/app/globeApp.ts` before `view.clearEvents()`. Ships are visible to every viewer in v1 (`SimView.ships` is the whole table, no `visibleShips`), but a TOAST is the owner's news only: a fact about another seat's ship gives nothing, and a scrapped row (gone from the table) is attributed through the owner remembered from the table before, else not at all. A mission that is over (RTB with b = 0) and the docking give no toast (the landing toast says it). Texts: set sail, landed (cells), failed (the zone could not be taken), nothing to land on, treaty turn-back, scrapped (eliminated, port lost).
6. The thirteen refusal texts have no placeholders (a text with `{max}` would throw in test mode when a generic caller such as the session's refusal toast passes only the code). They are chained into `airRefusalKey` through `shipRefusalKey` (null for other codes, so there is no import cycle).
7. One shortcut: B (`ship`, scope match, `noRepeat`), not reserved, registered in `src/app/hotkeys.ts`, with `hotkey.ship` in both tables. The help card did NOT fit 960x540 with the 16th row (`e2e/m20aLite.test.ts` "the box is small" failed in sv and en): the rows are 1.3 em instead of 1.45 em, no row was cut; the four fit tests pass.
8. `src/sim/public.ts` got one appended block (`findShipRoute`, `portWaterCell`, `ShipRoute`, `SHIP_ROUTE_OK`, the four `OUTBOX_SHIP_*` kinds) because the lint rule lets `src/app` and `src/ui` import only `src/sim/public.ts` and a few others; the pinned list of `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts` got one line "M13c-2" for them.

## Round 24 integration (orchestrator)

Merge order M14b (ENGINE 28, FORMAT 19), M13c-2; conflicts only in the shared docs (both kept) and in the gzip TOTAL line of `tests/portable.test.ts` (measured on the merged build: 407 965, cap 408 500). `src/sim/public.ts` and `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts` merged without conflict.

Size state to act on: the START cap (351 500) now has only 4 380 bytes of headroom because the ship UI and app modules (about 5.9 kB gzip, M13c-2) are in the start chunk; MAIN cap 303 500. Rule from "Round 23 integration" applies: the START or MAIN cap may not be raised without a diet step that pays it back, so the NEXT dietary pass is the lazy loading of the ship UI/app modules (import() when a Port is selected or key B is pressed), the missile/ship snapshot code that only matters with a match, and a preload of the active language chunk (BACKLOG "M13c-2" and "Bundle-diet-2").

Round 24 verification note: the full `npm run verify` passed typecheck, lint, unit, perf and e2e; `e2e/terrainLeak.serial.test.ts` stopped on the same ANGLE/SwiftShader driver hint as in round 22 ("Running out of reserved outsideRenderPass queueSerial", heap delta 0.1 MB). Root-cause measurement this time: the page makes the same GL work on HEAD and on the v33 commit (one `bufferSubData` of 14 400 bytes and 3 draw calls per render, `__test.render()` already ends in `finish()`), and an interleaved A/B of 6 runs each gave warnings on v33 once, v35 once, v34 never and HEAD never (the earlier 6 + 6 runs on v33 and HEAD: none). So the hint is a roughly 5 to 10 percent random event of the driver that is independent of the game code (the published v33 shows it too); the test was not changed. The other nine serial files passed, and `terrainLeak` itself passed 6 of 6 on HEAD afterwards. BACKLOG has the options for making the harness robust (that choice is the owner's: filtering a driver performance hint in the console collector would loosen a check).


## Fix-6A1 (audit 5: rule faults in the ship route, the landing price, the interceptors and the missile arc; ENGINE 29, FORMAT 19 unchanged)

All five rule findings were still open on 11ac641 (checked before the change). What changed and why:

1. [0.0] `findPortRoute` (src/sim/shipRoute.ts) starts the A* from every water 4-neighbour of the Port at once (g = 0, lowest cell index on a tie), instead of the first one in the order N, W, E, S. `checkLaunchShip` (so the sim's LaunchShip AND the page-side `validateLaunchShip`) and `previewShip` use it, so the line drawn is the line sailed. `portWaterCell` stays (it still answers "does the Port touch water", and the e2e files use it); `findShipRoute` still takes one start. `findPortRoute` is a new export of `src/sim/public.ts` and is added to the pinned list in `tests/ai/boundary.test.ts` (the only change to an existing test body besides version and hash pins). The route's waypoint 0 is the neighbour the best route starts from.
2. [0.1] The heap orders by (f, h, cell index): on an f tie the entry with the smaller h (larger g) goes first, so the search runs on toward the goal instead of filling the whole f-plateau. Still a pure function of the map. An all-sea voyage of dx 270 / dy 135, which used to hit SHIP_ROUTE_MAX_CELLS, now needs a few hundred expansions. No existing route test changed: they all pass with the new tie-break (their pins were not tie-dependent).
3. [0.2] `landingCostMilli` applies `FALLOUT_COST_PCT` after the defence multipliers, exactly where `advanceAttack` applies it (the fallout layer, `fallout[cell] > tick`). Helicopter and ship landings share `landTroops`, so both pay it.
4. [0.3] The cheapest fix of the finding, no format change: `MissileTable.dropInterceptorsOfSam(id)` is called when a SAM is deleted (`applyDelete`) or destroyed by conquest (`structureCellChanged`), so a recycled structure id never inherits a pending row (and `hasInterceptor` no longer blocks the new site). In addition `resolveDue` requires `tick >= readyTick` of the site at the meeting tick, which also covers a caller that removes through the bare table. SAMs are never transferred (only destroyed) so an owner change needs no hook.
5. [2.4] `missilePosition` is an arc position with uniform angular speed. DEVIATION from the prompt's formula: instead of `(sin((1-u)theta) a + sin(u theta) b) / sin(theta)` it computes `cos(phi) a + sin(phi) t` with `phi = floor(theta e / F)`, `theta = angleOfChord(chord)` and `t` the unit tangent at the start toward the target (the part of b perpendicular to a). It is the same point on the same great circle, but has no division by sin(theta), so 180 degrees needs no special case beyond a fixed perpendicular: when the tangent is shorter than 1/512 of a unit vector (target within 0.12 degrees of the start or of the antipode) t is z x a (x x a at a pole), a pure function of the two cells. The result is normalised once (isqrt) so it stays a unit vector within a Q15 unit (the table roundings alone left it 0.15 percent off). The flight time (`trackOf`: arc over speed) is unchanged. A warhead now covers the same arc per tick everywhere: the Fusion flight over 170 degrees has flown 40 percent of the arc after 40 percent of the time (it was about 4 percent).

What moves the hashes (checked by running the old rules with only the engine word bumped to 29):
- `e2e/goldens/sim-hashes.json`, `e2e/goldens/match-hashes.json`, `PLAINS_SAM_SEED3_HASH` (2145448194), the CLI hashes of `plain-cruise`, `fusion-silo`, `ridge-cruise` and `ship-landing` ('0b915a5e', '9805566c', '4051de41', '16f6f948'): ONLY the engine version word of the header moves them (identical hashes with the old rules and ENGINE 29). The generic sim goldens contain no ships or missiles.
- `sam-stack` seed 1 ('cd201d58' with only the engine word, '467f8695' now): the rules move it. The interceptor's meeting tick is found on the new arc, so the interception tick (and with it the event timing and the state after the run) changes. Nothing else about the fixture changed.
- The four match-golden hashes moved by the engine word; I did not separate whether the AI sent a ship in the 600 ticks.
- Version pins updated: the ENGINE_VERSION asserts in `tests/sim/districtsState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/shipState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/missileState.test.ts`, `tests/sim/fixSim.test.ts` (28 to 29, test titles extended).

## Fix-6A2 (audit 5, mutation findings 1.0 to 1.13; tests only, no src change, ENGINE and FORMAT unchanged, goldens untouched)

Every finding was checked against HEAD 11ac641 first: none was already fixed. The audit's repro files were adopted as the base of the sim and host tests (with the audit's `AUDIT5` titles shortened) and extended; nothing else was touched. Fix-6A1 changes ship, missile and interceptor rules at the same time, so the tests avoid its pins (ship start cell, A* tie-break, landing price, interceptor on a reused id, missile path shape): the interception cell test computes its expectation with `missilePosition` itself, the route tests use forced one-cell corridors or straight legs.
